# 2,030 everyday problems an app could solve

2030 distinct problems across 36 categories: 20 general-life categories first, then 16 specialized ones.

Each entry: **title** — the problem as people experience it · *who* · frequency · pain (1–5) · app-solvable (1–5) · current workaround · source.

Source labels: `reddit-quoted` = a page attributing the complaint to a Reddit thread; `hn`/`forum`/`article`/`app-review` = found on such a page; `knowledge` = well-known problem added from general knowledge, not tied to a specific page.

Source mix: knowledge 1559, article 298, app-review 81, forum 51, reddit-quoted 21, hn 20

# Part 1 — General, everyday life


<a id="home-household"></a>

## Home & household

*cleaning, laundry, chores, home maintenance, appliances, organization, storage*


### Appliances

1. **Appliance shows an error code** — The washer flashes 'E24' and the manual is long gone, so you hunt for the model sticker and dig through repair sites to learn what it means.  
    *anyone with modern appliances* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: googling the code, calling a repair line · source: [article](https://www.repairclinic.com/Repair-Library/Article/5/What-Your-Appliance-Error-Code-Really-Means) — "Many people have no idea where their appliance manual is"
2. **Finding the appliance model number** — Ordering a replacement part or filter requires a model number hidden on a plate inside a door or behind the unit, and nobody records it when they buy the appliance.  
    *homeowners and renters* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: pulling the fridge out, photographing the sticker · source: [article](https://www.appliancepartspros.com/b/how-to-find-your-appliances-model-number/) — "The model number will most likely appear on either a paper sticker or a metal plate"
3. **Missed product recalls** — Recalls on dehumidifiers, dishwashers or space heaters go unnoticed because nobody registers appliances or checks recall lists.  
    *every household* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
4. **Missing warranty when appliance breaks** — When a dishwasher dies people cannot tell whether it is still under warranty or extended coverage because the receipt and registration are lost.  
    *homeowners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: searching email for the receipt · source: [article](https://www.thisoldhouse.com/smart-homes/keep-track-of-home-maintenance-with-the-centriq-home-app) — "snap a product label... fetches the user manual, troubleshooting guides, parts info, safety recalls"
5. **Consumables for each appliance** — Each appliance needs its own filter, bag, descaler or pod type, and people cannot remember which size fits the vacuum or fridge when reordering.  
    *households with multiple appliances* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: reorder history on Amazon, keeping an old box · source: knowledge

### Chores & shared living

6. **Invisible mental load of the household** — One partner carries the constant background checklist of what needs buying, fixing, cleaning and scheduling, which never turns off and is invisible to the other partner even when physical chores look balanced.  
    *couples and parents, disproportionately women* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: keeping it all in one person's head, occasional arguments, 'Fair Play' style card systems · source: [article](https://www.healthline.com/health/relationships/mental-load) — "anticipation, planning, decision-making, and monitoring... the invisible, non-tangible tasks involved in running a household"
7. **Chore apps still require nagging** — Existing chore apps put one person (usually mom) in charge of setting up and policing tasks, so the mental load of remembering and reminding does not actually go away.  
    *parents and partners who manage the household* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: verbal reminders, shared lists, giving up on the app · source: [article](https://www.technologyreview.com/2022/05/10/1051954/chore-apps/) — "Chore apps were meant to make mothers' lives easier. They often don't."
8. **Dirty dishes left in the sink** — One person's dishes sit in the shared sink for days, sometimes with rotting food, and the others feel drained deciding whether to wash them, confront, or wait it out.  
    *people sharing a kitchen with roommates or partners* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: washing them yourself, notes, house meetings · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/renter-found-roommates-rotting-food-220000740.html) — "Renter who found roommate's 'rotting food' in the kitchen sink asks for a solution: 'I am so drained'"
9. **Unfair chore split with roommates** — Roommates have different ideas of what 'clean' means and nobody can prove who has or hasn't done their share, so irritation builds until someone leaves a passive-aggressive note.  
    *renters with roommates, students in shared housing* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paper chore chart on the fridge, group chat nagging, passive-aggressive notes · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.buzzfeed.com/alanna_marshall/most-passive-aggressive-roommate-notes) — "Chore chart coming soon. Until then... if I find your dirty dishes in the sink... I'm dumping it in your bed."
10. **Chore apps hide features behind paywalls** — Families try 'free' chore apps and find the useful features (multiple kids, recurring tasks, allowance) locked behind subscriptions, or the app crashes and loses their setup.  
    *families with children* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: switching between several apps, going back to paper charts · source: [app-review](https://www.mychoreboard.com/blog/best-free-chore-apps-2025/) — "Some 'free' versions hide all the useful features behind a paywall"
11. **Kids lose motivation for chore rewards** — Chore-and-allowance apps work for a couple of weeks and then children stop caring about the points, leaving parents back at square one.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: sticker charts, cash allowance, threats and bribes · source: [app-review](https://www.commonsensemedia.org/app-reviews/homey-chores-and-allowance) — "many families struggle with maintaining motivation and consistency"
12. **Rotating chores fairly over time** — Nobody wants the toilet every week, but hand-rotating tasks among housemates with different schedules and away weeks gets messy and someone always ends up with the bad jobs twice.  
    *shared houses, families with several kids* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: whiteboard rota, arguing · source: knowledge
13. **Feature-bloated family super-apps** — Chore apps morph into calendars, meal planners and messengers, so the simple job of tracking who does what gets buried and family members stop opening the app.  
    *families and couples using shared household apps* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: using a plain shared note or spreadsheet instead · source: [article](https://www.kiddikash.com/blog/best-chore-apps-2025) — "trying to become 'family super-apps'... which often results in feature bloat"

### Cleaning routines

14. **Not knowing where to start cleaning** — When the whole house is a mess the task feels so big that people freeze, make 'doom piles' of stuff they moved but never organized, and the overwhelm grows.  
    *people with ADHD, anyone behind on housework* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: throwing everything away, timers, body-doubling videos · source: [article](https://www.psychologytoday.com/us/blog/mythbusting-adhd/202306/is-your-adhd-making-you-a-doom-piler) — "DOOM piles, which stands for Didn't Organize, Only Moved"
15. **Cleaning products used unsafely** — People mix or use harsh chemicals without knowing which combinations are dangerous or what a product is actually meant for, following viral hacks that ruin surfaces.  
    *anyone cleaning their home* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: reading labels, guessing, viral tips · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.yahoo.com/lifestyle/people-sharing-worst-cleaning-tips-163000053.html) — "the amount of videos I see of people using wide varieties of chemicals with no gloves on"
16. **Cleaning schedule collapses after two weeks** — People set up a daily/weekly/monthly cleaning routine, miss a few days, and the app or checklist shows a wall of overdue tasks that makes them quit entirely.  
    *people trying to maintain a tidy home solo* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: restarting from scratch, ignoring the app · source: knowledge
17. **Cleaning tasks nobody remembers exist** — Things like the dryer vent, range hood filter, dishwasher filter and fridge coils are never on anyone's list, so they are only dealt with when something smells or breaks.  
    *homeowners and long-term renters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: noticing when there is a problem · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/articles/11-household-filters-could-forgetting-102000466.html) — "11 Household Filters You Could Be Forgetting To Clean"
18. **Deep cleans keep getting put off** — Infrequent jobs like cleaning gutters, washing windows, or descaling the shower head get postponed indefinitely because there is no natural trigger to do them.  
    *homeowners, renters before inspections* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: spring cleaning binges, guilt · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/12-home-maintenance-tasks-keep-200000780.html) — "12 Home Maintenance Tasks You Keep Putting Off but Really Need to Do ASAP"
19. **Guests coming, house not ready** — With visitors arriving in an hour there is no quick way to know which rooms matter most and what the minimum triage is, so people panic-clean the wrong things.  
    *hosts, families* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: shoving things in closets · source: knowledge
20. **Houseplants die from missed watering** — Each plant needs a different watering interval and people forget which were watered when, so plants alternately drown and dry out.  
    *houseplant owners* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: sticking a finger in the soil, watering everything on Sunday · source: knowledge
21. **Which cleaner for which stain** — A red wine spill on the couch or a rust mark on the sink sends you searching contradictory advice while the stain sets.  
    *anyone with fabrics, floors and surfaces* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: googling in a panic, trying random products · source: knowledge
22. **Hard to coordinate a hired cleaner** — Households with a cleaner cannot easily leave instructions, note what was skipped, or track which weeks were paid and which were cancelled.  
    *households employing a house cleaner* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sticky notes, text messages, cash envelopes · source: knowledge

### Home maintenance

23. **Comparing and tracking contractor quotes** — Getting three quotes for a roof or plumbing job means juggling PDFs, texts and voicemails with different scopes, and remembering who promised what by when.  
    *homeowners hiring tradespeople* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: spreadsheets, email threads · source: knowledge
24. **Home maintenance records are scattered** — Receipts, warranties, service dates and contractor names live in email, drawers and photo rolls, so when the furnace dies nobody can say when it was last serviced or if it is under warranty.  
    *homeowners* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a folder in a drawer, searching email · source: [article](https://www.homeplexi.com/blogs/best-home-maintenance-management-apps-2026) — "Homeowners Struggling with Tracking, Contractors & Projects"
25. **Not knowing what maintenance a house needs** — First-time homeowners have no idea which seasonal tasks (sump pump test, water heater flush, caulking, gutter cleaning) their specific house requires until something expensive fails.  
    *first-time homeowners* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: generic checklists from blogs, learning the hard way · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/11-detrimental-mistakes-first-time-211800808.html) — "57% of homeowners said they did not prioritize home maintenance enough"
26. **Renters unable to track repair requests** — Tenants report a leak or broken heater and then have no record of when they asked, what the landlord said, or how long it has been outstanding.  
    *renters* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: text and email threads, photos with timestamps · source: knowledge
27. **Forgetting to change the HVAC filter** — Air filters need replacing every one to three months but the interval is long enough that people forget, leading to higher bills and system breakdowns.  
    *homeowners and renters with forced-air HVAC* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: writing the date on the filter, subscription filter delivery · source: [article](https://techcrunch.com/2015/01/23/filtereasy-raises-1-2-million-to-help-you-remember-to-change-your-homes-air-filter) — "easy to forget because filters are only changed every few months or so"
28. **Forgetting which paint color is where** — Years after painting, touching up a scuff means guessing the brand, color code and finish for that room because the can label is gone.  
    *homeowners and long-term renters* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: keeping old cans, photographing lids, color-matching chips at the store · source: [article](https://getkeptapp.com/blog/keep-track-of-home-improvements/) — "so you can answer questions like 'what color is the hallway' later on"
29. **Seasonal prep sneaks up** — The first freeze arrives before hoses are disconnected and the AC is running before the unit was cleaned, because seasonal tasks depend on local weather, not a calendar date.  
    *homeowners in four-season climates* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: remembering after the damage · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/home-heating-mistakes-costing-money-144800098.html) — "These Home Heating Mistakes Are Costing You Money"
30. **Things that expire and get forgotten** — Smoke detector batteries, water filter cartridges, fire extinguishers, and toothbrush heads have replacement dates nobody tracks, so they lapse silently.  
    *every household* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: changing batteries at daylight saving time, otherwise none · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/articles/things-everyone-forgets-replace-shouldn-123000752.html) — "Things Everyone Forgets to Replace, But Shouldn't"
31. **Untracked home improvement spending** — Homeowners cannot account for thousands spent on improvements when selling or filing taxes because nothing was logged along the way.  
    *homeowners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: shoebox of receipts · source: [article](https://getkeptapp.com/blog/keep-track-of-home-improvements/) — "Most homeowners don't track home improvements and later realize they can't account for thousands"

### Laundry

32. **Clothes left in shared washer** — In apartment laundry rooms people leave finished loads sitting for hours, so others wait for a machine that is not even running or come back to find their wet clothes dumped on a dryer.  
    *apartment dwellers and students with shared laundry* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: moving the clothes yourself, waiting, signs in the laundry room · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.aol.com/articles/ever-ok-move-someones-laundry-141500058.html) — "One Reddit user returned... five minutes after their load was done and found their wet clothes on top of a dryer"
33. **Care labels are undecipherable** — Care labels look like hieroglyphics, so people guess the wash setting and end up with shrunk sweaters and ruined jeans.  
    *anyone doing laundry, especially younger people* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: googling symbols, washing everything cold, cutting tags off · source: [article](https://blogs.ifas.ufl.edu/jeffersonco/2020/01/29/cracking-laundry-care-code/) — "Most care labels can look like hieroglyphics"
34. **Clean laundry never gets folded** — Washed clothes pile up in baskets or on 'the chair' for days and get rewrinkled, because folding and putting away has no deadline and no one owns it.  
    *busy adults and families* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: living out of the basket · source: knowledge
35. **Forgetting the load in the washer** — You start a wash, get distracted, and remember it the next morning when it smells musty and needs rewashing.  
    *anyone who does their own laundry* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: phone timer, smart plug notifications · source: knowledge
36. **No free machine at peak times** — Everyone in the building tries to do laundry Sunday evening, and with two washers for dozens of apartments you haul the basket down only to find every machine taken.  
    *renters with shared laundry rooms* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: going at odd hours, checking repeatedly · source: [article](https://www.liberty.edu/champion/2025/01/22/the-dos-and-donts-of-doing-laundry-in-a-shared-space) — "attempting to do the chore when everyone else has the same task in mind"
37. **Sorting laundry for a mixed household** — Family laundry gets mixed up, socks vanish and someone's delicate item goes through a hot cycle because nobody knows whose clothes need what treatment.  
    *families and shared houses* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: separate hampers, labels, each person washes their own · source: knowledge
38. **Using too much detergent** — People eyeball detergent and overdose every load, leaving residue on clothes and buildup in the washer without knowing that is why things smell or feel stiff.  
    *households doing their own laundry* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: guessing from the cap lines · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.yahoo.com/lifestyle/people-sharing-worst-cleaning-tips-163000053.html) — "excessive use of laundry detergent... can actually lead to buildup on both clothes and in the washer"

### Organization & storage

39. **No home inventory for insurance** — After a burglary, fire or flood people cannot list what they owned or prove its value because nobody documented belongings beforehand.  
    *homeowners and renters with insurance* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: walk-through video once, receipts · source: knowledge
40. **Can't find the thing in storage** — Seasonal decorations, a spare cable or a document are 'somewhere' in the garage, attic or a bin, and finding them means opening every box.  
    *homeowners with garages, basements or storage units* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: labeling bins, memory, buying a duplicate · source: [article](https://www.goodreads.com/author_blog_posts/16980168-organize-me-now-the-ultimate-storage-organization-guide-a-chance) — "wonder whether an item they're looking for is under a pile of clothes or in a box behind the lawnmower"
41. **Buying duplicates of what you own** — People repurchase tape, batteries, lightbulbs or a specific screwdriver because they cannot remember owning one or where it is.  
    *every household* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
42. **Declutter decisions never get made** — Items sit in a 'maybe' pile for months because deciding what to sell, donate or trash, and then actually doing it, has no deadline.  
    *anyone decluttering, people with ADHD* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: bags in the trunk for weeks · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/lifestyle/home-filled-doom-piles-know-090003032.html) — "As more piles are created, the more difficult it becomes to tackle the job"
43. **Kids' outgrown clothes and gear** — Parents store bins of clothes in various sizes and cannot recall what is in which bin, so the next child has no winter coat while three sit in the attic.  
    *parents with more than one child* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: size-labelled bins, rummaging · source: knowledge
44. **Buying bins before decluttering** — People buy organizing products first and end up with more clutter, because they never took stock of what they actually have and want to keep.  
    *people trying to get organized* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: professional organizer advice, sort-and-purge first · source: [article](https://www.yahoo.com/news/professional-organizers-never-9-things-201800532.html) — "Buying cute bins before you know what you're keeping is a trap and leads to more clutter"

### Supplies & restocking

45. **Running out of household essentials** — You only discover the toilet paper, dish soap or detergent is gone at the moment you need it, because nobody tracks stock levels of everyday supplies.  
    *every household, especially shared ones* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: keeping a backup, subscribe-and-save, emergency store run · source: [forum](https://forums.anandtech.com/threads/noticed-that-i-was-out-of-toilet-paper-in-the-restroom-after-i-was-done.70260/post-23896035) — "Noticed that I was out of toilet paper in the restroom after I was done"
46. **Trash and recycling day confusion** — Collection days change for holidays and recycling alternates weeks, so bins go out on the wrong day or are missed entirely.  
    *homeowners and renters with curbside pickup* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: checking the neighbors' bins, municipal calendar PDF · source: knowledge
47. **Who used the last of it** — In shared homes the person who finishes a shared consumable rarely puts it on the list, so the next person is stuck and nobody knows who should buy the replacement.  
    *roommates and families* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: fridge whiteboard, group chat · source: knowledge
48. **Splitting shared household purchases** — Roommates buy paper towels, soap and cleaning products at different times and never settle up fairly because small purchases are not tracked.  
    *roommates* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Splitwise for big items, ignoring small ones · source: knowledge
49. **Whether something is recyclable** — Standing over the bins with a coffee cup or plastic film, people do not know what their local program accepts and contaminate the recycling.  
    *every household* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: guessing, 'wishcycling' · source: knowledge

<a id="money-budgeting"></a>

## Money & budgeting

*budgeting, bills, subscriptions, debt, saving, splitting costs, banking annoyances*


### Banking annoyances

50. **Money sent to the wrong person** — Peer-to-peer payment apps make it easy to pick the wrong contact from a list of similar names, and once sent the money is very hard to get back.  
    *anyone using Zelle, Venmo or Cash App* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: message the stranger and hope, or call the bank · source: [article](https://www.nasdaq.com/articles/3-things-do-if-you-zelle-or-venmo-wrong-person) — "3 Things To Do If You Zelle Or Venmo The Wrong Person"
51. **Overdraft discovered after the fee** — A low balance is only noticed when the overdraft fee shows up, because the bank's alert either does not exist or fires too late to move money.  
    *people with thin checking balances* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: check the balance obsessively · source: knowledge
52. **Pending charges hide the real balance** — The bank shows a balance that ignores pending card holds and scheduled payments, so the number on screen is not what is actually safe to spend.  
    *anyone running a low checking balance* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mental math and a buffer · source: [article](https://gulfnews.com/amp/story/your-money%2Fsaving-investment%2Fshould-i-be-worried-about-pending-transactions-on-my-credit-card-or-bank-statement-1.1702396726041) — "the annoyances of pending transactions in bank accounts"
53. **Bank exports only as PDF** — Many banks only offer statements as PDFs, so getting a year of transactions into a spreadsheet or budgeting tool means retyping or fighting with converters.  
    *spreadsheet budgeters and small-scale bookkeepers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: PDF-to-CSV converters or manual entry · source: knowledge

### Bills & due dates

54. **Annual bills blow up the budget** — Car insurance, property tax, registration and memberships arrive once or twice a year and get forgotten, so the month they land feels like a budget failure.  
    *anyone with annual or quarterly bills* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: sinking-fund spreadsheets, or absorb the hit · source: [article](https://marvinwealthexplained.substack.com/p/sinking-funds-the-reason-your-budget) — "forgetting to include quarterly and annual payments like insurance, property taxes, and car registration fees"
55. **Auto-pay fails after card replacement** — When a card is reissued after fraud or expiry, every service tied to it fails silently and the late fees and service cutoffs arrive weeks later.  
    *anyone whose card number changed* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: try to remember every merchant and update each one · source: knowledge
56. **Medical bills versus insurance statements** — Bills from providers arrive out of order with explanation-of-benefits letters, so it is unclear which bill is real, already paid, or should be disputed.  
    *US patients with insurance* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a folder of paper and phone calls · source: knowledge
57. **Bill due dates spread over many portals** — Utilities, rent, insurance and loans each live in a different website with its own login, and no single place shows what is due this week.  
    *anyone managing a household's bills* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminders and a password manager · source: knowledge

### Budgeting & planning

58. **Choosing what to cut each month** — When money is short, deciding which expense gets dropped this month is agonizing and there is no clear way to compare the trade-offs.  
    *households with tight budgets* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: gut feel · source: [forum](https://www.quora.com/What-is-the-most-difficult-part-of-budgeting) — "Prioritizing is difficult, as choices are tough and something will always have to be left out"
59. **Budget feels like punishment** — A budget reads as a list of things you can no longer do, so people avoid opening it and stop tracking within weeks.  
    *people who dislike budgeting* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: not budgeting · source: [article](https://www.fool.com/investing/2020/01/18/4-things-everyone-hates-about-budgeting.aspx) — "imagining themselves never shopping again, missing out on fun, and forever eating plain peanut butter sandwiches"

### Cash flow & paychecks

60. **Bills due before payday arrives** — Rent and bills hit on the 1st but the paycheck lands on the 15th, so every month there is a gap that gets bridged with a credit card or an overdraft.  
    *hourly and salaried workers on monthly or biweekly pay* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: hold back part of the previous check, or overdraft · source: [article](https://www.bankrate.com/banking/savings/how-to-budget-with-an-irregular-come) — "financial shortfalls happen 18% more when there is a greater mismatch between income timing and bill due dates"
61. **Income changes every month** — Freelancers, tipped and gig workers never know what next month brings, so a fixed monthly budget breaks the first time a slow month hits.  
    *freelancers, gig and tipped workers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: budget off the lowest recent month and keep a big buffer · source: [article](https://www.nerdwallet.com/article/finance/how-to-budget-with-a-fluctuating-income) — "almost one-quarter of U.S. consumers report that their income changes somewhat or a lot from month to month"
62. **Not knowing what is safe to spend** — The balance is known, but how much of it is already spoken for by rent, upcoming auto-pays and card statements is not, so every purchase is a guess.  
    *anyone paid monthly or biweekly* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mental math and a safety buffer · source: knowledge

### Couples & household

63. **Hidden debt between partners** — One partner carries a balance the other does not know about, and there is no low-stakes way to surface the full household debt picture.  
    *couples merging finances* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: an uncomfortable conversation · source: [article](https://jakeelm.substack.com/p/couples-and-money) — "about 20% of adults who are married or in a committed relationship report having secret debt"
64. **No shared view of partner spending** — Couples with separate accounts each think the other overspends, but neither can see the combined picture without exchanging screenshots.  
    *couples and married partners* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: monthly money talk or a joint spreadsheet · source: [article](https://jakeelm.substack.com/p/couples-and-money) — "63% of couples believe their significant other overspends in some way"
65. **Separating shared and personal spending** — Couples who keep individual accounts plus a joint one constantly have to decide which card to use and then sort transactions into 'ours' vs 'mine'.  
    *couples with mixed account setups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: rules of thumb and periodic true-ups · source: [article](https://dfpi.ca.gov/news/insights/personal-finance-for-couples-managing-joint-finances/) — "whether to merge everything into one joint account or maintain individual accounts and open a joint one"

### Debt

66. **Deciding which debt to pay first** — With several cards and loans at different rates and balances, it is unclear where an extra $100 should go and how much it actually shortens the payoff.  
    *people carrying multiple debts* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: snowball/avalanche articles and a spreadsheet · source: knowledge
67. **Credit card cycles out of sync** — Each card has its own statement close and due date, so keeping track of what is owed when, and avoiding interest, requires checking several apps.  
    *people with two or more credit cards* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: auto-pay everything in full and hope the balance covers it · source: knowledge

### Finance app frustrations

68. **Bank connections keep breaking** — Linked bank feeds silently disconnect every few days or weeks, so the budget shows stale balances until you notice and re-enter credentials.  
    *anyone using an aggregator-based budget app* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: reconnect repeatedly or import CSVs by hand · source: [forum](https://help.zoho.com/portal/fr/community/topic/bank-feeds-breaking-constantly) — "bank feeds breaking within days of fixing them by updating credentials"
69. **Forced migration when an app dies** — When a popular free budgeting app is shut down or folded into another product, users have to rebuild categories, rules and history somewhere new.  
    *former Mint users and similar* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: start over in a new app · source: [forum](https://www.teamblind.com/post/so-intuit-decided-to-replace-mint-with-credit-karma-which-i-think-was-a-mistake-h0wqnfu4) — "Intuit decided to replace Mint with Credit Karma which I think was a mistake"
70. **Budget app quit in the first month** — Setting up a serious budgeting app takes hours and a new vocabulary, so many people abandon it before it ever pays off.  
    *first-time budgeters* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: give up and wing it · source: [article](https://genwealth.io/articles/ynab-vs-monarch-vs-copilot-i-tested-all-3-for-90-days-heres) — "some users have rage-quit YNAB in month one due to it being too complicated"
71. **Budget apps cost real money** — The best-known budgeting apps charge over $100 a year, which feels absurd to someone budgeting precisely because money is tight.  
    *budget-conscious users* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: free apps with ads, or spreadsheets · source: [reddit-quoted](https://getfinny.app/blog/best-budget-apps-reddit-recommends-2026) — "Reddit users complain about the price of popular budgeting apps like YNAB, which costs $14.99/month"
72. **Falling back to spreadsheets** — No finance app fits, so people keep reverting to a hand-built spreadsheet that then needs manual data entry every week.  
    *detail-oriented budgeters* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Excel or Google Sheets · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=40108404) — "continually reverting to Excel spreadsheets and believe these apps often don't meet all their needs"
73. **Finance app locked to one platform** — A household with one iPhone and one Android cannot share the same budget because the app only exists on one platform.  
    *mixed-device households* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: one person tracks for both · source: [article](https://www.engadget.com/apps/best-budgeting-apps-120036303.html) — "only available to Apple users"
74. **Financial data locked in someone's cloud** — Years of categorized transactions live only inside a vendor's servers, and when the app shuts down or raises prices the history is effectively lost.  
    *long-term budget-app users* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: periodic CSV exports · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=45537796) — "struggling to find an app that offered data ownership, principled transaction handling, envelope budgeting"
75. **Free finance apps sell your data** — Free budgeting tools are stuffed with ads and monetize transaction data, and some follow up with sales calls from advisors.  
    *privacy-conscious users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pay for a private app or go offline · source: [forum](https://www.teamblind.com/post/why-do-all-of-the-budgeting-apps-suck-kyrsbf24) — "Mint is full of ads and sells your data, and Personal Capital advisors pester you with phone calls"
76. **Multi-currency budgets break apps** — People earning or spending in more than one currency find budget apps mishandle conversions and transfers, so totals never reconcile.  
    *expats, immigrants, frequent travelers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: separate budgets per currency · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=32417767) — "extremely slow transfers and inconsistencies with currency conversions"
77. **Month rollover in budget apps is clunky** — Closing out a month in envelope-style apps is slow and rigid, forcing awkward reassignments of leftover money every first of the month.  
    *zero-based budgeters* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: accept the ritual or move to a spreadsheet · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46566663) — "the way it rolls months annoys them, coupled with a clunky slow interface"
78. **Preset categories never fit** — Budget apps ship with fixed categories that do not match how a particular person actually spends, so transactions get forced into the wrong buckets.  
    *new budget-app users* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: misfile into 'Other' or move to a spreadsheet · source: [article](https://news.syr.edu/the-peel/2019/04/22/budgeting-app-reviews) — "categories are pre-set, making it difficult since budgets are not a one size fits all"

### Saving & goals

79. **One-off costs land as lump shocks** — Car repairs, holiday gifts and home fixes are predictable across a year, but they still arrive as painful lumps because nothing was set aside monthly.  
    *households without a dedicated savings bucket per expense* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: emergency fund or credit card · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/articles/one-simple-money-hack-keeps-172444974.html) — "always leaving your accounts in painful, unplanned lumps that felt like disasters"
80. **Many goals in one savings account** — Vacation, emergency fund and new-laptop money all sit in a single savings balance, so no one knows how much belongs to which goal.  
    *savers with more than one goal* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet or multiple bank accounts · source: knowledge

### Splitting costs

81. **Chasing roommates for their share** — One person pays the electricity or internet bill and then has to ask the others for money repeatedly, because they delay, and every reminder creates tension.  
    *renters with roommates* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: text reminders, a whiteboard, or quietly eating the difference · source: [forum](https://forums.anandtech.com/threads/roommates-and-bills.13271/post-22952985) — "they often delay payment, requiring repeated requests and creating tension"
82. **Bill-paying labor falls on one person** — In a shared household one person ends up holding every utility account, tracking due dates and fronting the money, and the others never see the work involved.  
    *roommates and multi-adult households* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: written agreement, or one person just does it · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/6-tips-splitting-expenses-roommate-220011552.html) — "The labor of paying bills can breed resentment when it doesn't fall evenly"
83. **Free bill-split apps cap daily expenses** — The free tier of the main bill-splitting app only allows three or four expenses a day and adds a delay when adding each one, so logging a weekend trip becomes a chore.  
    *roommates, couples and friend groups splitting costs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: wait until tomorrow, pay for Pro, or fall back to a group chat · source: [app-review](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/splitwise.com) — "cap of four expenses per day and includes a 10-second delay when adding expenses"
84. **Group trip costs across currencies** — On a trip abroad different people pay for different things in different currencies, and settling up afterwards means reconstructing exchange rates from memory.  
    *friends and families traveling together* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: one person keeps a notes list and estimates · source: knowledge
85. **Shared groceries impossible to split fairly** — Grocery runs mix shared staples with things only one roommate eats, so dividing the receipt evenly feels unfair and itemizing it by hand is tedious.  
    *roommates and couples with separate finances* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: split evenly and resent it, or keep separate shelves · source: [article](https://www.nasdaq.com/articles/how-split-living-costs-your-roommate-2016-07-06) — "grocery store expenses are often difficult to divide evenly, as different roommates may want different items"
86. **Surprise payment requests after outings** — Someone says 'I got this' at dinner or drives everyone to the store, then a payment request shows up the next morning with no prior agreement.  
    *friends, dates and social groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: agree on splitting in advance, or pay and grumble · source: [article](https://www.yahoo.com/lifestyle/7-times-never-venmo-request-174401326.html) — "would Venmo request every person in the car for gas money when driving to the grocery store"
87. **Tracking informal loans to friends** — Money lent to a friend or sibling lives only in memory, and there is no non-awkward way to keep a record or nudge them months later.  
    *anyone who lends to friends or family* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: forget, or bring it up awkwardly · source: knowledge
88. **Constant upsell prompts in split apps** — Every time you open the expense-splitting app you get nagged to upgrade, and the remaining free features feel confusing and half-useful.  
    *anyone splitting shared expenses* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: dismiss the prompt each time or switch apps · source: [app-review](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/splitwise.com) — "remaining free features pathetic, confusing, and largely useless"
89. **Small IOUs too awkward to request** — Amounts under about $20 feel too petty to send a request for, so they never get repaid and pile up into real money over a year.  
    *friends and coworkers who cover each other* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: let it go or hope it evens out · source: [article](https://thefiscalfemme.substack.com/p/venmo-etiquette-survey) — "62% of respondents said they'd only send a Venmo request for $20 or more"

### Subscriptions & trials

90. **Free trials convert without warning** — A free trial that needed a card ends silently and the first charge is the only notice, often for a service that was used once.  
    *anyone who signs up for trials* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminders or virtual cards · source: [article](https://gradynewsource.uga.edu/24248/) — "continue to charge after free trials end when customers forget to cancel"
91. **No proof a cancellation went through** — After cancelling a trial or subscription the charges keep coming, and there is no record of the cancellation to point to when disputing.  
    *subscribers of any online service* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: screenshots, support tickets, chargebacks · source: [forum](https://community.adobe.com/questions-27/canceled-free-trial-still-charged-623062) — "canceled free trial still charged"
92. **Collecting for a shared family plan** — One person pays for the group streaming or phone plan and has to collect small amounts from four people every month, forever.  
    *friends and families sharing plans* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: recurring payment requests, or stop asking · source: knowledge
93. **Recurring bills quietly increase** — A streaming plan, phone bill or insurance premium goes up a few dollars and nobody notices because the auto-pay keeps going through.  
    *anyone on auto-pay* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: none · source: [forum](https://www.quora.com/What-is-the-most-difficult-part-of-budgeting) — "fail to notice when a regular bill increases in price"
94. **Subscription trackers need manual entry** — Most subscription-tracking apps require typing in every service, price and renewal date and then keeping it current yourself, which is the same work you were avoiding.  
    *people with many subscriptions* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: spreadsheet or scrolling the bank statement · source: [article](https://www.whistleout.com/CellPhones/Apps/best-apps-to-track-and-cancel-subscriptions) — "requires entering every subscription by hand and keeping it current yourself"

### Tracking spending

95. **Small purchases drain the account** — It is the $20 snacks, takeout and work vending purchases, not the big bills, that quietly empty a checking account by the end of the month.  
    *people living close to their means* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: cash-only rules or willpower · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.nasdaq.com/articles/asked-reddit-how-should-i-trim-my-monthly-budget) — "$20 here, $20 there purchases being what claw away at checking accounts fastest"
96. **Amazon and Target purchases defy categories** — One Amazon or Target transaction can contain groceries, gifts and household items, so the budget app assigns it a random category and the totals are wrong.  
    *budget-app users who shop at big-box and online retailers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: open the order page and split the transaction by hand · source: [article](https://support.ynab.com/en_us/categorizing-transactions-a-guide-HyRl60sks) — "Amazon, Costco, and Target, where your purchases may be assigned to different categories each time"
97. **Cannot remember what a charge was** — Sitting down to review the month, there are too many transactions with cryptic merchant names and no memory of what was actually bought.  
    *anyone reconciling a statement* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: search email receipts or guess · source: [forum](https://www.quora.com/What-is-the-most-difficult-part-of-budgeting) — "too many transactions and you can't remember what you bought"
98. **Cash spending vanishes from tracking** — Anything paid in cash never shows up in bank-linked budget apps, so cash-heavy people have a hole in their spending picture unless they log every purchase.  
    *cash-envelope budgeters, tipped workers, market shoppers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: physical envelopes or notes app · source: [article](https://www.bankrate.com/personal-finance/cashless-cash-stuffing) — "regular trips to the bank or ATM to withdraw money can be time-consuming"
99. **Grocery spend hard to compare week to week** — Grocery costs swing week to week and there is no easy way to see whether prices rose, the basket changed, or spending simply crept up.  
    *households trying to control food costs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: compare receipts by eye · source: knowledge
100. **Refunds never matched to purchases** — A return is made but the refund arrives days later under a different name and amount, so whether the money actually came back is never confirmed.  
    *online shoppers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: scan statements and hope · source: knowledge
101. **Reimbursements owed by employer forgotten** — Work expenses fronted on a personal card get lost in the personal budget and some never get submitted or repaid.  
    *employees who front work costs* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photos of receipts in a folder · source: knowledge
102. **Piles of paper receipts to reconcile** — Receipts collect in wallets and drawers and matching them to card transactions for returns, warranties or reimbursements never happens.  
    *anyone who needs receipts for returns or expenses* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: shoebox · source: [article](https://www.fool.com/personal-finance/2007/05/10/10-things-i-hate-about-finances.aspx) — "paperwork, paying taxes, corralling piles of receipts"
103. **Transfers double-counted as spending** — Moving money between your own accounts or paying a credit card shows up as an expense in one place and income in another, inflating spending totals.  
    *anyone tracking several linked accounts* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manually mark each as a transfer · source: knowledge

<a id="food-groceries"></a>

## Food, cooking & groceries

*meal planning, recipes, grocery shopping, leftovers, food waste, eating out*


### Eating out and takeout

104. **No safe menu items for restrictions** — Diners with celiac disease or allergies walk into restaurants with no idea what they can eat and sometimes have to leave, which makes social meals stressful.  
    *people with allergies or medical diets* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling ahead, eating before going out · source: [forum](https://glutenfreeguidebook.substack.com/p/the-trouble-with-gluten-friendly/comments) — "walking into restaurants with no idea what they can eat"
105. **Collecting a group takeout order** — Ordering for an office or family means chasing everyone's choices in chat, transcribing them into the app, and then collecting money.  
    *office lunch organizers and families* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group chat plus a spreadsheet · source: knowledge
106. **Delivery fees and minimums surprise at checkout** — A takeout order that looked affordable doubles after service fees, delivery charges and tip are added at the last step.  
    *delivery app users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: comparing apps, pickup instead · source: knowledge
107. **Estimating portions when eating out** — People tracking nutrition cannot estimate portion sizes at restaurants, so their logs become guesswork on days they eat out.  
    *people tracking calories or macros* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: skip logging, guess · source: [forum](https://www.indiehackers.com/post/app-to-help-with-nutrition-meal-tracking-would-you-use-this-8fd18a3137) — "Portion estimation is a significant pain for nutrition clients who get messy when eating out"
108. **Group restaurant choice lost in chat** — Friends drop map links and opinions into a group chat, the thread scrolls away, and nobody remembers what was agreed when they meet.  
    *friend groups and coworkers eating together* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: one person decides for everyone · source: [forum](https://github.com/Klay376014/what-to-eat/issues/1) — "the thread scrolls away, and by the time the group is standing on a street corner nobody remembers what was agreed"
109. **Splitting the bill with shared dishes** — Dividing a restaurant bill fairly when people shared appetizers, drank differently and want tax and tip included requires awkward math at the table.  
    *groups dining out* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: split evenly, Venmo requests later · source: [article](https://kirbyyardley.substack.com/p/splithero-a-ux-case-study-d046141d55cd) — "Splitting a bill may require intense calculations, especially ... when you factor in shared dishes and drinks"
110. **Too many delivery options, brain freezes** — Opening a delivery app to hundreds of restaurants leads to 15-45 minutes of scrolling without deciding, several times a week.  
    *frequent delivery and takeout users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ordering the same thing every time · source: [article](https://medium.com/@shaiksameerrrr24/how-i-built-food-decider-the-app-that-solves-what-to-eat-in-30-seconds-2f8c24901f97) — "I open the app, see 200 restaurants, and my brain just freezes"
111. **Remembering what you liked at a restaurant** — Returning to a restaurant, diners cannot remember which dish they loved or hated last time and re-order something disappointing.  
    *regular restaurant goers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photos in camera roll, notes app · source: knowledge

### Grocery lists

112. **List sync fails between household phones** — Shared lists do not update reliably across partners' devices, so one person buys what the other already crossed off or misses new additions.  
    *households sharing a list* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: screenshots and texts · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/507605066/shopping-list-ease-grocery/reviews) — "difficulty setting up sharing, with syncing issues between household members' devices"
113. **Shared list only one person updates** — Household shopping lists fail because only one partner adds items, the other never installs the app, and bought staples disappear from the list when they need to recur.  
    *couples and families sharing shopping* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: texting items, fridge whiteboard · source: [article](https://gethomsy.com/blog/meal-planning/shared-grocery-list-app) — "A shared grocery list fails when only one person adds to it ... and bought items vanish when they need to recur"
114. **Shopping list rebuys food already at home** — Auto-generated grocery lists from meal plans ignore what is already in the fridge or pantry, so people buy duplicates of onions, rice and herbs that then go to waste.  
    *anyone generating lists from recipes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manually cross out items after checking the fridge · source: [article](https://www.summitplate.com/blog/best-meal-planning-app-with-grocery-list) — "asks what you already have so the list does not rebuy food that needs using"
115. **Accidentally deleting list items, no undo** — Swiping to check off an item deletes it instead, and with no undo the item is forgotten at the store.  
    *grocery list app users* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: re-typing from memory · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/331302745/listonic-grocery-shopping-list/reviews) — "can lead to accidental deletions, and there isn't an undo button"
116. **List not organized by store aisle** — Items on the list are in the order they were added, so shoppers zigzag across the store and backtrack for things they missed.  
    *in-store grocery shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually reordering or memorizing the store layout · source: [article](https://gethomsy.com/blog/meal-planning/shared-grocery-list-app) — "items aren't organized by store section"
117. **Repeated ingredients not merged on list** — When several recipes in a week call for the same ingredient, the list shows it multiple times in different units instead of one combined, shop-ready quantity.  
    *people planning multiple recipes per week* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mental math in the aisle · source: [article](https://www.foodieprep.ai/blog/meal-planning-apps-with-builtin-grocery-lists-a-2026-sidebyside-review) — "Repeated onions, rice, herbs, and proteins should collapse into one shop-ready quantity"
118. **Voice assistant list additions break** — People rely on shouting 'add milk to the list' at a smart speaker while cooking, and when the integration breaks the item never reaches the list.  
    *smart speaker owners* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Siri, or walking to the phone · source: [article](https://www.pann-app.com/blog/anylist-review) — "The Alexa skill for AnyList has been broken for most users since March 2026"

### Grocery shopping

119. **Delivery substitutions are wildly wrong** — Grocery delivery shoppers replace out-of-stock items with unrelated products, and the app shows them substitutes the customer never chose.  
    *online grocery delivery customers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: mark 'no substitutions', refund requests · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.cheapism.com/instacart-shopping-fails-according-redditors/) — "Instacart is notorious for showing us replacements that you guys didn't choose"
120. **Grocery stress drives 'struggle meals'** — Rising food costs make people scramble for cheap meals from few ingredients, but they have no easy way to plan a week under a fixed budget.  
    *households on tight budgets* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: searching 'struggle meals', rice and beans · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/articles/more-people-searching-struggle-meals-164650215.html) — "More people searching for 'struggle meals' as grocery 'stress' continues"
121. **Grocery prices change unnoticed** — Shoppers cannot tell whether their usual items got more expensive or which store is cheaper this week, so the bill creeps up.  
    *budget-conscious shoppers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: keeping receipts, spreadsheet price books · source: [article](https://groceriestracker.com/blog/best-grocery-budget-apps-2026) — "helps you spot when your usual items get more expensive"
122. **Items go out of stock after ordering** — Online orders arrive missing key ingredients because stock changed between ordering and picking, leaving a planned recipe unmakeable.  
    *online grocery customers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: extra trip to the store · source: [app-review](https://capterra.com/p/229777/Instacart/reviews/) — "things can go out of stock between ordering and delivery"
123. **Reading labels for allergens takes forever** — Shoppers with allergies or diets must read every ingredient list in the aisle, and reformulated products catch them out.  
    *people with allergies, vegans, celiacs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: buying the same brands forever · source: knowledge
124. **Which store carries which item** — Specialty ingredients are only stocked at certain stores, and shoppers make wasted trips or cannot remember where they found them last time.  
    *cooks using international or specialty ingredients* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling stores, memory · source: knowledge
125. **Comparing unit prices across pack sizes** — Shelf labels use inconsistent units and promotions, so deciding whether the multipack or the loose item is cheaper takes calculator work in the aisle.  
    *price-conscious shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: phone calculator · source: knowledge
126. **Fragmented loyalty apps and digital coupons** — Each store has its own app where coupons must be clipped in advance, and shoppers forget to clip or cannot find the coupon at checkout.  
    *grocery shoppers using loyalty programs* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: clipping everything, giving up on coupons · source: knowledge

### Leftovers and food waste

127. **Date labels cause premature tossing** — Confusion between best-before, sell-by and use-by dates leads people to throw out meat, dairy and produce that is still fine.  
    *all grocery shoppers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: throw it out to be safe · source: [article](https://refed.org/uploads/consumer-food-waste-report-2025-final.pdf) — "Leftover waste/spoilage and overreliance on date labels are identified as two of the primary contributors to food waste"
128. **Food wasted despite trying not to** — Nearly everyone says they try to avoid waste, yet most still throw food away weekly because they lose track of what they have and when it spoils.  
    *typical households* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: [article](https://olioapp.com/business/2026/03/09/90-of-people-try-not-to-waste-food-so-why-are-we-still-throwing-it-away/) — "9 in 10 respondents say they actively try to avoid wasting food, only 18% reported throwing away nothing"
129. **Forgotten leftovers found weeks later** — Leftovers get pushed to the back of the fridge, forgotten, and thrown out once discovered, wasting both the food and the effort of cooking it.  
    *anyone who cooks at home* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: clear containers, tape labels with dates · source: [article](https://www.seattle.gov/utilities/environment-and-conservation/my-home/reduce-food-waste/love-your-leftovers) — "two reasons we end up throwing out our leftovers: we forget about them, or we get bored"
130. **Leftovers nobody wants to eat** — Households cook too much of a dish, nobody wants to eat the same thing again, and the leftovers sit until they are thrown away.  
    *families cooking large batches* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: freeze portions, eat it anyway · source: [article](https://www.feedstuffs.com/market-news/survey-finds-u-s-households-waste-6-2-cups-of-food-per-week) — "People who frequently throw out leftovers because no one wants to eat them estimate wasting approximately 12 cups of food each week"
131. **Half a bunch of herbs rots** — Recipes call for a handful of cilantro or parsley but stores sell whole bunches, so the rest turns to mush in the fridge every time.  
    *home cooks buying fresh herbs and produce* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: jar of water in fridge, freezing chopped herbs · source: [forum](https://www.hungryonion.org/t/i-can-never-seem-to-get-through-a-bunch-of-cilantro-before-it-goes-bad/5170) — "I can never seem to get through a bunch of cilantro before it goes bad"
132. **Not knowing if leftovers are still safe** — People cannot remember when a container was cooked and guess whether 3-4 days have passed, erring on throwing it out.  
    *households storing leftovers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sniff test, masking tape dates · source: [article](https://www.nbcnews.com/better/lifestyle/guide-leftovers-how-store-them-safely-when-toss-them-ncna868316) — "cooked leftovers are good in the fridge for 3 to 4 days"

### Meal planning

133. **ADHD makes planning and cooking overwhelming** — People with ADHD find the chain of deciding, shopping, prepping and cooking from scratch too many steps, so they skip meals or default to takeout.  
    *adults with ADHD or executive-function difficulties* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: limit cooking from scratch to a few nights · source: [article](https://jennyedenberkmsed.substack.com/p/cooking-and-meal-planning-for-distractible) — "limit the number of workday evenings they have to create meals from scratch"
134. **Dinner decision fatigue every evening** — By dinner time people are too mentally drained to decide what to cook, so the question 'what's for dinner' is dreaded daily and often ends in takeout.  
    *working adults and parents* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: weekly formulas like Taco Tuesday, meal kits, takeout · source: [article](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250127681988/en) — "46% of Americans admitting they would willingly ditch social media forever just to escape the hassle of planning meals"
135. **Same three meals for picky household** — The household cook is stuck making the same few meals over and over because family members reject everything else, and gets fed up.  
    *parents and partners cooking for picky eaters* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: cook separate meals or stop cooking for them · source: [reddit-quoted](https://creators.yahoo.com/lifestyle/story/she-stopped-cooking-for-her-picky-husband-and-son-and-the-internet-is-cheering-her-on-085452743.html) — "she got fed up making the same three meals again and again"
136. **Sole cook burnout for the family** — One person carries the full mental load of planning and cooking every meal for a household where nobody else cooks, and finds it exhausting.  
    *primary household cooks* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: [article](https://www.foxnews.com/food-drink/woman-finds-meal-prep-exhausting-slammed-not-cooking-fancier-foods.amp) — "the responsibility of cooking and meal prep as honestly exhausting"
137. **Conflicting dietary needs in one household** — One family member needs calorie-dense food, another wants vegetables, another has an allergy, and planning a single dinner that satisfies everyone is a weekly headache.  
    *families with mixed diets* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: cooking multiple variants, or ignoring some needs · source: [article](https://erinhmoon.substack.com/p/lil-treasures-108-giveaways/comments) — "meal planning that is not boring and meets all their family's needs"
138. **Couples argue over what to eat** — Partners repeatedly get stuck in 'I don't know, what do you want?' loops and the nightly negotiation over dinner becomes a source of friction.  
    *couples and cohabiting partners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: one person always decides, or coin flips · source: [article](https://getsync.substack.com/p/meal-planning) — "Deciding what to eat can sometimes feel like a major negotiation between partners"
139. **Meal plan breaks with irregular schedules** — Shift work, late meetings and other household members unexpectedly cooking make a plan made on Sunday useless by Wednesday.  
    *shift workers and busy families* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: stop planning, freeze extra portions · source: [article](https://healthunlocked.com/weight-loss-support/posts/130666854/struggling-with-planning) — "Shift patterns and unpredictability from other household members cooking make advance planning difficult"
140. **Meal planners cannot rotate own recipes** — People want a fixed set of their own recipes to rotate every few weeks and produce a weekly shopping list, but existing planners are rigid, diet-specific and overcomplicated.  
    *home cooks who plan weekly dinners* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheets, paper lists, or abandoning planning apps · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=32316604) — "a list of recipes which I rotate round every 4-6 weeks, which then creates a shopping list each week, minus any ingredients I still have"
141. **Packing varied school lunches daily** — Parents run out of lunchbox ideas the child will actually eat, and end up packing the same sandwich every day.  
    *parents of school-age children* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pinterest, repetition · source: knowledge
142. **Meal plan ignores nights out** — A weekly plan does not account for a dinner out or late meeting, so an ingredient bought for that night spoils.  
    *people with active social calendars* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: checking the calendar manually · source: knowledge

### Pantry and inventory

143. **Bulk purchases expire before use** — Warehouse-club packs of yogurt, bread or produce are bigger than the household can eat, and half is thrown out.  
    *warehouse-club and bulk shoppers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: freezing, splitting with neighbors · source: knowledge
144. **Running out of staples unnoticed** — Coffee, rice or toilet-paper-level basics run out at the worst moment because nobody notices the last one being used.  
    *households* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: keeping a backup, emergency store runs · source: knowledge
145. **Buying duplicates of pantry staples** — Unsure whether there is cumin or soy sauce at home, shoppers buy another and end up with three open bottles.  
    *all home cooks* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photographing the pantry before shopping · source: knowledge
146. **Not knowing what is in the freezer** — Frozen portions and meats pile up unlabelled, so people cannot tell what is there or how old it is and buy more of the same.  
    *households with a chest or drawer freezer* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: masking tape labels, occasional clear-outs · source: knowledge
147. **Opened condiments of unknown age** — Jars and sauces are opened, pushed into the fridge door and nobody knows if they were opened last month or last year.  
    *households* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: guessing, writing dates on lids · source: knowledge

### Recipes and cooking

148. **Forgetting to thaw meat in time** — Dinner plans fall apart because the chicken is still frozen at 6pm; nobody remembered to move it to the fridge the night before.  
    *households cooking from frozen* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: cold-water thaw, takeout · source: knowledge
149. **Meal prep boredom by midweek** — Batch-cooked Sunday meals taste fine on Monday but by Thursday people are sick of them and order takeout anyway.  
    *meal preppers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: prep components instead of full meals · source: [article](https://www.vice.com/en/article/a-deep-dive-into-the-intense-world-of-the-rmealprepsunday-subreddit/) — "who would want to eat leftovers all day, every day, for a week straight"
150. **Missing one ingredient mid-recipe** — Halfway through cooking the cook discovers they lack buttermilk or an herb and does not know what substitute will work.  
    *home cooks* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: frantic web search, skip the ingredient · source: knowledge
151. **Nutrition unknown for home-cooked meals** — People tracking macros cannot easily get per-serving nutrition for a recipe they cooked themselves, so they log estimates.  
    *calorie and macro trackers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: entering each ingredient manually · source: knowledge
152. **Planners reject your own family recipes** — Meal planning apps only work with their own recipe database, so people with established family recipes cannot use them to plan or shop.  
    *cooks with personal recipe collections* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual lists · source: [forum](https://www.indiehackers.com/post/roast-our-ai-meal-planning-app-3ae7250940) — "apps that only offer recipes from their own database don't work for users who have their own established recipes"
153. **Recipes that simply do not work** — Online recipes contain errors or missing steps, so people must read comments first to find out whether a recipe actually works and what to fix.  
    *home cooks trying new recipes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: reading comments, sticking to trusted authors · source: [article](https://nishachittal.substack.com/p/the-online-recipe-debate) — "reading the comments on online recipes before making them to try and catch errors, omissions"
154. **Scrolling past life stories for recipes** — Online recipes bury ingredients and steps under ads, autoplay videos and long personal essays, which is maddening with wet hands mid-cook.  
    *anyone cooking from online recipes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: jump-to-recipe buttons, reader mode, recipe extractors · source: [article](https://www.goodreads.com/author_blog_posts/16368856-six-of-the-worst-things-about-recipe-websites) — "Spare me the ads and the boring life stories about your grandma and hurry up and just give me the recipe"
155. **Timing several dishes to finish together** — Cooking a meal with three components means juggling start times so everything is hot at once, and something usually ends up cold or burnt.  
    *people cooking multi-dish meals or hosting* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: multiple phone timers, scribbled schedules · source: knowledge
156. **Inconsistent cup-to-gram conversions** — Cookbooks and sites disagree on what a cup of flour weighs, and scaling volume recipes up or down yields awkward fractions and failed bakes.  
    *bakers and cooks using foreign recipes* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: conversion charts, guessing · source: [article](https://davidlebovitz.substack.com/p/measuring-cups) — "metric conversions for 1 cup of flour run the gamut ... ranging from 120 grams to 142 grams"
157. **Phone screen locks mid-recipe** — While cooking with messy hands the phone locks or the page scrolls away, and the cook cannot see the next step or amount.  
    *people cooking from a phone* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: propping the phone, tapping with a knuckle · source: knowledge
158. **Recipes from scraps of paper and screenshots** — Family recipes live on index cards, screenshots, browser bookmarks and texts from mom, so finding the right one takes longer than cooking.  
    *home cooks with scattered recipe collections* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app, photo album · source: knowledge
159. **Saved social media recipes never cooked** — People save dozens of TikTok and Instagram recipes, but the saves are just pointers to posts that vanish and are never surfaced when planning dinner.  
    *social media users who cook* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: screenshots, Collections folders · source: [article](https://www.vimafitness.com/blog/save-recipes-from-tiktok-instagram/) — "of the people who save those recipes, only about a third say they always or often actually make them"
160. **Scaling a recipe for one or eight** — Recipes serve four, but the cook lives alone or is hosting eight, and scaling every ingredient, pan size and cooking time by hand invites errors.  
    *singles and hosts* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mental math, making too much · source: knowledge

### Shared households

161. **Groceries gone before you touch them** — Food bought for a planned meal is eaten by a roommate or their guest before it can be cooked, ruining the plan and the budget.  
    *people in shared housing* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: locking freezers, confrontation · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.aol.com/articles/man-says-roommate-eats-groceries-230000805.html) — "Roommate Eats All His Groceries Before He Has a Chance to Even 'Touch Them'"
162. **Roommate uses shared staples, never replaces** — In shared kitchens milk, eggs and coffee get used by others without being replaced, and there is no record of who bought what.  
    *renters with roommates* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: labels, fridge dividers, mini fridges · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.newsweek.com/roommate-fridge-conflict-viral-reddit-2125192) — "kept 'accidentally' using shared items like milk, eggs, and coffee without consistently replacing them"
163. **Splitting grocery costs between roommates** — Shared groceries are bought by whoever goes to the store, and settling who owes what for shared versus personal items is a monthly argument.  
    *roommates and shared households* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Splitwise, rough estimates · source: knowledge

<a id="health-fitness"></a>

## Health, fitness & sleep

*exercise, weight, sleep, medication, symptoms, doctor visits, hydration*


### Exercise motivation

164. **Losing motivation after a missed week** — One missed week from travel or illness breaks the streak and the habit, and most people never restart the routine.  
    *casual exercisers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
165. **Finding a workout partner with matching schedule** — Exercising alone is easy to skip, but finding someone nearby at the same level and free at the same hours is hard.  
    *people who train better with accountability* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ask friends or pay a trainer · source: knowledge
166. **Neglecting physio exercises between sessions** — Physiotherapists hand out a sheet of rehab exercises and patients forget the form, the reps and to do them at all.  
    *people recovering from injury* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paper handout on the fridge · source: knowledge
167. **Sitting all day without moving** — Remote and desk workers go hours without standing, stretching or walking and only notice when their back hurts at night.  
    *desk-bound office and remote workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: watch stand reminders that get ignored · source: knowledge

### Food & weight tracking

168. **Ads interrupt every food log** — Logging a single meal in a free calorie tracker means sitting through a full-screen, unskippable video ad, so people log less or give up.  
    *people counting calories on free-tier apps* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: pay for premium, switch apps, or stop logging · source: [reddit-quoted](https://nutrola.app/en/blog/what-does-reddit-think-about-calorie-tracking-apps-2026) — "I'm trying to log my lunch and I'm watching a 30-second ad for candy bars."
169. **Daily manual logging becomes unsustainable** — Entering every meal and workout by hand takes so much effort that most people abandon the app within a month or two.  
    *anyone starting a health-tracking habit* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: log sporadically or quit · source: [article](https://www.ready4s.com/blog/7-things-people-hate-in-fitness-apps) — "hard for users to spend time logging their food every single day, which is why many users abandon these apps"
170. **Duplicate food entries with conflicting calories** — Searching for a common food returns dozens of user-submitted entries with wildly different calorie counts, and you never know which one is right.  
    *anyone using a crowd-sourced food database* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pick the entry with the most confirmations or type the label by hand · source: [reddit-quoted](https://nutrola.app/en/blog/what-does-reddit-think-about-calorie-tracking-apps-2026) — "the same food can have wildly different calorie counts depending on which entry you select"
171. **Barcode scan returns stale nutrition data** — Manufacturers reformulate products but the barcode stays the same, so the scanner silently logs last year's calories and macros.  
    *people tracking macros closely for weight loss* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: compare scan result to the physical label every time · source: [article](https://nutrola.app/en/blog/myfitnesspal-barcode-scanner-not-accurate-better-options-2026) — "the barcode often stays the same, and the old MyFitnessPal entry does not get updated"
172. **Basic logging features moved behind paywall** — Features people relied on daily, like barcode scanning and macro goals, got removed from the free tier, breaking an established habit.  
    *long-time free users of calorie trackers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: migrate to another app and lose history · source: [app-review](https://unstar.app/app/com.myfitnesspal.android?country=au&platform=android) — "essential features like barcode scanning now requiring a paid subscription"
173. **Daily scale fluctuations feel like failure** — Weight swings two or three pounds day to day from water and salt, so a single weigh-in hides the real trend and demoralizes people who are actually losing.  
    *people trying to lose or gain weight* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: weigh weekly only, or keep a spreadsheet with a moving average · source: knowledge
174. **Eating out with no nutrition info** — Independent restaurants and takeaway have no listed calories, so a single dinner out blows up a whole day of careful tracking.  
    *calorie counters who eat out weekly* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: pick a generic entry and hope · source: knowledge
175. **Logging home-cooked meals and leftovers** — A pot of stew split over four days has no barcode; estimating a portion of a mixed recipe is guesswork and people give up on accuracy.  
    *people who cook at home and track intake* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: enter a rough guess or a similar restaurant item · source: knowledge

### Hydration

176. **Hydration reminders not worth the effort** — Persistent nagging plus the effort of tapping a glass each time outweighs any benefit, so nobody keeps hydration tracking up.
