    *anyone who has tried a water tracker* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: keep a marked bottle on the desk · source: [article](https://nuunlife.com/blogs/news/get-the-scoop-on-water-tracking-apps) — "no one felt the use of these water tracking apps were worth the effort it took to use them"
177. **Ad before logging one glass** — A long ad plays each time the app opens, just to record a single glass of water.  
    *free-tier water tracker users* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: log less often · source: [app-review](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.mobilecreatures.drinkwater&hl=en_US) — "long ads they have to wait for every time they open the app to track just one glass of water"
178. **Hydration target ignores heat and exercise** — Fixed daily water goals do not change on a hot day or after a long run, so the target is either too low or feels arbitrary.  
    *active people and outdoor workers* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: drink to thirst · source: knowledge
179. **Water app scolds for batch logging** — Logging the day's water in one go at night gets you told off for 'drowning your plant', so the gamification punishes the honest user.  
    *people who log hydration retrospectively* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: stop using the app · source: [article](https://nuunlife.com/blogs/news/get-the-scoop-on-water-tracking-apps) — "the app would chastise them for "drowning" their plant"
180. **Water reminder hijacks the whole screen** — Hydration reminders open the full app instead of showing a normal notification, interrupting whatever you were doing.  
    *people using water-reminder apps at work* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: turn reminders off · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/653031147/waterminder-water-tracker/reviews) — "the whole app opens up on the display instead of being a normal notification that pops up"

### Medication

181. **Managing a parent's medication list remotely** — Adult children coordinating an elderly parent's ten prescriptions across several doctors and pharmacies have no shared, current list of what, when and why.  
    *family caregivers* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: shared note or paper list that goes stale · source: knowledge
182. **Complex dosing schedules are confusing** — Tapering steroids, alternate-day doses or 'take with food, two hours from the other one' rules are impossible to hold in your head.  
    *patients on multiple or tapered medications* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: handwritten chart on the fridge · source: knowledge
183. **Missed doses go unnoticed for days** — With no feedback loop, people discover they skipped their thyroid or blood pressure medication for two days only when they feel off.  
    *people on long-term daily medication* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: check the pill box count · source: [forum](https://healthunlocked.com/thyroiduk/posts/151025567/didn-t-take-levo-for-two-days-forgot-them) — "Didn't take levo for two days (forgot them)"
184. **Running out before the refill arrives** — Refills need requesting days ahead, but people only notice the bottle is empty on the last pill and then miss doses waiting for the pharmacy.  
    *people on repeat prescriptions* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminder set by hand · source: knowledge
185. **Unsure whether today's pill was taken** — Halfway through the morning you cannot remember if you already took the tablet, and both skipping and double-dosing are risky.  
    *anyone on a daily medication* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: pill organizer with day compartments · source: [article](https://www.medicalnewstoday.com/articles/drugs-did-i-take-my-medication) — "If you're unsure whether you've taken your medication, talk with your doctor or pharmacist before taking another dose."
186. **Medication reminders for time-zone travel** — Crossing time zones, it is unclear whether to take the pill at local 8am or home 8am, and fixed reminders fire at the wrong hour.  
    *travelers on timed medication* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: keep the phone on home time · source: knowledge
187. **Side effects not linked to medication changes** — A new headache or nausea starts and nobody remembers that a dose was changed three weeks ago, so cause and effect never get connected.  
    *people starting or adjusting medication* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### Sleep

188. **Bedtime keeps drifting later** — Without a cue, scrolling pushes bedtime later each night until the week's sleep debt is huge and mornings are miserable.  
    *night owls and phone scrollers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: phone alarms that get dismissed · source: knowledge
189. **Not knowing why sleep was bad** — Some nights are terrible and some fine, but caffeine timing, alcohol, late screens, exercise and stress are never lined up against the sleep data to show what mattered.  
    *people with inconsistent sleep* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: guess · source: [article](https://www.callondoc.com/en/blogs/sleep-tracking-guide-how-to-monitor-your-sleep-and-improve-insomnia)
190. **Shift work wrecks sleep schedule planning** — Rotating shifts make it unclear when to sleep, nap and stop drinking coffee for each new rotation, and generic sleep advice assumes a 9-to-5.  
    *nurses, factory and hospitality shift workers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
191. **Manual sleep diary is tedious** — Doctors ask for a two-week sleep diary of bed time, wake time and awakenings, but filling it in by hand each morning is easily forgotten.  
    *people being assessed for insomnia* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper diary from the clinic · source: [article](https://www.sleepfoundation.org/sleep-diary)
192. **Sleep tracker scores cause sleep anxiety** — Seeing a bad sleep score in the morning makes people feel worse and lie awake worrying about the next number, a pattern now called orthosomnia.  
    *wearable owners with light sleep issues* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: stop wearing the device at night · source: [article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthosomnia)
193. **Snoozing through the alarm repeatedly** — Hitting snooze five times means waking late and groggy, and standard alarms do nothing to break the loop.  
    *heavy sleepers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: put the phone across the room · source: knowledge

### Symptoms & doctor visits

194. **Booking an appointment is a hassle** — Getting an appointment means phone queues and callbacks during work hours, and many people simply skip care rather than deal with it.  
    *working adults needing routine care* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: call during lunch break and hope · source: [article](https://www.notablehealth.com/blog/notable-survey-61-of-patients-skip-medical-appointments-due-to-scheduling-hassles) — "61% of patients skip medical appointments due to scheduling hassles"
195. **Forgetting symptoms before the appointment** — By the time the visit comes, the pattern of symptoms over the past weeks is a blur, so the doctor gets a vague summary.  
    *people with intermittent or chronic symptoms* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app or paper journal · source: [article](https://dev.to/booshpooshy/building-a-health-symptom-tracker-app-from-concept-to-launch-5alm) — "family members struggle to remember symptoms between doctor visits and communicate effectively with healthcare providers"
196. **Forgetting what the doctor said** — Instructions given verbally in a ten-minute consult are half-forgotten by the time you get home, including dosage changes and follow-up steps.  
    *patients, especially older adults* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bring a family member to take notes · source: knowledge
197. **Medical history scattered across providers** — Each clinic has its own portal, so past diagnoses, vaccinations and imaging are spread across logins and nobody has the full record, especially after moving.  
    *people who have changed doctors or moved* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: keep a folder of printouts · source: knowledge
198. **Spotting triggers for migraines or gut flares** — Migraine, IBS or eczema flare-ups seem random, and without foods, sleep, weather and stress logged side by side the trigger never emerges.  
    *people with trigger-driven chronic conditions* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: food diary on paper, guessing · source: knowledge
199. **Test results with no explanation** — Lab results land in the portal before the doctor has looked, full of flagged values and jargon, and no one calls to explain.  
    *patients using online health portals* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: google each value and worry · source: knowledge
200. **Tracking a child's fever overnight** — Parents up at 3am cannot recall the last temperature reading or when the last dose of paracetamol was given.  
    *parents of young children* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scribbled notes on a receipt · source: knowledge
201. **Forgetting questions in the exam room** — Sitting in a paper gown, people forget every question they meant to ask and remember them in the car park.  
    *anyone at a doctor visit* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: write a list on paper beforehand · source: [article](https://www.health.harvard.edu/press_releases/get-the-most-out-of-a-visit-to-the-doctor) — "it's easy to forget all the questions that brought you in to see the doctor in the first place"
202. **Overdue checkups and screenings** — Dental cleanings, eye tests, smear tests and vaccine boosters each have their own interval and quietly slip years overdue.  
    *adults managing their own preventive care* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: wait for the clinic to send a letter · source: knowledge
203. **Unpredictable waiting-room time** — Patients wait an average of nearly half an hour past their slot with no idea how long it will be, making it hard to plan work around visits.  
    *anyone with in-person appointments* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: block out the whole morning · source: [article](https://www.softwareadvice.com/resources/patient-pain-points/) — "an average of 28.8 minutes waiting before they can see a doctor"
204. **Parking and commute stress for appointments** — Finding the right building, parking and department at a hospital campus adds stress and lateness to every visit.  
    *patients at large clinics and hospitals* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: leave an hour early · source: [article](https://www.softwareadvice.com/resources/patient-pain-points/) — "32% say commute-related issues are a top pain point for in-person medical appointments"

### Workout logging

205. **Years of workout history can't be exported** — When a workout app shuts down, changes pricing or gets abandoned, users discover there is no way to export years of logged lifts.  
    *long-term strength trainees* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually copy PRs into a spreadsheet · source: [reddit-quoted](https://setgraph.app/ai-blog/best-workout-tracker-app-reddit) — "Reddit is full of frustrated users who lost years of data due to not checking data export policies upfront"
206. **Equipment taken, need substitute exercise** — The planned machine or rack is occupied and you have to improvise an equivalent exercise on the spot without knowing what matches.  
    *people training in busy commercial gyms* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: wait, or skip the exercise · source: knowledge
207. **Fitness data scattered across devices** — Runs live in one app, lifts in another, steps on the watch and weight on a smart scale; nothing shows the full picture together.  
    *people with several fitness apps and wearables* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual spreadsheet or ignore some sources · source: knowledge
208. **Logging a set takes too many taps** — Entering weight and reps between sets takes 30 seconds of fiddling while tired, which ruins rest timing and makes people skip logging.  
    *gym-goers who log every set* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: log after the workout from memory · source: [article](https://setgraph.app/ai-blog/best-app-to-log-workout-tested-by-lifters) — "speed matters when logging takes 30 seconds versus 3 minutes per session"
209. **Not knowing when to progress weight** — Without looking back through weeks of logs it is unclear whether you have stalled or should add weight, so progress drifts.  
    *self-coached lifters* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: keep a notebook and eyeball it · source: knowledge
210. **Too many options, no clear guidance** — Fitness apps offer hundreds of programs and settings but never tell a beginner what to actually do today or how to eat around it.  
    *beginners starting to exercise* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ask friends or follow random YouTube routines · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/app/id1208224953) — "overwhelm them with too many options and fail to provide clear guidance on nutrition"
211. **Workout app useless without gym signal** — Basement and warehouse gyms have no reception, and apps that require connectivity to load a routine or save a set fail right when you need them.  
    *lifters training in gyms with poor signal* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: screenshot the plan or write it on paper · source: [article](https://setgraph.app/ai-blog/best-app-to-log-workout-tested-by-lifters) — "Apps that require constant connectivity become frustrating in basement gyms"
212. **Can't name exercise variations separately** — Doing the same movement at different angles or grips in one session, the app treats them as one exercise and the progress history gets muddled.  
    *intermediate lifters with varied programs* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: add notes to each set · source: [app-review](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=gymworkout.gym.gymlog.gymtrainer&hl=en_US) — "wish they could change the names of certain exercises when doing the same one multiple times at different angles"
213. **Deciphering the plan mid-gym-floor** — Standing next to a rack trying to work out what the app means by its prescribed sets and percentages wastes time and looks awkward.  
    *people following programmed training plans* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pre-calculate weights at home · source: [article](https://zapier.com/blog/best-fitness-tracking-apps/) — "standing in the middle of the gym trying to figure out what the app is telling you to do"
214. **Forced warm-ups on back-to-back workouts** — Guided workout apps make you repeat the full warm-up for a second class even though you are already warm, and there is no skip.  
    *users of guided video workout apps* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scrub through the video manually · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/app/id1208224953) — "can't skip repetitive elements like warm-ups in back-to-back workouts"

<a id="mental-health-habits"></a>

## Mental health, habits & focus

*stress, anxiety, motivation, habit building, procrastination, focus, journaling*


### Focus & distraction

215. **Freezing when facing a new task** — Being overwhelmed by information or a decision leads to total shutdown where nothing gets done at all.  
    *people with ADHD or anxiety* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: waiting for the pressure to become unbearable · source: [article](https://www.healthline.com/health/adhd/what-is-adhd-paralysis) — "ADHD paralysis occurring when someone is overwhelmed by information, starting a new task, or making a decision"
216. **Focus apps easy to bypass** — Screen-time limits and site blockers are dismissed with one tap when the urge hits, so they only work when they are not needed.  
    *people trying to cut phone or social media use* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: stricter blockers that eventually get uninstalled · source: [article](https://theconversation.com/focus-apps-claim-to-improve-your-productivity-do-they-actually-work-271388) — "A lack of focus boils down to difficulties with self-regulation"
217. **Hyperfocus makes time disappear** — People with ADHD lose hours in one task, miss meals and appointments, and only notice when it is dark outside.  
    *adults with ADHD* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: alarms that get silenced · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/ua/app/adhd-timer-collection/id6634579526) — "struggle with time blindness and hyperfocus"
218. **Losing the thread after an interruption** — A Slack ping or knock at the door wipes working memory, and it takes 15-20 minutes to reconstruct where the task was.  
    *remote and office knowledge workers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sticky notes with 'where I was' · source: knowledge
219. **Not knowing when to START a task** — Time blindness means people know the deadline but misjudge when they must begin, so they are always late or rushed.  
    *adults with ADHD, chronic late-runners* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: leaving early 'just in case', still late · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/latered/id6757766191) — "a task app that understands time blindness that shows you when to START tasks"
220. **Cannot re-enter deep work after lunch** — The afternoon slump plus a full inbox means the second half of the day never reaches real concentration.  
    *office workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: coffee · source: knowledge
221. **Not knowing where phone hours actually go** — At the end of the day people cannot account for the 4-5 hours spent on the phone or which apps ate them.  
    *smartphone users trying to cut usage* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: built-in screen time reports rarely opened · source: [article](https://alternativeto.net/software/stay-focused--stay-productive) — "show how much time you spend on your smartphone"
222. **Open browser tabs as anxiety pile** — Dozens of tabs kept 'to read later' create constant low-grade stress and make it hard to find the tab actually needed.  
    *researchers, students, developers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: tab-grouping extensions, periodic mass close · source: knowledge
223. **Rigid Pomodoro breaks kill flow** — A 25-minute timer buzzes right when concentration finally arrives, and stopping to rest destroys momentum.  
    *developers, writers, students* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ignore the timer or stop using it · source: knowledge

### Habit building

224. **Broken streak collapses the whole system** — Missing a single day resets a habit streak to zero, and the whole routine falls apart after that one miss.  
    *anyone using streak-based habit apps* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: uninstall the app or start a new one · source: [reddit-quoted](https://habitdoom.com/blog/streak-anxiety-habit-trackers) — "The streak apps were the worst, break the streak once and the whole system collapsed"
225. **Guilt stacked on top of the missed habit** — After breaking a streak the person feels worse about themselves than before they started tracking, adding guilt to the original problem.  
    *people who tried habit trackers and quit* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: stop tracking altogether · source: [reddit-quoted](https://habitdoom.com/blog/streak-anxiety-habit-trackers) — "Like cool, now I have guilt on top of the original problem."
226. **Restarting from zero feels pointless** — After 50+ days of progress, a reset to day one feels so bad that people would rather delete the app than begin again.  
    *long-term habit tracker users* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: uninstall · source: [article](https://habitdoom.com/blog/streak-anxiety-habit-trackers) — "starting over from zero feels worse than just uninstalling"
227. **Building the habit of using the habit app** — People forget to open the tracker itself, so days go unlogged and the record looks like failure even when the habit happened.  
    *casual habit tracker users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: backfill from memory, often wrongly · source: knowledge
228. **Habit checkbox becomes meaningless tapping** — After a few weeks people tap the box out of reflex without actually doing the habit, so the tracker's data stops meaning anything.  
    *habit tracker users past the first month* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: delete the habit and re-add it · source: knowledge
229. **Habits tied to a trigger are never scheduled** — People know 'after coffee, journal' works but their apps only offer fixed clock reminders, which fire at the wrong moment.  
    *people trying to stack habits onto existing routines* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: set multiple alarms and dismiss most of them · source: knowledge
230. **No way to log a partial or reduced habit** — A 5-minute walk instead of a 30-minute run counts as a fail in most trackers, so people skip logging rather than record a 'lesser' version.  
    *people building exercise, reading or meditation habits* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mark it done anyway and feel like a cheat · source: knowledge
231. **Routine breaks completely when travelling** — Vacations, illness or a schedule change break every habit at once and there is no gentle 'pause' mode, so people quit.  
    *frequent travellers, shift workers, parents* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: stop tracking until life settles, often never resuming · source: knowledge
232. **Tracking too many habits at once** — People add 8-12 habits on day one, get overwhelmed by the daily checklist, and abandon all of them within weeks.  
    *new-year resolution makers, self-improvement beginners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none, or paring down after burnout · source: knowledge

### Journaling

233. **Expecting daily entries and instant wisdom** — People set an every-day, deep-insight standard, fail it within a week, and conclude journaling is not for them.  
    *journaling beginners* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: quit · source: [article](https://thatjournalingguy.substack.com/p/why-most-people-fail-at-journaling/comments) — "trying to write every single day, demanding instant wisdom from themselves"
234. **Journaling lapses because it is optional** — Without a ritual or trigger, journaling is skipped on busy days, then for weeks, until the notebook is abandoned.  
    *people who want to journal* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: buying a new notebook and restarting · source: [article](https://anshulkumar.substack.com/p/why-your-journaling-habit-keeps-failing) — "they need a ritual and to pair it with a trigger to maintain the habit"
235. **Nowhere private enough to be honest** — People censor their journal because a partner, roommate or child might read the notebook or unlock the phone.  
    *people sharing homes or devices* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: write in code, avoid difficult topics · source: knowledge
236. **Fear of ruining the first page** — The pristine first page of a new journal is so intimidating that people never write anything at all.  
    *new journalers, stationery lovers* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: leave the first page blank · source: [article](https://ayachan91.substack.com/p/how-i-start-a-new-journal) — "Fear of messing up the first page is a real challenge for journalers"
237. **Journal app suggestions feel irrelevant** — Auto-generated prompts based on photos and locations rarely match what the person actually wants to reflect on.  
    *users of Apple Journal and similar apps* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ignore prompts and free-write · source: [article](https://www.tomsguide.com/opinion/i-tried-apples-journal-app-for-a-week-heres-what-i-found)
238. **Old entries never get re-read** — Years of journal entries sit unread because there is no easy way to surface what was written on a theme or on this day last year.  
    *long-time journalers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scrolling back manually · source: knowledge

### Mood tracking

239. **Cannot explain feelings to the doctor** — At appointments people cannot remember how the last month actually went, so they say 'fine' or exaggerate the worst day.  
    *patients with depression or anxiety* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app, memory · source: knowledge
240. **Forgot to log the mood in the moment** — By evening people cannot recall how they felt at noon, and many apps make adding past entries awkward.  
    *mood tracker users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: apps that allow adding past data · source: [article](https://apps.apple.com/qa/app/simple-mood-log/id6449779464) — "add past data if they forget to record their mood immediately"
241. **Mood tracker only collects numbers** — Months of daily mood scores produce no insight into what drives good and bad days, so the effort feels pointless.  
    *mood tracker users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: stop logging · source: [article](https://keelwellness.app/blog/why-your-mood-tracker-isnt-helping/) — "apps that just collect numbers without providing meaningful insights, meaning users do the work without getting the reward"
242. **Mood, sleep and cycle data live in separate apps** — Sleep, exercise, menstrual cycle and mood are tracked in different apps, so people cannot see how they relate.  
    *people investigating mood patterns* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual spreadsheets · source: knowledge
243. **Upgrade nags on every log** — Being asked to go premium at every check-in makes a two-second mood log feel like a sales pitch.  
    *free-tier mood app users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: switch apps · source: [article](https://keelwellness.app/blog/why-your-mood-tracker-isnt-helping/) — "repeatedly asking to upgrade to paid versions at every interaction"
244. **Checked once, then forgotten** — Mood apps get opened for a week and then become another icon nobody taps.  
    *people trying mood tracking* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: [article](https://keelwellness.app/blog/why-your-mood-tracker-isnt-helping/) — "apps becoming something you check once and forget"

### Motivation & procrastination

245. **Big vague task never gets started** — A task like 'write the report' sits on the list for weeks because it has no obvious first step and feels too large to begin.  
    *students, freelancers, knowledge workers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: panic near the deadline · source: knowledge
246. **Late-night bedtime procrastination** — People stay up scrolling to reclaim personal time, know they will regret it tomorrow, and cannot make themselves stop.  
    *working adults, parents* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: phone in another room, rarely enforced · source: knowledge
247. **Motivation vanishes after the first week** — A new goal is exciting for days, then the initial energy fades and there is nothing to carry the effort through the boring middle.  
    *anyone starting fitness, learning or side projects* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: restarting with a new plan each time · source: knowledge
248. **Not noticing the drift into procrastination** — An hour passes before people realise they slid from the task into browsing; nothing interrupts the drift as it happens.  
    *office and remote workers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: random check-in apps or manual timers · source: [article](https://alternativeto.net/software/don-t-waste-today-) — "a procrastination tracker that will randomly ask if you're procrastinating or not"
249. **Self-imposed rewards and punishments don't stick** — People invent bribes and penalties to make themselves work, but with no one enforcing them they quietly stop following the rules.  
    *people who struggle to sit down and work* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none that lasts · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=16411662) — "using rewards and punishments to motivate themselves to work"
250. **Wanting it done, not wanting to do it** — People confuse wanting an outcome with wanting to do the work, then are baffled why they never start.  
    *knowledge workers, students* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: forcing themselves with deadlines · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=36184605) — "people often conflate 'wanting something done' with 'wanting to do something'"
251. **YouTube is the most compelling thing available** — With nothing more engaging planned, hours vanish into YouTube or feeds instead of the intended task.  
    *remote workers, students, solo developers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: site blockers that get disabled · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=45085014) — "you pass the time with stuff like YouTube because that is the most compelling thing available to you"
252. **No accountability partner available** — People want someone to check in with about goals but friends are busy and online accountability groups fizzle within weeks.  
    *solo learners, freelancers, people working alone* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: posting goals publicly on social media · source: knowledge
253. **Productive procrastination hides avoidance** — People clean, organise or reply to email all day and feel busy while the one important scary task stays untouched.  
    *perfectionists, freelancers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### Stress & anxiety

254. **Burnout builds unnoticed until collapse** — Stress creeps up over months with no early warning, and people only recognise burnout after they crash.  
    *software engineers, healthcare workers* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: periodic self-assessment surveys · source: [article](https://www.strongdm.com/blog/software-engineer-burnout) — "Tools like surveys, journals, or self-assessments can monitor stress and burnout levels"
255. **Panic hits with no plan at hand** — During a sudden anxiety spike people cannot remember any coping technique and fumble through apps looking for one.  
    *people with panic or anxiety disorders* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: call a friend, ride it out · source: knowledge
256. **3 a.m. rumination loop** — Waking at night with a worry that loops for hours, with no way to park the thought and get back to sleep.  
    *anxious adults* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: notebook by the bed · source: knowledge
257. **Free trial needs a card and renews silently** — Trials require payment details, renew without warning, and cancelling or getting a refund is a fight.  
    *anyone trying wellness apps* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calendar reminder to cancel · source: [article](https://www.medicalnewstoday.com/articles/calm-app) — "canceling their subscription or receiving refunds for erroneous charges is difficult"
258. **Work bleeds into evenings with no boundary** — With laptop and Slack always at hand there is no clear end to the work day, so stress never switches off.  
    *remote workers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: trying to set rules, rarely kept · source: [article](https://softices.com/blogs/stress-management-strategies-for-software-engineers) — "setting clear boundaries between work and personal life, is crucial for preventing burnout"
259. **Cannot tell which anxiety apps are credible** — Most anxiety apps were built without clinicians and almost none were tested, so users cannot tell useful from junk.  
    *people seeking self-help for anxiety* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: trial and error, therapist recommendations · source: [article](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28504859/) — "the majority (67.3%) of currently available anxiety apps were found to lack the involvement of health care professionals"
260. **Forgetting to breathe during a stressful day** — People know breathing exercises help but never remember to pause for one until the stress has already peaked.  
    *office workers, caregivers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reminder apps like iBreathe · source: [article](https://marinette.extension.wisc.edu/covid-19-resources/mental-health-apps/) — "you can set reminders to take breathing breaks throughout your day"
261. **Losing track of what worked last time** — People forget which coping strategies actually helped in past bad weeks, so each episode starts from scratch.  
    *people managing recurring anxiety or low mood* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
262. **Meditation app paywalls before you can try** — Free tiers are so thin that people hit the paywall before they know whether the app helps at all.  
    *people trying meditation for anxiety* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: YouTube guided meditations · source: [article](https://unstar.app/blog/mental-health-app-reviews-what-users-say-about-wellbeing-apps-2026) — "Headspace was great when it had a meaningful free tier. Now you can barely try it before hitting the paywall"
263. **Subscription cost itself causes anxiety** — Paying high yearly fees for an anxiety app feels wrong and creates money worry, undermining the point of using it.  
    *budget-conscious users with anxiety* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: cancel and rely on free breathing exercises · source: [article](https://unstar.app/blog/mental-health-app-reviews-what-users-say-about-wellbeing-apps-2026) — "the subscription prices give them anxiety"
264. **Sunday-evening dread with no outlet** — Anxiety about the coming work week ruins Sunday nights every week and there is no structured way to defuse it.  
    *employees in stressful jobs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: distraction, alcohol · source: knowledge
265. **Therapy homework forgotten between sessions** — Worksheets and exercises assigned in therapy are lost or forgotten, so each session restarts the same conversation.  
    *therapy clients* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper handouts, phone notes · source: knowledge
266. **Too much content to choose from** — Hundreds of meditations and sleep stories make the app menu overwhelming, so a stressed person gives up before picking one.  
    *stressed users of large wellness apps* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: replay the same one session · source: [article](https://psychcentral.com/reviews/headspace-vs-calm) — "Calm's menus can feel a bit crowded sometimes due to the massive amount of content available"

<a id="time-productivity"></a>

## Time management & personal productivity

*scheduling, reminders, to-do lists, calendars, running late, forgetting things*


### Calendars & scheduling

267. **Calendar silently allows double booking** — Accepting an invite that overlaps an existing event produces no warning, and the clash is only discovered when both meetings start.  
    *anyone with a busy calendar* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: eyeball the day view before accepting · source: [forum](https://support.google.com/calendar/thread/702910/calendar-doesn-t-alert-double-booking-event-conflict?hl=en) — "Calendar doesn't alert double-booking event conflict"
268. **Email ping-pong to find a meeting slot** — Agreeing a time with two or more people takes days of proposing, declining and re-proposing before anything lands on a calendar.  
    *professionals, freelancers, anyone organizing a group* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: booking links, polls, or one person dictating · source: [article](https://theintelligence.com/33841/address-meeting-schedulings-pain-points/) — "the most laborious part of the meeting-planning process is the back and forth"
269. **Event and its notification in different time zones** — After travel or a device settings change, the calendar shows one time while the alert fires at another, so reminders arrive hours off.  
    *travelers and remote workers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manually re-check every event after landing · source: [forum](https://support.google.com/calendar/thread/279356981/calendar-and-notifications-are-on-two-different-time-zones?hl=en) — "Calendar and notifications are on two different time zones."
270. **Work and personal calendars clash unseen** — A dentist appointment in the personal calendar is invisible to the work booking page, so colleagues book meetings on top of it.  
    *people with separate work and personal accounts* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually copy busy blocks between calendars · source: [article](https://syncdate.app/blog/avoid-double-booking-google-calendar) — "the double-booking problem continues even with Google Calendar"
271. **Your time or my time confusion** — Proposing a meeting time across zones triggers a chain of clarifying emails, and a wrong guess means someone shows up an hour early or misses it.  
    *remote and distributed teams, freelancers with overseas clients* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: world clock sites, writing times in both zones · source: [article](https://overlappr.com/blog/how-to-schedule-meetings-across-time-zones/) — "the email ping-pong of "Wait, did you mean 3pm YOUR time or MY time?""
272. **Booking page shows wrong time zone** — A public booking form displays slots in UTC or the host's zone, so guests book the wrong hour or abandon the form.  
    *consultants, coaches, anyone sharing a booking link* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: add a note asking guests to convert times · source: [app-review](https://www.capterra.com/p/213340/Google-Calendar/reviews/) — "the time zone is wrong, showing up in the booking form as UTC"
273. **Daylight-saving shifts break cross-zone plans** — Recurring calls with people abroad silently move by an hour for a few weeks each spring and autumn, and someone always misses one.  
    *distributed teams, families abroad* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: remembering to warn everyone in advance · source: knowledge
274. **Same people always get the 7am call** — Across a distributed team the inconvenient early or late slots keep landing on the same colleagues because nobody tracks the fairness of meeting times.  
    *distributed teams spanning many time zones* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: [article](https://www.read.ai/articles/how-to-handle-time-zone-differences-in-team-scheduling) — "colleagues who always seem to be the ones dialing in at 7 a.m. or 9 p.m."

### Forgetting things

275. **Capturing a thought while hands are busy** — A task comes to mind while driving, cooking or in the shower and there is no frictionless way to catch it before it evaporates.  
    *everyone* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: voice assistant, texting yourself · source: knowledge
276. **Forgetting essentials on the way out** — Keys, wallet, badge, lunch or the parcel to return get left behind because there is no checkpoint at the door.  
    *commuters, parents, anyone rushing out* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: bowl by the door, mental checklist · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/mx/app/recaller/id6751175471) — "refresh your memory before you head out"
277. **Forgetting short background timers** — Laundry in the washer, the oven, the parking meter and the kettle are forgotten because starting them and remembering them are separate acts.  
    *everyone at home, drivers using metered parking* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: voice-assistant timers, alarms · source: knowledge
278. **Infrequent chores never come back** — Every-few-months jobs like replacing a filter, flipping the mattress, renewing a passport or a car inspection are forgotten because nothing repeats that slowly in the head.  
    *homeowners, renters, car owners* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: none, or stickers with dates · source: knowledge
279. **Remembering to bring the right things** — Gym kit on Tuesday, library books on Thursday, the form for the school trip: what to pack depends on the day's events and gets forgotten.  
    *parents, students, commuters* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: packing the night before, sticky notes · source: knowledge
280. **Sticky notes become invisible** — Visual reminders left in plain sight stop registering within days, so the note on the door no longer prevents forgetting.  
    *people who use physical or on-screen visual cues* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: move the note somewhere new · source: [article](https://kristenlynnmcclure.substack.com/p/making-reminders-work-for-your-adhd) — "the visual reminder becomes visual noise, which you can stop seeing even after a short while"
281. **Waiting-for items fall through the cracks** — Things other people owe you (a reply, a refund, a document) are not tasks you can do, so they live nowhere and are only remembered weeks later.  
    *anyone who delegates or deals with services* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: starred emails, a 'waiting' tag · source: knowledge

### Procrastination & follow-through

282. **Scary task grows scarier each day** — A big, vague, urgent item sits ignored on the list and every day it stays there it becomes harder to even look at.  
    *procrastinators, students, freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none, or panic near the deadline · source: [article](https://authorautomations.com/p/why-your-to-do-list-is-failing-you) — "the longer they sit, the bigger and scarier they become"
283. **Planning eats the time for doing** — Rewriting, re-sorting and re-tooling the to-do list becomes the activity, and the tasks themselves are never started.  
    *productivity-app enthusiasts, perfectionists* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: [forum](https://www.quora.com/How-do-I-stop-making-to-do-lists-and-over-planning-things-that-don-t-get-done-as-a-result-of-spending-all-my-time-on-making-to-do-lists) — "spending all my time on making to do lists"
284. **Snoozing a task until it is forgotten** — Hitting 'remind me later' repeatedly pushes an item past its usefulness, and the app never escalates or flags how many times it has been deferred.  
    *anyone who uses snooze* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
285. **System breaks when life changes** — A routine that worked during term time or one job collapses after a schedule change, and rebuilding it from scratch is so costly people give up.  
    *students, new parents, shift workers, job changers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: abandon the system for weeks · source: [article](https://healthyadhd.substack.com/p/the-problem-with-to-do-lists) — "the system that worked in one phase of life can become useless when an entire schedule changes"

### Reminders & notifications

286. **Dismissed reminder is gone for good** — One accidental swipe clears a reminder and it never returns, so the thing it protected is forgotten until it is too late.  
    *anyone who relies on reminders* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: nagging-style apps that repeat every minute · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/tt/app/insistent/id869486104) — "it'll annoy you every minute until you deal with the reminder"
287. **Quiet ping missed while in deep focus** — A single soft notification is not noticed when the user is absorbed, stressed or overstimulated, and there is no second chance.  
    *people with ADHD, deep workers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: loud alarms for everything, which then get disabled · source: [article](https://adhdwisdomtools.substack.com/p/time-blindness-no-more-the-adhd-guide) — "a soft ping on your phone is too quiet and too easy to miss"
288. **Reminder fires too late to act** — An alert 15 minutes before an appointment across town arrives after the moment one needed to leave, so it announces lateness rather than preventing it.  
    *people with ADHD and anyone with in-person appointments* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: set multiple manual alerts for each event · source: [article](https://noodl.prepshotz.com/blog/adhd-and-forgetting-appointments/) — "A notification at 9:15 for a 9:30 appointment isn't a reminder—it's an announcement."
289. **Reminders decay into ignorable noise** — Every notification demands a micro-decision, and after enough of them the user swipes them all away without reading, including the important ones.  
    *people who rely on phone notifications* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: turn everything off, or nothing off · source: [article](https://imbusybeingawesome.com/adhd-reminders/) — "every time a notification pops up, it presents a micro-decision"
290. **Default reminder lead time is wrong** — Every event alerts a fixed 10 or 30 minutes ahead regardless of whether it is a call at the desk or a trip across town, so alerts are ignored or too late.  
    *calendar users with mixed in-person and remote events* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: edit the reminder on each event by hand · source: [article](https://www.androidauthority.com/fixed-four-biggest-google-calendar-annoyances-how-3708184/) — "increased the default reminder time in Google Calendar to one hour instead of the default 30 minutes"
291. **Reminder tied to a person, not a time** — 'Ask Mom about the recipe' has no time attached; it needs to surface the next time you call or see her, which no time-based reminder can do.  
    *everyone* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: notes app list per person · source: knowledge

### Running late

292. **Morning routine loses track of time** — Between phone scrolling, the shower and breakfast there is no sense of how far behind the morning is until it is already too late to be on time.  
    *people who are always late in the morning, ADHD adults* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: multiple alarms · source: knowledge
293. **No travel buffer between appointments** — Two in-person events get booked adjacent on the calendar even though it takes 25 minutes to get between them.  
    *field workers, parents, people with errands* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually block 'travel' events · source: knowledge
294. **Getting-ready time not counted** — Leave-by alerts count only the drive, not the shower, dressing and finding keys, so people are still reliably 15 minutes late.  
    *people who are always late in the morning* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: set the alarm earlier and still run late · source: knowledge
295. **Leave-by alerts ignore transit and bikes** — Departure reminders assume driving or walking, so people who take buses, trains or bikes get leave times that are simply wrong.  
    *city dwellers without cars* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: check the transit app separately and guess · source: [article](https://techcrunch.com/2025/04/26/latelys-new-gamified-app-helps-people-arrive-on-time) — "only offers options for driving or walking and doesn't provide time estimations for public transportation or biking"
296. **Meeting overruns cascade into lateness** — One meeting running ten minutes over makes the next one late, and the whole afternoon slides with no automatic warning to the later attendees.  
    *people with back-to-back meetings* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manually message 'running 5 late' · source: knowledge

### Shared & family scheduling

297. **Missed pickups when term schedules change** — Each new school term brings new activity times and locations, and the old calendar entries linger until a missed pickup exposes the gap.  
    *parents of school-age children* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: re-enter everything by hand each September · source: [article](https://www.usecalendara.com/blog/family-calendar-app-faq) — "usually in September after a missed pickup"
298. **Family calendar goes stale** — Entering activities becomes one parent's chore, the chore lapses, the calendar stops matching reality and everyone stops trusting it.  
    *households with kids' activities* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paper calendar on the fridge, group chat · source: [article](https://gethomsy.com/blog/family-organization/organization-family-calendar-actually-works) — "entry became someone's chore and the chore stopped happening — so the calendar went stale"
299. **Shared events only remind the creator** — A shared family event alerts the person who entered it, but not the partner or teen who actually needs to show up.  
    *families and couples coordinating logistics* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: text everyone the morning of · source: [forum](https://www.mumsnet.com/talk/_chat/4843562-what-online-apptool-do-you-use-as-a-shared-family-calendar-that-sends-reminder-prompts-to-everyone) — "shared family calendar that sends reminder prompts to everyone?"
300. **Shared reminders across iPhone and Android** — Mixed-platform households cannot share a single reminder or list that pings both phones, so each system only covers half the family.  
    *couples and families with different phone platforms* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: messaging apps, duplicate entries · source: [forum](https://discussions.apple.com/thread/255838418)
301. **Verbal plans never reach the calendar** — 'Let's grab lunch Thursday' agreed in conversation is never written down, and one side forgets or double-books.  
    *friends, couples, casual social planners* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: text to confirm the day before · source: knowledge

### Time awareness

302. **Chronically underestimating task duration** — Tasks are planned to take 30 minutes and take two hours, so every daily plan collapses by noon and nothing is learned from the miss.  
    *nearly everyone who plans a day* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: double all estimates · source: [article](https://www.goodreads.com/author_blog_posts/19059835-what-is-the-biggest-challenge-you-face-when-it-comes-to-managing-your-ti) — "difficulty estimating task length (10%)"
303. **Due dates without start times** — A task with a deadline gives no cue about when work must begin, so multi-hour tasks are started an hour before they are due.  
    *students, people with ADHD, deadline-driven workers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually create a separate 'start X' task · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/latered/id6757766191) — "shows you when to START tasks — not just when they're due"
304. **Interruptions derail the planned day** — Colleagues, messages and calls break the schedule repeatedly and there is no easy way to re-slot what was displaced.  
    *office and remote knowledge workers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: do-not-disturb modes, working late · source: [article](https://www.goodreads.com/author_blog_posts/19059835-what-is-the-biggest-challenge-you-face-when-it-comes-to-managing-your-ti) — "Too many interruptions (40%)"
305. **Suddenly it is 8 PM** — Hours vanish into one activity and the user only notices when the day is gone and planned tasks were never touched.  
    *people with time blindness, hyperfocus, remote workers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: hourly chimes, visual timers · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/gb/app/set-adhd-task-manager-timer/id6753867602) — "prevent the "Wait, it's already 8 PM?!" experience"
306. **No idea where the day went** — At the end of a busy day it is impossible to say what the hours were spent on, so the same leaks repeat tomorrow.  
    *remote workers, freelancers, students* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual time tracking that is abandoned within a week · source: knowledge
307. **Wasting the 20-minute gap** — A short unexpected gap between meetings is lost to scrolling because it is not obvious which small task fits it.  
    *office workers, students* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### To-do lists

308. **Day starts with 100 overdue tasks** — Rescheduled and overdue tasks pile into today, so each morning opens with a wall of red that is exhausting rather than useful.  
    *heavy to-do app users* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scripts that auto-reschedule, or bulk-postponing everything · source: [article](https://pypi.org/project/todoist-scheduler) — "having a large list of tasks creates 'cognitive drag' and stresses users out"
309. **Easy tasks crowd out important ones** — With no visible priority the user ticks off small quick wins all day while the thing that matters stays untouched.  
    *anyone working from a flat list* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: eat-the-frog rules, stars and flags · source: [article](https://planningwithchloe.substack.com/i/104006094/trap-three-the-to-do-list-isnt-structured-based-on-whats-important) — "people tend to take the easiest task off the list rather than focusing on what's important"
310. **Overlong list, no idea what to start** — Dozens of open tasks stare back and the user freezes, unsure which one to actually work on right now.  
    *anyone who captures everything into one list* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pick three on paper each morning · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/todo-3-only-3-tasks-per-day/id1627396349) — "putting too many tasks on a to-do list can cause users to feel overwhelmed and unsure which task to work on"
311. **Tasks buried in chat and email** — Requests arrive in Slack threads, texts and emails and never make it into the task list, so they are lost once the message scrolls away.  
    *knowledge workers, group-chat organizers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: flagging messages, 'mark unread' · source: knowledge
312. **Guilt from constantly rescheduling tasks** — Pushing the same task from day to day feels like failure, and the growing pile of postponements discourages opening the app at all.  
    *people with more tasks than time* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: delete the task, or ignore the overdue count · source: [article](https://todoist.substack.com/p/august-actual-productivity/comments) — "you should feel zero guilt for btw, it happens to the best of us"
313. **No usable log of what was completed** — Once a task is checked off it disappears, so at review time or when writing a status report there is no record of what actually got done last week.  
    *knowledge workers who report on their work* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: keep a separate done-list or search email history · source: [forum](https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/discussions/to-do/todo-could-be-much-more---bugs--basic-features-missing/2149615) — "todo apps lack a record of completed work that can be referred to for reports"
314. **One slip breaks the dependent chain** — When a task slips, the tasks that depend on it keep their old dates, so the plan is instantly wrong and must be rebuilt by hand.  
    *people planning projects, events, moves* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: drag every date manually · source: knowledge
315. **Recurring task misfires when done out of order** — Completing a recurring task early or late does not move it to the correct next occurrence, so the list shows it due again at the wrong time.  
    *anyone using a to-do app for chores and routines* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually edit the due date after each completion · source: [forum](https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/discussions/to-do/todo-could-be-much-more---bugs--basic-features-missing/2149615) — "apps failing to skip to the next recurring date when tasks are completed out of order"
316. **Tasks out of sync between phone and laptop** — A task ticked off on the phone still shows as open on the laptop, or an edit is lost, so people stop trusting the list.  
    *people who use a to-do app on more than one device* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: force-refresh, or keep the list on one device only · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=28997345) — "Sync issues across platforms plague many apps since their beginning."
317. **Complex repeat patterns need duplicate tasks** — A task that repeats Monday, Wednesday and Friday, or on the first weekday of the month, cannot be expressed, forcing several redundant copies.  
    *people with irregular routines (medication, classes, shifts)* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: create three separate recurring tasks · source: [app-review](https://www.capterra.com/p/149339/Todoist-for-Business/reviews/?page=2) — "doesn't have a very robust recurring task management system (e.g., MWF in a single week)"
318. **Tasks rot in the list for years** — Someday-maybe items sit in the list indefinitely, never surfaced or retired, cluttering every view and making the list feel dead.  
    *long-term to-do app users* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: periodic purge, or start a fresh app · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=28997345) — "tasks that have lingered in pending status for years"

<a id="commuting-transport"></a>

## Commuting & getting around

*driving, traffic, parking, public transit, ride-hailing, cycling to work*


### Carpooling & multimodal

319. **Car in the shop, commute broken** — When the car is unavailable for a few days people scramble to piece together transit, rides and borrowed cars for each day.  
    *single-car households* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ask family, expensive ride-hails · source: knowledge
320. **Carpool logistics buried in text threads** — Once a carpool exists, who is driving, who is sick, and who owes gas money is tracked in a messy group chat that people miss.  
    *established carpool groups* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: group text, mental accounting · source: [article](https://pavecommute.app/why-traditional-carpooling-apps-struggle-with-daily-commutes/) — "they manage them through informal channels like text rather than apps"
321. **Carpool match takes too long** — Commuters try a carpool app, cannot find a match on their route and schedule within a few days, and abandon it.  
    *would-be carpoolers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ask coworkers directly, drive alone · source: [article](https://pavecommute.app/why-traditional-carpooling-apps-struggle-with-daily-commutes/) — "if they can't find a match quickly, they leave the platform because it offers no added value"
322. **Combining modes into one trip** — A commute that mixes driving, a train and a bike share means juggling three apps, three payments and no single ETA.  
    *multimodal commuters* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: several apps open at once · source: [article](https://medium.com/@fyjzz007/the-hidden-struggles-of-daily-commuters-and-how-technology-can-help-uncover-solutions-6c174b75d47b) — "integrated multimodal platforms... commuters' demand for seamless integration"
323. **Hybrid days and commute planning** — Office days shift week to week, so carpool partners, parking permits and transit passes are never aligned with the days someone actually goes in.  
    *hybrid workers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calendar juggling, over-buying passes · source: knowledge

### Cycling to work

324. **Bike lane blocked by snow or debris** — Snow, glass and construction shrink or close bike lanes with no warning, pushing riders into car traffic mid-route.  
    *year-round bike commuters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: scout the route, ask local cycling groups · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/2008/05/19/bicycle-commuting-cheap-but-not-without-its-problems/?gen=1) — "Snow on road sides reducing space for bikes increases conflicts with cars"
325. **Nowhere secure to park the bike** — Riders do not know before arriving whether the office or destination has secure bike parking or a shower, so they risk theft or skip riding.  
    *bike commuters to offices and appointments* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ask reception, lock to a signpost · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/2008/05/19/bicycle-commuting-cheap-but-not-without-its-problems/?gen=1) — "Needing secure bike storage and facilities to clean up after riding"
326. **Bike commute takes far longer** — New bike commuters underestimate how much longer riding takes than driving, arrive late, and cannot predict the time for a given route and fitness level.  
    *new bike commuters* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: trial rides on the weekend, add a buffer · source: [article](https://www.axios.com/local/charlotte/2016/09/07/bike-commuting-64648) — "Commute time is significantly longer than driving, requiring significant time adjustments"
327. **Bike lights dead when leaving** — Rechargeable bike lights are flat exactly when it gets dark early, and riders find out at the bike rack after work.  
    *bike commuters in fall and winter* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: spare lights, charge at desk · source: knowledge
328. **Bike rack on bus already full** — Cyclists reach the bus stop with a bike and the two-slot front rack is taken, so they wait for the next bus or ride the whole way.  
    *bike-and-bus commuters* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: leave earlier, ride the full distance · source: knowledge
329. **Rain decision before leaving** — Cyclists must decide by 7am whether the ride home at 6pm will be wet and dangerous, and general weather apps do not answer that question for a route.  
    *bike commuters* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: check hourly forecast, keep transit fare as backup · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/2008/05/19/bicycle-commuting-cheap-but-not-without-its-problems/?gen=1) — "Riding in rain is uncomfortable and hazardous in traffic"
330. **Tires and tubes wear out unnoticed** — City debris chews through tires and tubes, and riders only find out via a flat on the way to work because nothing tracks wear or mileage.  
    *daily bike commuters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: carry a spare tube, inspect by eye · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/2008/05/19/bicycle-commuting-cheap-but-not-without-its-problems/?gen=1) — "Bike tires and tubes wear quickly due to city street debris"
331. **Winter layering eats commute time** — Picking the right layers for the temperature and wind takes trial and error each morning and adds ten minutes to getting out the door.  
    *cold-weather bike commuters* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: notes on what worked last time · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/2008/05/19/bicycle-commuting-cheap-but-not-without-its-problems/?gen=1) — "Managing layers in winter is annoying and can add 10 minutes to commute time"

### Driving & traffic

332. **Bad commute ruins the day** — An unexpectedly bad drive in leaves people stressed, late and rattled, and they had no warning that today was going to be worse than usual.  
    *car commuters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: check traffic before leaving, leave early every day · source: [article](https://www.fleetnews.co.uk/news/2013/2/25/new-research-reveal-uk-commuters-drive-7000-miles-a-year/46294) — "68 percent of Americans said their whole day could be ruined by a bad commute"
333. **Chained school drop-off commute** — Dropping kids at school then getting to work involves fixed windows and pickup lines, and navigation apps do not plan around the timed stop.  
    *working parents* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: leave extra early, mental scheduling · source: knowledge
334. **Everyone gets the same detour** — When traffic hits, the navigation app sends every driver down the same side street, so the detour becomes as jammed as the original route within minutes.  
    *drivers who rely on Waze or Google Maps* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ignore the reroute, use local knowledge · source: [article](https://www.cbsnews.com/losangeles/news/popular-waze-navigation-app-driving-some-people-crazy) — "when everyone receives the same detour, the detour becomes as mobbed as the regular route"
335. **Not knowing when to leave** — Commute time swings by 20-40 minutes day to day, and drivers have no reliable way to know the right departure minute for their specific route and arrival time.  
    *drivers with fixed start times* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: leave early, check maps repeatedly while getting ready · source: [article](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/ibm-global-commuter-pain-survey-traffic-congestion-down-pain-way-up-129446188.html) — "Traffic Congestion Down, Pain Way Up"
336. **Saved trips vanish from nav app** — Planned or saved commute routes disappear from the navigation app and the app lags or crashes mid-drive, forcing drivers to re-enter destinations at the wheel.  
    *daily drivers using navigation apps* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: re-enter the address, switch apps · source: [app-review](https://www.phonearena.com/news/waze-one-star-reviews-play-store_id151357) — "the app crashing, traffic support missing, planned trips disappearing, a lag that makes the app hard to use"
337. **Surprise toll bills by mail** — Cashless toll roads, rental cars and out-of-state trips produce toll invoices weeks later with admin fees, and drivers cannot tell which trips they were for.  
    *drivers on cashless toll roads* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: keep a transponder, log trips manually · source: knowledge
338. **Cheapest gas on the actual route** — Gas price apps show stations nearby, not which one is on the way home with the smallest detour, so drivers overpay or waste time.  
    *car commuters* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: GasBuddy plus mental math · source: knowledge

### EV charging on the commute

339. **Public charger broken on arrival** — EV commuters drive to a charger and find it dead, throwing an error, or stuck on a payment screen, with no way to know beforehand.  
    *EV drivers relying on public charging* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: check recent check-ins on PlugShare, keep a fallback · source: [article](https://www.kbb.com/car-news/study-electric-car-chargers-are-expanding-broken-frustrating) — "72% said they had encountered a non-working charger"
340. **Charging spots occupied or ICE'd** — All chargers are taken, often by gas cars or fully charged EVs that never left, and there is no real-time availability or turn-taking.  
    *EV commuters charging at work or lots* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: leave a note, come back later · source: [article](https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/tesla-owner-sounds-off-concerning-023000568.html) — "parking lots with gas-powered vehicles in EV charging spots, with all spots consistently filled"
341. **Too many charging payment apps** — Each charging network needs its own account, card or app, so drivers juggle half a dozen logins and fail to start a session.  
    *EV drivers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: install every network app, carry RFID cards · source: [article](https://www.fleetnews.co.uk/news/ev-drivers-face-payment-anxiety-due-to-complicated-charging-experience) — "too many ways to pay and users still using multiple payment methods"

### Fares & costs

342. **Commuter benefit receipts and claims** — Pre-tax transit or parking benefits require collecting receipts and filing claims each month, and unused balances quietly expire.  
    *employees with commuter benefits* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: shoebox of receipts, forgetting · source: knowledge
343. **Logging mileage for reimbursement** — Employees who drive between sites reconstruct trips at month end from memory for mileage claims, and lose money on trips they forgot.  
    *workers driving for work* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notebook in the car, guessing · source: knowledge
344. **Multiple agencies, multiple fare apps** — A trip crossing a bus agency, a regional rail line and a ferry means separate tickets and apps, with no single pass or fare cap.  
    *commuters in multi-agency regions* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: several fare apps, physical cards · source: knowledge
345. **Monthly pass or pay per ride?** — With variable office days riders cannot tell whether a monthly pass or per-trip fare is cheaper, and they find out only after the money is spent.  
    *part-time transit commuters* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: guess, spreadsheet · source: knowledge
346. **Tracking true cost of the commute** — Drivers have no idea what their commute really costs in fuel, tolls, parking and wear, so they cannot compare it with transit or moving closer.  
    *car commuters weighing options* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: rough estimate once a year · source: knowledge

### Parking

347. **Paid in app, ticketed anyway** — The driver paid through the app but the enforcement system did not see it; the app blames the city and the city blames the app, and nobody resolves it.  
    *drivers who received a ticket despite paying* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: contest the ticket with screenshots · source: [app-review](https://unstar.app/blog/parkmobile-paid-still-got-ticket-app-not-working-reviews-2026) — "the company pointed them to the city for responsibility, while the city pointed back to the app"
348. **Reserved garage is full on arrival** — A prepaid parking reservation is not honored because the garage is full, and the only remedy offered is future credit rather than a refund.  
    *drivers pre-booking event or downtown parking* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: arrive very early, have a backup garage in mind · source: [forum](https://www.tripadvisor.com/ShowTopic-g28969-i39-k14958006-Bad_experience_with_Spothero_for_parking-District_of_Columbia.html) — "the garage was full and SpotHero did not honor the reservation"
349. **App directs to wrong garage entrance** — Navigation drops the driver at the wrong entrance or an incorrect address for a booked lot, and the gate will not open, so they park elsewhere and pay twice.  
    *drivers using reserved parking* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: read the confirmation fine print, call the lot · source: [forum](https://www.bbb.org/us/il/chicago/profile/parking-facilities/spothero-0654-88576696/complaints) — "the app's directions led users to the wrong entrance, resulting in parking tickets"
350. **Is alternate-side parking suspended today?** — Street-sweeping and alternate-side rules change with holidays and suspensions, so residents either move the car needlessly or wake up to a ticket.  
    *on-street parkers in cities with sweeping rules* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: check the city website, neighbor group chats · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/-/id6503993830) — "Over 62,000 New Yorkers use this app for instant updates on parking rules and suspensions"
351. **Park-and-ride lot full early** — The commuter lot fills by 7:30 and drivers only find out on arrival, then miss the train hunting for street parking.  
    *suburban rail commuters who drive to the station* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: arrive earlier every day, second lot as backup · source: knowledge
352. **Parking session alert comes too late** — The expiry notification from the parking app arrives with a minute or less to spare, or after the session ended, so people get ticketed while in a meeting.  
    *drivers using pay-by-phone street parking* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: set a separate phone alarm · source: [app-review](https://unstar.app/blog/parkmobile-spothero-paybyphone-parkwhiz-passport-parking-apps-ranked-2026) — "expiration notifications that arrive too late, service fees and surge pricing that inflate the advertised rate"
353. **Wrong parking zone number entered** — In dense areas zone and lot numbers are confusing or adjacent, so drivers pay for the wrong zone and still get a ticket.  
    *drivers in downtown street parking* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: double-check the sign, take a photo of it · source: [app-review](https://unstar.app/blog/parkmobile-spothero-paybyphone-parkwhiz-passport-parking-apps-ranked-2026) — "zone and lot confusion in dense urban areas"
354. **Forgetting where the car is parked** — In large garages or unfamiliar streets people forget the level, row or block where they left the car and wander for ten minutes.  
    *drivers using large lots and garages* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: photo of the pillar sign, drop a map pin · source: knowledge
355. **Meter expiring mid-meeting** — Street meters have a 2-hour cap and the driver has to physically return or re-pay while in the middle of work, or accept a ticket.  
    *drivers parking for appointments and errands* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: phone alarm, choose a garage instead · source: knowledge
356. **Parking fees inflate the posted rate** — Service fees and event surcharges get added at checkout so the price on the sign or listing is never what you actually pay, making comparisons useless.  
    *drivers paying via parking apps* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pay at the meter, compare apps manually · source: [app-review](https://unstar.app/blog/parkmobile-spothero-paybyphone-parkwhiz-passport-parking-apps-ranked-2026) — "service fees and surge pricing that inflate the advertised rate"
357. **Phantom parking charges after leaving** — A parking session keeps running or re-charges after the driver has left, and a $6 refund takes weeks of back-and-forth.  
    *drivers using pay-by-phone parking* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: screenshot the stop time, dispute via email · source: [app-review](https://unstar.app/blog/parkmobile-spothero-paybyphone-parkwhiz-passport-parking-apps-ranked-2026) — "phantom sessions that charge after departure, and refund processes that take weeks for a $6 dispute"
358. **Residential permit and visitor passes** — Street parking permits expire, visitor passes run out, and guests get ticketed because renewal and issuing is a clunky city process nobody remembers.  
    *residents in permit-parking zones* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calendar reminder, paper passes in the glovebox · source: knowledge

### Public transit reliability

359. **Ghost buses on the tracker** — The tracker shows a bus coming in 6 minutes, riders wait, and it never appears because the app mixed scheduled runs with real-time data or the run was canceled.  
    *bus riders in cities with unreliable feeds* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: wait for two predictions to agree, call a ride-hail · source: [article](https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/ghost-buses-may-no-longer-232500561.html) — "buses that show up on transit tracker apps but fail to arrive in real life"
360. **Station elevator out of service** — Riders with strollers, wheelchairs or luggage arrive to find the only elevator broken, with outage information buried or not published.  
    *riders needing step-free access* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: check agency alerts page, ask staff · source: knowledge
361. **Bus chronically late on every route** — The bus is routinely late so riders never know whether to wait, walk, or give up, and they end up leaving far earlier than needed as insurance.  
    *daily bus commuters* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: leave early, check agency tracker, text a coworker · source: [article](https://www.pressreader.com/canada/valley-journal-advertiser/20180814/281913068947494) — "Transit riders' biggest complaint has always been that buses regularly arrive late along every route."
362. **Buses vanish and reappear on tracker** — Real-time bus positions jump, disappear, and come back spontaneously, so riders cannot tell if the vehicle is two stops away or was pulled from service.  
    *regional bus commuters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: refresh repeatedly, compare two different apps · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/nj-transit-bus-time-knows-083103134.html) — "buses appearing or disappearing from the tracker spontaneously"
363. **Missing the bus by seconds** — You check the arrival time, walk to the stop, and watch the doors close as you arrive because the prediction did not account for your walking time.  
    *transit riders who check arrivals before leaving* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: pad an extra 5 minutes, run · source: [article](https://www.pressreader.com/canada/valley-journal-advertiser/20180814/281913068947494) — "getting there only to find that you've missed it by a split second or the doors close as soon as you get there"
364. **Real-time arrival times simply wrong** — Predicted arrival times are off by many minutes or missing for entire routes, so riders stop trusting the app and revert to guessing.  
    *riders on agency-built transit apps* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: use the printed schedule, ask other riders · source: [article](https://www.deccanherald.com/india/karnataka/bengaluru/it-works-but-can-be-better-users-of-bus-tracking-app-2743986) — "never showing information of buses on some routes, and the expected time of arrival of buses is also incorrect"
