365. **Unreliable service disruption updates** — Delays, reroutes and platform changes are announced late, inconsistently, or only in-station, so commuters find out after they are already stuck.  
    *rail and subway commuters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: follow agency social accounts, group chats with coworkers · source: [article](https://medium.com/@fyjzz007/the-hidden-struggles-of-daily-commuters-and-how-technology-can-help-uncover-solutions-6c174b75d47b) — "Delayed trains, unaffordable costs, and exhausting distances... unreliable real-time updates"

### Ride-hailing

366. **Driver accepts then cancels** — Drivers accept the trip, see the destination or wait for surge, then cancel, leaving the rider restarting the request and now late.  
    *ride-hail riders, especially short or awkward trips* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: book two apps at once, add a tip up front · source: [article](https://unstar.app/blog/ride-sharing-app-reviews-uber-lyft-bolt-grab-2026) — "Drivers accepting rides and then canceling, often after seeing the destination or waiting for surge"
367. **Fare higher than the estimate** — The ride-hail fare shown at booking is not what gets charged; surge or fees appear after the ride, and there is no chance to cancel when the price jumps.  
    *ride-hail users* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: screenshot the estimate, dispute afterwards · source: [article](https://unstar.app/blog/ride-sharing-app-reviews-uber-lyft-bolt-grab-2026) — "frustration about unpredictable prices and hidden fees that appear after the ride"
368. **Finding the driver in a crowd** — At airports, malls and events the pickup pin is wrong or ambiguous, the rider and driver circle each other, and the ride gets canceled or charged a wait fee.  
    *ride-hail riders at busy venues* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: call the driver, walk to a landmark · source: [article](https://medium.com/design-bootcamp/3-common-ux-problems-in-apps-like-uber-you-might-have-faced-them-6795ab2c27da) — "difficult to find their drivers at crowded places like airports, malls or at any large events"
369. **No human for ride disputes** — Refund and overcharge disputes go to an automated support flow that loops without resolution, so small fare errors are simply eaten.  
    *ride-hail riders with a billing problem* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: chargeback with the card issuer, give up · source: [app-review](https://www.consumeraffairs.com/travel/uber.html?page=4) — "Customer support was frequently described as unresponsive or automated"
370. **Payment fails, ride silently dropped** — A card decline or payment glitch cancels the request without a clear message, and sometimes money is held even though no ride was confirmed.  
    *ride-hail riders* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: re-add card, keep a second payment method · source: [article](https://medium.com/@raveenaamarasiriwardena/solving-what-stops-the-ride-a-ux-case-study-focused-on-clarity-trust-and-user-experience-c4c98395f60e) — "Payment failures caused cancellations or ride confirmation delays"
371. **Splitting a shared ride's cost** — Coworkers or friends share a ride-hail and then chase each other for their share, with tips and surge making the math awkward.  
    *people sharing ride-hails* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Venmo requests, letting it slide · source: knowledge

### Transit comfort & crowding

372. **Overcrowded trains and buses** — Commuters board packed vehicles with no way to know which run or which car will have space, so they get crushed or watch full vehicles pass.  
    *peak-hour transit riders* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: shift departure time, walk to an earlier stop · source: [article](https://www.scribd.com/document/950032101/2-Problem-Validation) — "Overcrowding is perceived as the most painful commuting issue"
373. **Guessing which car is least crowded** — Seating, temperature and crowding vary wildly between cars and runs, and riders cannot tell before boarding where they will get a seat or a working AC.  
    *subway and commuter rail riders* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: learn from experience, walk to the far end of the platform · source: [article](https://medium.com/@fyjzz007/the-hidden-struggles-of-daily-commuters-and-how-technology-can-help-uncover-solutions-6c174b75d47b) — "comfort factors like seating, temperature, and crowding, as well as delay frequency"
374. **Which door for the fastest exit** — Riders do not know which car and door lines up with the stairs at their exit station, so they lose minutes and miss connections.  
    *subway and commuter rail riders* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: memorize over months · source: knowledge

<a id="shopping-consumer"></a>

## Shopping & consumer life

*online shopping, returns, deliveries, price tracking, warranties, customer service, scams*


### Choosing & buying

375. **Guessing clothing sizes online** — Brand sizing is inconsistent and shoppers do not know their own measurements, so they buy two or three sizes and return the rest.  
    *online apparel shoppers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bracketing sizes and returning, reading fit comments · source: [article](https://scet.berkeley.edu/reports/sizemeup/) — "40% of online returns are a result of an incorrect size selection"
376. **Inconsistent sizing between brands** — A medium in one brand is a large in another, and nothing remembers which size fit last time from each store.  
    *repeat apparel buyers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app with sizes per brand, or memory · source: [article](https://www.practicalecommerce.com/Increase-Apparel-Conversions-with-These-Sizing-Tips) — "many brands are not consistent with sizing"
377. **Forced app downloads and accounts** — Retailers push shoppers to install yet another app or create an account to check out or get the deal, and people resent the clutter and data exposure.  
    *in-store and online shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: guest checkout, deleting the app afterwards · source: [article](https://www.retaildive.com/news/consumers-arent-buying-goods-with-retail-apps/561299/) — "Thirty-seven percent of respondents said they did not want to download retail apps because they already have too many"
378. **Promotional email flood** — Every purchase adds another daily marketing newsletter, and the real order updates get lost among sales blasts.  
    *online shoppers* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: unsubscribing one at a time, throwaway email addresses · source: knowledge
379. **Rebuying what you already own** — People buy a second charger, spice or tool because they forgot they already had one at home.  
    *busy households* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: texting a family member to check, or none · source: knowledge
380. **Wishlists scattered across stores** — Items saved for later live in a dozen retailer accounts and browser tabs, so people forget what they wanted or buy duplicates.  
    *planners and gift buyers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: bookmarks, screenshots, notes app · source: knowledge

### Customer service

381. **Chargeback and dispute timelines** — When a refund never arrives, filing a card dispute involves unclear deadlines, evidence requirements and weeks of silence.  
    *consumers stiffed by a merchant* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling the bank and waiting · source: knowledge
382. **Chatbots that do not understand** — Support chatbots misread the issue and loop through canned answers, forcing customers to rephrase repeatedly before getting anywhere.  
    *anyone contacting customer service* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: typing 'agent' repeatedly, calling instead · source: [article](https://www.customerexperiencedive.com/news/customer-service-stressful-chatbots-hold-times/734127/) — "top customer service pain point was 'talking to a bot that doesn't understand me'"
383. **Dreading long hold times** — People put off resolving billing errors or broken products because they expect to sit on hold for an hour, so problems linger unresolved.  
    *consumers with any service issue* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling at odd hours, speakerphone while working · source: [article](https://www.customerexperiencedive.com/news/customer-service-stressful-chatbots-hold-times/734127/) — "Two-thirds of respondents say they hesitate to contact customer service because they expect long hold times"
384. **Getting a human on the line** — Escalating from an automated system to a real person is deliberately hard, with hidden menu options and no obvious path.  
    *consumers with non-standard problems* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pressing 0 repeatedly, GetHuman-style cheat sheets · source: [article](https://www.customerexperiencedive.com/news/customers-losing-patience-automated-customer-support/823434/) — "56% say it's difficult to escalate to a live agent when needed"
385. **Repeating the story to every agent** — Each transfer or follow-up call starts from zero: order number, what happened, what was promised last time, because there is no shared record the customer controls.  
    *consumers with multi-contact issues* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: keeping notes in a text file, asking for case numbers · source: [article](https://www.vonage.com/resources/articles/top-customer-communication-pain-points/) — "65% of consumers say that contacting customer service multiple times to resolve a single issue is a key pain point"
386. **Slow, vague email support replies** — Support emails take days to answer and the answers are often unclear or do not address the question, stretching a simple issue over weeks.  
    *consumers dealing with online retailers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: chasing with follow-up emails, posting on social media · source: [article](https://hiverhq.com/blog/customer-support-survey) — "51% of customers say that it takes too long to get a response from support agents via email"
387. **Tracking an open complaint's promises** — Agents promise callbacks, refunds 'in 5-7 days' or replacement shipments, and the customer has no easy way to track what was promised, by whom, and when to chase.  
    *consumers with unresolved disputes* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app, screenshots of chat transcripts · source: knowledge

### Deliveries & packages

388. **Packages stolen from the porch** — Deliveries left unattended get taken, and the recipient must prove theft to the retailer or carrier to get a replacement.  
    *urban residents and apartment dwellers* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: shipping to work, lockers, doorbell cameras · source: [article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Package_theft) — "Taking possession of a package or its contents, outside of a residence or business"
389. **Failed delivery without a knock** — The driver never rings or even approaches the door, yet the tracking says 'attempted delivery' and the recipient has to chase a redelivery or pick up at a depot.  
    *apartment dwellers and people working from home* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: watching for the truck, doorbell cameras, complaining to the carrier · source: [forum](https://forums.anandtech.com/threads/god-i-hate-ups.284042/post-27770454) — "watching the truck stop and leave in under 5 seconds without the driver even walking to the door"
390. **Marked delivered but not received** — Tracking says delivered, nothing is at the door, and the shopper must figure out whether it went to a neighbor, the wrong address, or was stolen, then convince support.  
    *online shoppers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: checking with neighbors, waiting 48 hours, filing claims · source: knowledge
391. **Waiting home all day for a delivery** — Signature-required or large-item deliveries come with a vague all-day window, so people burn a day off waiting and still sometimes get nothing.  
    *people receiving furniture, appliances or signature packages* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: refreshing tracking, calling the carrier for an ETA · source: [forum](https://community.ebay.com/t5/Shipping/A-day-wasted-waiting-on-FedEx-AGAIN/td-p/29013607/jump-to/first-unread-message) — "A day wasted waiting on FedEx AGAIN"
392. **Grocery delivery substitutions** — Delivered groceries include unwanted substitutions or missing staples, discovered only when unpacking, and reporting each item for credit is tedious.  
    *grocery delivery users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: checking the app during shopping, filing refunds afterwards · source: knowledge
393. **Manually entering tracking numbers** — Order confirmation emails contain the tracking info, yet apps fail to pull it automatically and the user must copy numbers by hand for every order.  
    *frequent online shoppers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: copy-paste from email, or relying on each store's own app · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/1223471316/shop-package-order-tracker/reviews) — "can't figure out how to pull tracking information from order confirmation emails"
394. **Missing items in a delivered order** — Multi-item orders ship in parts, and the shopper cannot tell whether a missing item is still coming or was left out of the box until they audit every shipment.  
    *people placing large online orders* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: rereading shipping emails and comparing to the box contents · source: knowledge
395. **No single view of what arrives when** — With several packages in flight, there is no clear list ordered by expected arrival, so people cannot plan who needs to be home.  
    *households with multiple incoming orders* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scrolling through many emails and apps · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/290986013/deliveries-a-package-tracker/reviews) — "aren't displayed in chronological order, making it annoying to scan the entire list"
396. **Tracking updates lag hours behind** — Aggregator apps show stale status, so people still have to open each carrier's site to learn where a package really is.  
    *anyone expecting a delivery* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: visiting the carrier site directly · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/507014023/aftership-package-tracker/reviews) — "having to visit the actual carrier tracking website to find out where their package is"
397. **Coordinating deliveries with household** — Roommates or family members do not know whose package arrived, who signed for it, or where it was put, leading to lost and mistakenly opened parcels.  
    *shared households and roommates* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: group chats and sticky notes · source: knowledge
398. **Notification spam for old packages** — Tracking apps keep pinging about packages delivered months ago or ones the user never tracked, burying the alerts that matter.  
    *package tracker app users* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: muting notifications entirely · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/1509640911/track-your-packages-1track/reviews) — "constantly send notifications about packages that were delivered months ago"

### Prices & deals

399. **Fake pre-sale price hikes** — Retailers raise the price days before a sale event and then 'discount' it back to the normal price, so shoppers cannot tell if a sale is real.  
    *deal hunters during Prime Day, Black Friday and similar events* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: checking CamelCamelCamel or Keepa price history manually · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.aol.com/articles/sales-scam-redditor-catches-amazon-192037156.html) — "bumped up to $119 right before Black Friday, then dropped back to $89 with a 26% off tag"
400. **Comparing true total cost across stores** — The cheapest listed price often loses once shipping, tax, membership fees and return shipping are added, and comparing that total across several sites is manual work.  
    *budget-conscious online shoppers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: opening several tabs and adding items to carts to see final totals · source: knowledge
401. **Permanent 'discounts' that mean nothing** — Many products are 'on sale' more than half the time, so the strikethrough price is fiction and shoppers cannot judge what a fair price really is.  
    *anyone comparison shopping online* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: ignoring list prices; asking friends what they paid · source: [article](https://techradar.com/pro/tech-deals-in-2025-navigating-ghost-discounts-and-fake-reviews) — "most discounts being available more than half the time"
402. **Price drops right after purchase** — The thing you bought last week goes on sale, and you do not know whether the store offers a price adjustment or how to claim it before the adjustment window ends.  
    *online and big-box shoppers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: noticing by chance and calling support, or rebuying and returning · source: knowledge
403. **Price history only exists for Amazon** — Price history tools cover Amazon well but not most other retailers, so buyers of furniture, appliances or clothing elsewhere still shop blind.  
    *shoppers buying from non-Amazon stores* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: screenshotting prices and checking back later · source: [article](https://www.foxnews.com/tech/three-ways-to-find-the-real-deals-on-amazon) — "CamelCamelCamel browser extension tells you the pricing history of any Amazon item"
404. **Shrinkflation and unit-price confusion** — Package sizes shrink while prices stay flat, and comparing per-unit prices across brands and pack sizes in the aisle or online is tedious.  
    *grocery shoppers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reading tiny shelf unit-price labels or mental math · source: knowledge
405. **Coupon codes that never work** — Shoppers try a string of expired or ineligible coupon codes at checkout and waste time before giving up or paying full price.  
    *online shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: browser coupon extensions with hit-or-miss results · source: knowledge
406. **Waiting for a restock or price target** — A wanted item is out of stock or above budget, and the shopper has to remember to keep checking back across multiple stores.  
    *shoppers hunting a specific product* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: retailer-specific stock alerts, bookmarks, periodic manual checks · source: knowledge

### Returns & refunds

407. **Forgotten return deadlines** — Items bought online sit in the closet until the return window quietly closes, so people end up keeping things they never wanted.  
    *frequent online shoppers, especially clothing buyers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminders, leaving the box by the door, or just eating the cost · source: [app-review](https://returntrack.app/) — "Stop losing money on forgotten returns"
408. **Not knowing when refund lands** — After mailing a return, the shopper has no idea whether the retailer received it or when the money will actually hit their card, so they keep checking statements.  
    *online shoppers who return items* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually checking bank statements and retailer order pages · source: [article](https://finance.yahoo.com/news/refundly-launches-help-consumers-track-130000804.html) — "alerts users the moment their refund hits their credit card"
409. **Per-item return policy exceptions** — A receipt with ten items can have different return windows and rules per item (final sale, electronics 15 days, etc.), and nobody reads the fine print until it is too late.  
    *people who buy many categories at once* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: assume everything is 30 days and hope · source: [app-review](https://refundhaul.com/) — "matches it to the store's policy and subcategory exceptions"
410. **Returns scattered across retailers** — Each retailer has its own return process, portal, label and deadline, so managing several open returns at once means juggling many emails and accounts.  
    *households ordering from many stores* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: searching email for order confirmations one by one · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/rebox/id6737307270) — "managing and returning online orders from all retailers in one simple app"

### Scams & trust

411. **Counterfeits from marketplace sellers** — Commingled inventory and third-party sellers mean a branded product may arrive counterfeit or 'not as described', and proving it for a refund is a hassle.  
    *marketplace shoppers buying brand-name goods* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: buying only 'sold by' the brand, checking seller ratings · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=31624401) — "Anyone else quickly losing confidence in Amazon?"
412. **Fake tracking numbers from sellers** — Scam or sloppy sellers supply tracking numbers that never move or belong to someone else's shipment, leaving the buyer unsure whether to wait or dispute.  
    *marketplace buyers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: waiting past the estimate and then opening a dispute · source: [forum](https://community.shopify.com/t/how-to-identify-and-report-a-scam-online-store/3432/30) — "provide fake tracking numbers"
413. **Is this online store legit?** — A store with a great price and a plausible site may be a scam, and checking domain age, reviews and contact details takes research most shoppers skip.  
    *anyone buying from an unfamiliar site* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: searching '<store> scam' on Reddit, Trustpilot lookups · source: [forum](https://community.shopify.com/t/how-to-identify-and-report-a-scam-online-store/3432) — "search Reddit for '[store name] review' or '[store name] scam'"
414. **Social media ad scam stores** — Instagram and Facebook ads push heavily discounted products from cookie-cutter template shops that ship nothing or junk, and shoppers cannot tell them from real brands.  
    *social media users, gift buyers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: only buying from known brands · source: [forum](https://community.shopify.com/t/how-to-identify-and-report-a-scam-online-store/3432) — "advertise heavily discounted products via Facebook/Instagram ads, provide fake tracking numbers or ship worthless items"
415. **Trusting product reviews** — Shoppers rely on star ratings but know many reviews are bought or merged from unrelated products, and fake-review checkers only guess.  
    *anyone buying on marketplaces* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Fakespot/ReviewMeta, reading only 1-star and 3-star reviews · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=33843733) — "these are just algorithms that don't actually know which reviews are fake"
416. **Phishing 'delivery problem' texts** — Fake USPS/FedEx texts about a held package look just like real notifications, and people expecting a parcel cannot tell which alerts are genuine.  
    *anyone awaiting deliveries* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: never clicking links, checking the carrier site manually · source: knowledge
417. **Review-request overload** — Every purchase triggers a stream of emails and app prompts begging for reviews, which clutters the inbox and trains people to ignore all post-purchase mail, including the important ones.  
    *online shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: unsubscribing, filters · source: [article](https://www.npr.org/2026/02/26/nx-s1-5685107/online-review-survey-feedback-fatigue-amazon-shopping) — "increasingly an obligatory burden for shoppers and sellers alike"

### Subscriptions & billing

418. **Forgotten recurring charges** — Free trials convert and small monthly charges pile up unnoticed across cards, and the person cannot list what they are actually paying for.  
    *households with many digital services* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: reviewing statements line by line once a year · source: [article](https://www.kron4.com/news/national/is-there-an-easier-way-to-cancel-subscriptions/) — "FTC received about 70 consumer complaints a day in 2024 about recurring subscriptions"
419. **Locked out of the account to cancel** — A subscription tied to an old email or lost login keeps charging because the company will not cancel without access to that account.  
    *people who changed jobs or emails* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: bank-level block on the merchant · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.nerdwallet.com/finance/learn/is-there-an-easier-way-to-cancel-subscriptions) — "With that email no longer accessible, the service said it was unable to process a cancellation"
420. **Subscriptions designed to be uncancellable** — Cancelling a subscription means hunting hidden buttons, sitting through retention pitches or being told to phone during business hours.  
    *anyone with recurring subscriptions* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: cancelling the card, emailing repeatedly · source: [reddit-quoted](https://www.nerdwallet.com/finance/learn/is-there-an-easier-way-to-cancel-subscriptions) — "One frustrated Reddit user recently shared that canceling a subscription seemed impossible"
421. **Unused gift cards and store credit** — Gift cards, store credits and refund vouchers get forgotten in drawers or email, and their balances are lost or expire.  
    *gift recipients, frequent returners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a pile of cards in a wallet, checking balances by phone · source: knowledge
422. **Loyalty points expiring unnoticed** — Points and rewards across dozens of retailer programs expire or sit unredeemed because there is no single view of balances and deadlines.  
    *shoppers enrolled in multiple loyalty programs* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: logging into each program occasionally · source: knowledge

### Warranties & receipts

423. **Product recalls go unnoticed** — Recalls for items already in the home (cribs, heaters, food) are announced but owners rarely hear about them because nobody connects purchases to recall notices.  
    *parents and homeowners* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none, or luck via news · source: knowledge
424. **Warranty expiry dates unknown** — By the time an appliance or gadget breaks, nobody remembers when it was bought or whether it is still under warranty, so claims are missed.  
    *homeowners and gadget owners* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: digging through email or a drawer of receipts · source: [app-review](https://warranty-keeper.com/) — "push notification 30 days before a warranty is about to expire"
425. **Receipts and manuals lost** — Paper receipts fade or get tossed, and product manuals vanish, so when something needs a return, warranty claim or repair the proof and instructions are gone.  
    *everyone who buys durable goods* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: shoebox of receipts, photographing them, searching email · source: [article](https://techwiser.com/best-warranty-and-manual-organizer-apps-to-keep-tracks-of-products/) — "keep track of purchase dates, expiration timelines, and serial numbers in one place"
426. **Serial numbers and proof of purchase** — Warranty claims and insurance reports demand serial numbers, model numbers and purchase proof that are printed on the item or long-gone packaging.  
    *people filing warranty or theft/insurance claims* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photographing labels after the fact, if the item is still around · source: [article](https://www.xda-developers.com/self-hosted-app-keep-track-device-warranties/) — "add adjacent essentials like invoices or product receipts as well as user manuals"
427. **Extended warranty worth-it decision** — At checkout the shopper must decide in seconds whether an extended warranty or protection plan is worth it, with no data on failure rates or what the card already covers.  
    *electronics and appliance buyers* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: guessing, or defaulting to 'no' · source: knowledge

<a id="relationships-social"></a>

## Friends, family & social plans

*keeping in touch, planning meetups, group decisions, gifts, birthdays, shared chores*


### Birthdays & occasions

428. **Birthday reminder arrives too late to act** — The reminder fires on the day itself, when it is too late to buy or post a gift, so the best you can manage is a text.  
    *gift-givers with friends and family far away* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually set a second reminder a week earlier · source: [forum](https://www.quora.com/Is-there-any-app-to-remember-birthdays)
429. **Guest list and headcount for a birthday party** — Kids' and adult parties need a headcount for food and venue, but invites go out through three channels and replies trickle in inconsistently.  
    *parents and party hosts* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Evite/Partiful, manual tally · source: knowledge
430. **Losing birthdays after leaving Facebook** — People who relied on Facebook to surface birthdays quit the platform and suddenly miss most friends' birthdays because the dates live nowhere else.  
    *people who deleted social media* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: ask friends for dates; Google Calendar contacts sync · source: [article](https://favshq.com/blog/never-miss-a-birthday-again-the-5-best-tools-to-remember-birthdays) — "Facebook ... has been the go-to tool for remembering birthdays for years"
431. **Card and gift deadlines for mailed occasions** — Remembering to post a card or gift early enough for it to arrive abroad in time is a separate calculation each occasion, and it is usually late.  
    *people with friends and family overseas* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: e-cards, apologetic late gifts · source: knowledge

### Family coordination

432. **Family calendars that never stay in sync** — Each family member uses a different calendar app and the group chat acts as a third one, so pickups and appointments get double-booked.  
    *families with kids and working parents* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: one parent manually re-enters everything · source: [article](https://whennot.com/blog/best-scheduling-tools-for-families-comparison) — "the family group chat serves as a third 'calendar,' nothing stays synced"
433. **Finding a call time across time zones** — Calling parents or a friend abroad means mentally converting time zones every time and missing the narrow window when both are awake and free.  
    *expats, immigrants, long-distance families* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: world clock app, standing Sunday call · source: knowledge
434. **Keeping grandparents in the loop on kids** — Grandparents want regular photos and updates but are not in the fast-moving group chats, so they feel left out or get a dump once a month.  
    *parents with distant grandparents* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: separate family chat, printed photos · source: knowledge
435. **Calendar apps mangling event times** — Typing 'vets at 2pm' in a title auto-sets a time and adds an hour, so shared events end up wrong and someone shows up late.  
    *families using shared calendar apps* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually correct each entry · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/1003205988/looping-family-calendar/reviews) — "automatically puts the time from all day to the specified time and adds an hour, which is annoying"
436. **Family location sharing drains batteries** — All-in-one family apps keep GPS running to show where everyone is, so teens' phones die and they turn sharing off.  
    *parents of teens using family apps* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: disable location; text 'where are you' · source: [app-review](https://www.growmaple.com/blog-posts/best-family-calendar-app) — "a subset flag battery drain from continuous location tracking"

### Gifts

437. **Duplicate gifts from an unshared wishlist** — Grandparents and siblings buy the same toy or book because the child's or couple's wishlist lives in a text to one person only.  
    *extended families at Christmas and birthdays* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Amazon wishlists, group text 'I've got the Lego' · source: knowledge
438. **Gift ideas forgotten by the time they matter** — A friend mentions something they'd love in March; by their December birthday you have no idea what it was and panic-buy a candle.  
    *anyone who buys gifts for friends and family* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: keep a running notes list, if disciplined · source: [article](https://seventhings.substack.com/p/give-gifts-better) — "Coming up with the perfect gifting idea for everyone out of nowhere is nearly impossible"
439. **No idea what to get someone** — Facing a birthday for a sibling-in-law or coworker with no clue of their tastes, people default to gift cards and feel bad about it.  
    *everyone, several times a year* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ask a mutual friend; gift cards; ChatGPT prompts · source: [article](https://www.tomsguide.com/ai/chatgpt/7-chatgpt-prompts-im-using-to-improve-my-gift-giving-heres-how-to-try-them)
440. **Organising a Secret Santa across a group** — Drawing names anonymously, honouring exclusions (couples, last year's pairs), sharing budgets and wishlists all by hand is fiddly and error-prone.  
    *families, friend groups and offices at holidays* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: names in a hat; free drawname sites · source: [article](https://giftlist.com/blog/10-secret-santa-gift-exchange-rules-everyone-should-know) — "set one budget, draw names randomly and keep them anonymous, honor exclusions, share wish lists"
441. **Forgetting what you gave last year** — You cannot recall whether you already gave Dad that whisky or Mum that scarf, and risk repeating a gift.  
    *regular gift-givers* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: search order history · source: knowledge
442. **Gifting people you rarely see** — For relatives seen once or twice a year it is hard to remember conversations from months ago that would hint at a good gift.  
    *people with extended family or distant friends* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: write notes immediately after seeing them · source: [article](https://gumshoe.substack.com/p/how-to-give-better-gifts) — "it's hard to remember conversations from months ago, but writing down thoughts immediately after seeing them helps"

### Group decisions

443. **Conflicting opinions derail group trips** — Every trip decision (destination, hotel, activities) triggers disagreement between budget-minded and comfort-minded friends, and the planner absorbs the friction.  
    *friend groups planning holidays* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: one leader decides for everyone; long voice calls · source: [article](https://www.blukyte.co/post/is-group-trip-planning-giving-you-a-headache-here-s-how-to-make-it-easy-and-fun) — "53% of group travelers experience frustration during planning due to conflicting opinions"
444. **Group vacations straining friendships** — Mismatched expectations about budget, pace and schedule turn a week away into low-grade conflict that damages the friendship afterward.  
    *friends who travel together* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: talk it out beforehand, or avoid group trips · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/lifestyle/8-most-traumatic-things-taking-163033721.html) — "62% of people have had a friendship strained by a group vacation"
445. **Deciding where to eat as a group** — 'Where should we eat?' produces twenty messages of 'I don't mind' and one holdout with a dietary restriction, and nobody wants to be the one to choose.  
    *friend groups and couples* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: someone finally picks; coin flips; swipe apps · source: [article](https://sapor.app/) — "dinner stops costing twenty messages and one holdout"
446. **Tracking dietary restrictions across friends** — When hosting or booking, you have to remember who is vegan, gluten-free or allergic to nuts, and someone always gets forgotten.  
    *hosts and restaurant-pickers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ask again every time · source: knowledge
447. **Deciding a movie or game for the group** — Picking what to watch or play takes longer than the activity itself as everyone vetoes and nobody commits.  
    *friends and families on a night in* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: random pick, host decides · source: knowledge

### Keeping in touch

448. **Always being the one who initiates** — One person in a friendship does all the reaching out and never knows whether the other cares or is just busy, and eventually stops trying.  
    *the 'initiator' friend* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: wait and see if they notice; confront them · source: [article](https://socialself.com/blog/why-friends-stop-keeping-in-touch/)
449. **Friend group shrinking after 25** — As people settle down, they stop investing in the wider circle and are left with few people to call for a casual weekend plan.  
    *late-20s and 30s adults* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: meetup groups, hobby clubs · source: [article](https://www.stylist.co.uk/long-reads/friendship-groups-best-friends-family-whatsapp-social-media-relationship-advice/350372) — "Social circles shrink at the age of 25"
450. **Friendships fading from sheer busyness** — Jobs, partners and kids take over, months pass without a message, and a good friend becomes someone you 'should really call'.  
    *adults 25+* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: guilt-driven sporadic catch-ups · source: [forum](https://www.quora.com/Is-it-common-for-adults-to-lose-touch-with-their-friends-due-to-changes-in-peoples-lives-over-time) — "Jobs, partners, kids etc all become primary things in someone's life and take up a lot of time"
451. **Birthday reminders without meaningful contact** — A once-a-year 'HBD!' is all a relationship gets; the real gap is the eleven silent months in between, which no birthday app addresses.  
    *people with many casual friendships* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Facebook birthday notifications · source: [article](https://www.myamira.com/blog/birthday-reminder-apps) — "the issue is not forgetting birthdays, but the absence of contact in between them"
452. **Forgetting who you haven't spoken to in ages** — With dozens of friends spread across cities, you lose track of how long it has been since you last talked to each, and the quiet ones slip away.  
    *people with large, dispersed social circles* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scroll message history; personal CRM apps · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=14895474) — "know how long it has been since you last spoke ... remind you when it's time to reach out"
453. **Remembering details people told you** — A friend mentioned their mother's surgery or their kid's name months ago and you cannot recall it when you next meet, which feels careless.  
    *anyone with many acquaintances or infrequent friends* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: notes app, or bluffing · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=25270001) — "Remember everything about your friends, family and etc."
454. **Reminder apps feel like a chore list for friends** — Setting 'contact Sam every 3 weeks' turns friendship into ticket-closing and induces guilt when reminders pile up unanswered.  
    *people who tried personal CRM apps* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: delete the app · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/-/id6741321870) — "a mindful companion instead of an annoying reminder manager"
455. **Sharing photos after an outing** — Everyone took photos on their own phone and they never all end up in one place; the group asks 'can you send those pics?' for weeks.  
    *friend groups and families after events* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: shared albums, WhatsApp compression · source: knowledge

### Money between friends

456. **Collecting everyone's share for a group booking** — The person who books the villa or tickets fronts hundreds of dollars and then spends weeks chasing friends with different amounts and due dates.  
    *group trip organisers* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet plus repeated Venmo requests · source: [article](https://buoyanttravel.com/stories/5-reasons-group-trip-planning-always-goes-wrong-and-what-to-do-instead) — "For the group planner, chasing payments can get overwhelming, with so many people having various due dates and amounts."
457. **Friends who never pay you back** — You cover dinner or tickets and the small IOUs pile up; asking feels petty, not asking breeds resentment.  
    *anyone who fronts money for friends* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Splitwise, mental tally, letting it go · source: [article](https://www.splitwise.com/) — "track bills and other shared expenses, so that everyone gets paid back"
458. **Expense-splitting app paywalls** — The go-to splitting app now limits free users to a few entries a day and shows countdown ads, so logging a trip's receipts becomes a chore.  
    *Splitwise free users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: switch apps, wait until tomorrow, spreadsheet · source: [app-review](https://kimola.com/reports/explore-why-splitwise-users-are-drifting-away-get-insights-now-app-store-in-155789) — "reached the maximum number of expenses they could input in a day and had to either upgrade ... or wait until tomorrow"
459. **Pooling money for a group gift** — Someone buys the joint present and then has to collect $15 from twelve people, some of whom never pay, while payment apps take fees.  
    *coworkers, friend groups, wedding guests* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Venmo requests, cash envelopes · source: [article](https://giftlist.com/blog/10-secret-santa-gift-exchange-rules-everyone-should-know) — "set a budget everyone agrees on, collect money fee-free, pick the gift, and present it"

### Planning meetups

460. **Chasing RSVPs before a gathering** — Hosts have no idea how many people are coming because half the invitees never answer, so they cannot book a table or buy food with confidence.  
    *anyone hosting dinners, parties or birthdays* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: individually DM each person the day before · source: [article](https://www.timeout.com/newyork/things-to-do/group-planning-free-mobile-apps-and-online-tools) — "create events inside a chat, complete with RSVPs"
461. **Finding a date everyone is free** — Getting five friends to a shared evening means endless back-and-forth of 'I can't do the 12th' with no single view of who is free when.  
    *busy adults with friends on different schedules* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Doodle/When2meet polls, repeated messages · source: [article](https://howbout.app/blog/making-plans/the-app-to-get-friends-together) — "share availability, create plans in one place, and skip the back-and-forth messaging"
462. **Group chat plans that never get decided** — A question to eight people at once is everyone's job and therefore no one's; a single Saturday plan takes dozens of messages and often dies in the thread.  
    *friend groups coordinating over WhatsApp/iMessage* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: one person unilaterally declares a plan, or the plan quietly dies · source: [article](https://flaky-app.com/blog/why-group-chats-kill-plans) — "The average group chat takes 50 messages to confirm one Saturday plan."
463. **Last-minute cancellations by flaky friends** — Plans made a week ago collapse the same day when one or two people bail, leaving the rest unsure whether the outing is still on.  
    *anyone with unreliable friends* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: over-invite, accept it, or stop making plans with that person · source: [article](https://msutoday.msu.edu/news/2023/01/cancelling-plans-research) — "80% of participants said that canceling plans would not affect their friendship, but they would be upset if... the reason was a lie."
464. **Plans stall in 'group chat limbo'** — A trip or outing gets enthusiastic agreement, then sits unresolved for weeks or months with no dates, no bookings and slowly dying energy.  
    *anyone organising a trip or outing with friends* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: an organiser nags repeatedly or gives up · source: [article](https://www.afar.com/magazine/how-to-plan-the-perfect-group-trip-without-the-stress) — "84% of travelers have had a group trip stall out at the planning stage... 'group chat limbo' for one to three months."
465. **Unequal workload on the trip organiser** — One friend ends up researching, booking and coordinating everything while others just show up, which breeds resentment and strains friendships.  
    *the 'planner friend' in every group* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none; quietly resent it · source: [article](https://medium.com/design-bootcamp/simplifying-the-trip-planning-experience-for-group-trip-leaders-a-product-design-case-study-ffc3e10eb547) — "fragmented communication, decision fatigue, and unequal workload distribution"
466. **Casual plans never land on a calendar** — Social plans feel too informal to bother putting in a calendar, so they get double-booked or forgotten until the reminder text arrives.  
    *people who keep social life in their head* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: memory, or manual calendar entry after the fact · source: [forum](https://www.indiehackers.com/post/how-do-you-validate-a-free-product-social-network-21eb3cd46a) — "Social plans are casual and informal, people avoid adding events to calendars"
467. **Every social app needs all your friends on it** — Planning tools only work if the whole group installs them, so the organiser ends up back in the group chat copying links for the holdouts.  
    *people who try planning apps with friends* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: fall back to text/email; use apps with guest links · source: [forum](https://www.indiehackers.com/post/idea-validation-social-networks-are-useless-and-how-to-fix-it-26e05c7fbf) — "must escape 'empty room syndrome' ... users won't likely find much use unless their friends are on it"
468. **Not knowing which friends are nearby or free tonight** — You would happily grab a drink if you knew a friend was in the neighbourhood and free, but finding out means messaging everyone individually.  
    *urban young adults* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: post in group chat 'anyone around?' · source: [article](https://theteardown.substack.com/p/learn-where-friends-are-by-talking)
469. **Plan details buried in the chat scroll** — The address, time and who is driving get lost hundreds of messages back in a noisy group chat, so people keep re-asking on the day.  
    *friend groups and families using chat to organise* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: pin a message, re-send the details, screenshot · source: [forum](https://www.indiehackers.com/post/how-do-you-validate-a-free-product-social-network-21eb3cd46a) — "group chats are noisy and disorganized with plans getting lost in endless scrolling"
470. **Potluck sign-ups with five desserts and no mains** — Without a shared list of who brings what, potlucks end up with duplicate dishes and gaps, and the host fields the same question ten times.  
    *hosts of potlucks, BBQs, office parties* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Sheet, SignUpGenius, group chat · source: knowledge
471. **Recurring meetups that quietly die** — The monthly board-game night or weekly run stops once someone forgets to send the reminder, and nobody restarts it.  
    *hobby and friend groups* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: one dedicated organiser · source: knowledge
472. **Ride and carpool logistics to events** — Who is driving, who needs a lift and who is sober gets sorted in a chaotic burst of messages an hour before leaving.  
    *friend groups going to events* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: last-minute texting · source: knowledge
473. **Cancelling plans without a clear message** — People agonise over how to cancel and end up inventing excuses, which research shows hurts friendships more than the cancellation itself.  
    *anyone who has to bail on a plan* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: templates, white lies · source: [article](https://amp.cbc.ca/lite/story/1.4243593)
474. **Rotating who hosts or organises** — The same person hosts every dinner or books every trip because there is no visible record of whose turn it is.  
    *regular friend and family gatherings* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none; the reliable person keeps doing it · source: knowledge

### Shared chores

475. **Reminding a partner about chores without nagging** — One partner carries the mental load of remembering every household task and either does it or nags, and both options cause friction.  
    *cohabiting couples* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: shared to-do apps, verbal reminders · source: knowledge
476. **Roommates' uneven cleaning habits** — Dishes and bathrooms get cleaned by whoever cracks first; the tidy roommate does most of it and there is no shared record to point to.  
    *renters with roommates* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: passive-aggressive notes, house meetings, chore wheels · source: [article](https://www.rent.com/blog/best-apps-for-roommates/) — "37 percent named cleaning habits as their top roommate pet peeve, far ahead of any money complaint"
477. **Re-negotiating chores every single week** — Who takes out the bins or buys toilet paper is argued afresh each week because nothing records the rotation or the last time it was done.  
    *shared households* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: fridge chart, chore apps · source: [article](https://cohabby.com/blog/best-apps-for-roommates/) — "turn those repeat negotiations into a system nobody has to think about"
478. **Household chore apps too childish for adults** — Most chore trackers are gamified for kids with points and monsters, which feels patronising to adult roommates and gets abandoned.  
    *adult roommates and couples* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: shared notes or spreadsheets · source: [article](https://www.tidied.app/blog/best-chore-apps-roommates)

<a id="parenting-childcare"></a>

## Parenting & childcare

*babies, toddlers, school-age kids, school logistics, co-parenting, activities*


### Activities & transport

479. **Overlapping activity schedules per child** — With two kids in three activities each, practices, games and recitals overlap and change weekly, and one parent cannot be at two fields at once.  
    *parents of multiple school-age kids* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: shared calendar and constant renegotiation · source: knowledge
480. **Summer camp week-by-week puzzle** — Summer requires stitching together ten different camps with different hours, registration dates and drop-off locations, and popular ones fill within minutes of opening.  
    *working parents of school-age kids* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a color-coded spreadsheet in February · source: knowledge
481. **Birthday party RSVPs never come** — Parents send invites through backpacks or Evite and half the families never reply, so they cannot plan food, goody bags or venue headcount.  
    *parents hosting kids' parties* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: chase each parent individually · source: knowledge
482. **Carpool with only trusted families** — Parents want to share driving to practices but existing rideshare apps use strangers; coordinating with known families happens in messy group texts.  
    *parents of kids in activities* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: group text and a whiteboard · source: [article](https://tinybeans.com/floc-app-carpools-for-kids/) — "designed by parents for parents and doesn't rely on drivers you've never met"
483. **Not knowing the kid was picked up** — When another parent drives, the family at home has no confirmation the child got in the car or arrived, and texts asking for updates feel nagging.  
    *parents relying on carpools* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: text the driver · source: [article](https://gokid.mobi/parents/) — "Schedule rides, get notifications, driving directions, track your child"
484. **Sports schedule changes by text** — Coaches change practice times or cancel for weather via a team chat, and the parent driving that day is not the one who saw the message.  
    *parents of kids in team sports* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: forward screenshots to partner · source: knowledge
485. **Whose turn to drive this week** — Rotating carpool duty across four families for a twice-weekly practice collapses when someone is sick, and nobody remembers who covered last time.  
    *parents in carpool groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: shared spreadsheet that goes stale · source: [article](https://coolmomtech.com/2018/02/carpooling-figure8-app-review/) — "creates the events your kids need rides to, like, soccer practice or play rehearsal"
486. **Playdate scheduling ping-pong** — Setting up one playdate takes days of back-and-forth texts comparing two families' nap times, activities and custody weekends.  
    *parents of preschool and elementary kids* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: long text threads · source: knowledge

### Babies & toddlers

487. **Caregiver feed logs sync late** — When two caregivers log feeds and naps on their own phones, one person's entries show up on the other's timeline hours later or the next day, so the baby gets double-fed or a nap is missed.  
    *parents of newborns with a partner or nanny* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: text each other after every feed · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/779656557/baby-tracker-newborn-log/reviews) — "updates from one caregiver don't appear on another's timeline until several hours later or the next day"
488. **Deciding which pediatric symptom needs care** — At night a parent cannot tell whether a fever or rash warrants urgent care, the pediatrician's line is closed, and search results are terrifying.  
    *parents of young children* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: nurse hotline, Google · source: knowledge
489. **Medication doses across caregivers** — Parents, grandparents and daycare each give a child medicine and nobody knows when the last dose was, risking a double dose or a missed one.  
    *families with a sick child* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: sticky note on the bottle · source: knowledge
490. **Introducing solids without a plan** — Parents starting solids lose track of which foods (especially allergens) have been tried, when, and whether there was a reaction.  
    *parents of 6-12 month olds* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app list · source: knowledge
491. **Phone locks mid-nursing session** — While timing a feed one-handed the screen locks, forcing repeated unlocking and password entry all day with a baby on the other arm.  
    *breastfeeding parents* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: disable auto-lock for the whole phone · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/779656557/baby-tracker-newborn-log/reviews) — "annoying when the phone screen locks during nursing sessions, requiring them to unlock and enter passwords repeatedly"
492. **Potty training progress unclear** — Accidents and successes happen across home, daycare and grandparents, so nobody can see whether training is actually progressing or what time of day fails.  
    *parents of 2-3 year olds* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sticker chart · source: knowledge
493. **Tracking a toddler's tantrum triggers** — Parents suspect meltdowns follow skipped snacks or short naps but cannot see the pattern because nobody writes down when they happen.  
    *parents of 2-4 year olds* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: [article](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/-/id6502125545) — "keep track of your experience achieving zero tantrum days"
494. **Vaccine and checkup schedule drift** — Well-child visits and vaccine boosters fall behind because the next due date is only on a paper card from the last appointment.  
    *parents of children under 6* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: clinic reminders if lucky · source: knowledge
495. **Ads in the tracker at 3 a.m.** — Free baby trackers interrupt a half-asleep parent logging a night feed with full-screen ads, and the paid tier locks basic features behind a subscription.  
    *parents of newborns* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: pay, or tolerate it · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/1077177456/glow-baby-newborn-tracker-log/reviews) — "the ads were a little annoying"
496. **Kid milestones and memories scattered** — First words, heights and school photos are spread across phone camera rolls, texts and the daycare app, and never make it into any kind of record.  
    *parents of young kids* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a baby book that stops at month three · source: knowledge
497. **Missed entries break pattern charts** — If a feed or nap is logged late or skipped, the app's pattern graph shows empty gaps instead of a usable trend, making the whole chart untrustworthy.  
    *parents tracking newborn sleep and feeds* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: keep a paper log alongside · source: [app-review](https://justuseapp.com/en/app/779656557/baby-tracker-newborn-log/reviews) — "missing activity in patterns means when it's logged it's not part of the patterns graph, showing only empty gaps"
498. **Sharing baby photos with grandparents** — Relatives want constant photos but parents do not want to post publicly, so pictures go out in scattered texts and nobody has the full set.  
    *parents of babies* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group chats, shared albums · source: [article](https://tinybeans.com/apps-every-new-parent-needs/) — "an easier way to share pictures of your toddler with friends and family"
499. **Toddler's funny sayings get forgotten** — Parents want to remember the odd words and phrases a toddler invents, but they are gone by the next day unless typed somewhere at that moment.  
    *parents of toddlers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: notes app or nothing · source: [article](https://tinybeans.com/apps-every-new-parent-needs/) — "a digital dictionary for the magical, hilarious, and unrepeatable things your toddler says"

### Childcare & sitters

500. **Arranging last-minute childcare cover** — When school closes or a sitter cancels, parents scramble through contacts to find anyone available that day, with no view of who is free.  
    *working parents* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: text everyone, take a sick day · source: [article](https://muthalife.substack.com/p/mental-load-i-have-apps-for-that) — "arranging childcare"
501. **Daycare waitlists with no visibility** — Parents put their name on six daycare waitlists, pay fees, and never learn where they stand or when a spot might open.  
    *expectant and new parents* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: call each center monthly · source: knowledge
502. **Finding a vetted babysitter fast** — Parents want a Saturday-night sitter but the only options are strangers on marketplaces or asking friends' sitters, with no idea of availability or rates.  
    *parents of young children* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups, friend referrals · source: knowledge
503. **Splitting sitter pay in a nanny share** — Two families sharing a nanny argue over hours, sick days, holidays and who owes what, with tracking done in a spreadsheet nobody updates.  
    *nanny-share families* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: spreadsheet and Venmo · source: knowledge
504. **Daycare daily report buried in feed** — The daycare app posts naps, meals and diapers as a scrolling feed with photos, so finding out whether the child ate lunch means scrolling past twenty updates.  
    *parents with kids in daycare* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ask the teacher at pickup · source: knowledge
505. **Sitter handoff notes every time** — Every babysitter or grandparent needs the same rundown (allergies, bedtime routine, pediatrician number, wifi) retyped into a text or explained at the door.  
    *parents who use sitters* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a laminated sheet on the fridge · source: knowledge

### Co-parenting

506. **Co-parent communication breakdowns** — Logistics messages between ex-partners get lost among emotional ones, and there is no neutral, searchable record of who agreed to what about pickups and appointments.  
    *separated co-parents* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: email only, or a mediator · source: [article](https://www.healthline.com/health/parenting/co-parenting-lessons-learned-along-the-way) — "communication breakdowns, scheduling conflicts, and differing parenting styles"
507. **Custody schedule breaks on surprises** — A sick day, a work trip or a school closure forces the whole custody calendar to be renegotiated by text, and swapped days are never recorded anywhere.  
    *separated or divorced co-parents* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: text threads and a paper calendar · source: [article](https://dralizapressman.substack.com/p/navigating-co-parenting-conflicts) — "scheduling can be disrupted when unexpected situations arise that require renegotiation"
508. **Ex unilaterally demands schedule changes** — One parent asks to change the custody schedule with no record of the original agreement, so each request turns into an argument about what was previously agreed.  
    *co-parents with informal arrangements* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: screenshot old texts · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/lifestyle/ex-demanded-change-custody-schedule-210021858.html) — "ex demanded change custody schedule"
509. **Splitting child expenses with an ex** — Shared costs for shoes, camps and copays pile up as receipts in texts, and reconciling who owes whom becomes a monthly fight.  
    *separated co-parents* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet, screenshots, or letting it go · source: knowledge
510. **Both parents not on the school list** — School emails and emergency calls go to one parent only, so the other learns about early pickups or sick days second-hand or not at all.  
    *co-parents and non-custodial parents* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: forward every email manually · source: knowledge
511. **Kid's stuff at the wrong house** — The soccer cleats, inhaler or library book are at the other parent's home on the day they are needed.  
    *kids splitting time between two homes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: duplicates of everything · source: knowledge
512. **New partners muddle handoff plans** — When a step-parent or new partner starts doing pickups, nobody knows who is actually collecting the child on a given day and the school has no updated list.  
    *blended families* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: day-of texts · source: [article](https://dralizapressman.substack.com/p/navigating-co-parenting-conflicts) — "Custody schedules are often a source of contention, especially when there are new partners in the mix"

### Kids & screens

513. **Screen-time limits enforced inconsistently** — Each parent, grandparent and sitter enforces a different screen rule, and built-in controls are easy for kids to bypass or for parents to forget to set.  
    *parents of kids with tablets* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: device parental controls, arguments · source: knowledge
514. **Kids never answer the phone** — A parent trying to confirm a pickup or curfew cannot get a teen or tween to answer calls or texts, even though the phone is clearly in use.  
    *parents of tweens and teens with phones* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: call repeatedly, threaten to take the phone · source: [article](https://www.phonearena.com/news/Parents-kids-not-calling-you-back--Frustrated-mom-develops-app-that-remotely-locks-devices-until-they-do_id59435) — "Parents, kids not calling you back?"

### Mental load & household

515. **Default parent carries all reminders** — One parent silently holds every appointment, form and childcare arrangement in their head because if they do not do it, it does not get done.  
    *primary caregivers in two-parent homes* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none, or nagging · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/lifestyle/why-moms-feel-default-parent-143000160.html) — "If I don't do it, it won't get done."
516. **Invisible mental load cannot be shared** — Anticipating needs (outgrown shoes, upcoming vaccines, snack duty) lives only in one parent's head, so a partner willing to help has no list to take from.  
    *couples trying to split parenting labour* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: talk about it, which changes nothing · source: [article](https://muthalife.substack.com/p/mental-load-i-have-apps-for-that) — "remembering appointments, tracking school supplies, planning meals, arranging childcare, and anticipating needs before they arise"
517. **Morning routine chaos** — Getting kids dressed, fed, teeth brushed and out the door on time takes constant verbal prompting and ends in yelling most days.  
    *parents of young school-age kids* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: picture checklists, waking up earlier · source: knowledge
518. **Daily school lunch packing fatigue** — Packing a lunch the child will actually eat, every single school day, while honoring nut-free rules and avoiding the same three things.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: same lunch every day · source: knowledge
519. **Kids only accept one parent** — Children insist on one parent for bedtime or comfort, so tasks cannot actually be rebalanced even when the other parent is willing.  
    *parents of toddlers and preschoolers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: gradual handoff routines · source: [article](https://allisondaminger.substack.com/p/both-of-our-kids-have-a-strong-preference) — "He could do it - but they'd scream"
520. **Outgrown clothes and next sizes** — Parents discover on a cold morning that no winter coat fits, and do not know which sizes are in the closet, in bins or promised as hand-me-downs.  
    *parents of growing kids* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bins in the garage, guessing · source: knowledge
521. **Chores and allowance tracking** — Chore charts on the fridge are ignored within a week and allowance is paid inconsistently, so kids argue about what was done and what is owed.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper chart, cash jar · source: knowledge

### School logistics

522. **Separate app per teacher and class** — Each teacher picks their own messaging app (ClassDojo, Seesaw, Bloomz, Band...), so a family with two kids may need eight logins that all send notifications.  
    *parents with multiple kids in school* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: install everything, mute most, miss things · source: [article](https://ashleegadd.substack.com/p/too-many-apps-too-little-time) — "not just 40 different communication apps but more like 400 and counting"
523. **Too many school communication apps** — Announcements, grades, bus times, lunch menus and sports schedules arrive across four or more apps plus email, so parents never know where a given piece of information lives.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: check every app each evening, screenshot things into a family chat · source: [article](https://foxnews.com/tech/parents-inundated-overwhelming-school-apps.amp) — "a tangle of disjointed communication platforms for classroom announcements, grades, bus times, lunch menus, sports schedules"
524. **Tracking school event dates per child** — Early dismissals, spirit days, picture day and open houses are spread across several calendars per child; parents keep four apps plus multiple calendars for one kid.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually re-enter everything into a shared Google Calendar · source: [article](https://www.aol.com/lifestyle/parents-signed-classroom-updates-got-090044232.html) — "some parents keeping tabs on four apps plus multiple calendars just for one child"
525. **Apps that add work instead of saving it** — School and parenting apps meant to help instead pull attention, time and energy, each demanding its own notifications, logins and check-ins.  
    *all parents* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: delete apps, rely on word of mouth · source: [article](https://foxnews.com/tech/parents-inundated-overwhelming-school-apps.amp) — "they pull from limited resources of attention, time and energy, becoming overwhelming to manage"
526. **Back-to-school supply list scramble** — Each grade publishes a different supply list with specific brands and quantities; parents cross-check three stores and still end up missing items.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photo of the list, multiple shopping trips · source: [article](https://motherloadnewsletter.substack.com/p/issue-1-the-various-loads-of-motherhood) — "The mental load of preparing for a new school year includes supply lists"
527. **Forgetting special-day items** — Library day, gym shoes, show-and-tell and crazy-hat day each need something specific on a specific weekday, and the reminder was in a newsletter three weeks ago.  
    *parents of elementary kids* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: whiteboard by the door · source: knowledge
528. **Homework and assignment visibility** — Parents cannot see what homework is due until a missing-assignment email arrives, because the portal is hard to read and the kid says 'nothing'.  
    *parents of middle and high schoolers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: check the portal weekly, nag · source: knowledge
529. **Paper permission slips and forms** — Permission slips, physical forms and consent sheets come home crumpled in backpacks, must be signed on paper and returned by a date nobody wrote down.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: fridge magnet and hoping · source: [article](https://motherloadnewsletter.substack.com/p/issue-1-the-various-loads-of-motherhood) — "supply lists, open house nights, meeting teachers, endless forms, physicals, and permission slips"
530. **Parent WhatsApp groups misused for noise** — Class group chats fill with off-topic chatter and complaints, burying the one message about tomorrow's costume day that actually matters.  
    *parents in class group chats* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: mute the group and hope someone tells you · source: [article](https://www.tes.com/news/parents-stop-misusing-class-messaging-apps) — "parents stop misusing class messaging apps"
531. **Remembering which channel holds what** — Teacher email, classroom portal, WhatsApp parent group and paper forms each carry different information, so parents spend cognitive energy remembering where to look for a given fact.  
    *parents of school-age kids* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: search email and chats repeatedly · source: [article](https://sarapetersen.substack.com/p/too-many-apps) — "cognitive energy to remember where to find different pieces of information"

<a id="pets"></a>

## Pets

*dogs, cats, other pets: feeding, vet, training, sitters, walking, health records*


### Costs & insurance

532. **Surprise vet bills** — Treatment costs are unclear until the invoice arrives, and owners cannot compare prices or budget for likely annual expenses.  
    *all pet owners, especially on tight budgets* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking for estimates, care credit cards · source: [article](https://www.avma.org/javma-news/2020-04-01/pain-points)
533. **Pet insurance claim paperwork** — Submitting claims requires itemised invoices and medical notes that owners have to chase from the clinic, then wait weeks for reimbursement.  
    *insured pet owners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photographing receipts, emailing the clinic · source: knowledge
534. **Tracking total pet spending** — Food, litter, vet, grooming and sitters are spread across many stores and cards, so owners have no idea what the pet costs per month.  
    *budget-conscious owners and multi-pet homes* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: general budgeting apps · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/app/my-pet-the-pet-record-maker/id566849086) — "track incidents, weight, height & length, medications, allergies, expenses, pet boarding"

### Feeding & nutrition

535. **Which human foods are toxic** — In the moment a pet grabs a grape, an onion scrap or a lily leaf, owners scramble to find out whether it is dangerous and how much is too much for the pet's weight.  
    *dog and cat owners* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: frantic web searches, calling the vet or a poison hotline · source: knowledge
536. **Not knowing if the pet was fed** — In a household with several people, nobody is sure whether the dog or cat already ate, so the pet gets fed twice or skipped entirely.  
    *families and roommates sharing a pet* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group chat messages, a whiteboard on the fridge, or asking the pet's begging behaviour · source: knowledge
537. **Overwhelming pet food choices** — Choosing the right food takes days or months because there is no easy way to filter brands by the pet's age, allergies, breed and budget.  
    *new pet owners and owners of pets with sensitivities* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reading forum threads and asking the vet or pet store staff · source: [article](https://www.zigpoll.com/content/what-are-the-most-common-user-pain-points-you've-identified-when-pet-owners-interact-with-your-services-and-how-do-you-prioritize-these-in-your-product-development-cycle) — "taking days to months to find the right food as there is no easy way to filter and narrow down relevant information"
538. **Portion sizes for weight management** — Owners are told the pet is overweight but cannot work out how many grams per meal to give across kibble, wet food and treats, and lose track of what treats added up during the day.  
    *owners of overweight dogs and cats* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: guessing from the bag chart, measuring cups · source: knowledge
539. **Small-animal cage and tank upkeep** — Rabbit, hamster, reptile and fish owners forget water changes, substrate swaps, UV bulb replacements and enclosure temperature checks.  
    *owners of small animals, reptiles and fish* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calendar reminders, forum care sheets · source: knowledge
540. **Food transition schedules** — Switching a pet to a new food needs a gradual ratio change over 7-10 days, and owners forget which day they are on and cause stomach upsets.  
    *owners changing diets or brands* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sticky note on the food bin · source: knowledge
541. **Running out of pet food unexpectedly** — The bag of kibble or box of litter runs out on a night when stores are closed, because nobody tracks consumption against what is left.  
    *all pet owners* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: subscription deliveries that arrive too early or too late; emergency store runs · source: knowledge

### Health records & vet

542. **Emergency vet decision at 2am** — When a pet vomits or limps at night, owners cannot tell whether it can wait until morning or needs the expensive emergency hospital right now.  
    *all pet owners* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Googling symptoms, calling a 24h hotline · source: knowledge
543. **Finding a vet with open appointments** — Clinics are booked out for weeks and new-patient waitlists are long, so owners phone clinic after clinic looking for anyone who can see the pet soon.  
    *owners in areas with vet shortages* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling around, urgent care clinics · source: [article](https://www.avma.org/javma-news/2020-04-01/pain-points)
544. **Medical history scattered across vets** — After moving or switching clinics, the pet's history is split between old clinics, emergency hospitals and paper printouts, so each new vet starts from scratch.  
    *owners who have moved or used multiple clinics* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: requesting record transfers by fax or email, keeping a folder · source: [app-review](https://vetrecord.app/) — "digitize, store, and share your pet's complete medical history, including vaccinations, medications, weight, and vet notes"
