    *international and rural travelers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: screenshots, printouts, offline Google Maps areas · source: knowledge
723. **Mobile data abroad guesswork** — Travelers land without knowing whether their plan roams, what it costs, or which eSIM to buy, and end up with a surprise bill or no maps at the airport.  
    *international travelers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: airport SIM kiosks, forum recommendations · source: knowledge
724. **Foreign apps in the wrong language** — Local transport, taxi and ticketing apps abroad show only the local language or currency, so travelers can't tell what they are buying.  
    *international travelers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: screenshot into a translation app, ask a local · source: [article](https://www.applause.com/press-release/travel-apps-facing-delays/) — "localization problems (12%)"
725. **Luggage in the check-in gap** — Arriving at 9am with a 3pm hotel check-in, or a late flight after an 11am checkout, leaves hours of dragging luggage around with no plan for it.  
    *city-break travelers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking the hotel to hold bags, luggage storage services · source: knowledge
726. **Rental car return details forgotten** — Fuel policy, return time, drop-off location and the pre-existing damage photos are all in different places, and errors cost money at return.  
    *road-trippers and car renters* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photos in camera roll, keeping the paper contract · source: knowledge
727. **Tipping and etiquette unknowns** — Travelers don't know whether to tip, how much, or whether to haggle in each new country, causing awkward moments at every restaurant and taxi.  
    *first-time visitors to a country* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: googling at the table · source: knowledge

### Packing

728. **Baggage weight over the limit** — Checked and cabin weight limits differ per airline and per leg of a trip, and souvenirs push the bag over on the way home, leading to fees at the counter.  
    *travelers on mixed-airline itineraries* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: luggage scale, wearing heavy clothes · source: knowledge
729. **Forgetting the one essential item** — Travelers arrive without the one thing they truly needed (medication, adapter, swimsuit) while hauling a bag of stuff they never touch.  
    *anyone who packs their own bag* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: handwritten lists, buying replacements at destination · source: [forum](https://www.fodors.com/community/united-states/does-anyone-else-hate-to-pack-461221) — "Not having the one thing they really need or dragging around a bunch of crap they don't need"
730. **Last-minute items left behind** — Things still in use the morning of departure (toothbrush, chargers, moisturizer, running shoes) can't be packed in advance and are the ones most often forgotten.  
    *anyone leaving early in the morning* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sticky note on the door, mental checklist · source: [forum](https://www.fodors.com/community/united-states/does-anyone-else-hate-to-pack-461221) — "moisturizers used in the morning or running shoes cannot be packed until the morning of departure"
731. **Repacking at every hotel change** — On multi-stop trips the bag has to be unpacked and repacked every few days, and items get left behind in drawers or lost in the shuffle.  
    *multi-city travelers, road-trippers, backpackers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: packing cubes, never fully unpacking · source: [forum](https://www.fodors.com/community/united-states/does-anyone-else-hate-to-pack-461221) — "Unpacking on the trip, repacking on the trip, and especially unpacking when returning home"
732. **Reusable packing lists by trip type** — Every trip the packing list gets rebuilt from scratch, and lessons from last time (bring a laundry bag, skip the second pair of shoes) are forgotten.  
    *frequent travelers, parents packing for several people* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: old notes, generic checklists online · source: knowledge
733. **Will my carry-on fit this airline** — Cabin bag size and weight rules differ per airline and fare class, so travelers never know if their usual bag will be gate-checked or fined until they are at the airport.  
    *budget-airline and carry-on-only flyers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: searching each airline's site before every flight · source: [forum](https://www.fodors.com/community/united-states/does-anyone-else-hate-to-pack-461221) — "Packing carry-on bags is especially frustrating, especially when flying on planes with size restrictions"
734. **Packing clothes you never wear** — People pack outfits for an imagined version of themselves on holiday and end up wearing the same three things, so bags are heavy and choices at the destination feel wrong anyway.  
    *leisure travelers, especially on longer trips* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: capsule wardrobe advice, guessing · source: [forum](https://www.fodors.com/community/united-states/does-anyone-else-hate-to-pack-461221) — "whatever they take, they'll want something else when they get there, but they hate to overpack"
735. **Packing for unknown weather** — Travelers pack for the season rather than the actual forecast and arrive with no rain jacket or with sweaters in a heatwave.  
    *all leisure travelers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: checking a weather app the night before · source: knowledge
736. **Post-trip unpacking pile** — The suitcase sits half-unpacked for days after returning, laundry mixes with clean clothes and receipts and souvenirs get lost in the bag.  
    *frequent travelers, families returning from holiday* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: none · source: [forum](https://www.fodors.com/community/united-states/does-anyone-else-hate-to-pack-461221-printthread) — "especially unpacking when returning home is particularly hated"

### Planning & research

737. **Analysis paralysis over choices** — With hundreds of hotels, flights and restaurants to compare, people spend evenings researching and still can't commit, sometimes until prices have risen.  
    *self-planning travelers, perfectionists* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking friends, giving up and booking whatever · source: [forum](https://www.fodors.com/community/united-states/excessive-planning-336970-printthread) — "the planning often getting bogged down in overthinking and 'analysis paralysis'"
738. **Juggling many apps for one trip** — Flights live in one app, hotel in another, trains in a third and activities in email, so travelers constantly switch apps to answer simple questions like 'what time is check-in'.  
    *all travelers, especially on multi-leg trips* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: forwarding emails to TripIt, screenshots in a folder · source: [article](https://www.applause.com/press-release/travel-apps-facing-delays/) — "only 9% of survey respondents go to a single app for all of their travel planning"
739. **Planning trips with young kids** — Parents must plan around naps, kid-friendly food, stroller access and activities that suit both a toddler and a teenager, and generic travel guides ignore all of it.  
    *parents travelling with children* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: parenting Facebook groups, family travel blogs · source: [article](https://mihirpshah.substack.com/p/travel-planning-101-for-busy-families) — "deciding what to pack, planning kid-friendly activities, and dealing with the cost of travel"
740. **Research scattered across tabs and blogs** — Planning one trip means bouncing between blogs, review sites, booking sites, maps and saved posts, with no single place where the findings accumulate.  
    *anyone planning their own trip* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: dozens of browser tabs, Notes app, Google Docs · source: [article](https://medium.com/tripock/why-travel-planning-is-frustrating-3161a90a229a) — "Information for travel planning is dispersed among multiple platforms and blogs, making the process slow and frustrating"
741. **No single trip organizer that works** — Even technical users find no tool that combines bookings, plans, maps and notes cleanly, so they fall back to spreadsheets and email folders.  
    *frequent independent travelers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Google Sheets, Notion templates, TripIt for confirmations only · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=44006107) — "Why is there no good travel organizing app?"
742. **Saved places lost across apps** — Restaurants and sights get saved in Google Maps, Instagram bookmarks, TikTok and screenshots, and are impossible to find again once you are actually in the city.  
    *social-media-driven travelers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Maps lists, scrolling through saved posts · source: knowledge

<a id="learning-study"></a>

## Learning & studying

*self-study, courses, languages, memorization, note-taking, staying consistent*


### Course completion

743. **Losing the thread after a break** — Returning to a course or textbook after two weeks away, the learner can't remember where they were or what earlier lessons covered, so they restart from the beginning or quit.  
    *Self-paced learners with busy lives* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Re-watch earlier videos, skim old notes · source: [article](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2022.902070/pdf) — "High online attrition remains both a concern and a mystery"
744. **Paid courses never completed** — People buy an expensive online course, watch the first few modules, and quietly abandon it, leaving money wasted and a lingering sense of failure.  
    *Adults buying self-paced online courses* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Buy another course, promise to restart 'next month' · source: [article](https://wmtips.substack.com/p/not-completing-the-course-is-one) — "80% of people who paid $500 for an online course never completed it"
745. **Understanding in the moment, blank later** — Concepts make perfect sense while watching the explanation but can't be reproduced a week later, and the learner doesn't realize until it's tested.  
    *Students in math, science, programming* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Rewatch videos, redo exercises · source: knowledge
746. **Course progress not synced across platforms** — Learning happens on Coursera, YouTube, a textbook and a podcast, and no single place shows overall progress toward the goal.  
    *Learners mixing many resources* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheet trackers, Notion boards · source: knowledge
747. **Long video lessons with no chapters** — Two-hour recorded lectures can't be skimmed or revisited by topic, so finding the one explanation you need means scrubbing blindly.  
    *MOOC and recorded-lecture students* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Watch at 2x, take timestamp notes manually · source: knowledge
748. **Shame after dropping a course** — Abandoning a course feels like an embarrassing personal defeat, which makes people avoid the platform entirely instead of picking it back up.  
    *Anyone who has quit a course midway* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Avoid the app, tell nobody · source: [article](https://natashaleewj.substack.com/p/the-empty-seats-are-worth-40000) — "dropping out as feeling like a shameful intellectual defeat"

### Focus & consistency

749. **Cramming instead of spacing** — Even knowing spacing works better, learners leave everything to the night before because nothing prompts small sessions earlier.  
    *Students with exam dates* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calendar reminders that get ignored · source: knowledge
750. **Distractions during online study sessions** — Studying on the same device that hosts social media and messages means sessions fragment into scrolling, and the planned hour yields fifteen real minutes.  
    *Anyone studying at a laptop or phone* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Site blockers, phone in another room · source: [article](https://par.nsf.gov/biblio/10536123-investigating-online-versus-face-face-course-dropout-why-do-students-say-leaving) — "driven by factors like distraction, lack of preparation"
751. **Motivation fades after week two** — Initial enthusiasm for a new subject dies after a couple of weeks, and with no visible payoff the habit dissolves before it forms.  
    *New-year-resolution learners, hobbyists* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Restart with a new app, public commitments · source: knowledge
752. **Starting the session is the hardest part** — Sitting down to study triggers procrastination; the learner reorganizes the desk, checks messages and loses an hour before opening the book.  
    *Students and adult learners with ADHD or not* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Pomodoro timers, body doubling, caffeine · source: knowledge
753. **Study plan breaks after one missed day** — A rigid schedule collapses the first time life intervenes, and there's no easy way to reflow the remaining work to the deadline.  
    *Exam candidates, anyone with a study timetable* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Redo the whole plan by hand or abandon it · source: knowledge
754. **Studying alone with no accountability** — Without classmates or a teacher expecting anything, there's nobody to notice when you skip, so self-study quietly stops.  
    *Solo self-learners and remote students* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Virtual study rooms, Discord groups, study buddies · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/qa/app/studystream/id6461722416?l=ar) — "study together, stay accountable, and boost productivity"
755. **Not knowing what to study today** — Each session starts with deciding what to work on, which wastes energy and often ends in doing the easiest, most familiar thing.  
    *Self-directed learners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: To-do lists, random choice · source: knowledge
756. **Too many study techniques, no system** — Learners know about Pomodoro, active recall, Feynman and spaced repetition but have no way to combine them into one workflow for the material in front of them.  
    *Self-learners who read about study science* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Juggle several separate apps and timers · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/pg/app/miwiz/id1660339949) — "learning techniques such as Feynman, Spaced Repetition, Active Recall or Pomodoro"
757. **Study time not tracked** — Learners feel they study 'a lot' but have no record of actual hours per subject, so they can't see imbalances or prove effort to themselves.  
    *Students, people learning alongside a job* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Toggl, paper logs, guessing · source: knowledge

### Language learning

758. **Can understand but can't speak** — After months of app lessons a learner can read and pick answers but freezes in a real conversation because nothing ever required producing speech under pressure.  
    *Intermediate language learners* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pay for tutors on iTalki, language exchange meetups · source: knowledge
759. **Forgetting vocabulary weeks later** — Words learned in a language app feel solid on the day but are gone a month later, and the app never brings them back at the right moment.  
    *Language learners* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manual flashcards, re-doing old lessons · source: knowledge
760. **Intermediate plateau with no path** — Once past beginner content, apps run out of structure and learners don't know what to study next or how to tell if they're improving.  
    *B1-B2 language learners* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Random YouTube, graded readers, guesswork · source: knowledge
761. **Content too hard or too easy** — Finding reading or listening material at exactly the learner's level is a constant hunt; most native content is overwhelming and course content is boring.  
    *Language learners seeking input* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Children's shows, graded readers, subtitles · source: knowledge
762. **No feedback on pronunciation** — Practicing alone, learners have no idea whether their pronunciation is understandable and keep reinforcing mistakes.  
    *Self-taught language learners* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Record yourself and compare, ask native friends · source: knowledge
763. **Streak anxiety replaces real learning** — Learners do a trivial one-minute lesson at 11:55pm just to keep a streak alive, then feel they've 'studied' when nothing was learned.  
    *Duolingo-style app users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Streak freezes, accept the fake progress · source: knowledge
764. **Words looked up while reading are lost** — Reading a foreign article or book, the learner looks up dozens of words and none of them are captured anywhere for later review.  
    *Language learners reading native content* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Copy-paste into a spreadsheet, or don't bother · source: knowledge

### Memorization & flashcards

765. **Flashcard review backlog piles up** — Miss a few days of spaced-repetition reviews and the due pile balloons into hundreds of cards, so people dread opening the app and quit entirely.  
    *Anki/SRS users, language learners, med students* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Delete the deck, reset scheduling, or grind through a huge session · source: [reddit-quoted](https://flashrecall.app/blog/anki-flashcards-reddit) — "Anki is clunky, ugly, and kind of a part-time job if you're not careful."
766. **Making flashcards is slow and tedious** — Turning notes, textbook pages or vocabulary lists into good cards takes longer than studying them, so people put it off.  
    *Students and self-learners using flashcards* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Download premade decks or skip flashcards altogether · source: [reddit-quoted](https://flashrecall.app/blog/anki-flashcards-reddit) — "Making cards can be slow and annoying."
767. **Rereading feels productive but isn't** — Learners reread and highlight because it feels fluent, then blank in the exam because they never tested themselves; there's no cue to switch to recall.  
    *Students preparing for exams* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Make practice questions by hand, past papers · source: knowledge
768. **Illusion of mastery from easy cards** — Reviewers mark cards as 'easy' when they only recognize the answer, so weak knowledge is scheduled far out and forgotten by exam day.  
    *Flashcard users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Be strict with self-grading, reset intervals · source: knowledge
769. **Memorizing sequences and procedures** — Ordered material such as steps, formulas, dates or anatomy lists doesn't fit flashcards well and gets muddled when items look alike.  
    *Medical, law and history students, musicians* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Mnemonics, hand-drawn diagrams, repetition · source: knowledge
770. **No practice questions for niche material** — For a specific textbook, work manual or certification there are no ready quizzes, so learners can't test themselves without writing questions first.  
    *Professional certification candidates, self-learners* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Hunt for question banks, write own questions · source: knowledge
771. **Premade decks don't stick** — Shared decks contain someone else's context and phrasing, so cards feel meaningless and don't get remembered even after many reviews.  
    *Learners who download shared decks* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Edit cards one by one or rebuild the deck from scratch · source: [reddit-quoted](https://flashrecall.app/blog/anki-flashcards-reddit) — "Don't rely only on premade decks. Make your own cards. That's where the learning happens."
772. **Spaced-repetition tools have steep learning curve** — Getting settings, intervals, card templates and add-ons right takes hours of reading guides, and getting it wrong silently wrecks retention.  
    *New Anki/SRS users* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Watch tutorial videos, copy other people's config · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/threads?id=Alt4Alt) — "Naive use of it leads to a lot of pain and tons of problems, you have to learn how to use anki."
773. **Clunky flashcard app interface** — Existing flashcard tools are ugly and awkward on mobile, so the daily review feels like a chore rather than a quick habit.  
    *Anyone reviewing cards on a phone* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Tolerate it or switch between several apps · source: [hn](https://news.ycombinator.com/threads?id=Alt4Alt) — "none of them are perfect from a usability point of view"

### Note-taking

774. **Can't listen and write at once** — Trying to write everything down during a lecture or video means missing what's being said, and trying to listen means notes are full of gaps.  
    *Students in lectures, people following video courses* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Record audio and re-listen later, borrow classmates' notes · source: [article](https://www.bates.edu/student-academic-support-center/files/2026/02/Common-Issues-in-Note-Taking-Studying.pdf) — "Can't write and listen at the same time"
775. **Not knowing what was important** — After a lecture or chapter the notes are a wall of text with no sense of which points actually matter for the exam or the skill.  
    *Students and course-takers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask classmates, compare to slides, re-read everything · source: [article](https://www.bates.edu/student-academic-support-center/files/2026/02/Common-Issues-in-Note-Taking-Studying.pdf) — "You came out of the lecture and... have no idea what was important"
776. **Notes scattered across apps** — Study notes live partly in Notion, partly in a paper notebook, partly in phone photos and voice memos, so nothing can be found when needed.  
    *Students and lifelong learners* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Periodic migrations that never finish · source: knowledge
777. **Handwritten notes aren't searchable** — Paper or tablet handwriting is preferred for retention but the notes can't be searched or linked later, so they become dead weight.  
    *Students who handwrite notes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Retype key notes, photograph pages · source: knowledge
778. **Highlights that are never revisited** — Kindle highlights, PDF annotations and web clippings accumulate in the hundreds and are never seen again, so reading produces no retained knowledge.  
    *Heavy readers and researchers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Readwise-style resurfacing, manual export · source: knowledge
779. **Lectures move faster than notes** — Lecturers move at a pace that outruns handwriting or typing, so key definitions and steps get lost mid-sentence.  
    *University students* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Abbreviations, photographing slides, asking to slow down · source: [article](https://www.bates.edu/student-academic-support-center/files/2026/02/Common-Issues-in-Note-Taking-Studying.pdf) — "easy to miss crucial information while trying to keep up with the professor's pace"
780. **Messy incomplete notes never cleaned up** — Rushed notes from one class are supposed to be tidied before the next, but with several classes and activities that never happens and the notes rot.  
    *Students juggling several subjects* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Rewrite notes at exam time, or give up on them · source: [article](https://www.bates.edu/student-academic-support-center/files/2026/02/Common-Issues-in-Note-Taking-Studying.pdf) — "rushing to complete their notes before the next class... can result in messy and incomplete notes"
781. **Notes from videos need pausing and scrubbing** — Taking notes from a video course means constantly pausing, rewinding and losing the place, and the notes have no link back to the timestamp.  
    *People learning from YouTube or MOOCs* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Speed up video and rely on memory, screenshots · source: knowledge
782. **Struggling to condense notes** — Summarizing a chapter or lecture into a concise, organized note is hard, so people either copy everything verbatim or write nothing usable.  
    *Learners without strong writing skills, non-native speakers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Highlight the whole page, copy slides · source: [article](https://www.bates.edu/student-academic-support-center/files/2026/02/Common-Issues-in-Note-Taking-Studying.pdf) — "students need to filter out the most important information and write it in a concise and organized manner"
783. **Tinkering with note systems instead of studying** — Hours go into perfecting templates, tags and folder structures in a notes app while the actual material stays unread.  
    *Productivity-app enthusiasts, students* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Switch apps again, adopt yet another system · source: knowledge

### Self-study planning

784. **Can't tell if you're actually improving** — Without exams or a teacher, self-learners have no measure of progress and can't tell whether a month of effort moved them forward.  
    *Independent learners of any subject* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Occasional self-tests, feeling it out · source: knowledge
785. **Exam-day knowledge gaps unknown** — Approaching a certification or exam, the learner doesn't know which topics are weak, so revision time goes to comfortable areas.  
    *Certification and exam candidates* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Practice tests, gut feeling · source: knowledge
786. **No roadmap for a self-taught subject** — Wanting to learn statistics or music theory alone, learners can't tell what to study in what order or which resources are worth the time.  
    *Adults learning outside school* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask forums for curricula, copy university syllabi · source: knowledge
787. **Nobody to ask when stuck** — Getting stuck on one concept at 10pm with no teacher or classmate means either hours of searching or giving up on the session.  
    *Solo learners of technical subjects* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Stack Overflow, Discord servers, waiting days for forum replies · source: knowledge
788. **Prerequisite gaps discovered mid-course** — Halfway through a course the learner hits material that assumes knowledge they don't have and has no idea what to go back and learn.  
    *Learners jumping into intermediate courses* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pause course, search for the missing basics · source: knowledge
789. **Tutorial hell with no projects** — Learners watch endless coding or design tutorials, follow along fine, then can't build anything unaided and don't know how to break out.  
    *Self-taught programmers and designers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Random project ideas from forums, bootcamps · source: knowledge
790. **Hoarding courses and bookmarks** — Dozens of Udemy courses, saved YouTube playlists and bookmarked tutorials pile up, and choosing which to start becomes its own paralysis.  
    *Curious adults, lifelong learners* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Spreadsheet of resources, buy more on sale · source: knowledge

<a id="work-office"></a>

## Everyday work & office life

*meetings, coworkers, tasks, remote work, tools that don't talk to each other*


### Admin & approvals

791. **Chasing approvals stuck in someone's inbox** — Leave requests, purchase orders and document sign-offs wait on a single approver with no visibility or reminders.  
    *employees and coordinators* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Nudge by chat; escalate to manager · source: knowledge
792. **Expense reports and receipts piling up** — Receipts get lost and expense claims are filed late because the process is tedious and disconnected from the card and calendar.  
    *travelling and client-facing staff* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Photo folder of receipts; month-end scramble · source: knowledge
793. **Coordinating team leave and coverage** — Nobody can see at a glance who is off next week, so coverage gaps are discovered too late.  
    *managers and small teams* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Shared calendar; leave spreadsheet · source: knowledge

### Communication overload

794. **Message overload across chat, email and tools** — Important requests arrive via Slack, Teams, email, Jira comments and text, so things get missed and nothing is prioritized.  
    *knowledge workers* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Check each app in rotation; mark unread · source: knowledge
795. **Losing a message inside a long thread** — A key decision was made deep in a chat thread and later nobody can find it or remember which channel it was in.  
    *teams using Slack/Teams* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Search with guessed keywords; ask again · source: knowledge
796. **Company announcements missed in the noise** — Important all-hands or policy updates go out in a busy channel or newsletter and half the company never sees them.  
    *all employees* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Repeat announcements; manager cascades · source: knowledge
797. **Email threads with 'reply all' pile-ups** — A simple request spawns a 30-reply thread where the actual answer and the current status are impossible to spot.  
    *office workers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Scroll to the newest reply and hope · source: knowledge
798. **Not knowing if a colleague is actually available** — Presence dots say 'available' when someone is in a call, or 'away' when they are working, so you never know when to interrupt.  
    *remote and hybrid workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask 'got a sec?' and wait · source: knowledge

### Focus & interruptions

799. **Context switching between projects** — Jumping among three projects means reloading tabs, docs and mental context each time, losing 15 minutes per switch.  
    *people on multiple projects* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Browser tab groups; separate desktops · source: knowledge
800. **Notifications interrupting focused work** — Constant pings pull attention away every few minutes, and muting everything means missing something genuinely urgent.  
    *individual contributors* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Do Not Disturb; check every hour · source: knowledge
801. **Coworker walk-ups breaking concentration** — In open offices, people interrupt with questions with no way to signal you are in deep work.  
    *office workers in open-plan offices* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: Headphones; book a focus room · source: knowledge
802. **Estimating and tracking where the day went** — At the end of the day you cannot say what you actually did, because time went to interruptions and meetings rather than planned work.  
    *knowledge workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Time-tracking apps; guessing on timesheets · source: knowledge

### Hybrid & remote logistics

803. **Not knowing who is in the office today** — You commute in to collaborate and find your teammates chose to work from home that day.  
    *hybrid workers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ask in chat the night before; shared spreadsheet · source: knowledge
804. **Coordinating team in-office days** — Teams try to align on the same office days but preferences shift weekly and there is no easy way to converge.  
    *hybrid teams and managers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Fixed anchor days; group chat polls · source: knowledge
805. **Tracking who is attending in person vs remote** — Hybrid meetings fall apart when nobody knows who will be physically present and whether a room and video link are both needed.  
    *hybrid teams* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask in chat the morning of · source: knowledge

### Knowledge & documentation

806. **Figuring out who owns what** — You need approval or an answer and have no idea which person or team is responsible, so you ask around for days.  
    *employees at mid to large companies* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Org chart; ask your manager · source: knowledge
807. **Finding the latest version of a file** — Documents exist as email attachments, Drive copies and Teams uploads, and nobody knows which is current.  
    *office workers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: 'Final_v3_FINAL' filenames; ask the author · source: knowledge
808. **Not knowing where to search for something** — Information could be in email, chat, the wiki, a shared drive or a ticket, so a single question means five searches.  
    *everyone* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ask a colleague instead of searching · source: knowledge
809. **Onboarding new colleagues with tribal knowledge** — New hires spend weeks discovering unwritten processes, acronyms and who owns what, asking the same people repeatedly.  
    *new hires and their buddies* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Onboarding doc that is out of date · source: knowledge
810. **Same question asked repeatedly** — People keep asking the same how-to questions in chat because the answer is buried in old threads and not in any findable place.  
    *team leads and senior staff* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pin messages; write a wiki nobody reads · source: knowledge
811. **Decoding internal acronyms and jargon** — Every meeting uses acronyms and project codenames that are not documented anywhere.  
    *new and cross-functional employees* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask quietly; search chat history · source: knowledge

### Meetings & follow-up

812. **Action items lost after the meeting** — Decisions and to-dos discussed in a call are not written down or are buried in a notes doc nobody re-opens, so they silently drop.  
    *teams that meet regularly* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: One person types notes; ad hoc follow-up emails · source: knowledge
813. **Catching up on a missed meeting** — When you miss a meeting you have to watch a full recording or ask a colleague, because there is no short summary of what was decided.  
    *people juggling overlapping meetings* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Watch recording at 2x; ask a teammate · source: knowledge
814. **Meeting recording and transcripts scattered** — Recordings sit in Zoom, Teams and Drive with no way to search across them for what was said about a topic.  
    *teams that record meetings* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Rewatch; ask attendees · source: knowledge
815. **Preparing for a 1:1 with your manager** — Topics you meant to raise are forgotten by the time the 1:1 arrives, and past commitments are not tracked.  
    *employees and managers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Running notes doc; memory · source: knowledge

### Meetings & scheduling

816. **Back-to-back meetings with no gaps** — Meetings are booked end-to-end so there is no time to take notes, use the bathroom, or join the next call on time.  
    *people in meeting-heavy roles* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Manually shorten meetings to 25/50 minutes; arrive late · source: knowledge
817. **Finding a slot across many calendars** — Booking a meeting with 5+ people means opening each person's availability, guessing at time zones, and going back and forth for days.  
    *team leads, project managers, EAs* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Scheduling assistant, Doodle polls, email ping-pong · source: knowledge
818. **Late invites that collide with focus time** — Meetings drop onto the calendar for later the same day, breaking planned deep-work blocks with no negotiation.  
    *individual contributors* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Block fake meetings on the calendar · source: knowledge
819. **Meetings with no agenda or purpose** — Invites arrive with a title only, so attendees cannot prepare, decide whether to attend, or tell if it could have been an email.  
    *anyone who receives meeting invites* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask the organizer in chat; decline blind · source: knowledge
820. **Time zone mistakes with distributed colleagues** — Meetings get booked at 6am for someone or during daylight-saving shifts, and people show up an hour off.  
    *distributed and global teams* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: World clock tabs; mental arithmetic · source: knowledge
821. **Recurring meeting nobody needs anymore** — A weekly sync set up months ago keeps recurring with dwindling relevance, but no one feels empowered to cancel it.  
    *teams with standing meetings* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Quietly skip; hope the organizer cancels · source: knowledge
822. **Scheduling a meeting for a two-minute question** — A quick question to a colleague turns into a 30-minute calendar slot three days out because there is no lightweight way to catch them when both are free.  
    *office and remote workers in scheduled cultures* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Slack ping and wait, or book a 30-min slot · source: [app-review](https://apps.apple.com/gr/app/tweelin/id6448123000) — "transforming 1:1 scheduled meetings into impromptu conversations presented at the right time for both parties"
823. **Waiting for the meeting host to arrive** — Attendees sit in a video lobby or empty room for 5-10 minutes because the host is in another call or forgot.  
    *everyone in meeting-heavy roles* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Message the host; leave after 10 minutes · source: knowledge

### Office logistics

824. **Conference room double-booked or squatted** — You booked a room but someone else is in it, or the booking system shows free rooms that are actually occupied.  
    *hybrid and office workers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Wander looking for a free room; knock · source: knowledge
825. **Desk phone or video call setup failing** — Joining a call means fiddling with the wrong mic, camera or room system while everyone waits.  
    *office and hybrid workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: Join early; use phone instead · source: knowledge
826. **Hot desk booking friction** — Booking a desk requires a clunky portal, desks get booked and not used, and you end up sitting far from your team.  
    *hybrid office workers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Book every day just in case; squat · source: knowledge
827. **Booking and tracking shared equipment** — Loaner laptops, cameras and adapters get taken without a record, so nobody knows who has them.  
    *office teams sharing gear* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Sign-out sheet; asking around · source: knowledge
828. **Shared kitchen and supplies running out** — Coffee, milk or printer paper run out and nobody knows who is responsible for reordering until someone complains.  
    *office staff, office managers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Sticky notes; complaining in chat · source: knowledge

### Reporting & status

829. **Collecting input from many people for one deliverable** — Assembling a report needs contributions from ten people, each replying in a different format and at a different time.  
    *coordinators, chiefs of staff* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Email deadline; chase individually · source: knowledge
830. **Filling in weekly timesheets from memory** — Timesheets are due Friday and you reconstruct the week from calendar and memory, producing inaccurate entries.  
    *consultants, agencies, billable staff* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Look at calendar and git logs · source: knowledge
831. **Visibility of what remote teammates are working on** — Without hallway contact, managers and peers do not know what others are working on, leading to duplicate effort and micromanagement.  
    *remote teams* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Daily standups; status posts · source: knowledge
832. **Writing status updates for multiple audiences** — Every week you rewrite the same progress into a standup, a manager email, a Jira ticket and a Slack post.  
    *individual contributors and leads* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Copy-paste and lightly edit · source: knowledge

### Tasks & follow-up

833. **Following up on things you asked others for** — You sent five requests to five people and have no list of what is outstanding, so you rely on memory to chase them.  
    *managers, coordinators* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Flagged emails; a notes file of 'waiting for' · source: knowledge
834. **Out-of-office handoffs falling through** — When someone is on leave, their pending requests and knowledge are not visibly handed to anyone and things stall until they return.  
    *teams of any size* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Out-of-office auto-reply; ad hoc handover email · source: knowledge
835. **Prioritizing when everything is urgent** — Requests arrive from multiple stakeholders each claiming top priority, with no shared way to see the queue and negotiate order.  
    *individual contributors serving many teams* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Manager escalation; first-come-first-served · source: knowledge
836. **Untracked requests received in chat** — A coworker asks for something in a DM, you say 'sure', and it never becomes a task anywhere so it is forgotten.  
    *everyone* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Copy to a to-do list manually; emoji reactions · source: knowledge

### Tools that don't talk

837. **Tasks scattered across many apps** — Work to-dos live in Jira, Asana, email flags, sticky notes and chat, so there is no single view of everything you owe.  
    *knowledge workers* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A personal notes file duplicating everything · source: knowledge
838. **Calendar and task list out of sync** — Deadlines live in the task tool and time lives in the calendar, so you overcommit because you never see both together.  
    *individual contributors* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manually block time for big tasks · source: knowledge
839. **Copy-pasting data between systems** — The same information (ticket number, customer name, hours) must be re-entered in a CRM, a timesheet and a project tool because they are not connected.  
    *operations and admin staff* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manual re-entry; spreadsheets · source: knowledge
840. **Too many logins and password prompts** — Every workday begins with re-authenticating to a dozen tools, each with its own MFA prompt and session timeout.  
    *employees at companies with many SaaS tools* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Password manager; keep tabs open · source: knowledge

### Wellbeing & boundaries

841. **Feeling isolated from coworkers when remote** — Casual social contact disappears remotely, and virtual coffee chats feel forced or never get scheduled.  
    *remote workers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Random coffee bots; team socials · source: knowledge
842. **Forgetting to log off or take breaks at home** — Working from home blurs the day, so you skip lunch, work past hours and sit for hours without moving.  
    *remote workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Phone alarms; calendar blocks · source: knowledge

<a id="job-hunting"></a>

## Job hunting & careers

*applications, resumes, interviews, ghosting, tracking, career growth*


### Application tracking

843. **Job hunt burnout and motivation loss** — Months of applications with little response erode motivation; people have no sense of progress and swing between binge-applying and avoiding it entirely.  
    *long-term unemployed and laid-off workers* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: support subreddits, arbitrary daily quotas · source: knowledge
844. **Logging applications for unemployment benefits** — Unemployment insurance requires a weekly log of work-search activities with employer, date and method; people reconstruct it from memory and risk losing benefits.  
    *unemployment claimants in the US* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper log, spreadsheet · source: knowledge
845. **Losing track of dozens of applications** — After sending 50+ applications, people cannot remember where they applied, which resume version they sent, or what stage each is at. Rejections and interview invites arrive with no context.  
    *active job seekers applying at volume* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: messy spreadsheet or Notion board that goes stale within a week · source: knowledge
846. **Rejections buried in email inbox** — Interview invites, rejections and scheduling links land among newsletters and spam; people miss a response deadline or reply days late.  
    *active job seekers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Gmail filters, checking spam folder daily · source: knowledge
847. **Job posting disappears after applying** — When an interview is scheduled, the original listing has been taken down and the candidate can't re-read the requirements they were supposed to prepare against.  
    *candidates who applied weeks earlier* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Wayback Machine, saved PDFs if remembered · source: knowledge
848. **No data on what's actually working** — Job seekers can't tell whether referrals, cold applications, or a particular resume version yield interviews because nothing is measured.  
    *job seekers applying at volume* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: gut feel · source: knowledge

### Applying

849. **Re-typing resume into every ATS form** — Workday, Taleo and similar portals ask you to upload a resume and then manually re-enter every job, date and school anyway; the auto-parse mangles the fields.  
    *anyone applying to mid-size and large employers* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: browser autofill, copy-paste from a master doc, giving up on the application · source: knowledge
850. **Rejected within minutes by auto-filter** — An automated rejection arrives seconds after applying, and the applicant has no idea which knockout question or keyword triggered it.  
    *job seekers applying through ATS portals* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: guess, reapply with a different answer, ask on forums · source: knowledge
851. **Creating a new account per employer** — Every company career site demands its own login and password, so job seekers end up with dozens of throwaway accounts and password resets to apply once.  
    *job seekers applying through corporate career pages* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: password manager, reusing the same password · source: knowledge
852. **Writing a cover letter per application** — Employers still require cover letters, and writing a fresh, non-generic one for each role takes 30-60 minutes that rarely seems to be read.  
    *job seekers in cover-letter-heavy fields* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: one template with the company name swapped in · source: knowledge

### Career growth

853. **Forgetting accomplishments at review time** — By the annual review, employees can't recall what they shipped or fixed in Q1, so self-assessments undersell their work and promotions get delayed.  
    *employees at companies with performance reviews* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a 'brag doc' that's rarely updated, scrolling old Slack and Jira · source: knowledge
854. **Unclear path to promotion** — Employees are told they're 'not quite there yet' for the next level with no concrete criteria, and can't track their progress against whatever is expected.  
    *individual contributors seeking promotion* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ask manager repeatedly, compare with peers · source: knowledge
855. **Keeping certifications from lapsing** — Professional certifications (PMP, CPA CPE, nursing CEUs, cloud certs) expire on different cycles, and people discover too late they're out of compliance or need renewal hours.  
    *licensed and certified professionals* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminders, provider emails · source: knowledge
856. **Skills gap for the next role** — People want to move into a new title (e.g., analyst to product manager) but can't tell which skills or certifications actually matter versus listicle noise.  
    *career changers and upskillers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reading job postings, asking on forums · source: knowledge

### Finding jobs

857. **Applying to ghost job postings** — Listings stay up for months with no intent to hire, or are reposted weekly; applicants waste effort on roles that were never real or were already filled.  
    *all job seekers using job boards* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: check posting date, cross-check the company's own site, ask on forums · source: knowledge
858. **Salary hidden in job postings** — Many listings omit pay, so candidates go through screens before discovering the range is far below expectations.  
    *job seekers outside pay-transparency states* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Glassdoor and Levels.fyi guesses, asking the recruiter on the first call · source: knowledge
859. **Current employer noticing job search** — Turning on 'open to work' or updating LinkedIn risks tipping off a manager, so employed job seekers hide activity and lose reach.  
    *employed people looking discreetly* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: recruiter-only visibility settings, not updating profile · source: knowledge
860. **Job alerts flooded with irrelevant roles** — Saved-search email alerts send dozens of loosely matching listings daily (wrong seniority, wrong field), so people stop reading them and miss the good ones.  
    *passive and active job seekers with alerts set up* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: unsubscribe, check boards manually instead · source: knowledge
861. **Remote filter returns hybrid/onsite jobs** — Selecting 'remote' on a job board still returns hybrid roles, 'remote within 50 miles of office', or location-restricted jobs, wasting reading time.  
    *remote-only job seekers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: read every posting's fine print, use niche remote boards · source: knowledge
862. **Same job listed on five boards** — Aggregators show the same role from LinkedIn, Indeed, ZipRecruiter and staffing agencies, so people accidentally apply twice or cannot tell the original source.  
    *job seekers using multiple boards* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually search the company name before applying · source: knowledge

### Ghosting & follow-up

863. **Ghosted after interviews with no closure** — Candidates complete several rounds, are told they'll hear back in a week, then never hear anything; they keep hoping and delaying other decisions.  
    *candidates in mid-to-late interview stages* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: polite follow-up emails, checking the posting to see if it closed · source: knowledge
864. **Not knowing when to follow up** — After an interview or application, people agonize over whether it's been long enough to nudge the recruiter, and forget to do it once the date passes.  
    *candidates waiting on employers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminders set inconsistently, asking friends · source: knowledge

### Interviews

865. **Interview loop that never ends** — Companies add a sixth or seventh round, a take-home, a 'culture chat', and the candidate can't tell how many stages remain or when a decision will come.  
    *candidates at tech and corporate employers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: ask recruiter for process outline, crowdsourced Glassdoor interview reports · source: knowledge
866. **Interview schedule juggling across time zones** — Multiple companies propose slots by email, in different time zones, while the candidate is also hiding interviews from a current employer; double-bookings and missed calls happen.  
    *employed candidates interviewing at several companies* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: personal calendar entries, fake dentist appointments · source: knowledge
867. **No feedback after rejection** — Rejections come with a form email and no reason, so candidates cannot learn whether it was the resume, the interview answers, or salary and keep repeating mistakes.  
    *candidates rejected after interviews* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: ask the recruiter (rarely answered), self-reflection · source: knowledge
868. **One-way video interviews feel impossible** — Asynchronous recorded interviews give 30 seconds to think and one take, with no human feedback; candidates freeze and get no idea how they did.  
    *entry-level and high-volume role applicants* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: practice with a webcam, look up common questions · source: knowledge
869. **Recalling stories for behavioral questions** — Asked 'tell me about a time you handled conflict', candidates blank on relevant examples even though they have several, because the stories aren't organized anywhere.  
    *anyone preparing for interviews* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a doc of STAR stories reread the night before · source: knowledge
870. **Unpaid take-home assignments piling up** — Several companies at once assign 4-10 hour projects with deadlines; candidates burn weekends and cannot judge which ones are worth doing.  
    *engineers, designers, marketers in interview processes* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: decline some, do them late at night · source: knowledge
871. **Forgetting interviewer names and details** — After a panel of five people, candidates can't remember who asked what or who to address thank-you notes to, and mix up details between companies.  
    *candidates in multi-round processes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scribbled notes, LinkedIn stalking afterwards · source: knowledge
872. **Practicing answers out loud alone** — People know their answers ramble but have no one to mock-interview with, and rehearsing in a mirror doesn't surface filler words or pacing problems.  
    *introverted or first-time job seekers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: record on phone, ask a friend or partner · source: knowledge
873. **Researching a company before the interview** — Preparing means opening 15 tabs (news, Glassdoor, financials, product, interviewer LinkedIn) the night before, and it's never consolidated for the next round.  
    *candidates with an interview scheduled* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: ad hoc googling, notes app · source: knowledge

### Networking & referrals

874. **Finding who can refer you** — Referred candidates get far better response rates, but figuring out which second-degree connection works at the target company and how to ask is slow and uncomfortable.  
    *job seekers targeting specific companies* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: LinkedIn search, cold messages · source: knowledge
875. **Cold outreach messages go unanswered** — Messages to hiring managers and recruiters get no reply, and the sender doesn't know whether the message was bad, ignored, or never seen.  
    *job seekers doing direct outreach* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: send more messages, copy templates · source: knowledge
876. **Informational interviews with no follow-through** — After a coffee chat, people forget to send a thank-you, act on the advice, or update the contact later, so the relationship dies.  
    *students and career changers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app, memory · source: knowledge
877. **Losing touch with professional network** — Former colleagues and managers who could refer you are forgotten until you need them, making outreach feel awkward and transactional.  
    *mid-career professionals* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: LinkedIn birthday reminders, sporadic messages · source: knowledge

### Offers & negotiation

878. **Offer deadline before other interviews finish** — An exploding offer requires a decision in three days while a preferred company is still mid-process; people don't know how to ask for extensions or accelerate others.  
    *candidates interviewing at several places* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: email negotiations, guessing · source: knowledge
879. **Comparing offers with different comp structures** — One offer has RSUs vesting over four years, another a bonus and better 401k match, a third is remote; candidates can't line them up on an apples-to-apples basis.  
    *candidates with multiple offers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: homemade spreadsheet, asking friends · source: knowledge
880. **Job description differs from real job** — Candidates accept a role and discover the duties, team or remote policy are different from what was posted; there's no way to check beforehand.  
    *recently hired employees* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: ask pointed questions in interviews, Glassdoor reviews · source: knowledge
881. **Layoff rumors and company health unknown** — Candidates accept roles at companies that lay off weeks later; there's no easy way to gauge financial stability or recent layoffs before joining.  
    *candidates evaluating offers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: news searches, layoffs.fyi, Blind · source: knowledge
882. **Not knowing what to ask for** — When asked for salary expectations, candidates have no reliable number for their role, level and city, and fear lowballing or pricing themselves out.  
    *all candidates, especially early-career* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Glassdoor, Levels.fyi, asking peers · source: knowledge
883. **Reference contacts go stale** — When asked for references, people scramble to find current emails for former managers and to warn them a call is coming, sometimes for multiple companies at once.  
    *candidates at final stage* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: LinkedIn messages, old contacts list · source: knowledge

### Recruiters & scams

884. **Detecting fake recruiter job scams** — Job seekers get texts and emails from 'recruiters' offering easy remote jobs, sometimes using real company names; it's hard to tell real outreach from scams that ask for fees or bank details.  
    *anyone with a public resume or LinkedIn profile* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: google the sender, check the domain, ask on forums · source: knowledge
885. **Recruiter spam for wrong roles** — Agency recruiters message about positions far below level, in the wrong city, or in an unrelated field, and repeatedly across platforms.  
    *people with visible LinkedIn or resume-board profiles* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ignore, set profile to not open to work, block senders · source: knowledge
886. **Same role pitched by multiple agencies** — Contract seekers get the same job from three staffing firms and must remember which agency they authorized to submit them, or risk being disqualified for double submission.  
    *contractors and IT consultants* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: notes app or email search · source: knowledge

### Resumes

887. **Not knowing if ATS can parse resume** — Applicants fear their two-column or graphical resume is being silently rejected by parsing software but have no way to see what the ATS actually extracted.  
    *job seekers with designed or non-standard resumes* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paste resume into a plain-text box and eyeball it, pay for a scanner tool · source: knowledge
888. **Resume gaps and explaining them** — Layoffs, caregiving or health leave leave months-long gaps, and people don't know how to present them on the resume or answer about them in interviews.  
    *job seekers with employment gaps* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: years-only dates, advice threads · source: knowledge
889. **Tailoring the resume for every posting** — Each listing wants slightly different keywords, so people rewrite bullets for every application, then lose track of which version went where.  
    *job seekers targeting ATS-screened roles* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: keep 3-5 resume variants in a folder, hand-edit each time · source: knowledge
890. **LinkedIn, resume and portfolio out of sync** — Updating a job title or dates means editing the resume, LinkedIn, a personal site and several job-board profiles separately; they drift and contradict each other.  
    *professionals maintaining an online presence* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: update each manually, usually forgetting one · source: knowledge
891. **Quantifying achievements on the resume** — Advice says 'use numbers', but people don't remember metrics from roles two jobs ago and end up writing vague duty-based bullets.  
    *job seekers updating resumes* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: estimate, dig through old emails · source: knowledge
892. **Resume formatting breaks on export** — A resume that looks fine in Word or Google Docs shifts to two pages or loses bullets when converted to PDF or uploaded, and each fix breaks something else.  
    *everyone maintaining a resume* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: fiddling with margins and font sizes · source: knowledge

## Housing: renting, roommates & moving

*landlords, leases, roommates, moving, apartment hunting, home buying*


### Apartment hunting

893. **Scam and duplicate listings** — Apartment searches are polluted by fake listings, bait-and-switch prices and the same unit posted five times.  
    *apartment hunters* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Reverse image search; avoiding deals that look too good · source: knowledge
894. **Comparing units across many sites** — Candidates live across several sites, texts and emails, making it hard to compare price, commute and features side by side.  
    *apartment hunters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheet or notes app · source: knowledge
895. **Listings gone by the time you call** — Units marked available are already rented, wasting hours of calls and tours on stale inventory.  
    *apartment hunters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Message dozens of listings at once · source: knowledge
896. **Repeating the same application info** — Every application asks for the same pay stubs, references and IDs, and each charges a fee with no shared file.  
    *apartment hunters* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Folder of PDFs re-uploaded each time · source: knowledge
897. **True monthly cost is hidden** — Listed rent excludes utilities, parking, fees and broker commission, so real cost is unknown until the application stage.  
    *apartment hunters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask each landlord individually · source: knowledge
898. **Commute and neighborhood unknowns** — It is hard to judge commute time at rush hour, noise, safety and grocery access for an unfamiliar address before touring.  
    *apartment hunters and relocators* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Google Maps and street view · source: knowledge
899. **Forgetting details after tours** — After viewing several units in a day, memories blur and tenants cannot recall which one had the broken window or good light.  
    *apartment hunters* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Photos and hurried notes · source: knowledge
900. **Rental application rejection blackout** — Applicants hear nothing after applying and do not know whether to keep searching or wait.  
    *apartment hunters* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Follow-up calls · source: knowledge
901. **Tour scheduling back-and-forth** — Arranging viewings means trading texts with multiple agents around work hours, often with no-shows.  
    *apartment hunters* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Text and phone tag · source: knowledge

### Finding roommates

902. **Vetting a stranger as roommate** — Finding a replacement roommate means judging strangers from a few messages, with no easy way to check references, habits or reliability.  
    *renters filling a room* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups, Craigslist, gut feel · source: knowledge

### Home buying

903. **Closing paperwork and deadlines** — Inspection contingencies, appraisal, insurance binder and lender document requests arrive in a rush with hard deadlines.  
    *home buyers under contract* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Agent reminders; email searches · source: knowledge
904. **Estimating real monthly ownership cost** — Buyers see the list price but struggle to add taxes, insurance, HOA dues, PMI and maintenance into a true monthly figure.  
    *home buyers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Mortgage calculators that omit most costs · source: knowledge
905. **Inspection report is hard to prioritize** — A 60-page inspection lists everything from cosmetic to structural and buyers cannot tell what to negotiate or budget for.  
    *home buyers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask the agent or inspector · source: knowledge
906. **Rent versus buy uncertainty** — Renters cannot tell whether buying makes sense for their city, savings and time horizon without a spreadsheet they distrust.  
    *renters considering buying* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Online calculators · source: knowledge
907. **Tracking homes viewed and rejected** — House hunters see dozens of listings and cannot remember which they toured, what they disliked or what it later sold for.  
    *first-time home buyers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Saved listings on portals; spreadsheet · source: knowledge

### Landlord and maintenance

908. **Maintenance requests going unanswered** — A broken heater or leaking tap is reported by text and then nothing happens; the tenant has no record and no way to escalate.  
    *renters* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Repeated calls and texts · source: knowledge
909. **Knowing tenant rights locally** — Tenants do not know what notice periods, deposit limits or repair obligations apply in their city, and rules differ by jurisdiction.  
    *renters facing a dispute* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Googling; calling a tenant hotline · source: knowledge
910. **Landlord entering without notice** — Landlords or contractors show up unannounced and tenants lack a simple log of entries to support a complaint.  
    *renters* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
911. **No paper trail with landlord** — Conversations happen by phone or in passing, so when a dispute arises the tenant cannot prove what was promised or when.  
    *renters* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Follow-up emails when remembered · source: knowledge
912. **Rent increase arrives with no context** — A renewal offer with a big increase gives the tenant no way to check comparable rents or legal caps before responding.  
    *renters at renewal* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Browsing listings manually · source: knowledge
913. **Automating rent payment reliably** — Landlords accept only checks or one specific app, so tenants juggle transfers and fear a missed or late payment fee.  
    *renters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Calendar reminder; bank bill pay · source: knowledge
914. **Rent receipts and payment proof** — Cash or bank-transfer rent leaves no receipt, so tenants cannot prove payment history for disputes or future applications.  
    *renters paying informally* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Screenshots of bank app · source: knowledge

### Lease and deposit

915. **Getting the deposit back on time** — After moving out, tenants wait weeks past the legal deadline with no itemized deductions and no idea how to push back.  
    *renters after move-out* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Emails and demand letters · source: knowledge
916. **Move-in condition never documented** — Tenants forget to photograph scuffs and stains at move-in and later lose deposit money for pre-existing damage.  
    *renters* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Phone photos scattered in camera roll · source: knowledge
917. **Lease renewal deadline sneaks up** — Notice-to-vacate or renewal windows pass unnoticed, locking tenants into another year or a month-to-month premium.  
    *renters* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calendar entry if remembered · source: knowledge
918. **Subletting or breaking a lease** — Tenants who must leave early cannot easily find a qualified sublettor or figure out the exact penalty and process.  
    *renters relocating* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups; asking the landlord · source: knowledge
919. **Understanding dense lease clauses** — Leases are long PDFs full of fees, auto-renewals and penalties that tenants sign without understanding.  
    *renters signing a lease* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Skim and hope · source: knowledge
920. **Tracking recurring fees beyond rent** — Pet rent, parking, trash, amenity and 'admin' fees vary monthly and tenants cannot tell if they are being overcharged.  
    *renters in managed buildings* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
921. **Renters insurance lapses unnoticed** — A required renters policy auto-renews or lapses silently, and tenants cannot find the certificate when the landlord asks.  
    *renters* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Search email inbox · source: knowledge

### Moving logistics

922. **Coordinating the moving timeline** — Utilities cutover, movers, elevator booking, key handoff and cleaning all must line up on one day and one is always missed.  
    *people moving* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Long checklist in notes app · source: knowledge
923. **Getting honest moving quotes** — Mover quotes vary wildly, hide fees, and reviews are hard to trust, so people overpay or get held hostage on delivery.  
    *people moving* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Call several companies; read reviews · source: knowledge
924. **Changing address everywhere** — Banks, employer, subscriptions, DMV, insurers and delivery apps each need an address update and some are forgotten for months.  
    *people moving* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: USPS forwarding plus memory · source: knowledge
925. **Not knowing what is in which box** — After a move, needed items are buried in unlabeled or vaguely labeled boxes for weeks.  
    *people moving* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sharpie labels; opening every box · source: knowledge
926. **Overlapping rent during a move** — Lease dates rarely line up, forcing double rent or a scramble for storage and a place to stay in between.  
    *renters moving between leases* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Negotiate dates; short-term storage · source: knowledge
927. **Deciding what to sell or toss** — Before a move, deciding what to keep, sell, donate or discard is overwhelming and left to the last night.  
    *people moving* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Facebook Marketplace and trash bags · source: knowledge
928. **Finding friends with a truck** — Rounding up helpers and a vehicle for a DIY move means chasing favors and last-minute cancellations.  
    *young renters moving* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Group text pleading · source: knowledge
929. **Utility setup and shutoff timing** — Electricity, gas and internet must be stopped at the old place and started at the new one, and gaps or overlaps cost money.  
    *people moving* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Call each provider · source: knowledge

### Roommate coordination

930. **Chores rotation that nobody follows** — Dishes and trash rotations agreed verbally are forgotten within weeks and one person silently ends up doing everything.  
    *renters with roommates* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Paper chart on the fridge; passive-aggressive notes · source: knowledge
931. **Guests staying over without warning** — A roommate's partner effectively moves in, using utilities and space, with no agreed limit or way to raise it without conflict.  
    *renters with roommates* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: Awkward conversation or none · source: knowledge
932. **Missing packages in shared entryway** — Deliveries left in a common hall or lobby vanish or get taken inside by a roommate who forgets to mention it.  
    *renters in multi-unit buildings* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Ask in group chat; ship to work · source: knowledge
933. **Quiet hours and noise disputes** — Roommates with different schedules clash over late-night noise and early alarms and have no neutral record of agreed rules.  
    *roommates and shift workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: House rules discussed once, then forgotten · source: knowledge
934. **Roommate agreement never written down** — House rules on cleaning, guests, pets and moving out are agreed verbally and then disputed months later.  
    *new roommate households* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Template found online, rarely signed · source: knowledge
935. **Shared bathroom and kitchen scheduling** — Morning bathroom time and kitchen use collide when several people leave for work at the same hour.  
    *households of three or more* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Ad hoc negotiation · source: knowledge

### Roommate money

936. **Collecting rent before the due date** — One roommate is on the lease and must gather everyone's portion by the first; late transfers put their own credit and deposit at risk.  
    *lease-holding roommates* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Reminder texts and nagging · source: knowledge
937. **Departing roommate's final settle-up** — When someone moves out mid-lease, nobody can reconstruct who owes what for the deposit, last utilities and shared furniture.  
    *roommates at move-out* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Argument over text; someone eats the loss · source: knowledge
938. **Rent shares for unequal rooms** — Roommates argue over how much each should pay when bedrooms differ in size, closets, windows or private bathrooms.  
    *new roommate groups* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Guessing, or a one-off spreadsheet · source: knowledge
939. **Splitting shared bills unevenly** — Utilities, internet and streaming arrive on different days in one person's name; that person fronts the money and then has to chase everyone else for their share.  
    *renters with roommates* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Venmo requests and a group chat; Splitwise for some households · source: knowledge
940. **Shared furniture ownership at split** — Couches and TVs bought jointly become disputes when the household breaks up because nobody recorded who paid.  
    *roommates and cohabiting couples* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
941. **Shared groceries nobody tracks** — Milk, toilet paper and dish soap get bought by whoever notices first, so the same person always pays and resentment builds.  
    *roommates and couples* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Mental tally or 'you get it next time' · source: knowledge

<a id="digital-life"></a>

## Digital life & devices

*passwords, files, photos, backups, screen time, phone storage, accounts*


### Accounts & subscriptions

942. **Free trials that silently convert to paid** — A 7-day trial charges the card because nobody reminded you before the cutoff, and cancellation after the fact is refused.  
    *app and streaming subscribers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminders, virtual cards, disputing charges · source: knowledge
943. **Losing access when a login is through a dead social account** — Accounts created with 'Sign in with Facebook/Twitter' become unreachable when that social account is deleted or suspended.  
    *people who quit a social network* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: recovery via support, recreating accounts · source: knowledge
944. **No single view of all recurring subscriptions** — Subscriptions are spread across App Store, Google Play, PayPal, cards and direct debits, so the true monthly total is unknown.  
    *households with many subscriptions* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bank statement audits, spreadsheets · source: knowledge
