945. **Old email address still used for recovery** — The recovery email on key accounts is an ISP or school address that no longer exists, discovered only at the moment of lockout.  
    *people who changed email provider* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: auditing accounts one at a time after a scare · source: knowledge
946. **Deleting an account requires a hidden process** — Closing an account means hunting through settings, emailing support or mailing a form, so the accounts just stay open.  
    *anyone trying to reduce their footprint* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: justdelete.me style guides, giving up · source: knowledge
947. **Forgotten accounts on hundreds of sites** — Over 15 years you have signed up for hundreds of services; you have no list of them, and each one holds your name, email and often card details.  
    *long-time internet users* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: searching inbox for 'verify your email', ignoring it · source: knowledge
948. **Family plan seats and who pays** — Family sharing for music, storage and streaming is split across several people's accounts and nobody knows who is on which plan or who is paying.  
    *families and friend groups sharing plans* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: group chats, Venmo requests · source: knowledge

### Backups & data loss

949. **Cloud account suspension would take everything** — All photos, email and documents sit in one Google or Apple account; a false-positive suspension or lost login would erase digital life.  
    *people all-in on one ecosystem* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: takeout exports done once, never repeated · source: knowledge
950. **Nobody actually tests their backups** — A backup exists in theory, but it has never been restored, and the failure is discovered only when the drive dies.  
    *anyone who backs up* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: hoping, occasional manual check · source: knowledge
951. **Phone backup silently stopped months ago** — iCloud or Google backup failed quietly because storage was full, and the last good backup turns out to be six months old.  
    *smartphone users* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: noticing the warning banner, paying for storage · source: knowledge
952. **Chat history lost when switching platforms** — Years of WhatsApp or iMessage history do not move between Android and iPhone, or are lost when a backup is tied to the old cloud.  
    *phone switchers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: third-party transfer tools, screenshots · source: knowledge
953. **External backup drive is out of date** — The backup drive in the drawer was last plugged in over a year ago because there is no nudge to do it.  
    *people with a manual backup habit* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminder they ignore · source: knowledge
954. **No inventory of what needs backing up** — Data lives on a phone, laptop, two USB sticks, a game console and a NAS, and nobody has a list of what is where and what is protected.  
    *households with many devices* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### Files & documents

955. **Important documents buried in email attachments** — The lease, insurance policy or child's vaccination record exists only as an attachment in some email from years ago.  
    *most adults* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: searching email, asking the sender again · source: knowledge
956. **Can't open old file formats** — Files from a decade ago (.pages, .wps, old camera RAW, WordPerfect) won't open on current software and the originals are irreplaceable.  
    *long-term computer users* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: online converters, keeping an old machine · source: knowledge
957. **Downloads folder is a landfill** — Every PDF, installer and attachment lands in Downloads and stays there for years; finding last month's invoice means sorting through hundreds of files.  
    *laptop users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: occasional purge, search by date · source: knowledge
958. **Files split across three cloud drives** — Work uses OneDrive, personal uses Google Drive, old files are in Dropbox, and you cannot search all of them at once.  
    *people with work and personal devices* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: remembering where things live, checking each one · source: knowledge
959. **Final_v2_FINAL(3).docx versioning chaos** — Multiple copies of a document with slightly different names exist on desktop, email and cloud, and nobody knows which is the latest.  
    *students, freelancers, small teams* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: naming conventions nobody follows, checking timestamps · source: knowledge
960. **Losing track of what was shared with whom** — Documents shared via cloud links years ago remain accessible to ex-colleagues, exes and strangers, and there is no easy audit.  
    *cloud drive users* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manually reviewing sharing settings, never · source: knowledge
961. **Scanned receipts and forms are unsearchable images** — Photos of paper documents pile up with names like IMG_4521 and no text search, so retrieving one for taxes or warranty is slow.  
    *renters, freelancers, parents* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scanner apps used inconsistently, a shoebox · source: knowledge

### Passwords & logins

962. **Nobody can access accounts if you die** — Partners and family have no way to reach photos, bank logins, domains and subscriptions if the account owner dies or is incapacitated.  
    *adults with a family* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a sealed envelope, a note in a drawer, nothing · source: knowledge
963. **Two-factor codes tied to an old phone** — After switching phones the authenticator app is gone and every 2FA-protected account has to be recovered one by one, sometimes with support tickets.  
    *anyone who upgrades a phone* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: screenshots of recovery codes, SMS fallback, support tickets · source: knowledge
964. **Security questions with forgotten made-up answers** — Years ago you gave a joke answer for 'first pet' and now the bank or airline account is unrecoverable.  
    *anyone with older accounts* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: trying common variants, calling support · source: knowledge
965. **Which accounts share a breached password** — A breach notice arrives for one site and you cannot easily tell which other accounts reuse the same or similar password.  
    *anyone with reused passwords* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: guessing from memory, haveibeenpwned checks per email · source: knowledge
966. **Not knowing which email a login uses** — You know you have an account but not whether it was created with your personal, work or old university email, so the reset email never arrives.  
    *people with multiple email addresses* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: trying each email in turn, searching inboxes for 'welcome' mails · source: knowledge
967. **Password reset loop on rarely used sites** — Every time you return to a site you use twice a year you can't remember the password, so you reset it, pick a new one, and forget that one too.  
    *anyone with dozens of low-frequency accounts* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: browser autofill, reusing one password, sticky notes · source: knowledge
968. **Shared household logins get out of sync** — One partner changes the Netflix, Wi-Fi or utility password and the other is locked out; nobody knows the current one.  
    *couples, roommates, families* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group chat scrollback, shared note, texting each other · source: knowledge
969. **Password manager mis-fills or saves the wrong site** — The manager offers three stale entries for one site, fills the username into the password box, or never prompts to save on some forms.  
    *password manager users* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual copy-paste, cleaning duplicates by hand · source: knowledge
970. **Site password rules reject generated passwords** — The generated 24-character password is refused for length or a banned symbol, forcing manual tweaking that isn't saved back to the manager.  
    *password manager users* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: edit the generated password, hope the manager updated it · source: knowledge

### Photos & videos

971. **Photos from an ex or a bad period keep resurfacing** — Memories features and widgets show photos you'd rather not see, and hiding or bulk-removing a person is clumsy.  
    *anyone after a breakup or loss* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual deletion, turning memories off · source: knowledge
972. **Photos scattered across phones, clouds and drives** — Pictures live in iCloud, Google Photos, an old laptop, WhatsApp exports and a partner's phone, with no single complete library.  
    *families, people who switched platforms* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: occasional manual consolidation, giving up · source: knowledge
973. **Scanned family prints lack dates and names** — Boxes of old prints get scanned but nobody records who is in them or when, so the context dies with the older relatives.  
    *families digitising albums* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: handwritten notes, ad hoc spreadsheet · source: knowledge
974. **Can't find a specific old photo** — You know a photo exists ('the one of grandma at the lake') but cannot find it among 40,000 images without a date.  
    *anyone with a large library* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: scrolling by year, asking family · source: knowledge
975. **Screenshots that are never revisited** — Hundreds of screenshots of recipes, addresses and memes sit in the camera roll, unsearchable and indistinguishable from real photos.  
    *most smartphone users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scrolling back, periodic mass deletes · source: knowledge
976. **Shared event photos never get collected** — After a wedding or trip, photos are spread across a dozen phones and group chats and most are never pooled in one place at full quality.  
    *friend groups, wedding guests, parents at school events* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: WhatsApp groups (compressed), shared albums someone forgets to invite people to · source: knowledge
977. **Thousands of near-duplicate photos** — Burst shots, screenshots and re-saved WhatsApp images bloat the library, and picking the best of ten near-identical frames by hand is tedious.  
    *smartphone photographers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual swiping in Photos, third-party duplicate finders · source: knowledge
978. **Videos silently eat most of the storage** — A handful of 4K videos take more space than years of photos, but the library doesn't make it obvious which clips are worth keeping.  
    *smartphone users with full storage* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: sorting by file size manually · source: knowledge

### Privacy & digital footprint

979. **Data broker profiles keep reappearing** — Personal address and phone number show up on people-search sites, and removal requests have to be repeated every few months.  
    *adults concerned about privacy* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual opt-outs, paid removal services · source: knowledge
980. **Old posts and accounts embarrass you years later** — Content from teenage years on forums and social media is still findable by employers and dates, and cleanup is per-post.  
    *young professionals* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual deletion, googling yourself · source: knowledge
981. **Wi-Fi password sharing with guests** — Guests ask for the Wi-Fi and the router password is a 20-character string on a sticker in a cupboard.  
    *hosts, families, Airbnb owners* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: reading it aloud, photographing the sticker, QR code taped up · source: knowledge

### Screen time & attention

982. **Doomscrolling in bed delays sleep** — Late-night scrolling pushes bedtime back and the phone is also the alarm, so it can't simply be left in another room.  
    *adults and teens* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bedtime mode, wind-down, willpower · source: knowledge
983. **Kids' screen limits negotiated per device** — Children's time is spread across tablet, console, TV and a friend's phone, and limits set on one device don't count the others.  
    *parents* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: verbal rules, separate parental controls per device · source: knowledge
984. **Opening the phone for one thing, losing 20 minutes** — You unlock to check the weather, get pulled into a feed, and forget what you came for.  
    *most smartphone users* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: notification purges, app timers · source: knowledge
985. **Screen time limits are trivially bypassed** — Built-in limits are dismissed with 'ignore for today' or 'one more minute', so they stop working within a week.  
    *adults trying to cut phone use* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: grayscale mode, deleting apps, dumb phone · source: knowledge
986. **Notification overload from every app** — Dozens of apps send promotional pings, so real messages are buried and the reflex is to check the phone constantly.  
    *smartphone users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: muting apps one by one, Do Not Disturb · source: knowledge
987. **Screen-time reports don't distinguish useful from wasteful use** — Weekly reports lump maps, reading and work chat together with feeds, making the number meaningless and easy to ignore.  
    *people tracking screen time* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ignoring the report · source: knowledge

### Storage & device upkeep

988. **'Storage almost full' at the worst moment** — The phone refuses to take a photo or update because storage is full, and clearing space in the moment means deleting things blindly.  
    *smartphone users with base storage* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: deleting apps and videos in a panic, 'offload' features · source: knowledge
989. **Battery dying earlier than expected each day** — Some app drains the battery in the background and identifying the culprit from the OS battery screen is confusing.  
    *smartphone users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: low power mode, uninstall guesses · source: knowledge
990. **Messaging app media fills the phone** — Auto-downloaded group-chat videos and forwarded memes quietly consume gigabytes, with cleanup buried deep in settings.  
    *WhatsApp and Telegram users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual 'manage storage' cleanups · source: knowledge
991. **Mystery 'Other' or 'System' storage** — Tens of gigabytes are labelled Other/System with no way to see what they are or safely remove them.  
    *phone and laptop users* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: factory reset, cache clearing guides · source: knowledge
992. **Old devices with data still on them** — Retired phones and laptops sit in a drawer with photos and logins on them, too risky to donate and too much work to wipe properly.  
    *most households* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: keeping them forever · source: knowledge
993. **Unused apps hog space and permissions** — Dozens of apps opened once still take space, send notifications and hold location and contacts permissions.  
    *smartphone users* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: occasional purge, OS offload feature · source: knowledge

<a id="safety-lost-items"></a>

## Safety, emergencies & lost things

*lost items, emergencies, home security, walking alone, contacts in a crisis*


### Checking in on people

994. **Checking on a parent who lives alone** — Adult children worry an elderly parent has fallen and are reduced to calling several times a day, which the parent finds intrusive and the child finds exhausting.  
    *adult children of elderly parents, people who live alone* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Daily phone calls; medical alert pendants; neighbours · source: knowledge
995. **Living alone with no one to notice** — People who live alone fear that if they fell ill or were injured at home, days could pass before anyone realised something was wrong.  
    *single adults, remote workers, people with health conditions* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Arranging daily texts with a friend; none for most · source: knowledge
996. **Teen curfew tracking damages trust** — Parents want to know a teenager arrived at a friend's house safely, but constant location tracking causes fights and teens disable it.  
    *parents of teenagers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Life360; demanding texts on arrival · source: knowledge

### Crisis contacts

997. **Emergency contacts hidden behind lock screen** — If you collapse, bystanders or paramedics cannot see who to call or your allergies because the phone is locked and Medical ID is rarely set up or found.  
    *everyone, especially people with medical conditions* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Phone Medical ID; wallet card; medical bracelet · source: knowledge
998. **Kids' emergency info unavailable to sitters** — Babysitters and grandparents do not have the paediatrician's number, allergy list, insurance details and parents' whereabouts written anywhere when something goes wrong.  
    *parents of young children, babysitters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A note on the fridge; texting details each time · source: knowledge
999. **Lost phone means lost emergency numbers** — When your phone is lost or dead you cannot remember any contact's number, so you cannot call family from a borrowed phone.  
    *everyone who relies on a smartphone contact list* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Memorizing one number; a paper card in the wallet · source: knowledge
1000. **Not knowing local emergency numbers abroad** — In a foreign country you do not know whether to dial 112, 911 or 999, what the poison line is, or where the nearest embassy and hospital are.  
    *travelers and expats* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Searching online in the moment; hotel front desk · source: knowledge
1001. **Emergency contact list goes stale** — The 'in case of emergency' contacts on your phone, work HR form and gym membership list an ex-partner or an old number, and nobody remembers to update them.  
    *adults after a move, breakup or family change* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1002. **No clear path to report scams** — After a phishing text, stalker message or suspicious visitor, people do not know which agency or platform to report to and give up.  
    *scam targets, harassment victims* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Blocking the number; posting a warning to friends · source: knowledge

### Emergency preparedness

1003. **Important documents inaccessible when evacuating** — Fleeing a fire or flood you cannot grab insurance policies, IDs and medication lists, and later you have no copies to file claims with.  
    *households in wildfire, hurricane or flood zones* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A folder in a closet; scattered cloud drive photos · source: knowledge
1004. **Emergency kit supplies expire unnoticed** — Water, food, batteries and medicines in the go-bag or car kit quietly pass their expiry dates, so the kit is useless when finally needed.  
    *households that keep emergency kits, preppers, drivers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calendar reminder once a year; checking during daylight-saving changes · source: knowledge
1005. **Power outage leaves you uninformed** — When the power goes out you cannot tell whether it is just your house, how large the outage is, or when it will be restored, and the utility's status page is hard to reach on mobile data.  
    *households, remote workers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Utility outage map; neighbourhood group chat · source: knowledge
1006. **Weather alerts too broad or too many** — Government weather and emergency alerts cover a whole county, so residents get woken for events far away and then silence alerts before the one that matters.  
    *residents of storm, flood and wildfire regions* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Multiple weather apps; local news; turning alerts off · source: knowledge

### Emergency response

1007. **Explaining where you are to dispatch** — On a highway, trail or in a large building you cannot give a useful address to the emergency operator and waste critical minutes describing landmarks.  
    *drivers, hikers, people in unfamiliar places* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Reading GPS coordinates from a map app; what3words · source: knowledge
1008. **Family cannot reach each other in a disaster** — During an earthquake, storm or outage the network is jammed, and nobody agreed in advance where to meet or who checks in with whom.  
    *families in disaster-prone areas* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Red Cross 'safe and well' pages; group texts that fail · source: knowledge
1009. **Forgetting what to do in a medical emergency** — When someone chokes, has a seizure or a suspected stroke, panic makes people forget the basic steps they learned in a first-aid course years ago.  
    *parents, caregivers, bystanders* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Calling emergency services and following the operator; Red Cross app · source: knowledge
1010. **Lost child in a crowded place** — At a fair, mall or beach a child wanders off and the parent has no current photo of what they are wearing, no venue contact and no plan agreed with the child.  
    *parents of young children* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Taking a photo at the entrance; writing a phone number on the child's arm · source: knowledge
1011. **Car breakdown on a road with no plan** — When the car dies at night you scramble to find the roadside assistance number, your policy number and a way to tell someone exactly where you are.  
    *drivers, especially on long trips* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Insurance card in the glovebox; searching online · source: knowledge
1012. **Deciding if a symptom is an emergency** — At 2am you cannot tell whether chest tightness, a child's fever or a deep cut warrants the ER, urgent care or waiting until morning, and the phone lines have long holds.  
    *parents, caregivers, anyone without a doctor on call* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Nurse hotlines; Googling symptoms; going to the ER to be safe · source: knowledge

### Home security

1013. **Package theft from the porch** — Deliveries left on the doorstep disappear before you get home, and coordinating delivery windows or a safe drop spot with each carrier is unreliable.  
    *online shoppers in apartments and busy streets* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Delivering to work or lockers; asking neighbours to grab parcels · source: knowledge
1014. **Security camera notification overload** — Doorbell and yard cameras ping for every passing car, cat and shadow, so dozens of daily alerts bury the one real intruder event.  
    *owners of doorbell and home cameras* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Motion zones; disabling notifications; paid AI person-detection tiers · source: knowledge
1015. **Uncertainty whether door locked, stove off** — Halfway to work you cannot remember whether the front door is locked, the garage closed or the iron off, and turn back or worry all day.  
    *commuters, people with anxiety or OCD* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Photo of the locked door; smart locks; saying it aloud · source: knowledge
1016. **Empty home looks empty while away** — On vacation the house is dark, mail piles up and bins are not put out, signalling to burglars that nobody is home, and asking neighbours for each task is awkward.  
    *travelers, second-home owners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Timer plugs; mail hold; asking a neighbour · source: knowledge
1017. **Fire and CO alarm testing forgotten** — Smoke and carbon monoxide detectors need monthly tests and battery changes, and expire after ten years, but nobody tracks when each unit was installed or checked.  
    *homeowners, landlords, renters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Change batteries when the clocks change; wait for the chirp · source: knowledge
1018. **Neighbourhood crime info is rumour-driven** — Residents hear about break-ins and car thefts via alarmist group posts, with no reliable view of what actually happened on their street this month.  
    *residents in suburbs and city neighbourhoods* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Nextdoor and neighbourhood WhatsApp groups; police blotters · source: knowledge
1019. **Security gear split across many apps** — The doorbell, indoor cameras, smart lock and alarm each have their own app and login, so checking the house means opening four apps and no one gets a single 'all secure' view.  
    *smart home owners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Sticking to one brand; home automation hubs for technical users · source: knowledge
1020. **Temporary access for guests and workers** — Giving a cleaner, dog walker or contractor access means handing out physical keys or sharing the alarm code, with no record of who came in when and no easy way to revoke.  
    *homeowners, short-term rental hosts* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Lockboxes; spare keys under mats; smart lock codes · source: knowledge
1021. **Who has the keys and codes** — In a household several people hold spare keys, know the alarm code and the safe combination, and when one is lost or a relationship ends nobody knows what needs changing.  
    *households, roommates, landlords* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### Lost while out

1022. **Lost pet search is chaotic** — When a dog or cat goes missing the owner must post in many neighbourhood groups, print flyers, call shelters and vets separately, all while panicking.  
    *pet owners* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups, Nextdoor, paper flyers, calling shelters · source: knowledge
1023. **Wallet theft recovery steps are scattered** — After a theft you must freeze cards, order a new license, file a police report and watch for fraud, but there is no ordered checklist with the right phone numbers and deadlines for your cards and country.  
    *theft and loss victims* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google searches while stressed; calling each bank · source: knowledge
1024. **Airline lost luggage tracking is opaque** — Delayed bags require a reference number, repeated calls and a claim form, with no clear status on where the bag is or when it will be delivered.  
    *air travelers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: AirTag in the suitcase; calling the airline daily · source: knowledge
1025. **Leaving items behind in rideshares** — You realize hours later that your phone, umbrella or bag stayed in the Uber or taxi, and reaching the driver means digging through the app for a support form with slow replies.  
    *commuters and travelers using rideshare* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Rideshare 'lost item' form; calling the driver via masked number for a fee · source: knowledge
1026. **No single place to report a lost item** — When you lose something in a city you have to guess whether it went to the bus company, the venue, the police or a nearby cafe, and each has its own form, phone line or none at all.  
    *city dwellers, tourists, students* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Calling each place; posting on local Facebook groups · source: knowledge
1027. **Not knowing what was in a lost bag** — After a bag or wallet goes missing you cannot recall exactly which cards, IDs and documents were inside, so you do not know what to cancel or replace.  
    *anyone who has lost a wallet, purse or backpack* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Scrolling old bank statements and photos; guessing · source: knowledge
1028. **Proving ownership of recovered items** — When a stolen bike or laptop turns up, police or the pawn shop ask for serial numbers and receipts you never recorded.  
    *bike, laptop and camera owners* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Searching email for the receipt; photos of the item · source: knowledge
1029. **Finders cannot contact the owner** — Someone picks up a set of keys, a wallet or a water bottle but has no way to reach the owner, so the item ends up in a drawer or the trash.  
    *anyone who finds or loses items in public* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Hand to nearest staff; post a photo on social media · source: knowledge
1030. **Forgetting items when leaving a place** — Chargers stay in hotel rooms, jackets at restaurants and sunglasses at friends' houses because there is no reminder to check your belongings before walking out.  
    *travelers, frequent diners, students* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Mental 'phone, wallet, keys' check; packing lists · source: knowledge
1031. **Kids' belongings vanish at school** — Water bottles, lunch boxes, hoodies and PE kit disappear weekly and parents have no way to see the school's lost-and-found pile without visiting in person.  
    *parents of school-age children* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Labelling everything; asking teachers by email · source: knowledge

### Misplaced everyday items

1032. **Bluetooth tracker alerts fire too often** — Item trackers send 'left behind' alerts every time you walk to the mailbox or leave keys at home deliberately, so you start ignoring them and miss the real one.  
    *AirTag, Tile and SmartTag users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Turning off alerts; setting trusted locations one by one · source: knowledge
1033. **Forgetting where you stored rarely used things** — Passport, spare keys, warranty cards or the holiday decorations were put somewhere 'safe' months ago and now nobody can remember which box, drawer or closet.  
    *households, movers, people with storage units* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Searching every closet; asking family in a group chat · source: knowledge
1034. **Keys, wallet, phone misplaced at home** — Several times a week you leave the house late because your keys, wallet or phone are not where you thought you put them, and you have no way to know which room to search first.  
    *almost everyone, especially people with ADHD or busy households* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Bluetooth trackers on some items; retracing steps; a bowl by the door · source: knowledge
1035. **Trackers from different brands in separate apps** — A household with AirTags, Tiles and Samsung tags must open three apps to check items, and one partner's Android phone cannot see the other's Apple tags at all.  
    *mixed-device families and couples* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Buying only one brand; sharing screenshots · source: knowledge
1036. **Lent items never come back** — Books, tools, cables and cash lent to friends and colleagues are forgotten by both sides, and asking months later feels awkward because nobody is sure who has what.  
    *friends, neighbours, coworkers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Notes app; just accepting the loss · source: knowledge

### Personal safety out alone

1037. **Discreet SOS without unlocking the phone** — In a threatening moment you cannot unlock the phone, open an app and type; you need one hidden gesture that alerts chosen people with your location.  
    *people in unsafe situations, domestic abuse survivors* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Phone power-button emergency SOS, which calls 911 rather than a friend · source: knowledge
1038. **Fake call excuse to leave a situation** — On a bad date or when someone is following you, you want a believable interruption that gives you a reason to walk away without escalating.  
    *people on first dates, solo travelers, women walking alone* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Texting a friend to call; pretending to answer the phone · source: knowledge
1039. **Meeting strangers from online listings** — Buying from marketplace listings or meeting a date means going to a stranger's location with nobody knowing the address, time or the person's details.  
    *online marketplace buyers and sellers, online daters* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Screenshotting the profile to a friend; meeting at police station lots · source: knowledge
1040. **No one knows you got home safe** — Walking home late you want someone to know when you arrive, but texting a friend at 1am is awkward and they may be asleep and not notice if you never text.  
    *students, shift workers, people walking alone at night* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Texting 'home' to a friend; staying on the phone · source: knowledge
1041. **Phone dies during a night out** — A dead phone late at night leaves you unable to call a ride, reach friends or navigate, and friends cannot find you either.  
    *young adults, festival and concert goers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Power banks; agreeing a meeting point in advance · source: knowledge
1042. **Running or hiking alone unmonitored** — Solo runners and hikers may fall or get lost with nobody expecting them back at a set time, and their phone may have no signal to call.  
    *solo runners, hikers, cyclists* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Telling a partner the route; satellite messengers for serious hikers · source: knowledge
1043. **Choosing a safer walking route at night** — Map apps pick the fastest route, which may go through unlit parks or empty streets; there is no way to prefer well-lit, busy streets after dark.  
    *pedestrians and cyclists at night* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Sticking to main roads from memory; taking a taxi · source: knowledge
1044. **Rideshare driver mismatch anxiety** — Getting into the wrong car is a real worry at night; checking plate, model and driver photo in a rush is easy to skip.  
    *rideshare passengers, especially solo women* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Asking the driver 'who are you here for'; sharing trip status · source: knowledge
1045. **Sharing live location drains battery** — Keeping location sharing on for safety uses so much battery that the phone dies exactly when you would need it, so people turn it off.  
    *users of Find My and Life360 style apps* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Sharing only for short trips; carrying a power bank · source: knowledge

---

# Part 2 — Specialized groups and situations


<a id="small-business-freelance"></a>

## Small business owners & freelancers

*invoicing, clients, quotes, scheduling, bookkeeping, gig work, side hustles*


### Admin & operations

1046. **Client passwords shared unsafely** — Clients send website, hosting and social media passwords over email or text, and freelancers store them in notes files.  
    *web and marketing freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app, password manager · source: knowledge
1047. **Contracts signed by screenshot or not at all** — Getting a proper signed agreement feels heavy, so work starts on a handshake and disputes have no paper trail.  
    *freelancers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: PDF via email, DocuSign trial · source: knowledge
1048. **Subcontractor payments and hours** — Farming out work to another freelancer means tracking their hours, invoices, and paying them on time from the client's payment.  
    *small agencies and freelancers who subcontract* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1049. **Subscription creep for business tools** — A dozen SaaS tools at $10-30 each pile up, some unused, and nobody reviews them.  
    *solo business owners* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: check bank statement occasionally · source: knowledge
1050. **Retyping answers to the same client questions** — Every client asks about turnaround time, payment terms, and revision policy, and the answers are retyped every time.  
    *freelancers* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: canned email snippets · source: knowledge

### Bookkeeping & taxes

1051. **Quarterly estimated taxes are a surprise** — Freelancers don't know how much to set aside and get hit with a large tax bill and penalties each quarter or year.  
    *self-employed in the US and similar* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: set aside a rough percentage · source: knowledge
1052. **Income from many platforms hard to total** — Money arrives via PayPal, Stripe, Upwork, Etsy, and bank transfers, and getting a true monthly income figure means logging into five dashboards.  
    *multi-platform freelancers and sellers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1053. **Mixed personal and business transactions** — Business expenses paid from a personal card and vice versa make it painful to separate what is deductible.  
    *side hustlers and new freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually tag bank transactions · source: knowledge
1054. **Receipts piling up unsorted** — Paper and email receipts accumulate for months and get sorted in a panic at tax time, with many lost or faded.  
    *sole proprietors* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: shoebox, photos in camera roll · source: knowledge
1055. **Sales tax or VAT across jurisdictions** — Selling to clients in different states or countries raises the question of whether to charge sales tax or VAT, at what rate, and when to file.  
    *online sellers and digital freelancers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ignore it or ask an accountant · source: knowledge
1056. **Unclear whether the business is profitable** — Revenue feels fine but after software subscriptions, fees, and taxes it's unclear what the real take-home is per month or per client.  
    *small business owners* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: annual accountant summary · source: knowledge
1057. **Home office expense apportioning** — Working out what share of rent, internet, and phone counts as a business expense is confusing and redone every year.  
    *home-based freelancers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: guess a percentage · source: knowledge
1058. **Invoices and bank deposits don't match** — Reconciling which deposit corresponds to which invoice, after fees and partial payments, takes hours each month.  
    *freelancers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual matching in spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1059. **Mileage tracking forgotten** — Business driving is deductible but logging trips manually is tedious, so most trips go unrecorded.  
    *mobile service providers, gig drivers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notebook, rough estimate · source: knowledge
1060. **Tax forms from many clients** — Collecting 1099-type forms from each client, or issuing them to subcontractors, involves chasing and tracking dozens of documents every January.  
    *US freelancers and small agencies* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: email reminders, folder · source: knowledge

### Client communication

1061. **Client feedback scattered across channels** — Feedback arrives via email, WhatsApp, text, Slack and phone calls, so revision requests get missed or contradict each other.  
    *designers, writers, developers* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: copy everything into one doc · source: knowledge
1062. **Endless revision rounds** — Contracts allow two revisions but nobody tracks the count, and clients keep sending 'one more small change' indefinitely.  
    *designers, video editors, writers* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual counting, arguing · source: knowledge
1063. **Waiting on client assets and content** — Projects stall because the client has not sent logos, copy, or logins, and it is unclear who is blocking whom.  
    *web designers, developers, marketers* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: nagging emails · source: knowledge
1064. **Clients ignoring working hours** — Clients text and call at night and on weekends expecting instant replies, and there is no polite automated boundary.  
    *solo freelancers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: mute phone, autoresponder · source: knowledge
1065. **Ghosted mid-project** — A client disappears after work has started, and there is no clear process for pausing, billing partial work, or closing out.  
    *freelancers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: send a few emails then give up · source: knowledge
1066. **No single view of client history** — Past projects, rates agreed, preferences, and contact people for each client live in memory or scattered files, so returning clients feel like strangers.  
    *freelancers with repeat clients* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet, email search · source: knowledge
1067. **Onboarding questionnaire chaos** — Collecting brand details, access, goals and preferences from a new client happens over many emails and is never in one place.  
    *agencies and freelancers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Form, email list · source: knowledge
1068. **Sharing large files and getting sign-off** — Sending deliverables via WeTransfer links that expire, then chasing a formal 'approved' response, drags on final delivery.  
    *photographers, video editors, designers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Dropbox, email 'looks good' · source: knowledge

### Gig platforms & side hustles

1069. **Income too irregular to budget** — Feast-or-famine months make it impossible to know whether a slow month is a crisis or normal, or how much can be safely paid out.  
    *freelancers* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: big emergency buffer, anxiety · source: knowledge
1070. **Gig platform fees hidden in earnings** — Platform take rates change and are buried in statements, so workers don't know their true hourly rate after fees, mileage and time waiting.  
    *gig workers and marketplace freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: rough math · source: knowledge
1071. **Juggling several gig apps at once** — Drivers and taskers run Uber, DoorDash, Instacart simultaneously and can't easily compare which app is paying best right now or track combined earnings.  
    *gig drivers and taskers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: switch between apps, notebook · source: knowledge
1072. **Marketplace order fulfillment tracking** — Small sellers lose track of which orders are made, packed, shipped, and which customers messaged about delays.  
    *small online sellers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: platform dashboard, sticky notes · source: knowledge
1073. **Supplies and materials running out** — Makers and small sellers run out of a key material mid-order because supply levels and reorder points are not tracked.  
    *makers, crafters, small sellers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: eyeballing the shelf · source: knowledge
1074. **Tracking marketplace proposals sent** — Winning work on Upwork or Fiverr requires sending many proposals, and tracking which ones were viewed, replied to, or hired is chaotic.  
    *marketplace freelancers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: platform inbox, spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1075. **Side hustle time competing with day job** — A side hustle gets random evening hours and there is no way to see whether the time invested is worth the money earned.  
    *side hustlers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### Invoicing & getting paid

1076. **Chasing overdue invoices by hand** — Freelancers spend hours every month writing awkward reminder emails for invoices that are 30, 60, 90 days late, and lose track of which client has been nudged how many times.  
    *freelancers and small agencies* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual email follow-ups, spreadsheet of due dates · source: knowledge
1077. **Clients disputing hours after the fact** — When an hourly invoice arrives the client questions what took so long, and the freelancer has no clear per-task breakdown to back it up.  
    *hourly freelancers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: vague time logs, memory · source: knowledge
1078. **Forgetting to invoice small add-ons** — Small extra requests done mid-project ('can you just tweak this') never get logged, so they are never billed and revenue leaks away.  
    *designers, developers, consultants* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: memory, sticky notes · source: knowledge
1079. **Milestone payments tracked by memory** — On a multi-milestone project it becomes unclear what percentage has been paid, what is outstanding, and which deliverable unlocks the next payment.  
    *freelancers on fixed-price projects* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet per project · source: knowledge
1080. **Check and wire payments with surprise fees** — Clients pay via methods that take weeks to clear or deduct wire and processing fees, so the amount received never matches the invoice and reconciling is a headache.  
    *freelancers with corporate or overseas clients* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: eat the fee, manually adjust in books · source: knowledge
1081. **Late-fee terms never enforced** — Contracts say 'late fees apply' but calculating and adding them requires manual work and an awkward conversation, so they are never charged.  
    *freelancers and contractors* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1082. **Multi-currency invoices and exchange losses** — Billing an overseas client means guessing the exchange rate, losing money on conversion, and then not knowing what to record as income in home currency.  
    *freelancers with international clients* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Wise/PayPal, manual conversion in spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1083. **Not knowing if an invoice was opened** — After sending an invoice you have no idea whether the client saw it, forwarded it to accounting, or it went to spam, so you can't tell when to follow up.  
    *freelancers billing by email* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: ask the client, wait and hope · source: knowledge
1084. **Processor fees eating small invoices** — Card processing fees of 3% plus a fixed amount make small invoices barely worth sending, and deciding whether to pass fees on to the client is confusing.  
    *freelancers with many small invoices* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: add a surcharge line or absorb it · source: knowledge
1085. **Recurring retainer invoices sent late** — Monthly retainer invoices depend on the freelancer remembering to generate and send them on the 1st, and forgetting delays cash flow by weeks.  
    *retainer-based freelancers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminder, copy last month's invoice · source: knowledge

### Marketing & leads

1086. **Following up with leads inconsistently** — Prospects who said 'maybe next quarter' are forgotten because there is no lightweight way to schedule a nudge.  
    *small business owners* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: CRM too heavy, memory · source: knowledge
1087. **Collecting testimonials and reviews** — Happy clients rarely leave a review unless asked at the right moment, and asking is awkward and forgotten.  
    *service businesses and freelancers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: occasional email ask · source: knowledge
1088. **Not knowing where clients come from** — Referrals, social media, marketplaces and cold outreach all bring work, but nobody records which source actually converts.  
    *small business owners* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: guess · source: knowledge
1089. **Portfolio always out of date** — Finished work never gets added to the portfolio or website because it takes effort to write it up and get client permission.  
    *designers, developers, photographers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: batch update once a year · source: knowledge

### Quotes & proposals

1090. **Scope creep with no change-order process** — Clients ask for 'small additions' beyond the agreed scope and, without a quick way to price and document extras, the freelancer either does them free or argues.  
    *designers, developers, contractors* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: awkward email negotiations · source: knowledge
1091. **Approval is a vague 'sounds good' email** — Work starts on a casual 'go ahead' reply with no formal sign-off on scope, price, or terms, which creates disputes later.  
    *freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: screenshot the email · source: knowledge
1092. **Not knowing what to charge** — New freelancers have no idea whether their hourly or project rate is reasonable for their skill, market and region, and rarely revisit it as they improve.  
    *new and mid-level freelancers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking peers, Reddit threads · source: knowledge
1093. **Quotes going stale without follow-up** — A quote is sent, the prospect goes silent, and there is no system to know how many quotes are open, their value, or when to check in.  
    *small service businesses* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet or memory · source: knowledge
1094. **Rewriting the same proposal from scratch** — Every proposal starts from a blank document or an old one with the wrong client name, and hours go into formatting instead of selling.  
    *consultants, agencies, freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Word template, find-and-replace · source: knowledge
1095. **Underquoting from unknown past project time** — Quoting a new job means guessing how long it will take, because nobody records how long similar past projects actually took versus what was quoted.  
    *freelancers and trades* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: gut feeling, rounding up · source: knowledge
1096. **Hourly versus project pricing confusion** — Deciding whether to bill hourly, per-project, or per-value on each job, and translating one into the other, is done ad hoc and leads to inconsistent pricing.  
    *freelancers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1097. **Quote versions lost in email threads** — Revised quotes go back and forth by email and nobody is sure which version the client actually approved.  
    *trades and service businesses* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: search email, ask client · source: knowledge

### Scheduling & booking

1098. **Last-minute cancellations and no-shows** — Clients cancel an hour before or simply don't show, wasting slots that can't be refilled and costing income.  
    *hairdressers, tutors, cleaners, coaches* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: deposit policy, texting reminders manually · source: knowledge
1099. **Capacity unknown when accepting new work** — Saying yes to a new project without knowing how many hours are already committed next month leads to overbooking and burnout.  
    *freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: rough mental estimate · source: knowledge
1100. **Double-booking across multiple calendars** — Personal, work and client calendars are not synced, so consultations get booked over each other or over personal commitments.  
    *consultants, coaches, service pros* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calendly plus manual checks · source: knowledge
1101. **Travel time missing from the schedule** — Mobile service businesses book appointments without accounting for driving time between jobs, causing lateness and dead time.  
    *cleaners, mobile groomers, handymen* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: guess a buffer · source: knowledge
1102. **Back-and-forth to find a meeting time** — Five emails to agree on a time for a 20-minute call, repeated for every prospect and client.  
    *consultants and freelancers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scheduling links · source: knowledge
1103. **Recurring client appointments drifting** — Standing weekly or monthly bookings slip when someone reschedules once and the recurrence is never re-established.  
    *tutors, trainers, cleaners* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual re-entry · source: knowledge
1104. **Time zone mistakes with remote clients** — Scheduling calls with clients in other time zones leads to missed meetings because someone converted the time wrong.  
    *remote freelancers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: world clock, asking twice · source: knowledge

### Time & project tracking

1105. **Too many small projects to track** — With eight clients and twelve small projects, deadlines and next steps are tracked in heads and inboxes, and something slips every week.  
    *freelancers with many clients* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Trello, notebook · source: knowledge
1106. **Deadlines promised without seeing workload** — Promising a delivery date on Friday without seeing that three other deadlines fall the same week.  
    *freelancers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar glance · source: knowledge
1107. **Forgetting to start or stop the timer** — Hourly work goes unbilled or over-billed because timers are started late or left running overnight.  
    *hourly freelancers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: estimate afterward · source: knowledge
1108. **Unpaid admin time invisible** — Emails, invoicing, and calls take a big chunk of the week but are never measured, so effective hourly rate is far lower than believed.  
    *freelancers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

<a id="students-college"></a>

## College & university students

*classes, deadlines, group projects, campus life, money, dorms*


### Campus life

1109. **Campus email overload** — The university sends dozens of newsletters, reminders, and announcements daily, and the one important message about a registration hold is buried.  
    *all students* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ignoring the inbox and missing things · source: knowledge
1110. **Making friends as a transfer or commuter** — Students who don't live in dorms or arrived after freshman year have no natural way to meet people and feel isolated.  
    *transfer, commuter, and international students* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: joining clubs at random · source: knowledge
1111. **Finding a ride home for breaks** — Students without cars need rides to the airport or hometown at holiday breaks and rely on chance posts to find someone going the same way.  
    *students without cars* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Facebook rideshare groups and expensive Ubers · source: knowledge
1112. **Hearing about campus events after they happen** — Club meetings, free food events, and guest talks are announced on flyers and in emails students never read, so they find out the next day.  
    *students wanting to get involved* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Instagram follows and a bloated inbox · source: knowledge
1113. **Lost campus printing and parking rules** — Students don't know where they can print, how many pages are free, or which lot they can park in without a ticket.  
    *new and commuter students* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking around and getting fined · source: knowledge
1114. **Missing the campus shuttle** — Shuttle and campus bus times are unreliable, and students either wait 20 minutes in the cold or watch it leave as they arrive.  
    *students on large or multi-campus universities* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: leaving far too early, or walking · source: knowledge
1115. **Dining hall menu and crowding unknown** — Students walk across campus to a dining hall only to find a menu they don't want or a 30-minute line.  
    *students on meal plans* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: checking a PDF menu that's often outdated · source: knowledge
1116. **Gym and rec center overcrowded** — Students show up to the campus gym at peak time and every rack and treadmill is taken.  
    *students who work out* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: guessing off-peak hours · source: knowledge

### Career & admin

1117. **Registration holds discovered on registration day** — A missing immunization form or an unpaid $15 fee blocks course registration and the student finds out when the classes fill.  
    *students at registration* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: checking the portal repeatedly the week before · source: knowledge
1118. **Tracking internship applications** — Students apply to 50+ internships with different deadlines, portals, and follow-up steps and lose track of where each stands.  
    *juniors and seniors job hunting* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a spreadsheet that gets abandoned · source: knowledge
1119. **Forgetting to log volunteer or co-curricular hours** — Programs and scholarships require documented hours, and students reconstruct months of activity from memory at the deadline.  
    *students in honors, service, or scholarship programs* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: guessing and hoping no one checks · source: knowledge
1120. **Keeping citations formatted correctly** — Every paper needs APA or MLA citations, and students lose points on formatting or spend hours fixing reference lists.  
    *students writing papers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Zotero, citation generators with wrong output · source: knowledge

### Classes & grades

1121. **Waitlisted class seat opens unnoticed** — A seat in a full required class opens for a few hours and is gone again before the student happens to refresh the registration page.  
    *students stuck on waitlists* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: refreshing the enrollment page dozens of times a day · source: knowledge
1122. **Building a conflict-free class schedule** — Choosing sections that satisfy degree requirements, avoid time overlaps, avoid 8 AM slots, and leave gaps for work takes hours of trial and error.  
    *students during registration* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper grids, multiple browser tabs, and screenshots of the catalog · source: knowledge
1123. **Degree requirements progress is opaque** — Students can't easily see which requirements are complete, which courses double-count, and whether they'll graduate on time, until an advisor meeting months away.  
    *students planning future semesters* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a printed degree audit and a hand-maintained checklist · source: knowledge
1124. **Not knowing current grade in a class** — Weighted categories, dropped lowest scores, and ungraded items make it impossible to tell whether a student is at a B+ or a C, so they can't decide where to focus.  
    *students tracking GPA or scholarship thresholds* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: building a spreadsheet from the syllabus weighting, often wrong · source: knowledge
1125. **Choosing professors with no reliable info** — Students pick sections blind, relying on outdated or gamed rating sites, and end up with a grader or teaching style that wrecks their term.  
    *students at registration* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking friends and reading Rate My Professors · source: knowledge
1126. **What score is needed on the final** — Before the final exam students want to know the minimum score to keep their letter grade, and have to recompute weights by hand every time.  
    *students before finals* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: generic 'final grade calculator' websites that ignore dropped scores · source: knowledge

### Deadlines & planning

1127. **Deadlines scattered across five course platforms** — Each course posts assignments in a different place (Canvas, a professor's website, email, a syllabus PDF), so students miss due dates they never saw in one list.  
    *students taking 4-6 courses per term* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually copying every syllabus into a calendar or notes app at the start of term · source: knowledge
1128. **Three exams landing in the same week** — Students only realize in week 9 that midterms for three classes fall within two days, leaving no time to study for each properly.  
    *students with heavy course loads* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: cramming, or asking a professor for an extension after the fact · source: knowledge
1129. **Big assignment not broken into steps** — A 20-page paper due in six weeks feels unstartable, so nothing happens until three days before and the whole thing is written in a panic.  
    *procrastination-prone students* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: all-nighters; vague intentions to 'start early' · source: knowledge
1130. **Missing the 11:59 PM submission cutoff** — Uploads at 11:58 fail because the file is too large or the portal is slow, and the assignment is marked late by a minute.  
    *students who work up to the deadline* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: screenshotting the upload error as evidence for the professor · source: knowledge
1131. **Submitting the wrong file version** — Students upload a draft instead of the final, or the wrong file entirely, and only notice after the portal locks.  
    *students submitting via LMS portals* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: emailing the professor begging to resubmit · source: knowledge
1132. **Syllabus dates changed mid-semester** — Professors shift due dates verbally in class or in a buried announcement, so the calendar the student built in week one is quietly wrong.  
    *students who plan from the syllabus* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking classmates in the group chat 'wait, is that still due Friday?' · source: knowledge
1133. **Wasting the gaps between classes** — Students have scattered 50-minute gaps across the day that are too short to go home and end up scrolling instead of working.  
    *students with fragmented schedules* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### Dorm & housing

1134. **Finding a compatible roommate or subletter** — Students need a roommate for next year or someone to take their lease over summer, and matching happens through random Facebook posts with strangers.  
    *students in off-campus housing* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Facebook housing groups and campus bulletin boards · source: knowledge
1135. **Laundry machines all taken** — Students haul laundry down to the basement to find every machine in use, or return to find their clothes dumped on the floor.  
    *dorm residents* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: going at 6 AM; some campuses have machine-status apps that rarely work · source: knowledge
1136. **Moving out every summer** — Students must pack, store, and move everything they own every May and August, and forget what's in storage or what they need to buy again.  
    *dorm residents* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: storage companies and a pile of unlabeled boxes · source: knowledge
1137. **Roommate chores never get done** — Dishes pile up, trash overflows, and the bathroom goes uncleaned because nobody agreed on whose turn it is.  
    *students in dorms and shared apartments* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: passive-aggressive notes and one person doing everything · source: knowledge
1138. **Roommate schedule and quiet hours clash** — One roommate has an 8 AM class and the other games until 3 AM; neither knows the other's schedule so conflicts happen daily.  
    *students sharing a room* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: arguments or a whiteboard on the door · source: knowledge
1139. **Dorm maintenance requests vanish** — A broken heater or leaking sink is reported through a clunky portal and no one knows if or when it will be fixed.  
    *dorm and campus-apartment residents* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reporting again, or bothering the RA · source: knowledge
1140. **Shared groceries disappear from the fridge** — Food bought by one roommate is eaten by another, and nobody tracks what is shared versus personal.  
    *students with shared kitchens* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: labels with names on them; mini-fridge in the bedroom · source: knowledge

### Group projects

1141. **Group member goes silent before deadline** — One teammate stops responding days before the due date, and the rest have no idea whether their part is coming or must be redone.  
    *students in assigned group projects* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: frantic messages, then doing the work themselves at 2 AM · source: knowledge
1142. **Finding a meeting time for six people** — Coordinating a group meeting across six class schedules, jobs, and time zones takes a week of back-and-forth polls.  
    *students in group projects* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: When2meet links and endless 'does Tuesday work?' messages · source: knowledge
1143. **No record of who actually contributed** — When peer evaluations come, students can't prove a freeloader did nothing, and the freeloader gets the same grade.  
    *students carrying group projects* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: screenshots of the group chat sent to the professor · source: knowledge
1144. **Unclear who is doing which part** — Group projects are divided verbally in one meeting, then two people do the same section while a third section is forgotten.  
    *students in group projects* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a pinned message in the group chat nobody rereads · source: knowledge
1145. **Group chat scattered across apps** — One group uses iMessage, another WhatsApp, another Discord, another Slack, and important messages and files get lost between them.  
    *students in multiple groups per term* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: searching four apps to find the file someone sent · source: knowledge
1146. **Merging five different document versions** — Each member writes their section in a different tool and format, and someone spends the last night stitching them into one consistent document.  
    *students finalizing group deliverables* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: one person copy-pasting everything into a single Google Doc · source: knowledge

### Health & wellbeing

1147. **Counseling center booked out for weeks** — When stress peaks during exams, the campus counseling center has a three-week wait and students don't know other options.  
    *students under academic stress* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: friends, or nothing · source: knowledge
1148. **Sleep schedule wrecked by late nights** — Assignments and social life push bedtime to 3 AM, then students sleep through 9 AM lectures and fall behind.  
    *most undergraduates* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: energy drinks and five alarms · source: knowledge
1149. **Eating badly on a student budget** — Students with tiny kitchens, no time, and little money end up on instant noodles and takeout, and don't know cheap easy meals.  
    *students cooking for themselves* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: same three meals on repeat · source: knowledge

### Money

1150. **Running out of money before month end** — Loan or allowance money arrives in a lump and is gone by week three, leaving students eating ramen until the next disbursement.  
    *students on loans, stipends, or allowances* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: vague mental budgeting; borrowing from friends · source: knowledge
1151. **Textbooks cost hundreds per term** — Required textbooks cost $100-300 each, and students don't know until week two whether the professor will actually use them.  
    *all students at term start* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pirated PDFs, library reserves, older editions, or skipping the book · source: knowledge
1152. **Balancing part-time shifts with classes** — Work schedules change weekly and collide with labs, exams, and study time, and the student has no single view to spot the conflicts.  
    *students working part-time* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: two separate calendars and hoping · source: knowledge
1153. **Scholarship deadlines slip by** — Dozens of small scholarships have different deadlines and requirements, and students miss most of them because tracking is too much work.  
    *students needing financial aid* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a bookmarked list that is never revisited · source: knowledge
1154. **Splitting costs with roommates** — Rent, utilities, toilet paper, and shared groceries are paid by whoever happened to be there, and nobody knows who owes whom.  
    *students in shared apartments* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Splitwise, Venmo requests, or letting it slide and resenting it · source: knowledge
1155. **Reselling used textbooks for almost nothing** — Bookstore buyback pays a fraction of the price while other students on campus would pay more, but there's no easy way to match them.  
    *students at term end* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Facebook groups and taped flyers · source: knowledge
1156. **Unexpected university fees on the bill** — Tuition statements include lab fees, tech fees, and health fees the student never saw coming, and it's unclear which can be waived.  
    *students paying their own bills* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: calling the bursar's office and waiting on hold · source: knowledge
1157. **Unused meal plan swipes expire** — Students pay for meal plans, then lose dozens of swipes or dining dollars at the end of each week or semester.  
    *students on mandatory meal plans* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: buying cases of snacks in the last week, or giving swipes away · source: knowledge
1158. **Finding which student discounts apply** — Software, transit, streaming, and local businesses offer student discounts, but students only find out by accident after paying full price.  
    *all students* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: UNiDAYS and word of mouth · source: knowledge

### Studying

1159. **Finding an empty study spot** — During midterms every library seat, study room, and outlet is taken, and students wander between buildings for half an hour.  
    *students on crowded campuses* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: arriving at 7 AM or texting friends to hold a seat · source: knowledge
1160. **No practice problems for this specific course** — Textbook problems don't match what the professor tests, and past exams are hoarded or unavailable.  
    *STEM students before exams* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Chegg, frat test banks, or begging older students · source: knowledge
1161. **Phone distraction during study sessions** — Students sit down to study and lose an hour to TikTok and group chats without noticing.  
    *nearly all students* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Forest app, phone in another room, or nothing · source: knowledge
1162. **Booking a library study room** — Study room reservation systems are clunky, rooms get no-showed and sit empty while others are locked out.  
    *students who study in groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: squatting in rooms and hoping the booker doesn't show · source: knowledge
1163. **Can't find a study partner for a class** — Students in a hard class don't know anyone in it and have no way to find others who want to study together.  
    *students in large lecture courses* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: awkwardly asking neighbors in lecture or posting in a Discord · source: knowledge
1164. **Lecture notes hard to review later** — Handwritten and typed notes from a fast lecture are disorganized, incomplete, and impossible to search when it's time to study.  
    *students in lecture-heavy courses* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: borrowing a friend's notes or rewatching the recorded lecture · source: knowledge
1165. **Making flashcards eats study time** — Turning slides and readings into flashcards takes so long that students run out of time to actually review them.  
    *students in memorization-heavy courses (bio, languages, law)* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Quizlet sets made by strangers that don't match the course · source: knowledge
1166. **Office hours conflict with other classes** — The only time a student can get help from a professor or TA overlaps another class or a shift, so questions go unanswered for weeks.  
    *students with packed schedules* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: long email threads or Piazza posts that get answered late · source: knowledge
1167. **Reading load impossible to plan** — Weekly reading assignments across courses add up to hundreds of pages and students can't judge how long each will take.  
    *humanities and social science students* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: skimming or skipping readings · source: knowledge
1168. **Recorded lectures too long to rewatch** — Reviewing for an exam means scrubbing through hours of 75-minute recordings to find the one part that was confusing.  
    *students in courses with recorded lectures* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: watching at 2x speed · source: knowledge

<a id="patients-caregivers"></a>

## Patients, chronic illness & caregivers

*chronic conditions, medications, appointments, caring for a sick or elderly relative*


### Appointments & scheduling

1169. **Getting to appointments without a car** — An elderly parent cannot drive, the caregiver works, and every appointment turns into a puzzle of who takes time off or how to arrange a ride.  
    *caregivers of non-driving relatives, patients on sedating meds* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: taking unpaid leave, rideshares, volunteer driver programs · source: knowledge
1170. **Phone-only booking during work hours** — The clinic can only be reached by phone between 9 and 4, the hold time is 30 minutes, and the patient is at work and cannot stay on the line.  
    *working patients and caregivers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: calling on lunch break, using speakerphone at the desk · source: knowledge
1171. **Referral status in limbo** — The GP says a referral was sent, the specialist says nothing arrived, and weeks pass with no one accountable for where it is.  
    *patients navigating referrals* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: calling both offices repeatedly · source: knowledge
1172. **Remembering what the doctor said** — Half of the instructions given during the appointment are forgotten by the time the patient gets home, and the after-visit summary is vague or missing.  
    *patients, especially elderly or anxious ones* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bringing a relative to take notes, recording on the phone if allowed · source: knowledge
1173. **Specialist waits stretching months** — The next available appointment is five months out, and there is no way to be notified when an earlier cancellation slot opens up.  
    *anyone waiting on a specialist referral* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling the office every few days asking about cancellations · source: knowledge
1174. **Juggling appointments across many providers** — Rheumatology, physio, bloodwork, dentist and GP all book separately, and the patient ends up with two visits in one week and none the next month, or a clash.  
    *patients with multiple specialists, caregivers managing a parent's care* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a shared Google calendar, a paper diary · source: knowledge
1175. **Pre-appointment prep requirements** — Some tests require fasting, stopping a medication, or bringing specific documents, and the instruction sheet is lost by the time the day arrives.  
    *patients scheduling lab work, scopes, imaging* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: rereading the letter, calling the clinic the day before · source: knowledge
1176. **Recurring lab draws and screenings slipping** — Quarterly thyroid or A1c checks, annual eye exams and repeat scans have no fixed appointment and get forgotten until the doctor asks why it was not done.  
    *patients with conditions requiring periodic monitoring* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: relying on the clinic to call, sticky notes · source: knowledge

### Caregiver coordination
