1177. **Caregiver burnout with no time off** — Full-time caregivers go months without a break because arranging respite care means finding, vetting and briefing someone, which is itself exhausting.  
    *spouses and adult children caring full-time* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: relying on relatives, skipping breaks entirely · source: knowledge
1178. **Handing off to a paid or relief caregiver** — When a home aide or relative takes over for a shift, they do not know what was given, eaten, or observed earlier that day, and details are lost in the handoff.  
    *families using rotating carers or home health aides* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a notebook on the kitchen counter, texts · source: knowledge
1179. **Managing a parent's affairs as proxy** — Acting for a parent means juggling their portal logins, insurance calls where the office will not talk to you, POA paperwork and utility bills, on top of your own life.  
    *adult children with power of attorney or healthcare proxy* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: folders of documents, calling with the parent on the line · source: knowledge
1180. **Managing a parent's meds from far away** — A long-distance caregiver cannot tell whether their parent took today's pills, and the parent insists they did even when the organizer says otherwise.  
    *long-distance caregivers of elderly parents* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: daily phone calls, automatic dispensers, neighbors checking in · source: knowledge
1181. **Siblings out of sync on a parent's care** — One sibling does most of the caregiving while the others ask 'how is Mom' in a group chat, and no one knows who spoke to which doctor or what was decided.  
    *adult children sharing care of an aging parent* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: WhatsApp groups, shared notes, phone-call relays · source: knowledge
1182. **Coordinating friends who want to help** — After a diagnosis, people offer to help but the patient has no energy to organize meals, rides and visits, so offers go unused while needs go unmet.  
    *patients in cancer treatment, post-surgery recovery, new caregivers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: meal-train sites, a friend acting as coordinator · source: knowledge
1183. **Repeating updates to everyone who asks** — After each appointment or hospital day the caregiver retells the same news to a dozen relatives and friends individually, which is draining during a crisis.  
    *caregivers and patients during acute illness* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group texts, CaringBridge-style pages, one designated relay person · source: knowledge
1184. **Tracking home-care agency hours and quality** — Families paying for in-home aides cannot easily verify which shifts were actually worked, who showed up, or what was done, and disputes with the agency follow.  
    *families employing home health aides* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sign-in sheets, cameras, asking the parent · source: knowledge

### Daily living & safety

1185. **Emergency info not reachable in a crisis** — When paramedics arrive, no one can quickly produce the patient's conditions, allergies, medications, DNR status and emergency contacts.  
    *patients with complex conditions, caregivers of elderly* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: fridge sheets, phone medical ID, wallet cards · source: knowledge
1186. **Wandering and getting lost** — A relative with dementia leaves the house unnoticed or gets disoriented on a familiar walk, and the family has no way to locate them quickly.  
    *caregivers of people with dementia* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: door alarms, GPS trackers in shoes, ID bracelets · source: knowledge
1187. **Daily check-ins on a parent living alone** — A caregiver worries every morning whether their elderly parent is up and okay, and a missed phone call triggers a panic or a drive over.  
    *adult children of elderly parents living alone* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: scheduled calls, neighbors, smart speakers, motion sensors · source: knowledge
1188. **Dementia patient asking the same question** — A parent with dementia asks what day it is or when the appointment is thirty times a day, and the caregiver's patience wears thin answering.  
    *caregivers of people with dementia* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: whiteboards, large-print calendars, dementia clocks · source: knowledge
1189. **Fall detection and help when alone** — A person with mobility issues or seizures fears falling when no one is around and being unable to reach a phone to call for help.  
    *elderly living alone, people with epilepsy, POTS, MS* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: pendant alarms, keeping the phone on a lanyard · source: knowledge
1190. **Medical supplies running out at home** — Catheters, ostomy bags, test strips, syringes and dressings run low without warning, and reordering through the durable-supply company takes days.  
    *patients using ongoing medical supplies, home carers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: eyeballing the box, keeping a backup stash · source: knowledge
1191. **Home exercise and physio adherence** — The physiotherapist hands over a sheet of exercises and the patient forgets the sets, does them wrong, or stops after a week with no feedback loop.  
    *patients in physio, cardiac or pulmonary rehab* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper printouts, YouTube videos · source: knowledge
1192. **Reading nutrition info for a restricted diet** — A renal, celiac or low-FODMAP diet means checking every label for potassium, gluten or specific sugars, which makes grocery shopping and eating out slow and stressful.  
    *patients on medically restricted diets and their caregivers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: memorized lists, printed guides, avoiding restaurants · source: knowledge

### Emotional & social

1193. **Doctor dismissing symptoms without documentation** — Patients, particularly women and people with chronic pain, feel dismissed when they cannot show a clear record of how often and how badly symptoms occur.  
    *people with chronic pain, endometriosis, autoimmune conditions* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: bringing a companion, switching doctors, printed logs · source: knowledge
1194. **Explaining an invisible illness to others** — Friends, employers and even family repeatedly ask why you cancelled again or 'still' feel sick, and the patient exhausts themselves re-explaining a condition that does not show.  
    *people with fibromyalgia, lupus, long COVID, mental illness* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: canned messages, withdrawing socially · source: knowledge
1195. **Finding others with the same rare condition** — A newly diagnosed rare-disease patient finds no one nearby who understands, and online groups are scattered across Facebook, Reddit and forums of varying quality.  
    *people with rare or uncommon diagnoses* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups, subreddits, patient advocacy orgs · source: knowledge
1196. **Health anxiety spirals from searching symptoms** — Between appointments the patient searches every new sensation and lands on worst-case diagnoses, which fuels anxiety and sometimes makes symptoms worse.  
    *patients with chronic conditions and health anxiety* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: trying not to google, asking online communities · source: knowledge

### Insurance & costs

1197. **Prior authorization delays and denials** — A medication or scan is prescribed, then insurance requires prior authorization, and the patient waits weeks not knowing whether it was submitted, pending or denied.  
    *patients on specialty drugs, needing imaging or procedures* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: calling insurer and doctor's office repeatedly · source: knowledge
1198. **Copay card and patient-assistance renewals** — Manufacturer copay cards and assistance programs expire yearly or run out of funds mid-year, and the patient discovers it at the counter when the price jumps.  
    *patients on specialty and biologic drugs* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calendar reminders, re-enrolling after the surprise · source: knowledge
1199. **Drug price varies wildly by pharmacy** — The same prescription costs $180 at one pharmacy and $25 with a coupon at another, and the patient only finds out by accident.  
    *uninsured and underinsured patients* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: GoodRx-style coupon sites, asking the pharmacist for cash price · source: knowledge
1200. **Understanding medical bills and EOBs** — A single hospital visit produces multiple bills from the facility, physician group, lab and anesthesiologist, plus insurer EOBs that do not match, and it is unclear what is actually owed.  
    *insured patients in the US* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: spreadsheets, calling billing departments, paying whatever comes · source: knowledge
1201. **FSA/HSA receipts and eligibility** — Receipts for eligible medical expenses are scattered across email and paper, and by year-end the patient has no idea what was already reimbursed or what still qualifies.  
    *people with FSA or HSA accounts* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a shoebox of receipts, photos in camera roll · source: knowledge
1202. **Tracking deductible and out-of-pocket progress** — Patients cannot tell how close they are to the deductible or out-of-pocket max, so they cannot plan whether to schedule a procedure this year or next.  
    *patients with high-deductible plans* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: insurer portal that lags weeks behind, spreadsheets · source: knowledge

### Medication management

1203. **Checking interactions across prescribers** — The cardiologist and the psychiatrist each prescribe without knowing what the other added, and nobody reviews the full list for interactions until a pharmacist happens to flag it.  
    *patients with multiple specialists, elderly on many meds* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking the pharmacist, googling each pair · source: knowledge
1204. **Controlled-substance refill timing window** — ADHD stimulants and pain meds can only be refilled within a narrow window and require a fresh prescription each time, so missing the day means going without.  
    *patients on Schedule II medications* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: marking the earliest fill date on a calendar and calling the office in advance · source: knowledge
1205. **Forgetting whether a pill was taken** — Twenty minutes after the usual dose time you cannot remember if you actually swallowed the pill or just thought about it, and double-dosing or skipping both have consequences.  
    *people on daily maintenance meds, especially memory-affecting conditions* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: weekly pill organizers, checking the blister pack, guessing · source: knowledge
1206. **Pharmacy has the drug out of stock** — You arrive to pick up a prescription and the pharmacy is out of that medication; you then call pharmacy after pharmacy to find one that has it in stock.  
    *patients on drugs in shortage (stimulants, GLP-1s, some antibiotics)* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling around, asking the pharmacist to check nearby stores · source: knowledge
1207. **Refill request stuck between doctor and pharmacy** — The pharmacy says it faxed the doctor, the doctor's office says nothing arrived, and the patient is the one making phone calls while the bottle is empty.  
    *patients whose prescriptions need periodic renewal* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling both sides repeatedly, asking for an emergency 3-day supply · source: knowledge
1208. **Tapering and titration schedules** — Doctors write 'take 2 tablets for 5 days, then 1.5 for 5 days, then 1...' and the patient has to compute today's dose every morning and remember where they are in the taper.  
    *patients tapering steroids, antidepressants, benzodiazepines, or titrating new meds* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper chart on the fridge, counting days from the start date · source: knowledge
1209. **Keeping an up-to-date medication list** — Every new doctor asks for a full list of current medications and doses, and the patient recites from memory or brings a bag of bottles, often missing recent changes.  
    *patients seeing multiple specialists, caregivers of elderly parents* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a folded paper list in the wallet, photos of bottles · source: knowledge
1210. **Refills running out on different days** — Each of six prescriptions comes due on a different date, so there is a pharmacy trip almost every week and something is always about to run out.  
    *patients on multiple long-term prescriptions* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: asking the pharmacy to sync refills, calendar reminders, keeping a spare bottle · source: knowledge
1211. **Timing meds around food and each other** — One pill must be taken on an empty stomach, another with food, a third two hours away from calcium, and the schedule falls apart on any non-routine day.  
    *patients with complex regimens (thyroid, osteoporosis, HIV, transplant)* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: handwritten schedules, phone alarms for each pill · source: knowledge
1212. **Travelling with medication supplies** — Before a trip you have to count out enough of each pill, figure out time-zone dose shifts, and make sure refrigerated drugs like insulin stay cold.  
    *travellers with chronic conditions* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: counting into baggies, cooler packs, guessing at time-zone timing · source: knowledge
1213. **Not knowing what a pill is** — A loose tablet is found in a pill organizer or on the floor and no one can tell which medication it is from the imprint and color.  
    *caregivers filling organizers, patients with many similar pills* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: searching pill identifier websites, calling the pharmacist · source: knowledge

### Records & paperwork

1214. **Disability paperwork and work accommodations** — Applying for disability benefits or accommodations means gathering years of records, doctor letters and forms with deadlines, all while too sick to manage it.  
    *people with disabling chronic illness* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: advocates, lawyers, family members doing the paperwork · source: knowledge
1215. **Imaging and records transfer between providers** — Getting an MRI or old records to a new specialist means faxes, mailed CDs, or driving a disc across town, and often the images do not arrive in time for the visit.  
    *patients switching or adding providers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: requesting CDs and carrying them, signing release forms repeatedly · source: knowledge
1216. **One patient portal per health system** — Each hospital and clinic has its own portal login, so lab results, messages and visit notes are scattered across five apps with five passwords.  
    *patients seen at multiple health systems* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: password manager, screenshots, printing everything · source: knowledge
1217. **Results released before the doctor explains** — Portals push raw lab and imaging results to the patient instantly, often on a Friday night, with alarming flagged values and no explanation until the appointment days later.  
    *patients with portal access* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: googling the terms, anxious waiting · source: knowledge
1218. **Tracking the story of a long diagnosis** — Over years of an undiagnosed condition the patient loses track of which tests were done, what was ruled out and which doctor said what.  
    *people with rare or hard-to-diagnose conditions* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: binders of printouts, long Word documents · source: knowledge
1219. **Lab results without trends or context** — Results arrive as a single number with a reference range, with no easy way to see whether it has been climbing for two years or what the previous values were.  
    *patients monitoring chronic markers (A1c, creatinine, TSH, cholesterol)* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually charting in a spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1220. **Re-telling the medical history every time** — Every new provider intake asks for the same surgeries, diagnoses, family history and dates, and the patient rewrites it on a clipboard from memory each time.  
    *patients with long histories, caregivers filling forms for a parent* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a typed summary carried in a folder · source: knowledge

### Symptom & condition tracking

1221. **Explaining symptoms from memory at appointments** — The doctor asks how often the migraines came and how bad they were over the last three months, and the patient can only say 'a lot, some were bad'.  
    *people with migraines, IBS, pain conditions, autoimmune flares* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app, paper diaries that get abandoned after two weeks · source: knowledge
1222. **Identifying flare triggers** — Symptoms flare unpredictably and the patient suspects food, sleep, stress or weather but cannot correlate them without months of consistent data.  
    *people with migraine, IBS, eczema, Crohn's, fibromyalgia* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: elimination diets, gut feeling, spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1223. **Insulin dosing math at every meal** — Counting carbs, applying a ratio, adjusting for current glucose and remaining active insulin has to be done before every meal, and mistakes cause highs or dangerous lows.  
    *people with type 1 diabetes and parents of diabetic children* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: pump bolus calculators, mental arithmetic, carb-counting books · source: knowledge
1224. **Pacing energy to avoid crashes** — Overdoing it on a good day causes a multi-day crash, but there is no easy way to see how much activity is 'too much' before it is too late.  
    *people with ME/CFS, long COVID, POTS, MS* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: spoon theory budgeting in the head, heart-rate monitors · source: knowledge
1225. **Bowel and bladder diaries on paper** — Specialists ask for a two-week diary of bathroom frequency and characteristics, which is awkward and easily forgotten on paper.  
    *IBD, IBS, urology and pelvic-floor patients* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper sheets from the clinic, notes app · source: knowledge
1226. **Fluid and sodium intake tracking** — Patients told to limit fluids or salt, or to hit a daily water target, lose count by mid-afternoon and have no idea whether they are over or under.  
    *kidney, heart failure, POTS and dialysis patients* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: measuring jugs, tally marks · source: knowledge
1227. **Logging vitals across several devices** — Blood pressure, glucose, weight and oxygen readings each live in a different device or app, and the doctor wants them together on one sheet.  
    *patients with hypertension, diabetes, heart failure* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: handwriting readings in a notebook, photographing the device screen · source: knowledge
1228. **Remembering side effects of a new drug** — A new medication is started and weeks later the patient cannot recall when the dizziness began or whether it started before or after the dose increase.  
    *patients starting or changing medications* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none, or scattered notes · source: knowledge
1229. **Tracking too tiring on bad days** — The days when a symptom log matters most are the days the patient is too exhausted or in too much pain to fill out a detailed form, so the data has gaps exactly where it counts.  
    *people with ME/CFS, long COVID, chronic pain, depression* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: backfilling from memory, giving up on tracking · source: knowledge

<a id="accessibility-neurodivergent"></a>

## Accessibility & neurodivergence

*ADHD, autism, dyslexia, blindness, deafness, mobility, executive function*


### ADHD & executive function

1230. **Task initiation paralysis on simple chores** — Knowing exactly what needs doing but being unable to start, even a two-minute task, and feeling shame that grows the longer it sits.  
    *adults with ADHD, autistic adults, people with depression* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Body doubling over video, bargaining with oneself, waiting for a deadline panic · source: knowledge
1231. **Breaking big tasks into steps** — A project like 'sort out taxes' has no obvious first step, so it stays on the list for months while the deadline creeps closer.  
    *adults with ADHD and executive-function difficulties* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Asking a friend to talk it through, AI chat prompts · source: knowledge
1232. **Controlled-substance refill timing chaos** — Stimulant prescriptions cannot be refilled early, pharmacies are frequently out of stock, and tracking the earliest refill date and which pharmacy has supply becomes a monthly scramble.  
    *adults and parents managing ADHD medication* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Calling pharmacies one by one, calendar notes, Reddit shortage threads · source: knowledge
1233. **Emotional dysregulation after minor setbacks** — A small piece of criticism or a perceived rejection triggers an outsized emotional crash that wipes out the rest of the day's plans.  
    *adults with ADHD (rejection sensitivity)* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: Therapy skills, venting to friends, avoidance · source: knowledge
1234. **Forgetting whether medication was taken** — Twenty minutes after your morning routine you cannot remember whether you took your stimulant, and doubling up or skipping both have real consequences.  
    *people on daily ADHD or psychiatric medication* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Pill organizers, photographing the bottle, counting remaining pills · source: knowledge
1235. **Reminders that get dismissed and forgotten** — A phone reminder fires while you are mid-task, you swipe it away intending to act later, and it is gone from your mind within seconds.  
    *adults with ADHD* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Setting five duplicate alarms or leaving the reminder unread on purpose · source: knowledge
1236. **Time blindness before appointments** — You cannot feel how much time is left before you need to leave, so you are either 40 minutes early or 20 minutes late, with nothing in between.  
    *people with ADHD* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Multiple countdown alarms, visual timers, asking a partner to nudge · source: knowledge
1237. **Transition between tasks feels impossible** — Stopping one activity to begin another, even a pleasant one, produces disproportionate resistance and the change gets delayed for hours.  
    *autistic adults and people with ADHD* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Advance warnings, rigid schedules, timers with escalating alerts · source: knowledge
1238. **Abandoned to-do apps graveyard** — Every task app works for two weeks, then the novelty fades, the list becomes a wall of overdue red, and opening it feels like being scolded.  
    *adults with ADHD* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Switching apps, paper lists, sticky notes on the monitor · source: knowledge
1239. **Doom piles and doom boxes** — Miscellaneous items get dumped in a pile or box 'to deal with later', and the pile grows until sorting it becomes overwhelming.  
    *people with ADHD* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Periodic purges, moving the box to another room · source: knowledge
1240. **Finding a body double on demand** — Working alongside another person helps you focus, but there is nobody available right when you need to start, and scheduling a session is itself a task.  
    *remote workers and students with ADHD* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Discord study rooms, Focusmate, calling a friend · source: knowledge
1241. **Hyperfocus erases meals and breaks** — You look up from a task and six hours have passed without eating, drinking, or moving, and you now feel awful.  
    *people with ADHD and autistic adults* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Interval alarms that get ignored · source: knowledge
1242. **Out of sight, out of mind items** — Anything put in a drawer, cupboard or folder effectively ceases to exist, leading to duplicate purchases, expired food and missed paperwork.  
    *people with ADHD* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Clear bins, leaving everything on surfaces, labeling · source: knowledge
1243. **Reading long emails and losing the ask** — A dense email or Slack thread contains one actual request buried in paragraphs, and you skim, miss it, and get chased later.  
    *workers with ADHD or dyslexia* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Asking the sender to bullet it, re-reading three times · source: knowledge

### Autism & sensory

1244. **Going nonverbal with no quick way to communicate** — During shutdown or overload speech becomes unavailable, and there is no fast way to tell people around you what is happening or what you need.  
    *autistic people with intermittent speech* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pre-written cards, typing in Notes and showing the screen · source: knowledge
1245. **Masking exhaustion goes untracked** — After a day of performing neurotypical behavior you crash, but the link between specific social loads and the burnout is invisible until it is too late.  
    *autistic adults, especially late-diagnosed* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Energy accounting ('spoons'), cancelling plans reactively · source: knowledge
1246. **Phone calls required for basic services** — Doctors, landlords and utilities insist on phone calls, and the unpredictability of live conversation causes hours of dread and postponed tasks.  
    *autistic adults, people with anxiety, deaf and hard-of-hearing people* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Scripting the call, asking a family member to call, avoiding the task · source: knowledge
1247. **Safe foods running out** — A narrow set of tolerated foods means a discontinued product, recipe change or empty shelf can leave nothing acceptable to eat.  
    *autistic people and those with ARFID* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Stockpiling, checking multiple stores, eating nothing · source: knowledge
1248. **Sensory overload in shops and transit** — Fluorescent lights, background music, crowds and announcements stack up until you have to leave a supermarket mid-shop or miss your stop.  
    *autistic adults, people with sensory processing differences* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Noise-cancelling headphones, sunglasses, shopping at odd hours · source: knowledge
1249. **Unexpected plan changes derail the day** — A cancelled appointment or a friend arriving late throws off a carefully structured day and the fallout can last hours.  
    *autistic adults and children* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Backup plans, over-communicating, avoiding plans with unreliable people · source: knowledge
1250. **Unknown sensory environment of new venues** — Before visiting a restaurant, clinic or venue you cannot find out how loud, bright or crowded it is, so you either avoid it or risk a meltdown.  
    *autistic people and parents of autistic children* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Phoning ahead, checking photos, arriving at opening time · source: knowledge
1251. **Decoding tone in text messages** — A short reply like 'ok.' or 'fine' can be read as anger or dismissal, leading to hours of rumination about whether someone is upset.  
    *autistic adults, people with rejection sensitivity* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Asking directly, screenshotting to friends for a second opinion, tone indicators · source: knowledge
1252. **Interoception blind spots** — Not noticing hunger, thirst, tiredness or needing the toilet until it becomes urgent or turns into a headache and irritability.  
    *autistic adults, people with ADHD* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Scheduled eating and drinking, tracking apps · source: knowledge
1253. **Unwritten social scripts for common situations** — Interactions like checking into a hotel, small talk with a barber or declining an invitation have unstated expectations that must be worked out from scratch each time.  
    *autistic adults* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Memorized scripts, rehearsing, watching how others do it · source: knowledge
1254. **Visual schedules that don't update** — Printed picture schedules for a child fall out of date the moment plans change, and remaking them with laminated cards is slow.  
    *parents and teachers of autistic children* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Velcro card boards, whiteboards, printing new cards · source: knowledge

### Blind & low vision

1255. **Touchscreen appliances and kiosks** — Flat touchscreen microwaves, washing machines, self-checkouts and ticket kiosks give no tactile or audio feedback, so they cannot be operated independently.  
    *blind and low-vision people* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Tactile bump stickers, memorizing button sequences, asking staff · source: knowledge
1256. **Unlabeled buttons in apps** — Screen readers announce 'button, button, button' because icons have no labels, making core features of mainstream apps unusable.  
    *blind and low-vision smartphone users* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Guessing by position, asking sighted help, emailing developers · source: knowledge
1257. **Finding the entrance and the right door** — GPS gets you to the address but not to the actual door, and once inside there is no way to find the reception desk, the right room or the toilet.  
    *blind and low-vision people navigating alone* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Asking passers-by, remote sighted assistance apps, arriving early · source: knowledge
1258. **Identifying cans and packages in the kitchen** — Cans, boxes and jars feel identical, and printed labels and cooking instructions are unreadable without sighted assistance.  
    *blind and low-vision people cooking at home* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Braille or rubber-band labeling systems, barcode scanning apps, memorizing shelf positions · source: knowledge
1259. **Inaccessible CAPTCHAs block sign-ups** — Image-based CAPTCHAs have no working audio alternative or the audio is unintelligible, so you cannot create accounts or log in.  
    *blind users and people with cognitive disabilities* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Asking sighted help, browser extensions, abandoning the site · source: knowledge
1260. **Low-contrast text and thin fonts** — Light grey text on white and hairline fonts are unreadable even with zoom, and zooming breaks layouts so buttons disappear off screen.  
    *low-vision and older users* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: High-contrast modes, browser zoom, reader view, custom CSS · source: knowledge
1261. **Which bus is this at the stop** — Several buses arrive at once and there is no audio announcement of the route number, so you may board the wrong one or miss yours.  
    *blind and low-vision transit riders* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Asking the driver, asking others at the stop, live vehicle tracking apps · source: knowledge
1262. **Images and memes with no alt text** — Friends share screenshots and memes in group chats and social feeds that arrive as silent 'image' to a screen reader, leaving you out of the conversation.  
    *blind and low-vision social media users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Asking what the picture is, AI image description tools · source: knowledge
1263. **Reading mail and bills that arrive on paper** — Letters, bills and official notices come in print and must be photographed and OCR'd one page at a time, or wait for a sighted person.  
    *blind and low-vision adults* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Phone camera OCR apps, family reading it, requesting large print or email · source: knowledge
1264. **Matching clothes and checking for stains** — Choosing an outfit that matches and is clean requires colour and stain information that is not available by touch.  
    *blind and colour-blind people* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Colour identifier apps, buying only neutral colours, labeling hangers · source: knowledge

### Cross-cutting & caregivers

1265. **Coordinating a child's therapy schedule** — Speech, occupational and behavioral therapy sessions, school meetings and specialist appointments overlap and change, with paperwork from each provider.  
    *parents of autistic or disabled children* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Shared calendars, binders, spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1266. **Requesting accommodations is unclear** — Working out what you are entitled to at work or university, how to word the request and what documentation is needed takes weeks of research and emails.  
    *neurodivergent and disabled students and employees* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Advocacy groups, templates from forums, asking others who have done it · source: knowledge
1267. **Autoplay and flashing content triggers** — Autoplaying videos, animated ads and flashing effects cause distraction, migraines or seizures, and per-site settings do not persist.  
    *people with ADHD, photosensitive epilepsy, vestibular disorders* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Browser extensions, reduced-motion settings, avoiding sites · source: knowledge
1268. **Explaining a condition to every new person** — Each new doctor, teacher or manager needs the same explanation of how your condition affects you and what helps, and it is exhausting to repeat.  
    *neurodivergent and disabled people, parents of disabled children* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Prepared 'about me' documents, hospital passports, memorized speech · source: knowledge
1269. **Keeping routines during travel** — Hotels, time zones and unfamiliar shops break carefully built medication, meal and sleep routines, and the disruption takes days to recover from.  
    *autistic people, people with ADHD, people on strict medication schedules* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Packing lists, bringing safe foods, extra alarms · source: knowledge
1270. **Screen-reader friendliness of a site unknown** — Before spending time on a new shop or service you cannot tell whether checkout will work with assistive tech, and you often find out at the last step.  
    *screen reader and keyboard-only users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Community recommendations, trial and error, sticking to known sites · source: knowledge
1271. **Waiting-room and queue uncertainty** — Sitting in an unpredictable waiting room with no idea how long it will be is distressing, and stepping out to regulate risks losing your place.  
    *autistic people, people with anxiety, people with ADHD* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Asking reception repeatedly, waiting in the car, headphones · source: knowledge

### Deaf & hard of hearing

1272. **Businesses that only take phone bookings** — Clinics, restaurants and tradespeople with no online booking or text option are effectively closed to you, or require a relay service that many hang up on.  
    *deaf people and non-speaking people* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Relay services, asking a hearing friend, choosing only businesses with online booking · source: knowledge
1273. **Group conversations in noisy restaurants** — Following several people talking over background noise is impossible, so you nod along and miss the plans being made.  
    *deaf and hard-of-hearing people, people with auditory processing disorder* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Live transcription apps, sitting with your back to the wall, leaving early · source: knowledge
1274. **Missing doorbells, alarms and announcements** — Deliveries are missed because the doorbell is not heard, and airport or train platform changes are announced only by loudspeaker.  
    *deaf and hard-of-hearing people* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Flashing-light doorbells, watching departure boards constantly, asking staff · source: knowledge
1275. **Video calls with fast-talking colleagues** — Meeting captions lag, speakers talk over each other, and lipreading fails when cameras are off, so you leave meetings unsure what was decided.  
    *deaf and hard-of-hearing remote workers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Asking for written summaries, recording and re-watching, live captioners · source: knowledge
1276. **Videos and live streams without captions** — Social media videos, online courses and workplace recordings often have no captions or auto-captions so wrong they are useless.  
    *deaf and hard-of-hearing people* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Requesting transcripts, third-party captioning tools, skipping the content · source: knowledge
1277. **Drive-throughs and intercoms** — Ordering at drive-throughs, buzzing into apartment buildings and speaking to parking-garage intercoms all depend on hearing a crackly speaker.  
    *deaf and hard-of-hearing people* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Going inside, pre-written cards, mobile ordering apps · source: knowledge
1278. **Hearing-aid battery and connectivity failures** — Rechargeable hearing aids die mid-day, Bluetooth pairing drops, and there is no warning until conversation suddenly vanishes.  
    *hearing-aid and cochlear-implant users* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Carrying spare batteries, restarting the phone, manufacturer apps · source: knowledge

### Dyslexia & reading

1279. **Mixing up similar-looking numbers** — Transposing digits in phone numbers, bank details or dates leads to failed payments and missed appointments, and the error is hard to spot on review.  
    *people with dyscalculia or dyslexia* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Copy-paste only, reading numbers aloud, asking someone to double check · source: knowledge
1280. **Reading aloud in meetings or class** — Being asked to read a passage aloud on the spot causes panic and stumbling, and there is no discreet way to preview or get support.  
    *students and employees with dyslexia* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Pre-reading, declining, disclosing the disability · source: knowledge
1281. **Reading on screens with dense text** — Long paragraphs in small fonts on white backgrounds cause letters to swim and lines to be re-read, so articles and documentation take three times longer.  
    *people with dyslexia and visual stress* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Browser reader mode, overlays, text-to-speech, giving up · source: knowledge
1282. **Scanned PDFs that can't be read aloud** — Work and school documents arrive as image-only PDFs that text-to-speech tools cannot read, so the content is locked away.  
    *students and workers with dyslexia, blind and low-vision users* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Running OCR manually, asking for an accessible version, skipping the reading · source: knowledge
1283. **Paper forms at clinics and offices** — Handwritten forms in waiting rooms must be filled in under time pressure with no spellcheck or dictation, causing embarrassment and errors.  
    *people with dyslexia or dysgraphia, people with tremor* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Bringing a companion, asking for the form in advance, leaving fields blank · source: knowledge
1284. **Spellcheck can't guess dyslexic spellings** — When a word is spelled phonetically or with letters transposed, standard spellcheck offers no useful suggestion, and searching for the word also fails.  
    *people with dyslexia* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Voice dictation, asking someone, describing the word to a search engine · source: knowledge

### Mobility & motor

1285. **Booking wheelchair-accessible taxis** — Ride-hailing apps rarely have accessible vehicles available, wait times are far longer, and drivers sometimes cancel on seeing a wheelchair.  
    *wheelchair users* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Booking hours ahead, local taxi firms, paratransit schemes · source: knowledge
1286. **Step-free route unknown before travel** — Map apps route you via stairs, broken lifts and kerbs without dropped curbs, and you only discover it when you are stuck in front of them.  
    *wheelchair users, people with mobility impairments, parents with prams* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Street View scouting, phoning venues, transit lift-status pages · source: knowledge
1287. **Accessible toilet location and access** — Finding an accessible toilet nearby, knowing whether it is locked, needs a key, or is being used as storage, is a constant unknown when out.  
    *wheelchair users and people with continence conditions* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Radar key schemes, crowd-sourced toilet maps, limiting fluid intake · source: knowledge
1288. **Blocked accessible parking and ramps** — Disabled bays are taken by non-permit cars and ramps are blocked by bins or delivery vans, and reporting it is slow or pointless.  
    *disabled drivers and wheelchair users* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Driving around, confronting drivers, reporting to the council · source: knowledge
1289. **Energy budgeting on bad pain days** — Not knowing how much energy tomorrow will hold means over-committing and then cancelling everything, or under-committing and losing days.  
    *people with chronic pain, fatigue or mobility conditions* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Spoon theory tracking, pacing diaries, keeping days empty · source: knowledge
1290. **Tiny touch targets and precise gestures** — Small buttons, swipe-to-delete gestures and drag-and-drop are error-prone with tremor or limited dexterity, leading to accidental taps and sends.  
    *people with tremor, arthritis, cerebral palsy or motor impairments* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Styluses, accessibility touch settings, voice control, asking someone · source: knowledge
1291. **Venue 'accessible' claims that are wrong** — Restaurants and hotels say they are accessible, but there is a step at the door, the lift is tiny or the accessible room has a bathtub.  
    *wheelchair users and people with limited mobility* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Calling to ask specific questions, asking for photos, relying on word of mouth · source: knowledge
1292. **Voice control fails in web forms** — Dictation and voice navigation work in some fields but not others, so a form has to be abandoned halfway or completed by someone else.  
    *people with limited hand use who rely on voice input* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Switching devices, browser extensions, asking for assistance · source: knowledge

<a id="hobbies-crafts"></a>

## Hobbies & crafts

*gaming, music, crafts, gardening, DIY, reading, board games, collecting*


### Board & tabletop games

1293. **Finding a consistent D&D session time** — The most common reason campaigns die is that four to six adults cannot find a recurring slot, and every session requires renegotiation.  
    *TTRPG players and game masters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: When2meet, Discord polls, giving up · source: knowledge
1294. **Running a tabletop RPG campaign's notes** — Game masters juggle NPC names, plot threads, maps and player backstories across notebooks and Google Docs, and players forget what happened last session.  
    *Dungeons & Dragons and other TTRPG groups* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Obsidian vaults, World Anvil, binders · source: knowledge
1295. **Board game night scheduling and headcount** — Game night depends on knowing exactly how many people are coming, because most games have hard player counts, and last-minute drops leave a five-player game with three.  
    *Board game hosts and groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Group chats, Doodle, guessing · source: knowledge
1296. **Missing pieces discovered mid-game** — A game is set up before anyone realizes a card or token is missing, and finding the publisher's replacement-parts form takes ages.  
    *Board game owners, especially secondhand buyers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Improvising with coins, publisher parts requests · source: knowledge
1297. **Re-teaching rules after months** — Nobody remembers the rules of a game played once last year, and reading the rulebook aloud kills thirty minutes of the evening.  
    *Casual board game groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: YouTube how-to-play videos, player-aid cards · source: knowledge
1298. **Choosing a game for the group tonight** — With a shelf of fifty games, hosts struggle to pick one that fits the player count, time available and the mix of newbies and veterans present.  
    *Board game collectors hosting groups* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: BoardGameGeek collection filters, arguing · source: knowledge
1299. **Scorekeeping for complex games** — End-game scoring in heavy euros involves many categories and errors are common, and score sheets run out or get lost.  
    *Board gamers playing strategy games* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Paper pads, phone calculators, official companion apps · source: knowledge
1300. **Tracking who won which games over time** — Groups want to know play counts, win rates and streaks for bragging rights but have no record of past plays.  
    *Regular board game groups* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: BG Stats app, spreadsheets, memory · source: knowledge

### Collecting

1301. **Building and tracking a deck for card games** — Players of Magic, Pokemon or Lorcana lose track of which cards are in which deck and which are loose, so building a new deck means dismantling another.  
    *Trading card game players* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Moxfield, Archidekt, deck boxes with labels · source: knowledge
1302. **Buying a duplicate at a shop or show** — At a convention, record store or flea market, collectors cannot remember whether they already own a particular issue, pressing or variant and buy it again.  
    *Comic, vinyl, trading card and coin collectors* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Photos on phone, want lists on paper, collection apps · source: knowledge
1303. **Cataloguing a large collection takes forever** — Entering hundreds of items one by one into a database is so tedious that most collectors never finish, leaving them with no record for insurance.  
    *Collectors with large accumulations* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Barcode-scanning apps, spreadsheets, giving up · source: knowledge
1304. **Not knowing a collection's total value** — Collectors of cards, coins, vinyl or figures have no idea what their collection is worth for insurance or sale, and prices shift constantly.  
    *Hobby collectors of any kind* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheets with manual price lookups, Discogs, TCGplayer · source: knowledge
1305. **Remembering paint recipes for miniatures** — Painters cannot reproduce a color scheme on the next batch of miniatures months later because the layered paint recipe was never written down.  
    *Miniature painters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Notebooks, photos, paint-recipe apps · source: knowledge
1306. **Storing and finding physical items later** — Collectors know they own an item but cannot find which box, binder or shelf it is in without emptying the closet.  
    *Collectors who store items in bins and binders* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Labels, memory, photos of box contents · source: knowledge
1307. **Tracking a want list across marketplaces** — The specific card or figure a collector needs shows up on eBay, Mercari or a forum at a good price and is gone before they see it.  
    *Collectors hunting specific items* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: eBay saved searches, checking multiple sites daily · source: knowledge
1308. **Tracking model kit and miniature backlog** — Miniature painters and model builders accumulate a 'pile of shame' of unbuilt kits and forget which paints they need for which project.  
    *Warhammer painters, Gundam and scale model builders* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Spreadsheets, photos, guilt · source: knowledge

### DIY & workshop

1309. **Forgetting paint colors and finishes used** — Touching up a wall years later fails because nobody remembers the exact paint brand, color code and sheen, and the leftover can has dried out.  
    *Homeowners and renters who paint* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Writing on the can, photos of the lid, guessing at the store · source: knowledge
1310. **Half-finished DIY projects around the house** — A weekend project stalls for months because the next step needs one part or one dry day, and by then the plan and measurements are forgotten.  
    *Homeowners doing their own repairs and builds* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Notes app, nagging from partner · source: knowledge
1311. **Multiple trips to the hardware store** — DIYers go to the store three times per project because the cut list or fastener count was estimated from memory and something was missed.  
    *Weekend woodworkers and home repairers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Handwritten lists, buying extra of everything · source: knowledge
1312. **Optimizing board cuts to reduce waste** — Figuring out how to cut a plywood sheet or lumber into needed pieces with minimal offcuts is error-prone on paper, and mistakes waste expensive material.  
    *Hobby woodworkers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Graph paper, online cut optimizers · source: knowledge
1313. **Tracking 3D printer filament and settings** — 3D printing hobbyists forget which filament spool has how much left and which temperature settings worked for it, leading to failed prints and mid-print run-outs.  
    *Hobby 3D printer owners* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheets, weighing spools, notes on the spool · source: knowledge
1314. **Saving project inspiration that is findable later** — Ideas from Pinterest, YouTube and Instagram get saved in five places, and when it is time to build the bookshelf nobody can find the video with the joinery detail.  
    *DIYers and makers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pinterest boards, browser bookmarks, screenshots · source: knowledge
1315. **Tool inventory and lending loss** — Tools lent to neighbors or friends never come back, and owners cannot remember who has the drill or the ladder.  
    *Homeowners with a workshop* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Memory, asking around · source: knowledge

### Fiber & paper crafts

1316. **Losing your place in a knitting pattern** — Knitters and crocheters lose count of rows and repeats when interrupted, then have to rip back hours of work after discovering the mistake.  
    *Knitters and crocheters* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Row counters, tally marks, sticky notes on the pattern · source: knowledge
1317. **Calculating fabric or yarn needed for a size** — Adjusting a pattern for a different size or gauge requires math many crafters get wrong, leading to running out of material or wasted purchases.  
    *Sewists, quilters, knitters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calculator and scrap paper, buying extra · source: knowledge
1318. **Craft supply hoard with unknown contents** — Beads, paper, paint, fabric scraps and tools fill drawers, and people rebuy items they already own because nothing is catalogued or findable.  
    *Scrapbookers, card makers, mixed-media crafters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Clear bins, labels, occasional purges · source: knowledge
1319. **Cross-stitch chart tracking on paper** — Following a large multi-color chart on paper means highlighting stitched areas by hand and losing the place when the printout gets folded or lost.  
    *Cross-stitchers and embroiderers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Highlighter pens, Pattern Keeper app, magnet boards · source: knowledge
1320. **Pricing handmade items for sale** — Makers who sell at craft fairs or Etsy underprice because they do not track material cost and hours per item and feel awkward charging for labor.  
    *Hobbyist makers who sell occasionally* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Rules of thumb, spreadsheets, guessing · source: knowledge
1321. **Too many works in progress abandoned** — Half-finished projects pile up in bags, and crafters forget which needles, yarn and pattern belong to which one, so nothing ever gets completed.  
    *Knitters, quilters, embroiderers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Project bags, Ravelry project pages, guilt · source: knowledge
1322. **Yarn stash nobody can inventory** — Crafters forget what yarn they own, in what weight and yardage, and buy duplicates or run short mid-project because they cannot check the stash from the store.  
    *Knitters, crocheters, weavers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ravelry stash pages, spreadsheets, bins with labels · source: knowledge

### Gardening

1323. **Frost warning missed overnight** — A surprise late frost kills seedlings because weather apps do not warn at the garden's microclimate level or in time to cover plants.  
    *Gardeners in temperate climates* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Checking forecasts nightly in spring, row covers · source: knowledge
1324. **Missing the sowing window for vegetables** — Gardeners realize in June they should have started tomatoes indoors in March, because seed timing depends on local frost dates that packets do not know.  
    *Home vegetable gardeners* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Almanac charts, seed packet math, spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1325. **Forgetting what was planted where** — By midsummer the plant labels have faded or blown away and gardeners cannot tell which tomato variety is which, so they cannot repeat successes next year.  
    *Vegetable and flower gardeners* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Paper garden journals, photos, plastic labels · source: knowledge
1326. **Forgetting when to water each plant** — Houseplant owners over- or under-water because every plant has different needs and they cannot remember when they last watered which one.  
    *Houseplant owners with more than a handful of plants* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Finger test, reminder apps like Planta, watering on a fixed day · source: knowledge
1327. **Glut of harvest with nowhere to go** — Zucchini and tomatoes all ripen at once and gardeners cannot use, preserve or give away enough before it rots.  
    *Vegetable gardeners with productive plots* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Leaving bags on neighbors' porches, canning marathons · source: knowledge
1328. **Identifying a pest or disease on a plant** — Yellow leaves or spots could be dozens of things, and searching images gives contradictory advice while the plant declines.  
    *Home gardeners and houseplant owners* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups, extension office, guessing · source: knowledge
1329. **Planning crop rotation between seasons** — Rotating plant families across beds each year to avoid disease requires remembering last year's layout, which nobody wrote down.  
    *Raised-bed and allotment gardeners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Graph paper sketches, memory · source: knowledge
1330. **Seed packets expiring unnoticed** — Half-used seed packets accumulate for years and gardeners sow old seed that fails to germinate, wasting a season's slot.  
    *Vegetable gardeners* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Shoebox sorting, germination tests · source: knowledge

### Hobby time & motivation

1331. **Finding a local hobby group or club** — Newcomers to a hobby or a city cannot easily find the local knitting circle, board game meetup or garden club, and Meetup listings are often dead.  
    *People new to a hobby or area* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups, library notice boards, asking at shops · source: knowledge
1332. **Hobby spending creeping out of control** — Small purchases of yarn, games, plants or supplies add up unnoticed, and people are shocked at the total when they finally look at the bank statement.  
    *Hobbyists in gear-heavy hobbies* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Budgeting apps, self-imposed 'no buy' months · source: knowledge
1333. **Balancing hobby time across several hobbies** — People with multiple hobbies feel they neglect all of them, cannot tell how much time each got this month, and lose momentum in the ones they skipped.  
    *Adults juggling several hobbies with work and family* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Calendar blocks, habit trackers · source: knowledge
1334. **Documenting progress to see improvement** — Hobbyists cannot see how much better their painting, playing or crafting has gotten because early work is not photographed or dated, so they feel stuck.  
    *Anyone learning a skill-based hobby* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Camera roll, Instagram accounts · source: knowledge

### Music practice

1335. **Finding accurate chords or tabs for a song** — Tab and chord sites are full of wrong versions, transposed keys, popups and paywalls, so players waste time verifying by ear which version is right.  
    *Guitarists, ukulele and piano players learning songs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Comparing multiple versions on Ultimate Guitar; slowing down YouTube · source: knowledge
1336. **Finding people to jam with nearby** — Hobby musicians struggle to find others of similar skill, taste and schedule in their area, and Craigslist-style ads attract flakes.  
    *Amateur musicians without a band* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups, music store bulletin boards, open mics · source: knowledge
1337. **Losing a practice streak and quitting** — Practice logs live in a notebook or memory, so progress is invisible and motivation collapses after a busy week with no sessions.  
    *Adult beginners on any instrument* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Habit-tracker apps, paper calendars · source: knowledge
1338. **Managing a band's shared setlist and lyrics** — Band members keep different versions of the setlist, keys and lyrics on paper and in group chats, leading to on-stage confusion about which key or arrangement was agreed.  
    *Amateur bands and church worship teams* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Docs, printed binders, WhatsApp threads · source: knowledge
1339. **Not knowing what to practice today** — Amateur musicians sit down with the instrument and noodle through the same songs for twenty minutes without structure, then feel they did not improve.  
    *Self-taught guitar, piano and other instrument learners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: YouTube lessons, paper practice plans, teachers · source: knowledge
1340. **Organizing hundreds of PDF sheet music files** — Sheet music is scattered across downloads folders, email attachments and photos, with no way to find a piece by composer, key or difficulty when it is needed.  
    *Pianists, choir singers, orchestral players* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: forScore, folders, binders · source: knowledge
1341. **Slowing down a song section to learn it** — Learning a solo or riff requires looping a few seconds at half speed without pitch change, and juggling a phone, a DAW and sheet music is clumsy.  
    *Instrumentalists learning by ear* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: YouTube speed settings, Transcribe!, Anytune · source: knowledge
1342. **Turning pages while both hands play** — Reading a long piece from a tablet or paper means stopping to flip pages mid-phrase unless someone is there to turn them.  
    *Pianists and classical instrumentalists* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Bluetooth foot pedals, memorizing, photocopies taped together · source: knowledge

### Reading

1343. **Forgetting what happened in the previous book** — When the next book in a series comes out a year later, readers cannot remember characters and plot and either reread or read confused.  
    *Fantasy, sci-fi and mystery series readers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Fan wikis, 'recaptains' sites, rereading · source: knowledge
1344. **Highlights and notes lost across formats** — Notes taken in a Kindle, a physical book and an audiobook never end up in one place, so readers cannot find the quote they remember.  
    *Nonfiction readers and students* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Readwise, photos of pages, sticky tabs · source: knowledge
1345. **Library holds returning all at once** — Library ebook holds placed months apart all become available in the same week, and readers cannot finish them before the loan expires and the queue restarts.  
    *Libby and library patrons* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Suspending holds manually, panic reading · source: knowledge
1346. **Tracking reading order across a series** — Series with novellas, prequels and spin-offs have confusing reading orders, and readers accidentally read book four before book three.  
    *Series readers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Author websites, fan-made reading order lists · source: knowledge
1347. **Book club scheduling and picking a book** — Book clubs stall on picking the next title and finding a date everyone can make, with debates dragging on in group chats.  
    *Members of informal book clubs* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Group chat polls, one person deciding · source: knowledge
1348. **Ever-growing to-be-read pile** — Readers keep buying and borrowing books faster than they read them and feel guilt and choice paralysis choosing the next one.  
    *Avid readers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Goodreads shelves, StoryGraph, notebooks · source: knowledge
1349. **Lending books and never getting them back** — Readers lend books to friends and forget who has what, and the book disappears permanently.  
    *People with physical book collections* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Memory, a sticky note on the shelf · source: knowledge

### Video games

1350. **Coordinating a raid or group session night** — Getting five to twenty adults with jobs and kids online at the same time across time zones takes days of Discord back-and-forth, and one no-show cancels the night.  
    *Guild and clan members in MMOs and co-op games* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Discord polls, Google Sheets sign-ups, Raid-Helper bots · source: knowledge
1351. **Forgetting which video games you already own** — Games are spread across Steam, Epic, GOG, PlayStation, Xbox, Switch and old physical discs, so people buy a game they already got free or bundled years ago.  
    *PC and console gamers with libraries on multiple storefronts* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manually searching each launcher before buying; spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1352. **Huge backlog of unplayed games** — Sales and subscriptions pile up hundreds of unplayed titles, and people freeze trying to pick what to play next, then buy more instead of playing what they own.  
    *Gamers who buy during Steam sales or use Game Pass* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Spreadsheets, Backloggd, random-picker sites · source: knowledge
1353. **Returning to a game after months away** — Picking a story game back up after a long break means having no idea what the plot was, what the controls do, or what quest was in progress.  
    *Adult gamers who play in bursts* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Watching recap videos on YouTube or restarting from scratch · source: knowledge
1354. **Screen-time limits that kill a game session** — Parents and gamers themselves want to cap play, but hard cutoffs mid-match cause rage-quits, penalties in ranked modes, and family fights.  
    *Parents of gaming kids; adults trying to limit play* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Console parental controls, kitchen timers, arguments · source: knowledge
1355. **Tracking which game subscription has which title** — Titles rotate on and off Game Pass, PS Plus, and Netflix Games, so people miss a game leaving the catalog mid-playthrough or pay for a game that was included.  
    *Subscribers to game subscription services* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Checking monthly 'leaving soon' blog posts · source: knowledge
1356. **Losing track of achievements and completion goals** — Completionists forget which collectibles, side quests or achievements they have left in a large open-world game and end up re-checking wikis constantly.  
    *Completionist gamers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Wiki checklists, printed guides, spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1357. **Sharing game clips across platforms** — Clips captured on PS5, Xbox or Switch are stuck in each platform's ecosystem, and getting them to a phone or Discord takes several steps and quality loss.  
    *Console gamers who share highlights with friends* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Uploading to Twitter/YouTube then re-downloading; USB drives · source: knowledge
1358. **Waiting for a wishlisted game's price drop** — People watch wishlists across several stores and still miss the historic-low sale on a game they wanted, or buy right before it goes on sale.  
    *Budget-conscious PC and console gamers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: IsThereAnyDeal, Steam wishlist emails, Deku Deals · source: knowledge

## Sports & outdoors

*running, cycling, hiking, gym, team sports, finding partners, courts and gear*


### Courts & facilities

1359. **Court booking systems are clunky** — Municipal booking sites open at odd hours, need separate logins per venue and release slots that vanish in seconds.  
    *regular court bookers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: alarm set for release time, refreshing pages · source: knowledge
1360. **Public court availability unknown** — People drive to the public tennis or basketball courts only to find every one taken, with no way to check beforehand.  
    *urban racket and court sport players* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: go early, drive around, give up · source: knowledge
1361. **Class booking window scramble** — Popular spin or yoga classes open bookings a week out and fill in a minute; waitlists notify too late to actually attend.  
    *boutique fitness members* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: alarms at booking time, refreshing the app · source: knowledge
1362. **Finding a gym while traveling** — Travelers do not know which gym near the hotel offers day passes, what equipment it has or whether it opens early enough.  
    *business travelers who lift* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Google Maps, hotel gym with two dumbbells · source: knowledge
1363. **Finding a pitch to rent** — Booking a five-a-side pitch or indoor court for a one-off game means calling several venues to compare price and availability.  
    *casual game organizers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: phone calls, venue websites · source: knowledge
1364. **Gym crowded at the wrong time** — Arriving to find every squat rack taken wastes the session; gym busyness data is coarse or missing.  
    *gym members* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Google popular times, learning by trial · source: knowledge
1365. **Membership and pass sprawl** — Gym contracts, climbing punch cards, pool passes and park permits expire or auto-renew silently and cost money unnoticed.  
    *people using several facilities* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: bank statement surprises · source: knowledge
1366. **Pool lane schedules hard to find** — Lap swimming hours change with swim lessons and clubs, and the schedule is a PDF that is often outdated.  
    *lap swimmers and triathletes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: phone the pool, memorize the timetable · source: knowledge
1367. **Tracking court time and player debts** — In a rotating tennis or badminton group, whoever books pays and the running tally of who owes whom is never settled.  
    *recurring racket-sport groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: rough memory, someone always overpays · source: knowledge

### Events & races

1368. **Race registration sells out unnoticed** — Popular races and lotteries open and fill within hours; people miss them because there is no reminder for the exact opening date.

1369. **Discovering local races and events** — Small 5Ks, gran fondos and trail races are scattered across dozens of registration sites and club pages, so people find out after the fact.

1372. **Race photos scattered and pricey** — Finding your own photos after a race means searching by bib on several photographer sites and paying a lot per image.

1373. **Finding a climbing or gym partner** — Rope climbers cannot climb without a belayer and lifters want a spotter, but partner boards at gyms are dead and awkward.

1374. **Missing a fourth for doubles** — Regular tennis, padel or pickleball groups constantly fall apart because one person cancels and there is no quick way to fill the slot.

1376. **Finding beginner-friendly sessions** — Adults wanting to try climbing, rowing or martial arts cannot tell which clubs welcome true beginners versus assume experience.

1380. **Bike maintenance intervals forgotten** — Chains wear past the point of ruining cassettes and brake pads wear to metal because nobody tracks mileage since the last service.

1388. **Unsure if form is correct** — Beginners fear injury from bad squat or deadlift form but cannot afford a coach and get contradictory advice online.

### Hiking & trails

1394. **Permits and reservations maze** — Backcountry permits, campsite reservations and timed-entry passes have different systems, release dates and lotteries per park.

    *solo hikers, trail runners, backcountry skiers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a text message, a note on the fridge · source: knowledge
1396. **Trail conditions unknown before driving** — Hikers drive two hours to find the trail snowed in, flooded, closed for logging or the road washed out.  
    *day hikers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: recent trail reviews, park websites, calling rangers · source: knowledge
1397. **Turnaround time and daylight** — Hikers misjudge pace and end up descending in the dark because they did not set a turnaround time based on sunset and remaining distance.  
    *day hikers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: rules of thumb, headlamp · source: knowledge
1398. **Hike difficulty ratings inconsistent** — A 'moderate' hike on one site is a scramble on another; people pick hikes that are far beyond or below their ability.  
    *casual hikers and families* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: read many reviews, look at elevation charts · source: knowledge
1399. **Trailhead parking full** — Popular trailheads fill by 7am on weekends and there is no way to know before arriving or a plan B nearby.  
    *weekend hikers near cities* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: leave before dawn, park illegally · source: knowledge

### Routes & navigation

1400. **Safe bike commute routing** — Map apps route cyclists onto fast roads or through hills; finding the quiet, protected-lane route takes weeks of trial.  
    *bike commuters* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking local cyclists, experimenting · source: knowledge
1401. **Finding a new safe running route** — Runners in a new city or on a trip do not know which streets are lit, have sidewalks, are safe at 6am, or avoid endless traffic lights.  
    *runners who travel or relocate* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: run out-and-back on a main road, ask hotel staff · source: knowledge
1402. **Same boring route every day** — People run the same loop for years because building a new loop of exactly the right distance from home is tedious.  
    *habitual runners* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: drag points on a map builder by hand · source: knowledge

### Team sports & pickup

1403. **Chasing teammates for match fees** — The person who books the pitch is permanently out of pocket, chasing ten people for a few dollars each week.  
    *amateur team captains* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheets, Venmo requests, absorbing the loss · source: knowledge

1405. **Pickup game headcount uncertainty** — Weekly pickup football or basketball never knows if 6 or 16 will turn up; organizers chase replies and games get cancelled last minute.  
    *pickup game organizers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: WhatsApp polls and nagging · source: knowledge
1406. **Amateur league fixtures and results** — Sunday league standings, fixture changes and referee assignments live in a Facebook group or someone's head; players show up at the wrong pitch.

1407. **Sub finder for league night** — When a regular cannot make league volleyball or softball, finding a qualified sub in time is a scramble through personal contacts.  
    *league team members* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: texting everyone you know, forfeiting · source: knowledge
1408. **Fair teams for pickup games** — Splitting a random group into balanced sides takes ten minutes of arguing and the game is still one-sided.  
    *pickup and rec league players* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: captains pick, shirts vs skins · source: knowledge
1409. **Stats for rec league games** — Amateur teams want simple goals, assists and minutes but scoring by hand mid-game is unrealistic and nobody keeps it up.

1410. **Keeping score in casual matches** — Friendly tennis, pickleball or table tennis games lose track of the score and disputes waste the booked hour.  
    *casual racket-sport players* · weekly · pain 1/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: shouting the score every point · source: knowledge

### Tracking & data

1411. **Fitness data siloed across apps** — Runs live in Garmin, rides in Strava, lifts in a notes app and swims nowhere; there is no single honest picture of weekly training load.  
    *multi-sport amateurs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual spreadsheets or paying for a third-party aggregator · source: knowledge
1412. **Niggles and pain go unlogged** — A sore knee that comes and goes is never written down, so the pattern behind it, such as a certain shoe or hill session, is never spotted.  
    *endurance athletes* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: memory, mention it to a physio months later · source: knowledge
1413. **Strava privacy around home** — Sharing activities exposes home location and routine; privacy zones are confusing and people still get identified.  
    *people sharing workouts publicly* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: start the watch a block away, keep activities private · source: knowledge
1414. **Fitness watch data confuses** — Training readiness, VO2 max and load scores contradict each other and how the athlete feels, with no plain explanation of what to do.  
    *wearable owners* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ignore the numbers, ask forums · source: knowledge
1415. **Forgot to start or stop the watch** — A run gets logged short, or a two-hour drive home gets recorded as a bike ride, and fixing the activity afterwards is fiddly or impossible.  
    *anyone tracking workouts on a wearable* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manually crop or add the activity · source: knowledge
1416. **GPS watch distance disagrees with course** — Race and route distances never match what the watch says, so pacing math is off and PRs feel unverifiable.  
    *recreational runners and cyclists* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ignore it or manually correct the activity · source: knowledge

### Training & coaching

1417. **Losing motivation without accountability** — Solo exercisers skip sessions when nobody expects them; streaks and badges feel hollow after a few weeks.

1418. **Returning from injury too fast** — After a strain, people do not know how to ramp mileage back up and re-injure themselves by jumping to their old volume.

1419. **Training plans ignore missed days** — Miss two runs because of a cold and the generic plan just marches on, so people either cram or quit the plan entirely.
