1420. **Not knowing a realistic race goal** — First-time half-marathoners have no idea what finish time to aim for or whether their training pace is sensible.  
    *new race entrants* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: online race calculators, asking forums · source: knowledge
1421. **Program stalls without adjustment** — When lifts plateau people do not know whether to deload, change rep ranges or eat more, and spreadsheets from forums do not adapt.  
    *intermediate lifters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: forum advice, random program hopping · source: knowledge

### Weather & conditions

1422. **Air quality and heat safety** — Wildfire smoke or heat indexes make outdoor training unsafe, but people only find out after starting and feeling terrible.

1423. **Surf, snow or river conditions** — Condition-dependent sports require checking several forecasts and webcams daily and still result in wasted trips.

## Neighborhood & community

*local events, lending/borrowing, volunteering, nonprofits, clubs, HOAs*


### Clubs and nonprofits

1426. **Board transitions lose institutional memory** — When the club president or PTA chair steps down, passwords, vendor contacts and how-we-do-things knowledge disappear with them.  
    *volunteer-run organizations* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a binder, if you are lucky · source: knowledge
1427. **Grant deadlines slip by** — Tiny nonprofits miss local foundation grant deadlines because tracking eligibility, due dates and reporting requirements lives in someone's head.  
    *small nonprofit directors* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calendar reminders and spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1428. **Member list in five places** — Club membership data lives in an old Excel file, a Mailchimp list, a PayPal export and the president's phone contacts, and none agree.  
    *club and small nonprofit admins* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manual reconciliation once a year · source: knowledge
1429. **Membership dues chasing** — The treasurer of a small club spends hours every renewal season emailing people individually to remind them to pay dues.  
    *club treasurers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mass emails, PayPal links, cash at meetings · source: knowledge
1430. **Booking shared community rooms** — Reserving the church hall, library room or clubhouse means calling someone who checks a paper calendar, and double bookings happen.  
    *clubs and neighborhood groups* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: phone calls and a wall calendar · source: knowledge
1431. **Club newsletter takes all week** — The volunteer who writes the monthly newsletter spends hours collecting updates from committee chairs who never reply on time.  
    *club communications volunteers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: nagging emails and Mailchimp · source: knowledge
1432. **Community garden plot management** — Garden coordinators track plot assignments, waitlists, dues and watering duties on paper, and abandoned plots go unnoticed for months.  
    *community garden coordinators and members* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheets and a corkboard · source: knowledge
1433. **Dead groups still listed** — Half the community groups you find online have not met in two years but still look active, wasting your time reaching out.  
    *people looking for clubs* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: message and wait for no reply · source: knowledge
1434. **Fundraiser ticket sales and check-in** — A small gala or pancake breakfast sells tickets by cash, check and Venmo, and the door volunteer has no reliable list of who paid.  
    *nonprofit event organizers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Eventbrite plus a paper list · source: knowledge
1435. **Reimbursement receipts for small orgs** — Volunteers pay for supplies out of pocket, lose receipts, and reimbursement takes months because the treasurer needs paper copies.  
    *volunteers and treasurers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: photos in text messages · source: knowledge
1436. **Shared login for org accounts** — Five volunteers share one Gmail and Facebook page login, so nobody knows who posted what or who changed the password.  
    *volunteer-run groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: password in a group chat · source: knowledge
1437. **Sports club practice scheduling** — Rec-league teams coordinate practices and game rides across a group chat where messages get lost and half the team never confirms.  
    *amateur sports team managers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: TeamSnap, WhatsApp · source: knowledge
1438. **Meeting attendance guessing** — Nobody knows how many members will come to the monthly meeting, so you order too much food or too few chairs.  
    *club organizers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: email RSVP that few answer · source: knowledge

### HOAs

1439. **Architectural request black hole** — You submit a request to paint your house or add solar and hear nothing for weeks, with no way to see where it sits in review.  
    *HOA homeowners doing improvements* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: repeated emails to the management company · source: knowledge
1440. **HOA rules buried in PDFs** — You cannot tell whether your fence color, shed or trash-can placement is allowed because the CC&Rs are a 90-page scanned PDF.  
    *HOA homeowners* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: email the property manager and wait · source: knowledge
1441. **Low turnout for HOA votes** — Annual meetings fail to reach quorum because proxies are paper forms nobody returns, so decisions stall.  
    *HOA boards* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: door-knocking for proxies · source: knowledge
1442. **Surprise HOA violation letters** — A violation notice arrives by mail with a fine, for something like a weed or a trash can left out, with no prior warning or photo.  
    *HOA homeowners* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: call to dispute it · source: knowledge
1443. **Visitor parking and guest passes** — Guests get towed because registering a visitor car requires a form during office hours or a phone call to a security line.

1444. **Volunteer HOA board overwhelmed** — Unpaid board members juggle vendor bids, owner complaints and finances in personal inboxes with no system, so things drop.

1445. **Amenity booking is first-come chaos** — Reserving the pool cabana, clubhouse or tennis court requires emailing the manager, and the same few families always get the slots.  
    *residents of HOAs and condos* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: email early and hope · source: knowledge
1446. **HOA dues payment friction** — Paying dues means mailing a check or using a clunky portal that charges a fee, and late fees hit when the mail is slow.

1447. **HOA meeting minutes unavailable** — Owners cannot find board meeting minutes, budgets or reserve studies because they are emailed once and never posted anywhere.

1448. **Reporting common-area problems** — A broken gate or burned-out streetlight in the community gets reported by five people to the wrong person and fixed by nobody.  
    *HOA residents* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Nextdoor complaints · source: knowledge

### Lending and borrowing

1449. **Borrowing a tool you use once** — You need a post-hole digger or tile saw for one afternoon and end up buying it because you do not know which neighbor has one.  
    *homeowners and DIYers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: buy it, rent from a hardware store, or post on Nextdoor · source: knowledge
1450. **Finding a trustworthy last-minute favor** — You need someone to grab your package, let the dog out or jump your car right now, but you do not know which neighbors are home and willing.  
    *residents without nearby family* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: knock on doors or post on Nextdoor and hope · source: knowledge
1451. **No-shows on free pickups** — Someone claims your free couch, you hold it for them, and they never come, so you re-post and repeat the cycle.  
    *people giving items away* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: first-come-first-served and porch pickup · source: knowledge
1452. **Awkwardness asking for items back** — A borrowed item has been gone for weeks and there is no neutral, non-confrontational way to nudge the borrower to return it.  
    *lenders* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: wait and stew, or send an awkward text · source: knowledge
1453. **Borrowed item returned damaged** — A tool comes back broken and there is no record of its condition when lent, leading to disputes about who pays.

1454. **Buy Nothing group scroll fatigue** — Free-item groups are an unsearchable stream of posts, so the crib you needed was claimed hours ago and your own offer gets buried.

1455. **Forgetting who borrowed your stuff** — You lent a ladder, a folding table and a pressure washer to different neighbors months ago and cannot remember who has what.  
    *generous neighbors and friends* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app or memory · source: knowledge
1456. **Tool library hours and inventory** — The local tool library or Library of Things has short hours and no live inventory, so you drive over to find the item checked out.  
    *tool library members* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: call ahead when open · source: knowledge
1457. **Sharing surplus garden produce** — Your zucchini or lemon tree produces far more than you can eat, and there is no easy way to offer it to nearby neighbors before it rots.  
    *home gardeners* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: leave a box at the curb · source: knowledge
1458. **Splitting bulk purchases with neighbors** — You want to split a mulch delivery, a Costco pack or a dumpster rental with neighbors but coordinating who pays what is messy.  
    *homeowners in dense neighborhoods* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Venmo requests and group chat · source: knowledge

### Local events

1459. **Local events scattered across platforms** — Neighborhood events are announced on Facebook groups, Nextdoor, church bulletins, library flyers and Instagram, so you only hear about the fun ones after they happened.  
    *residents, especially newcomers to a town* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: follow a dozen Facebook groups and check the library board · source: knowledge
1460. **Block party planning chaos** — Organizing a block party means chasing neighbors by text, paper flyers and door knocks to collect RSVPs, potluck dishes and permit fees.  
    *neighborhood organizers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: group text plus a shared Google sheet · source: knowledge
1461. **Cancelled events not communicated** — The organizer cancels a park cleanup or outdoor concert because of weather but the cancellation only appears in one place, so people still show up.  
    *attendees of outdoor community events* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: check the organizer's Facebook page before leaving · source: knowledge
1462. **Finding out about events too late** — You learn about the street fair or farmers market special the day after because nothing pushed it to you in advance.  
    *families and singles who want to go out locally* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1463. **Garage sale discovery is hit-or-miss** — Yard and estate sales are advertised with cardboard signs or buried Craigslist posts, so you cannot plan a route or know what is being sold.  
    *bargain hunters and resellers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: drive around Saturday morning following signs · source: knowledge
1464. **Going to events alone** — You want to go to a local trivia night or gallery opening but none of your friends are free, and there is no easy way to find others attending.  
    *singles and people new to a city* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: skip it or use Meetup · source: knowledge
1465. **Kid-friendly events hard to filter** — Community calendars do not say whether an event is suitable for toddlers, free, stroller-accessible, or indoors when it rains.

1466. **Street closure and permit confusion** — Getting a permit to close the street for a block party or run a 5K involves unclear city forms, deadlines and fees that differ by municipality.  
    *small event organizers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: call city hall repeatedly · source: knowledge

### Neighborhood communication

1467. **Reaching neighbors in an emergency** — During a gas leak, wildfire warning or power outage there is no quick way to alert or check on everyone on the block.  
    *block captains and residents* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: knock on doors · source: knowledge
1468. **Lost pet alerts do not reach nearby** — A lost dog post on Facebook reaches your friends across the country, not the twenty households within walking distance who might spot it.

1469. **Carpool coordination for local activities** — Parents on the same street drive separately to the same soccer field and school because nobody organizes a rotating carpool.  
    *parents in the same neighborhood* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ad hoc texts · source: knowledge
1470. **Checking on elderly neighbors** — Neighbors want to look in on an older resident living alone but there is no shared schedule, so some days nobody checks and other days three people do.  
    *caring neighbors and families of seniors* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: informal texting · source: knowledge
1471. **Finding a local recommendation quickly** — You need a plumber or babysitter your actual neighbors have used, but the same question gets asked in the group every week and answers scroll away.  
    *homeowners and parents* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: search the Facebook group, ask again · source: knowledge
1472. **Neighborhood watch has no roster** — Suspicious activity gets reported to a group chat that half the block is not in, and there is no shared log for patterns.

1473. **New resident has no orientation** — You move to a new neighborhood and have to piece together trash day, recycling rules, the good grocery store and local groups from scratch.

1474. **Nextdoor signal buried in noise** — The useful post about a water main break is buried under lost cats, rants about teenagers and political arguments.  
    *Nextdoor and neighborhood group users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: mute the app and miss things · source: knowledge
1475. **Not knowing your neighbors' names** — You have lived on the street for years and still do not know who lives in half the houses, which makes asking for help awkward.  
    *suburban and apartment residents* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1476. **Reporting potholes and streetlights** — Reporting a pothole or broken streetlight to the city means finding the right department and then never learning if anything happened.

1477. **Mutual aid requests get lost** — Neighborhood mutual aid runs on a Google Form and a group chat, so requests for groceries or rides fall through the cracks and nobody knows who took them.

1478. **Volunteer no-shows leave shifts empty** — Twenty people sign up for the food bank shift and eight appear, so coordinators over-recruit and still get stuck short-handed.

<a id="cars-vehicles"></a>

## Car & vehicle ownership

*maintenance, repairs, mechanics, EV charging, fuel, insurance, selling*


### Costs & fuel

1487. **Tracking true cost of ownership** — Fuel, insurance, repairs, registration and depreciation are spread across many accounts and receipts, so owners have no idea what the car really costs per month or per mile.  
    *budget-conscious owners, people weighing selling or going car-free* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: rough spreadsheet, none · source: knowledge
1488. **Finding the cheapest fuel nearby** — Fuel prices vary 20-40 cents between stations a mile apart, and crowdsourced price apps are often stale or wrong by the time you get there.  
    *commuters and high-mileage drivers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: GasBuddy, sticking to a known warehouse-club station · source: knowledge
1489. **Fuel rewards and loyalty juggling** — Grocery fuel points, station apps and credit-card categories each give discounts under different rules, and drivers can't tell at the pump which combination is actually cheapest.  
    *deal-seeking drivers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: picking one program and ignoring the rest · source: knowledge
1490. **Noticing declining fuel economy** — A gradual drop in MPG from a clogged filter, low tires or a failing sensor goes unnoticed because nobody tracks fill-ups consistently.  
    *owners of older cars* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: trip computer readout, Fuelly logging · source: knowledge
1491. **Reconstructing a fair fuel split** — Carpoolers and couples sharing a car have no clean way to split fuel and tolls in proportion to who drove where.  
    *carpoolers and co-owners* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: alternating who pays, rough Venmo requests · source: knowledge

### Dealing with mechanics

1492. **Not knowing if a repair quote is fair** — A shop quotes $900 for a job and the owner has no reference for whether parts and labor hours are reasonable for their car and region.  
    *anyone getting a car repaired* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling two more shops, RepairPal estimates, asking a car-savvy friend · source: knowledge
1493. **Final bill differs from the quote** — The invoice comes in higher than the verbal estimate with extra parts or shop fees, and the owner has no written record of what was originally agreed.  
    *anyone paying for repairs* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking for a written estimate, disputing at the counter · source: knowledge
1494. **Finding a trustworthy independent mechanic** — Online reviews for shops are sparse or gamed, and people moving to a new city have no idea who will do honest work on their particular make.  
    *people new to an area, owners of niche or European brands* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking on local Facebook groups or Nextdoor, brand-owner forums · source: knowledge
1495. **No status updates while car is in shop** — Once the car is dropped off, the owner has no visibility into whether it has been looked at, whether parts arrived, or when it will actually be ready, and has to keep calling.  
    *anyone with a car in for repair* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: repeated phone calls to the service desk · source: knowledge
1496. **Repair upsells at checkout** — Taking the car in for an oil change ends with a list of 'urgently recommended' items (flushes, filters, alignment) and no way to judge on the spot which ones are real.  
    *drivers using dealers and chain shops* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: saying no to everything, googling in the waiting room · source: knowledge
1497. **Arranging transport while car is in shop** — Dropping the car off means coordinating a ride, a loaner or a rental and then getting back to pick it up, often across two workdays.  
    *single-car households and commuters* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: rideshare, asking a partner or coworker · source: knowledge
1498. **Booking a service appointment by phone only** — Many independent shops only take bookings by phone during business hours, so scheduling means playing phone tag from work.  
    *working car owners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calling on lunch break, using dealer instead · source: knowledge

### Diagnostics & repairs

1499. **Roadside breakdown with no plan** — When the car dies on the highway, drivers don't know whether their insurer, credit card or manufacturer covers towing, who to call, or which shop to tow to.  
    *all drivers* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: AAA membership, googling on the shoulder · source: knowledge
1500. **Battery dying without warning** — Car batteries fail suddenly after 3-5 years, usually on a cold morning when the driver is already late, with no prior indication it was weakening.  
    *all drivers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: free battery test at a parts store, carrying a jump pack · source: knowledge
1501. **Deciding whether to repair or replace** — When an aging car needs a $1,500 repair, the owner can't tell whether it is worth it or the start of a cascade of failures on a car worth $3,000.  
    *owners of high-mileage cars* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: gut feeling, asking friends, forum posts · source: knowledge
1502. **Unexplained check engine light** — The check engine light comes on with no explanation, and the owner has no idea whether it is a loose gas cap or a $2,000 catalytic converter until they pay a shop for a diagnostic.  
    *owners of cars over five years old* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: free code read at a parts store, cheap OBD2 dongle plus googling codes · source: knowledge
1503. **Dashboard warning symbol confusion** — An unfamiliar amber icon lights up and the driver can't tell whether it means pull over now or schedule service sometime, without digging through the manual.  
    *drivers of newer or unfamiliar cars* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: googling a description of the symbol · source: knowledge
1504. **Describing a noise to a mechanic** — A squeak, clunk or hum only happens under certain conditions and is hard to describe accurately, so the shop can't reproduce it and the owner pays for diagnostic time that finds nothing.  
    *non-technical car owners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: recording a phone video while driving, trying to imitate the sound · source: knowledge
1505. **Missed recall notices** — Safety recalls are mailed to the address on file, which is wrong for used-car buyers and movers, so owners drive around unaware of open recalls.  
    *used-car owners and people who have moved* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: checking the NHTSA VIN lookup manually, hearing about it on the news · source: knowledge

### EV charging

1506. **Charging without home parking** — Apartment and street-parking EV owners have no reliable place to charge overnight and must build weekly trips around public chargers.  
    *urban renters with EVs* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: charging at work, extension cords, weekly DC fast charge sessions · source: knowledge
1507. **Charger queues at busy stations** — On holidays and weekends drivers arrive to find every stall occupied with no queue system, leading to awkward waiting and disputes over who was next.  
    *EV drivers on popular corridors* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: circling the lot, informal verbal queue · source: knowledge
1508. **Range anxiety on long trips** — Planning a road trip in an EV means guessing how weather, speed, elevation and load affect range and whether the next charger is safely reachable.  
    *EV drivers on unfamiliar routes* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A Better Routeplanner, adding big safety margins · source: knowledge
1509. **Too many charging network apps** — Each charging network requires its own app, account and payment method, so a road trip means managing five apps and RFID cards just to plug in.  
    *EV drivers who travel* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: signing up for everything in advance, roaming cards · source: knowledge
1510. **Idle fees from forgetting to unplug** — Drivers step away while charging, lose track of time and get hit with per-minute idle fees or angry notes from waiting drivers.  
    *EV drivers at fast chargers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: network app notifications that are often delayed · source: knowledge
1511. **Opaque public charging costs** — Charging is priced per kWh, per minute, with idle fees and session fees that vary by network and time of day, so a session's cost is unknowable until the receipt arrives.  
    *EV drivers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sticking to one network, ignoring the cost · source: knowledge
1512. **Timing home charging for cheap rates** — Time-of-use electricity tariffs make charging much cheaper at night, but the car and the wallbox schedulers are clunky and the tariff windows change seasonally.  
    *EV owners with home chargers on TOU tariffs* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: manually setting departure timers, plugging in late at night · source: knowledge
1513. **Tracking home charging electricity cost** — Home charging shows up as a bigger power bill with no breakdown, so owners can't separate car cost from household use or claim it for work reimbursement.  
    *EV owners, especially those with employer mileage schemes* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: smart charger apps, estimating from car kWh totals · source: knowledge

### Insurance & claims

1514. **Collecting info at an accident scene** — After a crash, shaken drivers forget to get the other party's insurance, plate, witness details and photos of the scene, which weakens the claim later.  
    *any driver involved in a collision* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: insurer app checklists few people have installed, phone camera · source: knowledge
1515. **Hit-and-run parking lot damage** — Owners return to a dented car with no note, no witnesses and no way to find out who did it, then face a deductible on their own policy.  
    *people who park in public lots* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking store security for camera footage, dashcam parking mode · source: knowledge
1516. **Insurance renewal price creep** — Premiums rise at every renewal for no stated reason, and shopping around means re-entering the same details on many quote sites.  
    *all insured drivers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: comparison sites, calling to threaten to leave · source: knowledge
1517. **Slow, opaque claims process** — After filing a claim, the owner waits weeks with no idea whether the adjuster has looked at it, what the payout will be, or when the body shop can start.  
    *drivers with an open claim* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling the claims line repeatedly · source: knowledge
1518. **Dashcam footage management** — Dashcams overwrite footage in loops, and when an incident happens the owner struggles to find, save and share the right clip before it is lost.  
    *dashcam owners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pulling the SD card, clunky vendor apps · source: knowledge
1519. **Understanding what the policy covers** — Owners don't know whether rental reimbursement, glass, roadside or gap cover is included until they need it and get denied.  
    *policyholders* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reading the PDF policy, calling the agent · source: knowledge
1520. **Proving mileage for low-mileage discounts** — Insurers offer cheaper rates for low annual mileage but verifying it means photographing odometers or installing intrusive tracking dongles.  
    *low-mileage and remote-working drivers* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: odometer photos, telematics programs · source: knowledge

### Maintenance tracking

1521. **Decoding the manufacturer service schedule** — The owner's manual buries the real maintenance intervals in dense tables that differ by driving conditions, so people don't know which items actually apply to them at 30k, 60k, 90k miles.  
    *owners of cars past warranty* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: asking the dealer, who upsells extra 'recommended' services · source: knowledge
1522. **Lost paper service records** — Receipts and invoices from various shops end up in a glovebox or lost entirely, so there is no coherent history to reference when a problem recurs or when selling.  
    *long-term car owners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: folder of paper receipts, photos in camera roll · source: knowledge
1523. **Not knowing when maintenance is due** — Owners lose track of when the last oil change, tire rotation, brake fluid or coolant service happened, so services are either missed or repeated needlessly.  
    *anyone who owns a car, especially multi-car households* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: windshield sticker from the shop, notes app, guessing · source: knowledge
1524. **Not knowing when tires are worn out** — Owners can't tell whether tread is at a safe depth or how many miles remain, and only find out when a shop says they need four new tires today.  
    *drivers who don't inspect their own cars* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: penny test, trusting the shop · source: knowledge
1525. **Seasonal tire swap logistics** — Twice a year winter/summer tires need swapping; owners forget to book early, shops are slammed for weeks, and stored tires are misplaced or their wear position forgotten.  
    *drivers in snow regions* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calendar reminder, chalk marks on tires · source: knowledge
1526. **Car wash and detailing timing** — Road salt, bird droppings and pollen damage paint if left, but owners forget how long since the last wash or wax and don't notice until the clear coat is stained.  
    *owners who care about resale condition* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: unlimited wash subscriptions, none · source: knowledge
1527. **Forgetting tire pressure checks** — Tire pressure drops with cold weather and slow leaks, but people only notice when the TPMS light comes on or a tire looks flat.  
    *all drivers, especially in cold climates* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: waiting for the dashboard warning · source: knowledge
1528. **Wiper, bulb and fluid part lookups** — Buying wiper blades, bulbs, oil or a cabin filter means figuring out the exact size or spec for the year, trim and engine, and parts-store lookups often give conflicting answers.  
    *DIY-lite owners* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: in-store paper catalog, owner's manual · source: knowledge

### Paperwork & compliance

1529. **Insurance card and documents not at hand** — When pulled over, drivers scramble through the glovebox for an expired insurance card or realize the registration is in another car.  
    *all drivers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: keeping PDFs in a phone folder, insurer app · source: knowledge
1530. **Missing registration renewal deadline** — Registration, inspection and emissions deadlines arrive by mail and are easy to miss, leading to fines and stops for expired tags.  
    *all vehicle owners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: windshield sticker, DMV mail reminders · source: knowledge
1531. **Multi-vehicle household record chaos** — Families with several cars, a motorcycle and a trailer lose track of which vehicle is due for what, whose insurance renews when, and where each title is.  
    *multi-vehicle households and small fleets* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet, file cabinet · source: knowledge
1532. **Toll and camera fines arriving late** — Toll-by-plate bills, congestion charges and camera fines arrive weeks later with added penalties, sometimes for a rental or a car already sold.  
    *drivers in metro areas and travelers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: transponder accounts per region, paying the penalties · source: knowledge
1533. **Tracking mileage for tax or reimbursement** — Self-employed people and employees must log business miles per trip with purpose and odometer readings, which is tedious and usually reconstructed inaccurately at tax time.  
    *gig workers, sales reps, small business owners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: MileIQ-style apps, paper log, guessing · source: knowledge
1534. **Warranty and extended coverage confusion** — Owners don't know if a failed part is covered under powertrain, bumper-to-bumper, emissions or an extended warranty, and dealers rarely volunteer that it is.  
    *owners of cars under six years old* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reading the warranty booklet, asking the dealer · source: knowledge

### Parking & daily use

1535. **Forgetting to move the car for street cleaning** — Alternate-side and street-cleaning schedules require moving the car on specific mornings, and forgetting once means a ticket or a tow.  
    *street parkers in big cities* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: phone alarms, neighborhood group chats · source: knowledge
1536. **Parking tickets from unclear signage** — Street parking rules stack multiple signs (street cleaning, permit hours, snow routes) and drivers get ticketed because they misread which rule applied that day.  
    *city drivers without off-street parking* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photographing the sign, asking neighbors · source: knowledge
1537. **Rental car damage disputes** — Renters get billed weeks later for scratches they didn't cause because they didn't document existing damage at pickup.  
    *frequent travelers and rental customers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: walk-around video at pickup · source: knowledge
1538. **Parking meter expiring mid-errand** — Meters and pay-by-app sessions expire while the driver is still in an appointment, and rushing back or extending requires remembering which of several parking apps was used.  
    *urban drivers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ParkMobile reminders, overpaying up front · source: knowledge
1539. **Sharing one car within a household** — Partners or roommates sharing a vehicle double-book it, don't know who has the keys or when it will be back, and argue about who used the fuel.  
    *households with more drivers than cars* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group chat, shared calendar · source: knowledge
1540. **Teen driver monitoring and coaching** — Parents of new drivers have no way to know whether their teen is speeding or braking hard, and logging supervised practice hours for the license is done on scraps of paper.  
    *parents of learner and teen drivers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: insurer telematics apps, trust · source: knowledge

### Selling & buying

1541. **Evaluating a used car before buying** — Buyers can't tell from a test drive whether a used car has hidden accident damage, flood history or deferred maintenance, and a pre-purchase inspection is hard to arrange quickly.  
    *used-car buyers* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Carfax report, bringing a friend, mobile inspection services · source: knowledge
1542. **Dealer fees and financing math** — Buyers face doc fees, add-ons and financing terms presented in a four-square worksheet designed to obscure the real out-the-door price and total interest.  
    *car buyers at dealerships* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking for the out-the-door number, bringing a calculator · source: knowledge
1543. **Lowballers and scammers on marketplace** — Listing a car privately brings a flood of 'is this available?' messages, absurd lowball offers, and scam attempts with fake payment or shipping stories.  
    *private sellers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ignoring most messages, selling to a dealer for less · source: knowledge
1544. **Safe payment and handover in a private sale** — Sellers worry about fake cashier's checks and counterfeit cash, and both sides are unsure how to handle title signing, bill of sale, plates and release of liability correctly.  
    *private sellers and buyers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: meeting at the buyer's bank, DMV website guides · source: knowledge
1545. **Assembling a private sale listing** — Writing a listing means digging up the trim, options, maintenance history and taking decent photos, and most listings end up vague and undersold.  
    *private sellers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: copying a dealer listing's format, VIN decoder sites · source: knowledge
1546. **Lease end mileage and wear surprises** — Lessees exceed mileage allowances or get charged for wear at turn-in because they never tracked pace against the allowance.  
    *people leasing cars* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: dividing miles by months remaining in their head · source: knowledge
1547. **Not knowing the car's current value** — Owners considering a sale or trade-in see wildly different numbers from KBB, dealer offers and online buyers and can't tell what the car is really worth.  
    *people thinking about selling* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: checking three valuation sites, getting a CarMax quote · source: knowledge

<a id="hospitality-retail-workers"></a>

## Restaurant, retail & service workers

*shift scheduling, tips, swaps, training, customers, side work*


### Communication

1548. **Documenting incidents for HR** — Workers experiencing harassment or wage theft have no organized record of dates, shifts and witnesses when they finally decide to report it.  
    *hourly workers with problematic managers* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Notes app, memory · source: knowledge
1549. **Reaching a manager during a rush** — A worker needs a manager override, a void or a key, and the manager is somewhere in the building unreachable while the line grows.  
    *cashiers and servers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Yell, send a runner, wait · source: knowledge
1550. **Work group chat invades personal time** — The staff WhatsApp group buzzes at all hours with memes, shift pleas and manager announcements, so important messages get buried and days off are never quiet.  
    *all hourly service staff* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Mute the chat and miss things · source: knowledge
1551. **Corporate policy updates buried in email** — Chains push policy changes through a corporate portal or email that hourly workers rarely open, so they violate rules they never saw.  
    *chain retail and restaurant employees* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Manager mentions it eventually · source: knowledge
1552. **Missed pre-shift meeting information** — Anyone who starts later or was off misses the pre-shift briefing about specials, promotions and policy changes, and gets blamed for not knowing.  
    *restaurant and retail staff on staggered starts* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ask coworkers, read the whiteboard · source: knowledge
1553. **Shift handoff notes lost** — Important notes from the previous shift, like a reserved table, a VIP, or an angry customer coming back, are passed verbally and forgotten.  
    *hosts, shift leads and front desk staff* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sticky notes on the host stand · source: knowledge

### Customers and orders

1554. **Allergen questions answered from memory** — A guest asks whether a dish contains nuts or gluten and the server has to run to the kitchen or guess, with serious consequences for getting it wrong.  
    *servers and counter staff* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ask the chef every time · source: knowledge
1555. **Delivery-app orders flood the kitchen** — Tablets from three delivery platforms ding independently and staff must retype each order into the POS, so orders get missed or duplicated during rushes.  
    *counter staff and cooks at restaurants on delivery apps* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Dedicate one person to the tablets · source: knowledge
1556. **Handling difficult customers alone** — A customer becomes abusive and the worker has no discreet way to summon a manager or security without escalating the situation.  
    *cashiers, servers and hosts* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Walk away to find a manager, code words · source: knowledge
1557. **Complicated modifications get lost** — Large parties with many substitutions overwhelm the ticket and a wrong plate comes out, which the server eats the blame and the tip for.  
    *servers handling large or picky tables* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Repeat the order back, write on a pad · source: knowledge
1558. **Reservations and walk-ins on paper** — Small restaurants track reservations in a paper book and phone messages, leading to double bookings and missed party-size notes.  
    *hosts at independent restaurants* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Paper book, sticky notes · source: knowledge
1559. **Return and refund policy edge cases** — Customers demand returns without receipts, past the window or on final-sale items, and the associate does not know the exact policy or their own authority.  
    *retail cashiers and customer service desk staff* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Call a manager for every exception · source: knowledge
1560. **Splitting checks for large groups** — A party of twelve wants the bill split by what each person had, and the POS makes it slow and error-prone while other tables wait.  
    *servers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Calculator on phone, arguments at the table · source: knowledge
1561. **Wait time estimates are guesses** — Hosts quote a wait time from gut feel and get yelled at when it is wrong, with no data on how long tables actually turn.  
    *hosts and hostesses* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Guess, pad the estimate · source: knowledge
1562. **Checking stock for a customer** — A customer asks if an item is in the back or at another location and the associate has to leave the floor or phone another store to find out.  
    *retail associates* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Walk to the stockroom, call other stores · source: knowledge
1563. **Regulars' preferences not remembered** — A regular expects their usual drink and seat, but the person on shift has never served them and has no way to look up what they order.  
    *bartenders, baristas and servers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Coworkers' memory, sticky notes at the bar · source: knowledge

### Life around shifts

1564. **Childcare when shifts change weekly** — Parents cannot book daycare or sitters until the schedule comes out, and by then the slots they need are gone.  
    *parents working hourly service jobs* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Family favors, swapping shifts · source: knowledge
1565. **Commute planning around odd shift times** — Late closes and early opens fall outside bus and train schedules, and workers scramble each week to figure out how they will get home at 1am.  
    *service workers relying on public transit* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask coworkers for rides, expensive rideshares · source: knowledge
1566. **Proving hours for benefits or loans** — When applying for an apartment, loan or health coverage, workers must show consistent hours and income, but their pay history is scattered across paystubs and tip records.  
    *tipped and part-time workers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Collect paystubs, bank statements · source: knowledge
1567. **Seasonal layoffs with no notice** — Seasonal and holiday workers do not know until the last week whether they are being kept on, making it impossible to line up the next job.  
    *seasonal retail and resort workers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Ask the manager repeatedly · source: knowledge
1568. **Uniform and dress code ambiguity** — Workers get sent home or written up for a shoe or shirt color that was never clearly written down, and replacing uniform items costs them money.  
    *retail and restaurant staff* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Ask coworkers what is allowed · source: knowledge

### Scheduling

1569. **Clopening shifts** — Being scheduled to close at midnight and open at 6am leaves too little time to sleep, and workers rarely notice the pairing until the schedule is posted.  
    *restaurant and retail staff who can both open and close* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Complain to manager, try to trade one of the shifts · source: knowledge
1570. **Hours cut without warning** — A worker expecting 30 hours opens the new schedule to find 12, with no explanation, and has no way to see the trend or compare hours with coworkers.  
    *part-time retail and restaurant staff* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Ask manager for more shifts, pick up others' shifts · source: knowledge
1571. **On-call shifts that never confirm** — Being told to keep a day free and call in two hours before to learn whether you are working, so the day is wasted whether or not you get paid.  
    *retail associates and banquet servers* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Call the store, wait by the phone · source: knowledge
1572. **Schedule changed without notification** — Managers edit the posted schedule after it goes up and nobody tells the affected worker, who then shows up on the wrong day or gets a no-call-no-show.  
    *hourly staff with managers who edit schedules in place* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Re-check the schedule every day, screenshot it as proof · source: knowledge
1573. **Schedule posted only days ahead** — The weekly schedule goes up two or three days before the week starts, so workers cannot plan appointments, childcare, classes or a second job.  
    *hourly restaurant and retail staff* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Text the manager to ask, keep everything tentative · source: knowledge
1574. **Availability form ignored** — Workers submit availability on paper or in an app but keep getting scheduled during times they marked unavailable, with no record of what they submitted.  
    *students and parents working hourly jobs* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Re-submit availability, keep a photo of the form · source: knowledge
1575. **Holiday time-off request lottery** — Everyone requests the same holiday weekends off and the approval rule is opaque, so workers do not know until late whether they can travel.  
    *restaurant and retail staff around holidays* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask months early, bargain with coworkers · source: knowledge
1576. **Juggling schedules from two jobs** — Workers holding two hourly jobs must manually cross-check two schedules in two different apps and catch overlaps before either manager notices.  
    *workers with two or more part-time service jobs* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Copy both schedules into a phone calendar by hand · source: knowledge
1577. **Schedule only visible in the back office** — The schedule is a printed sheet on the break-room wall, so staff have to drive in or ask a coworker to photograph it to learn their shifts.  
    *workers at small independent shops and restaurants* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Photograph the sheet, group-chat photos · source: knowledge
1578. **Time-off request lost or forgotten** — A request written in the manager's notebook weeks ago is forgotten and the worker is scheduled anyway, with no proof the request was made.  
    *workers at shops without a formal request system* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Text the manager and screenshot the reply · source: knowledge
1579. **Shifts not syncing to phone calendar** — The scheduling app the employer uses does not export shifts to Google or Apple Calendar, so the worker's personal calendar never shows when they work.  
    *hourly staff using employer scheduling apps* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manually type each shift into the calendar · source: knowledge

### Shift swaps and coverage

1580. **Finding someone to cover a shift** — When a worker is sick or has an emergency they must personally text every coworker one by one and beg for coverage, then prove to the manager that they tried.  
    *hourly restaurant and retail staff* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Mass texts, group chat pleas, Facebook group posts · source: knowledge
1581. **Last-minute call-outs leave shift short** — Someone calls out an hour before service and the manager has no quick way to reach available, qualified staff, so the remaining crew is slammed.  
    *shift leads and the coworkers left on the floor* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manager calls down the phone list · source: knowledge
1582. **Sent home early with no pay** — Workers show up for a scheduled shift, get cut after an hour because it is slow, and lose the income they planned on with no record to contest it.  
    *servers, hosts and retail associates* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Volunteer to stay for side work, track it in notes · source: knowledge
1583. **Swap agreed but never approved** — Two coworkers agree to swap, but the manager has to sign off and forgets, so both are unsure who is actually working and one may be written up.  
    *staff at employers requiring manager approval for swaps* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Keep pestering the manager, screenshot the agreement · source: knowledge
1584. **Open shifts announced in chaotic group chat** — Extra shifts are offered in a busy staff group chat and given to whoever replies first, so workers who were not glued to their phone miss out and the chat is full of noise.  
    *workers wanting more hours* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Turn on chat notifications and check constantly · source: knowledge
1585. **Swapping breaks certification rules** — A swap gets accepted and then rejected because the covering worker lacks the right training, alcohol certification, or would go into overtime.  
    *bartenders, servers and keyholders* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manager checks manually, swap denied late · source: knowledge

### Side work and closing

1586. **Broken equipment reported into a void** — The dish machine, printer or ice machine breaks and staff tell a manager verbally, then it stays broken for weeks with no visibility on whether a repair was requested.  
    *kitchen and floor staff* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Keep telling the manager, tape a sign on it · source: knowledge
1587. **Opening finds last night's tasks undone** — The opener discovers the closers skipped restocking or cleaning and has no record of who closed or a way to flag it without a confrontation.  
    *opening shift staff* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Complain to the manager, group chat callouts · source: knowledge
1588. **Running out of supplies mid-shift** — Nobody noticed the to-go containers or a key ingredient were low, so staff discover the shortage during the rush and there is no simple way to flag low stock.  
    *line cooks, baristas and shift leads* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sticky note on the manager's desk · source: knowledge
1589. **Side work checklist disputes** — Closing side work is assigned by a laminated sheet and whoever leaves first claims they did their part, so the last person redoes everything.  
    *servers and bussers on closing shifts* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Initial the sheet, argue with coworkers · source: knowledge
1590. **Temperature logs on paper** — Fridge and hot-hold temperatures must be logged several times a day on a clipboard that gets pencil-whipped or lost before the health inspector visits.  
    *cooks and shift leads* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Fill it in from memory at the end of the shift · source: knowledge
1591. **Uneven section and task assignments** — The same people always get the good sections or the easy side work, and there is no visible rotation so favoritism accusations are constant.  
    *servers and retail floor staff* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Complain, hope the manager rotates · source: knowledge

### Tips and pay

1592. **Paycheck does not match hours worked** — Hours are shaved when a manager edits clock-in times or forgets a shift, and the worker only notices if they kept their own log.  
    *all hourly service workers* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Photograph the time clock, keep a notebook · source: knowledge
1593. **Unpredictable weekly income** — Because tips and hours swing wildly, workers cannot predict whether rent will be covered this month or how much to set aside from a good week.  
    *tipped and part-time service workers* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Rough mental budgeting · source: knowledge
1594. **Credit card tips arrive on delayed paycheck** — Card tips are paid out two weeks later on the paycheck rather than nightly, and the worker cannot reconcile what they earned per shift with what they were paid.  
    *servers and bartenders at chains* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Keep the nightly checkout slips and compare · source: knowledge
1595. **Missed break not paid** — Workers regularly skip legally required breaks on busy shifts but the timesheet auto-deducts the break, and nobody tracks the missed-break premium they are owed.  
    *hourly workers in states with break laws* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Tell the manager, usually forgotten · source: knowledge
1596. **Tracking cash tips for taxes** — Cash tips vary every shift and workers rarely record them, so tax time means guessing and risking under- or over-reporting.  
    *servers, bartenders, baristas and delivery staff* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Notes app, envelope of cash, guessing · source: knowledge
1597. **Verifying tip pool math** — Tip-outs to bussers, bar and hosts are calculated by a manager on a spreadsheet the worker never sees, so there is no way to confirm the split is right.  
    *tipped restaurant staff in pooled houses* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Trust the manager, argue occasionally · source: knowledge
1598. **Walkouts and short drawers charged to staff** — A table dish-and-dashes or the drawer is short and the worker is expected to cover it, with no record of who was where or which transactions were affected.  
    *servers and cashiers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Pay it, argue with management · source: knowledge
1599. **Overtime and hour caps opaque** — Workers do not know how close they are to the weekly overtime or part-time hour cap, so they accept shifts that later get cancelled or trigger a warning.  
    *part-timers near benefit or overtime thresholds* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Count hours by hand · source: knowledge
1600. **Splitting a shared tip jar fairly** — Cafe and counter staff split a tip jar by hours worked at the end of the day and the math is done on a napkin, causing frequent disputes.  
    *baristas, counter service and ice cream shop staff* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Napkin math, one trusted person counts · source: knowledge
1601. **Which shifts actually pay best** — Servers cannot tell whether Tuesday lunch or Friday patio is worth fighting for because nobody records tips per section, shift and day.  
    *servers and bartenders choosing shifts* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Gut feel, tribal knowledge · source: knowledge

### Training and onboarding

1602. **Learning POS button locations** — Every restaurant's POS has its own maze of screens and modifiers, and new hires slow down the whole line while hunting for the right button.  
    *new servers, cashiers and baristas* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Shadow a coworker, cheat sheet taped to the terminal · source: knowledge
1603. **Memorizing the menu quickly** — New servers must learn dozens of dishes, ingredients, allergens and pairings in a few days with only a printed menu, and get quizzed before they can take tables.  
    *new restaurant servers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Flashcards, photos of the menu, eating everything · source: knowledge
1604. **Menu changes not communicated** — Specials, 86'd items and recipe changes are announced verbally at pre-shift, so anyone who missed it sells items the kitchen no longer has.  
    *front-of-house staff* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Whiteboard in the kitchen, asking the expo · source: knowledge
1605. **Product knowledge across huge inventory** — Retail associates are asked detailed questions about hundreds of products and have no fast way to look up specs, stock or comparisons on the floor.  
    *retail sales associates* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Run to the back, search the store website on their phone · source: knowledge
1606. **Training shadowing with no checklist** — New hires are told to follow someone around for three shifts and nobody tracks which tasks they have actually been shown, so gaps show up on their first solo shift.  
    *new hires and their trainers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ask a lot of questions, get yelled at later · source: knowledge
1607. **Forgetting table numbers and seat positions** — New servers struggle to remember the floor plan and which seat is position one, leading to auctioned plates and wrong deliveries.  
    *new servers and food runners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Hand-drawn map, ask the host · source: knowledge
1608. **Recipe and build specs scattered** — Drink builds and dish plating specs live in a binder, photos on someone's phone and memory, so consistency drops whenever a regular is off.  
    *bartenders, baristas and line cooks* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ask a coworker, greasy binder · source: knowledge
1609. **Remembering wine and cocktail details** — Guests ask about the wine list and staff cannot recall tasting notes, regions or pairings for forty bottles without a cheat sheet.  
    *servers at upscale restaurants* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Fold-up cheat sheet in apron · source: knowledge
1610. **Tracking expiring certifications** — Food handler cards, alcohol server permits and first-aid certs expire on different dates and workers only find out when a manager pulls them off the floor.  
    *servers, bartenders and cooks* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manager reminds them, or they get caught · source: knowledge

<a id="professions-healthcare-education-trades"></a>

## Nurses, teachers & tradespeople

*healthcare staff, teachers, contractors, electricians, field workers*


### Classroom management

1611. **Chasing missing and late work** — Teachers keep mental lists of who owes what and repeatedly remind students and parents about missing assignments, especially before grades close.  
    *K-12 teachers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Gradebook reports, individual emails, missing-work slips · source: knowledge
1612. **Documenting behavior incidents in the moment** — Teachers are expected to log every behavior incident with time, antecedent and response, but cannot stop teaching to fill in a form, so records are reconstructed after school.  
    *K-12 teachers and paraprofessionals* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sticky notes, memory, end-of-day emails · source: knowledge
1613. **Tracking IEP accommodations per student** — General-education teachers must remember which of 120 students get extended time, preferential seating or read-aloud, and IEP documents are buried in a separate system.  
    *middle and high school teachers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Printed accommodation lists in a binder, sticky notes on the desk · source: knowledge
1614. **Keeping up with changing school schedules** — Assemblies, testing weeks, early releases and rotating block days change which class meets when, and teachers repeatedly show up prepared for the wrong period.  
    *middle and high school teachers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Printed bell schedules taped to the desk, morning emails · source: knowledge
1615. **Learning 150 student names quickly** — Secondary teachers meet 120-180 new students each year and struggle for weeks to match names, faces and pronunciations, especially when rosters keep changing.  
    *middle and high school teachers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Seating charts, name tents, printed roster photos · source: knowledge
1616. **Lending out books and calculators** — Classroom libraries, calculators and chargers disappear because there is no quick way to record which student borrowed what and when.  
    *teachers with classroom libraries or shared equipment* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sign-out clipboard, honor system · source: knowledge
1617. **Managing supplies bought with own money** — Teachers spend hundreds of dollars a year on classroom supplies and lose track of what they bought, what is left and which receipts count for the tax deduction.  
    *K-12 teachers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Receipts in a drawer, Amazon order history, wish lists · source: knowledge

### Customer communication

1618. **Answering the phone while on a job** — Solo tradespeople lose new customers because they cannot answer calls while under a sink or on a roof, and voicemails go unreturned until evening.  
    *solo plumbers, electricians, handymen* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spouse answering the phone, voicemail, calling back late · source: knowledge
1619. **Asking happy customers for reviews** — Small contractors depend on online reviews but forget to ask, feel awkward asking in person, and the customers who do post are often the unhappy ones.  
    *small trade businesses* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Occasional text with a Google link, business cards · source: knowledge
1620. **Explaining repair options to the customer** — Techs struggle to explain why a repair costs what it does or why replacement is better, and customers feel upsold, leading to bad reviews and declined work.  
    *HVAC, plumbing and electrical service techs* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Verbal explanation, hand-written good-better-best on the invoice · source: knowledge
1621. **Reminding customers about maintenance** — Techs know a furnace filter, water heater flush or backflow test is due but have no easy way to remind past customers, so repeat business is lost to whoever advertises first.  
    *HVAC, plumbing and irrigation companies* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Postcards, spreadsheet of past jobs, nothing · source: knowledge

### Documentation & handoff

1622. **Charting hours after the shift ends** — Nurses stay 45 to 90 minutes unpaid after every shift finishing documentation because the EHR is too slow and click-heavy to chart in real time.  
    *bedside nurses and hospitalists* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Paper 'brain sheet' scribbled during the shift and transcribed later · source: knowledge
1623. **Incomplete verbal handoff at shift change** — Shift report is rushed and verbal, so the oncoming nurse discovers hours later that a pending lab, a family request or a due dressing change was never mentioned.  
    *nurses and nursing assistants* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: SBAR paper sheets, sticky notes, calling the previous nurse at home · source: knowledge
1624. **Rebuilding the nurse brain sheet daily** — Every shift, nurses hand-draw a report sheet with room, meds, drips, labs and to-dos for each patient, then lose track of which items are done as the day gets chaotic.  
    *med-surg, ICU and ER nurses* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Printed templates, folded paper, pen on gloves · source: knowledge
1625. **Timing multiple medication and task deadlines** — A nurse has six patients with overlapping med passes, vitals, blood-sugar checks and drip titrations and has to keep all the times in their head, leading to late meds.  
    *floor nurses* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Watch alarms, phone timers, writing times on the brain sheet · source: knowledge
1626. **Unsafe patient ratios with no record** — Nurses are assigned more patients than is safe but have no simple way to log each unsafe assignment so a pattern can be shown to management or the union.  
    *hospital and nursing-home nurses* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Paper 'assignment despite objection' forms, personal notes · source: knowledge
1627. **Before-and-after photos lost in camera roll** — Contractors take dozens of job photos a day for documentation and disputes, and later cannot find which photo belongs to which job or customer.  
    *contractors, electricians, plumbers, restoration techs* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Scrolling the camera roll by date, texting photos to self · source: knowledge
1628. **Hunting for equipment on the unit** — Staff waste time every shift looking for the one working IV pump, bladder scanner, wheelchair or vitals machine that someone borrowed and did not return.  
    *nurses, CNAs, therapists* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Walking room to room, asking on the unit group chat · source: knowledge

### Field logistics & routing

1629. **Crew timesheets reconstructed on Friday** — Crew members remember and report their hours per job at the end of the week, which leads to inflated or missing hours and arguments about who was where.  
    *small contractors with hourly crews* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Text messages, paper timesheets, foreman's notebook · source: knowledge
1630. **Customers not home for the service window** — Techs give a four-hour window, arrive, and the customer is out or does not answer, wasting the slot; customers meanwhile complain they had no idea when the tech was coming.  
    *plumbers, HVAC techs, cable and appliance installers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calling 30 minutes ahead, office phone reminders · source: knowledge
1631. **Dispatch changes not reaching the tech** — The office reschedules or reorders jobs and the tech in the field finds out late, arriving at a cancelled call or the wrong address.  
    *service technicians and dispatchers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Phone calls, group texts, paper route sheet · source: knowledge
1632. **Forgotten parts require a second trip** — A tech arrives at a job and discovers the fitting, breaker or part they need is not on the truck, forcing a supply-house run and a lost hour.  
    *plumbers, electricians, HVAC and appliance techs* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Mental truck inventory, over-stocking, calling ahead to the customer · source: knowledge
1633. **Home-health visit route and mileage** — Home-health nurses and aides plan a day of visits by hand, get stuck in traffic between far-apart patients, and reconstruct mileage from memory for reimbursement.  
    *home-health nurses, hospice staff, home aides* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Maps one stop at a time, paper mileage log · source: knowledge
1634. **Tracking tools that walk off jobsites** — Expensive tools are lent between crew members and vans and go missing, and nobody knows who had the drill or the ladder last.  
    *contractors with crews, electricians, plumbers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Engraving tools, spreadsheets, Bluetooth trackers · source: knowledge
1635. **Finding the shutoff or panel on a repeat customer** — Returning to a property months later, a tech has to rediscover where the main shutoff, panel, crawlspace access or gate code is because notes from the last visit were never saved.  
    *plumbers, electricians, HVAC and pest techs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Asking the customer, memory, photos buried in the camera roll · source: knowledge

### Grading & lesson prep

1636. **Grading piles of assignments on weekends** — Teachers spend evenings and weekends grading stacks of papers and entering scores one by one into the gradebook, with no fast way to give individual feedback.  
    *K-12 teachers, especially English and humanities* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Rubrics, peer grading, grading only some assignments · source: knowledge
1637. **Differentiating one lesson for many levels** — A single class holds students reading three grade levels apart plus IEP and English-learner accommodations, and producing versions of every worksheet takes hours.  
    *K-12 classroom teachers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Reusing old materials, generic tiered worksheets · source: knowledge
1638. **Recreating lesson plans in required formats** — Teachers already have lessons but must retype them into the district's mandated template with standards codes and objectives each week for administrators who rarely read them.  
    *K-12 teachers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Copy-pasting old plans, Google Docs templates · source: knowledge
1639. **Writing usable sub plans on a sick day** — A teacher who wakes up sick has to write detailed substitute plans, find materials and email them before 6 a.m., which makes many teachers come in sick instead.  
    *K-12 teachers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Generic emergency sub folder, asking a colleague to cover · source: knowledge
1640. **Writing detailed report card comments** — Several times a year teachers write individualized comments for every student under a strict character limit, and the comment bank in the SIS is generic and hard to search.  
    *K-12 teachers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Personal comment bank in a document, copy-paste with name swaps · source: knowledge

### Licensing & compliance

1641. **Apprentice hours logging for licensure** — Electrical and plumbing apprentices must document thousands of supervised hours by task category and signature, and lose hours when the paper log is incomplete or a journeyman leaves.  
    *trade apprentices* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Paper logbook, spreadsheet, employer payroll records · source: knowledge
1642. **Expiring certifications go unnoticed** — BLS, ACLS, TB tests, fit tests, OSHA cards and trade licenses each expire on different dates, and staff only find out when HR pulls them off the schedule.  
    *healthcare staff, contractors, field technicians* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calendar reminders set inconsistently, HR nag emails · source: knowledge
1643. **Multi-state license renewal chaos** — Travel nurses and multi-state contractors hold licenses in several states with different renewal dates, fees, fingerprinting and CE rules and keep missing one.  
    *travel nurses, multi-state electricians and plumbers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheet, agency recruiter reminders · source: knowledge
1644. **Permit and inspection scheduling** — Contractors juggle permit applications, inspection appointments and inspector callbacks across multiple jurisdictions with different portals, and a missed inspection stalls the job.  
    *electricians, plumbers, general contractors* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Calendar entries, calling the building department, sticky notes on the dashboard · source: knowledge
1645. **Redoing onboarding paperwork every contract** — Travel and agency staff re-submit the same immunization records, skills checklists, references and background forms for every 13-week assignment.  
    *travel nurses, agency clinicians, locum staff* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Personal PDF folder in cloud storage, emailing the recruiter · source: knowledge
1646. **Tracking CEUs across renewal cycles** — Licensed professionals collect continuing-education certificates from many providers and scramble at renewal time to find them and confirm they meet each state's category rules.  
    *nurses, therapists, electricians, teachers, plumbers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Email folder, physical binder, spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1647. **Daily safety toolbox talk paperwork** — Crews must document a daily safety briefing and job hazard analysis on paper, get everyone to sign, and turn it in, and the forms are often lost or filled out at the end of the week.  
    *construction crews and foremen* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Paper forms on a clipboard, photos of forms texted to the office · source: knowledge
1648. **Looking up code requirements on site** — Electricians and plumbers need to confirm a wire gauge, box fill, venting rule or local amendment while on a ladder and end up flipping through a paper codebook or forum posts.  
    *electricians, plumbers, HVAC installers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Paper codebook in the truck, Google, asking a coworker · source: knowledge
1649. **Studying for boards during clinical shifts** — New graduates and students preparing for NCLEX or a trade licensing exam have only scattered minutes between tasks and cannot fit in structured study.  
    *nursing students, apprentices, new graduates* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Flashcard apps, question banks, cramming on days off · source: knowledge

### Parent & family communication

1650. **Parent messages at all hours** — Parents text and email teachers at night and on weekends through several channels and expect immediate replies, and teachers have no way to batch, log or set boundaries on it.  
    *K-12 teachers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Separate apps like ClassDojo or Remind, personal email rules · source: knowledge
1651. **Keeping a paper trail of parent contacts** — Administrators ask 'when did you contact the parent?' and teachers must dig through emails, call logs and memory to prove they reached out about a struggling student.  
    *K-12 teachers and counselors* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheet contact log, notes in the SIS · source: knowledge
1652. **Scheduling parent-teacher conferences** — Setting up conference slots for 30 to 150 families means back-and-forth emails, double bookings, no-shows and families who need a translator or a phone slot.  
    *K-12 teachers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sign-up sheets, SignUpGenius, paper forms sent home · source: knowledge
1653. **Translating messages for non-English families** — Teachers need to send notes home to families who speak a dozen languages and rely on students to interpret or on machine translation that mangles school terms.  
    *teachers in linguistically diverse schools* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Google Translate, bilingual aides, sending English only · source: knowledge

### Quotes, invoicing & payment

1654. **Chasing overdue invoices from customers** — Small contractors finish a job, send an invoice and then spend weeks texting and calling customers who pay late or dispute the amount, straining cash flow.  
    *plumbers, electricians, landscapers, handymen* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manual reminder texts, taking deposits, late fees rarely enforced · source: knowledge
1655. **Job costing after the fact** — Contractors do not know whether a job actually made money because labor hours, supply-house receipts and fuel are scattered across receipts, texts and memory.  
    *small contracting businesses* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Shoebox of receipts, QuickBooks entered weeks later · source: knowledge
1656. **Missing the tax deadline for 1099 contractors** — Self-employed tradespeople get surprised by quarterly estimated taxes because they never set aside a percentage of each payment as it comes in.  
    *self-employed contractors and subcontractors* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Separate savings account, accountant reminders · source: knowledge
1657. **Unbilled change orders and extras** — Customers ask for 'one more thing' mid-job, the crew does it, and nobody writes it down, so the extra work is forgotten at invoicing or disputed later.  
    *remodelers, electricians, general contractors* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Texts to the customer, verbal agreement, scribbles on the original quote · source: knowledge
1658. **Wasting the day on no-show estimates** — Contractors drive across town to quote a job and the homeowner is not home, has already hired someone else, or only wanted a free price to check their other bid.  
    *plumbers, electricians, HVAC and remodel contractors* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Confirmation call the morning of, charging a trip fee · source: knowledge
1659. **Writing quotes at night from memory** — After a day in the field the tradesperson sits at the kitchen table trying to remember measurements, materials and photos from each site visit to write up estimates.  
    *solo contractors and small trade businesses* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Phone photos, notebook, voice memos, generic estimate templates · source: knowledge

### Shift scheduling & swaps

1660. **Self-scheduling published too late** — The unit schedule comes out only a week or two before it starts, so staff cannot book childcare, appointments or travel without guessing.
