    *hospital nurses and respiratory therapists* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Asking the scheduler informally, screenshotting draft schedules · source: knowledge
1661. **Tracking hours for overtime and differentials** — Paychecks routinely miss night, weekend or charge differentials and missed-break pay, and staff have no easy way to reconstruct exactly what they worked to dispute it.  
    *shift-based healthcare workers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Photos of the badge-clock screen, personal notes app, spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1662. **Being called off or floated last minute** — Staff get a call two hours before a shift saying they are cancelled or floated to another unit, with no visibility into how call-offs are being rotated fairly.  
    *hospital nurses and per-diem staff* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Personal tally of call-offs, complaining to the manager · source: knowledge
1663. **Juggling multiple per-diem agency apps** — Per-diem nurses and CNAs check four different staffing apps and portals each day to find open shifts, each with its own login, pay rate and cancellation rules.  
    *per-diem and agency healthcare workers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Manually checking each app, screenshots, a notes list of which app pays what · source: knowledge

### Wellbeing & burnout

1664. **Debriefing after a traumatic shift** — After a code, a patient death or a violent incident, staff go home with no structured way to process it, and formal EAP support feels inaccessible and slow.  
    *ER, ICU and hospice staff, first responders* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Venting to coworkers, online nursing communities · source: knowledge
1665. **No time to eat or drink on shift** — Twelve-hour shifts routinely pass without a real break, and staff realize at 6 p.m. they have not eaten or used the restroom since morning.  
    *nurses, techs, teachers with no planning period* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: Pocket snacks, charting a missed break for pay · source: knowledge
1666. **Sleep wrecked by rotating night shifts** — Nurses rotating between day and night shifts cannot figure out when to sleep, eat or take medication, and feel permanently jet-lagged.  
    *night-shift and rotating healthcare staff* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Blackout curtains, melatonin, trial and error · source: knowledge
1667. **Body-aches and injury tracking for physical work** — Nurses and tradespeople develop back, knee and shoulder problems from repetitive work but never log the strains, so by the time it is a workers' comp claim there is no history.  
    *nurses, CNAs, construction and trade workers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Ignoring it, occasional doctor visit · source: knowledge
1668. **Tracking uncompensated after-hours teacher work** — Teachers do hours of grading, planning, club supervision and emails outside contract time and have no record to support workload complaints or union bargaining.  
    *K-12 teachers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Rough estimates, occasional union surveys · source: knowledge

<a id="dating-events"></a>

## Dating, weddings & events

*dating logistics, weddings, parties, group trips, shared expenses, RSVPs*


### Attending as a guest

1669. **Coordinating rides to the venue** — Guests staying at the same hotel each book separate rideshares to a remote venue, then scramble for a ride back after the shuttle leaves.  
    *wedding and event guests* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask in the group chat who is driving · source: knowledge
1670. **Hotel room block deadline missed** — Guests forget the cut-off date for the discounted wedding hotel block and end up paying full rate or staying far from the venue.  
    *wedding guests travelling to a wedding* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: None; rely on the couple sending a reminder · source: knowledge
1671. **Too many weddings in one summer** — Guests invited to five or six weddings in a season cannot see the combined cost of travel, gifts, outfits and time off before committing, and end up overspending.  
    *people in their late twenties and thirties* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Rough mental math and regret · source: knowledge
1672. **Wedding invitation details buried in email** — Guests receive the venue address, hotel block code, dress code and schedule in scattered emails, texts and cards, and cannot find them on the day.  
    *wedding guests* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Search email inbox in the taxi · source: knowledge

### Dating logistics

1673. **Endless back-and-forth to pick a date time** — Scheduling a first date takes days of 'how about Thursday?' messages because neither person's availability is visible to the other, and the match often fizzles before a time is agreed.  
    *people using dating apps* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Propose two or three slots by text and hope one sticks · source: knowledge
1674. **No safe way to share date details** — People want a friend to know where they are, who they are meeting and when they should be home, but doing it means manually texting screenshots and remembering to check in afterwards.  
    *people meeting online matches in person, especially women* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Text a friend the match's name and share live location manually · source: knowledge
1675. **Choosing a first-date venue every time** — Each new date requires researching a bar or cafe that is quiet, mid-priced, roughly halfway between both people and open at the right hour, and the research restarts from zero every time.  
    *active daters in cities* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Keep reusing the same two default spots · source: knowledge
1676. **Deciding who pays on a date** — The moment the bill arrives is awkward every time: nobody knows whether to split, alternate or let one person cover it, and there is no agreed norm to fall back on.  
    *daters of all ages* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: Offer to split and read the reaction · source: knowledge
1677. **Forgetting details before a second date** — Before a second or third date, people cannot remember the other person's sibling names, job title or the story they told, and asking again looks like not listening.  
    *people in early dating* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Re-read the app chat history or wing it · source: knowledge
1678. **Losing track of multiple conversations** — When talking to several matches across two or three apps, people forget who mentioned what, which one they already asked about their job, and which conversation went cold.  
    *people dating on more than one app* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Scroll back through chats before replying, or keep a notes-app list · source: knowledge
1679. **Running out of date ideas** — Couples default to dinner and a movie because thinking of something new that fits budget, weather and both people's energy on a given evening is a chore in itself.  
    *couples, especially long-term ones* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Google 'date ideas near me' and pick the first result · source: knowledge
1680. **Scheduling dates around two calendars** — Couples with different work schedules or shift patterns struggle to find overlapping free evenings and end up going weeks without a proper date.  
    *couples with shift work or busy jobs* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Compare screenshots of calendars over text · source: knowledge
1681. **Cancelling a date politely and on time** — When plans need to change, people put off the awkward cancellation message until the last minute, wasting the other person's evening.  
    *people in early dating* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: Ghost or send a late apology · source: knowledge

### Gifts and registries

1682. **Deciding how much to give as a gift** — Guests agonise over the appropriate cash gift or registry amount for each wedding, which varies by relationship, region and whether they are bringing a plus-one.  
    *wedding guests* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Ask friends or search online etiquette guides · source: knowledge
1683. **Group gift collection is awkward** — Pooling money from coworkers or friends for a big gift means one person collecting cash and IOUs, nagging people who forgot, and fronting the shortfall.  
    *coworkers and friend groups* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Envelope passed around the office or a Venmo request thread · source: knowledge
1684. **Registry split across multiple stores** — Couples want items from several retailers, but managing registries in three places means guests buy duplicates and the couple cannot see one combined list of what was purchased.  
    *engaged couples and shower hosts* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Universal registry sites with inconsistent syncing · source: knowledge
1685. **Remembering friends' birthdays and anniversaries** — Without Facebook reminders, people miss close friends' birthdays or discover an anniversary the day of, with no time to get a gift or plan anything.  
    *adults maintaining friendships* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manual calendar entries that are incomplete · source: knowledge
1686. **Wedding thank-you note tracking** — After the wedding, couples cannot remember who gave which gift, which cards have been written and which addresses are missing, so notes go out months late or not at all.  
    *newlyweds* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Notebook by the gift table, often incomplete · source: knowledge

### Group trips

1687. **Group trip dies in the planning stage** — A friend-group trip gets enthusiastic agreement in the chat, then stalls for months because nobody commits to dates or a deposit and no one wants to be the organiser.  
    *friend groups planning holidays* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: One person takes charge and books it or the trip never happens · source: knowledge
1688. **Trip itinerary scattered across apps** — Flight confirmations, the Airbnb address, restaurant bookings and activity tickets for a group trip live in different inboxes and apps, so nobody but the organiser knows the plan.  
    *group travellers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A shared Google Doc or a long pinned message · source: knowledge
1689. **Group votes on trip decisions** — Choosing the destination, the rental house and the activities requires consensus among eight people, and the group chat produces opinions but never a decision.  
    *friend groups planning trips* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Emoji reactions on options or the loudest person decides · source: knowledge
1690. **Herding the group on the trip itself** — Once on the trip, gathering everyone for a reservation or departure means calling each person to find out where they are and when they will be ready.  
    *group trip organisers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Repeated messages and location sharing · source: knowledge
1691. **Packing and shared item duplication** — On a group camping or beach trip, three people bring speakers and nobody brings a first-aid kit because there is no shared list of who brings what.  
    *group trip participants* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A message asking 'who has a cooler?' · source: knowledge

### Parties and hosting

1692. **Dietary restrictions collected too late** — Hosts find out about vegan, gluten-free or allergy needs on the day, after the food has been cooked or the caterer has been paid.  
    *dinner party and event hosts* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Ask in the group chat and hope everyone replies · source: knowledge
1693. **Headcount unknown until the last minute** — Hosts have to buy food and drink for a party without knowing whether 8 or 25 people will actually turn up, because 'maybe' and silence are the most common responses.  
    *people hosting home parties* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Over-buy and eat leftovers for a week · source: knowledge
1694. **Party supply quantity guesswork** — Hosts do not know how much food, ice, drink and how many cups to buy for a given number of guests and party length, and consistently get it wrong.  
    *home party hosts* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Search 'how much alcohol for 30 people' each time · source: knowledge
1695. **Potluck dish coordination** — Without a sign-up, potlucks end up with six desserts, no mains and three people bringing the same pasta salad.  
    *potluck hosts and attendees* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A group text asking 'what is everyone bringing?' · source: knowledge
1696. **Recurring game night scheduling** — A standing board-game or poker night requires a poll every single week to find a night that works for at least four people, and it often gets cancelled.  
    *friend groups with regular meetups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: WhatsApp poll every week · source: knowledge
1697. **Surprise party secrecy breaks** — Organising a surprise party requires a guest list and chat that excludes exactly one person, and someone always adds them or mentions it in a shared thread.  
    *friends and family planning surprise parties* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Create a separate group chat and remind everyone to be quiet · source: knowledge
1698. **Collaborative party playlist arguments** — Guests want to add songs at a party but only the host's phone is connected, so the phone gets passed around and the music stops or someone deletes the queue.  
    *party hosts and guests* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Shared Spotify collaborative playlist made in advance · source: knowledge

### RSVPs and invitations

1699. **Chasing guests who never RSVP** — A third of invitees never respond by the deadline, and the couple has to personally message each one while the caterer waits for a final headcount.  
    *couples and event hosts* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manual text or call to each non-responder · source: knowledge
1700. **Guests say yes then flake** — People who confirmed drop out on the day with no notice, leaving hosts with empty seats, wasted food and rearranged plans.  
    *hosts of dinners and parties* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Send a reminder the day before and hope · source: knowledge
1701. **Kids' party RSVPs from unknown parents** — Parents invite a whole class but do not have the other parents' contact details, so RSVPs trickle in through paper slips, the teacher or not at all.  
    *parents of school-age children* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Paper invitations in backpacks and a class WhatsApp group · source: knowledge
1702. **Doodle polls that nobody fills in** — Hosts send a date poll and half the group never votes, so the organiser picks a date anyway and then gets complaints from the silent majority.  
    *anyone organising a group outing* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Nag individually or decide unilaterally · source: knowledge
1703. **Facebook events unusable for non-users** — Hosts who create a Facebook event cannot reach friends who left Facebook, and those friends miss out on updates and the RSVP entirely.  
    *hosts with friends off social media* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Duplicate the event in a text thread · source: knowledge
1704. **Guests forgetting the event exists** — People RSVP yes to a party three weeks out and then simply forget, because it never made it into their calendar.  
    *event hosts and guests* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Host sends a reminder text the day before · source: knowledge
1705. **Invitation buried in the group chat** — Party details posted in a busy group chat scroll out of view within hours, so people keep asking 'what time again?' and 'what's the address?'.  
    *friend groups organising via messaging apps* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Pin the message or repost it repeatedly · source: knowledge
1706. **Invite spam and hidden ads** — Free invitation services plaster ads on the invite, sell guest emails and send marketing to the guest list, embarrassing the host.  
    *people using free e-invite tools* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pay for premium or design a graphic in Canva and text it · source: knowledge
1707. **Invites that need an account to open** — Invitation platforms force guests to create an account or download an app just to see the details or RSVP, so older relatives and casual friends give up.  
    *event hosts and their guests* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Send a plain text with the info instead · source: knowledge
1708. **Plus-one confusion on invitations** — Guests cannot tell whether their invitation includes a partner or children, and hosts field awkward questions or get surprise extra attendees.  
    *wedding and formal event hosts and guests* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Address envelopes very carefully and answer texts · source: knowledge

### Shared expenses

1709. **Fronting the group Airbnb deposit** — One person pays the full accommodation up front for the group and carries hundreds or thousands of dollars of risk while others pay back late or drop out.  
    *group trip organisers* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Splitwise and repeated reminders · source: knowledge
1710. **Bachelor party cost disputes** — One organiser fronts the Airbnb, the activities and the bar tab for a bachelor or bachelorette weekend, then spends weeks chasing repayment from people who dispute what they owe.  
    *bachelor/bachelorette party organisers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Splitwise or a spreadsheet plus repeated Venmo requests · source: knowledge
1711. **Late joiners and early leavers split** — When some people join a trip for only two of five nights, working out a fair share of accommodation, groceries and the rental car becomes a spreadsheet nightmare.  
    *group travellers with partial attendance* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manual per-night calculations in a spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1712. **Chasing friends for small debts** — Being owed $12 here and $30 there by several friends adds up, but sending reminders feels petty so people silently absorb the loss.  
    *the friend who usually pays first* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Let it go or send a passive-aggressive Venmo request · source: knowledge
1713. **Expense app fees and paywalls** — Popular bill-splitting apps now limit the number of expenses per day or put basic features behind a subscription, so groups revert to spreadsheets mid-trip.  
    *users of bill-splitting apps* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Switch apps or use a shared note · source: knowledge
1714. **Group members not on the same payment app** — Settling up fails because one friend only has PayPal, another only Venmo, and the international friend has neither, so debts linger for months.  
    *mixed friend groups, especially cross-border* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Cash next time you meet · source: knowledge
1715. **Multi-currency trip expense tracking** — On a trip abroad, expenses get paid in different currencies by different people with different cards, and reconciling who owes whom in home currency is error-prone.  
    *friends travelling internationally* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Estimate exchange rates and round · source: knowledge
1716. **Splitting a restaurant bill by item** — A group of ten wants to pay only for what they ordered plus a share of shared plates, tax and tip, and the maths takes ten minutes while the server waits.  
    *friends eating out in groups* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: One person pays and everyone Venmos a rough amount · source: knowledge
1717. **Uneven room assignment and cost** — In a rented house one couple gets the master suite and one person gets the sofa, but everyone is asked to pay the same share, causing resentment.  
    *friends sharing holiday rentals* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Awkward negotiation in the chat or just split evenly · source: knowledge

### Wedding planning

1718. **Wedding budget spirals out of control** — Couples set a wedding budget, then hundreds of small line items (postage, tips, alterations, favors) push the total far past it before anyone notices.  
    *engaged couples* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: A shared spreadsheet that quickly goes stale · source: knowledge
1719. **Bridesmaid group chat chaos** — Bridal-party planning (dress orders, bachelorette dates, shower duties, cost splits) happens in a single group chat where decisions get buried under hundreds of messages.  
    *bridesmaids, groomsmen and maids of honour* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Pin messages or make a separate Google Doc nobody updates · source: knowledge
1720. **Collecting mailing addresses for invitations** — Getting a current postal address for 150 guests means individually texting people, and the replies arrive in different formats over several weeks.  
    *couples sending paper invitations* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Text everyone one by one and paste replies into a sheet · source: knowledge
1721. **Comparing vendor quotes apples-to-apples** — Photographer or caterer quotes each bundle different things (hours, travel, second shooter, tax, service charge), so comparing five of them fairly takes an evening of manual normalisation.  
    *couples booking wedding vendors* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Build a comparison spreadsheet by hand · source: knowledge
1722. **Day-of timeline shared with vendors** — The wedding-day schedule changes repeatedly and the photographer, DJ, caterer and bridal party each end up with a different version.  
    *couples, planners and bridal parties* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Emailing a PDF timeline and re-emailing on every change · source: knowledge
1723. **Guest list trapped in a messy spreadsheet** — The wedding guest list lives in a spreadsheet with inconsistent names, missing addresses, plus-one uncertainty and duplicate rows from both families editing it.  
    *engaged couples and parents* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Sheets with colour-coded columns · source: knowledge
1724. **Seating chart that keeps changing** — Every late RSVP, cancellation or family feud forces the seating chart to be reshuffled, and paper or sticky-note versions cannot keep up with the changes.  
    *couples and wedding planners* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sticky notes on poster board or a spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1725. **Tracking vendor deposits and due dates** — Each wedding vendor has its own deposit, balance due date, contract and contact person, and missing one payment or notice deadline can void a booking.  
    *engaged couples and their planners* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calendar reminders plus a folder of PDF contracts · source: knowledge
1726. **Venue and vendor availability checking** — Finding a venue means emailing twenty places to ask whether a given Saturday is free, waiting days for replies, and discovering the good ones are booked a year out.  
    *couples and large-event planners* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Mass emails and phone calls · source: knowledge
1727. **Guests ignore the wedding website** — Couples put hotel blocks, dress code and schedule on a wedding website, yet still field the same questions by text dozens of times because guests never open it.  
    *engaged couples* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Copy-paste the same answer to each guest · source: knowledge

<a id="sustainability-energy"></a>

## Sustainability & energy

*recycling rules, waste, energy bills, water, secondhand, repair*


### Energy bills

1728. **Energy bill higher than expected** — The monthly bill jumps and there is no way to tell whether it was the heating, the dryer, a rate change, or a meter misread.  
    *bill-paying households* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: compare last year's bill and shrug · source: knowledge
1729. **Estimated bills instead of actual readings** — Utilities bill on estimates for months, then send a huge catch-up bill when a real reading is finally taken.  
    *households without smart meters* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: submit meter readings manually and remember to do it every month · source: knowledge
1730. **Fixed-rate contract ending unnoticed** — A cheap fixed tariff quietly rolls onto an expensive variable rate when it ends, and the customer only notices after paying more for months.  
    *energy customers on fixed-term plans* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminder set a year ahead, if remembered · source: knowledge
1731. **Splitting utilities among housemates** — Utility bills arrive in one person's name and dividing them fairly, chasing payments, and handling someone who moved out mid-cycle is a recurring argument.  
    *renters sharing a house* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheet and group-chat reminders · source: knowledge
1732. **Utility tariffs impossible to compare** — Comparing electricity or gas plans means decoding standing charges, unit rates, time-of-use bands, exit fees, and promotional credits that all differ in structure.  
    *anyone shopping for an energy supplier* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: comparison sites that push sponsored deals · source: knowledge
1733. **Direct debit set far above usage** — The supplier sets the monthly payment well above actual consumption, building a large credit balance that is hard to get refunded.  
    *energy customers paying by direct debit* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: argue with customer service to lower the amount · source: knowledge
1734. **Remembering to submit meter readings** — Meter readings must be submitted in a narrow window each month, the meter is in an awkward cupboard, and forgetting means another estimated bill.  
    *households on manual meter reading* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: phone reminders and photographing the meter · source: knowledge

### Energy usage

1735. **Heating schedule does not match life** — The thermostat runs the same schedule every day even when the house is empty, and adjusting it for a changing shift pattern is fiddly.  
    *households with programmable heating* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manually turn heating on and off · source: knowledge
1736. **Time-of-use rates hard to act on** — On a time-of-use tariff the cheap hours change by season and weekday, and nobody remembers when to run the dishwasher or charge the car.  
    *households on time-of-use or dynamic pricing* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a sticky note on the appliance · source: knowledge
1737. **Which appliance eats the electricity** — Total consumption is known from the meter but nobody can tell whether the old fridge, the tumble dryer, or the always-on gaming PC is the real culprit.  
    *households trying to cut electricity use* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: buy a plug-in power meter and test appliances one by one · source: knowledge
1738. **Is my usage normal for my home** — People do not know whether their consumption is high or low for a home of their size and household count, so they cannot tell if there is a problem.  
    *energy-conscious households* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ask on forums with wildly different answers · source: knowledge
1739. **Smart meter display is useless** — The in-home display shows a live number in pounds or kilowatts but gives no history, no comparison, and no idea what is normal.  
    *households with smart meters* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: ignore the display · source: knowledge
1740. **Standby power drain unnoticed** — Chargers, TVs, consoles, and set-top boxes draw power all day and people have no sense of how much that adds to the bill.  
    *gadget-heavy households* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: switch everything off at the wall occasionally · source: knowledge

### Home efficiency

1741. **Landlord will not fix draughts or insulation** — Renters pay high heating bills for a poorly insulated flat and have no leverage or evidence to push the landlord to improve it.  
    *renters in older housing* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: draught excluders and extra jumpers · source: knowledge
1742. **Efficiency upgrades: which pays off** — Choosing between loft insulation, a heat pump, new windows, or solar is a guessing game without knowing costs, savings, and payback for this specific house.  
    *homeowners planning improvements* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: get three quotes and trust the salesperson · source: knowledge
1743. **Rebates and grants too hard to find** — Government and utility rebates for insulation, heat pumps, or efficient appliances exist but eligibility rules, deadlines, and paperwork are scattered and confusing.  
    *homeowners buying appliances or upgrades* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: miss the rebate, or find out after purchase · source: knowledge
1744. **Solar output and payback tracking** — Solar owners cannot easily see whether panels are generating what was promised, how much is exported, and whether the system is on track to pay back.  
    *households with rooftop solar* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: inverter app with confusing graphs · source: knowledge
1745. **Energy performance rating means nothing** — A home's efficiency certificate lists recommendations but gives no sense of what they would actually cost or save for the current occupants.  
    *home buyers and renters* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: ignore the certificate · source: knowledge
1746. **Using solar power at the right time** — Households with solar but no battery want to run the washing machine when the sun is out but cannot predict generation or remember to check.  
    *households with rooftop solar* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: look at the sky and guess · source: knowledge

### Recycling rules

1747. **Not knowing if an item is recyclable** — Standing at the bin holding a coffee cup lid, a pizza box, or a chip bag and having no idea whether it goes in recycling or trash for this specific town.  
    *anyone sorting household waste* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: guess, or search the council/city website each time · source: knowledge
1748. **Recycling rules change after moving** — Every city, county, or building has different recycling rules, so what was fine in the last place gets a whole bin rejected in the new one.  
    *people who recently moved or travel between homes* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: dig through the new municipality's PDF or ask neighbours · source: knowledge
1749. **Where to drop off hard-to-recycle items** — Batteries, light bulbs, paint, electronics, plastic film, textiles, and styrofoam cannot go in the curbside bin, and finding a nearby drop-off point with current hours is a research project each time.  
    *anyone clearing out a drawer or garage* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a bag of dead batteries accumulates in a drawer for years · source: knowledge
1750. **Plastic resin number confusion** — The triangle with a number on plastic looks like a recycling symbol but does not mean the item is accepted locally, and nobody knows which numbers their program takes.  
    *households trying to recycle correctly* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: throw all plastic in recycling and hope (wish-cycling) · source: knowledge

### Repair

1751. **Repair or replace decision** — When the washing machine or laptop breaks, there is no way to know if repair is worth it compared to the price and lifespan of a replacement.  
    *anyone with a broken appliance or device* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: get a diagnostic quote and guess · source: knowledge
1752. **Appliance maintenance forgotten** — Descaling the kettle, cleaning the dryer vent, changing the water filter, and servicing the boiler all get forgotten until something fails.  
    *homeowners and renters* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notice when performance drops · source: knowledge
1753. **Finding a trustworthy local repairer** — Finding someone who repairs shoes, phones, vacuums, or bikes nearby, with fair prices and real reviews, is harder than buying new.  
    *people wanting to repair rather than replace* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Maps and asking on local groups · source: knowledge
1754. **Identifying the right spare part** — Finding the exact replacement belt, filter, seal, or screen for an appliance model requires locating a model number plate and decoding part diagrams.  
    *DIY repairers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: photograph the label and search part sites · source: knowledge
1755. **Losing manuals and warranty info** — When something breaks nobody can find the receipt, the warranty end date, or the manual, so warranty claims are missed.  
    *households with many appliances and gadgets* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a drawer full of paper manuals and a hunt through email · source: knowledge
1756. **Clothing repair and alterations** — A broken zip or torn seam means the garment sits in a pile for months because finding a tailor and knowing what it should cost is unclear.  
    *anyone with damaged clothing* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: throw it out or attempt a YouTube fix · source: knowledge
1757. **Repair cafés hard to discover** — Volunteer repair cafés fix things for free but run on irregular dates in community halls, so most people never hear about them.  
    *people with broken small appliances or clothing* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge

### Secondhand buying & selling

1758. **Cross-posting secondhand listings** — Selling an item means writing the same listing and uploading the same photos on Marketplace, eBay, Vinted, Craigslist, and Gumtree, then deleting all of them when it sells.  
    *people selling used goods* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: copy-paste, or list on only one platform · source: knowledge
1759. **Marketplace buyers who ghost or lowball** — Most messages are 'is this available?' followed by silence, no-shows at the agreed time, or offers at a third of the price.  
    *people selling on local marketplaces* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: keep several buyers lined up and expect no-shows · source: knowledge
1760. **Marketplace scam messages** — Sellers and buyers are bombarded with fake payment links, overpayment scams, and 'send a verification code' tricks and cannot tell genuine buyers from fraudsters.  
    *anyone on classified marketplaces* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: cash only, meet in public, block suspicious accounts · source: knowledge
1761. **Borrowing tools instead of buying** — A drill or carpet cleaner is needed once a year, and there is no easy way to find a neighbour or tool library that will lend one.  
    *homeowners and renters doing occasional DIY* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: buy it and let it gather dust · source: knowledge
1762. **Donating items nobody will take** — Charity shops reject furniture without fire labels, mattresses, and old electronics, and finding a place that accepts a specific item takes hours.  
    *people decluttering or moving* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: drive around several shops or leave it on the kerb · source: knowledge
1763. **Giving items away for free** — Buy-nothing groups and freecycle threads involve dozens of comments, picking a recipient, and porch pickups that never happen.  
    *people giving away household items* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Facebook groups with first-come-first-served chaos · source: knowledge
1764. **Missing good secondhand deals** — Wanting a specific used item (a particular bike frame, a KitchenAid, a desk) means checking several apps daily because good listings go in minutes.  
    *bargain hunters* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: saved searches on each app, refreshed compulsively · source: knowledge
1765. **Pricing a used item** — No idea what a used bike, pram, or sofa actually sells for locally, so items either sit unsold for weeks or go for far too little.  
    *casual sellers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: scan sold listings by hand · source: knowledge
1766. **Thrift stores are hit and miss** — Thrifting a specific size or item means visiting many stores with no inventory search, so trips often yield nothing.  
    *thrift shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: visit regularly and hope · source: knowledge

### Sustainable habits

1767. **Greenwashing claims on products** — Shoppers cannot tell whether 'eco', 'natural', or 'carbon neutral' labels mean anything and have no quick way to verify a product's actual credentials.  
    *shoppers trying to buy sustainably* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: trust the label or ignore it · source: knowledge
1768. **Sustainable choices feel invisible** — Cycling, composting, and secondhand buying save money and emissions but there is no feedback, so motivation fades.  
    *people trying to build greener habits* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1769. **Tracking a personal carbon footprint** — People who want to cut their footprint have no realistic picture of where it comes from, and existing calculators are one-off surveys with rough averages.  
    *climate-conscious individuals* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: an annual online calculator · source: knowledge

### Waste collection

1770. **Bulky item pickup booking hassle** — Getting rid of an old mattress or sofa means finding out whether the city offers bulky pickup, what it costs, which items are allowed, and booking a slot weeks out.  
    *people replacing furniture or appliances* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: leave it on the kerb and hope, or pay a junk hauler · source: knowledge
1771. **Forgetting bin collection day** — Missing the weekly collection because the bins were not put out, or dragging them out on the wrong day, especially when the schedule alternates between recycling and garden waste weeks.  
    *households with kerbside collection* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: watch what neighbours put out, or a paper calendar on the fridge · source: knowledge
1772. **Holiday shifts to collection schedule** — Public holidays push collection by a day and nobody finds out until the bin is left uncollected for another week.  
    *households with kerbside collection* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: check the council website when the truck does not come · source: knowledge
1773. **Missed collection with no easy report** — When the truck skips a street, reporting it means finding the right form, entering the address again, and never hearing back whether it will be re-collected.  
    *residents whose bins were skipped* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: phone the council and wait on hold · source: knowledge
1774. **Shared bins overflowing in apartment blocks** — In shared buildings the recycling bins are full or contaminated by one neighbour, and there is no way to tell who or to get the bin emptied sooner.  
    *apartment and condo residents* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: complain to building management by email · source: knowledge

### Waste reduction

1775. **Compost contamination and what goes in** — Unclear whether meat, dairy, compostable packaging, tea bags, or coffee pods are allowed in the food waste caddy or backyard compost, and rules differ by program.  
    *households with food-waste collection or a compost bin* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: google each item or skip composting it · source: knowledge
1776. **Compostable packaging with nowhere to go** — Packaging labelled compostable or biodegradable is not accepted by curbside compost or recycling, so it ends up in landfill anyway and people feel misled.  
    *shoppers buying eco-labelled products* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: bin it · source: knowledge
1777. **Deposit return bottle hassle** — Returning bottles and cans for deposit means finding a working reverse-vending machine that accepts the brand, waiting in line, and dealing with rejected containers.  
    *residents in deposit-return regions* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: give up and put them in recycling, forfeiting the deposit · source: knowledge
1778. **Not knowing how much waste you produce** — People who want to cut waste have no idea how many bags they throw out per week or whether their efforts are making any difference.  
    *households trying to reduce waste* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1779. **Packaging waste from online orders** — Every delivery brings boxes, air pillows, and plastic film that pile up faster than they can be recycled, with no way to prefer low-packaging sellers.  
    *frequent online shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: break down boxes and hope the film is accepted somewhere · source: knowledge
1780. **Refill station locations unknown** — People who want to refill detergent, shampoo, or dry goods cannot find which nearby shops offer refills or what products they stock.  
    *low-waste shoppers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: word of mouth and Instagram · source: knowledge
1781. **Unwanted junk mail and catalogues** — A stream of catalogues, flyers, and credit offers arrives weekly and opting out requires contacting each sender separately.  
    *homeowners and renters with a mailbox* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: recycle it all, or a no-junk-mail sticker that half of senders ignore · source: knowledge

### Water

1782. **Hidden water leak found on the bill** — A running toilet or dripping tap goes unnoticed for months until the water bill doubles, and by then hundreds of litres are wasted.  
    *metered water customers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: read the meter with everything off and see if it moves · source: knowledge
1783. **No idea of daily water use** — Unlike electricity, water use is invisible: long showers, garden watering, and laundry all add up and nobody knows which matters.  
    *households trying to save water* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1784. **Rain barrel and garden watering timing** — Deciding whether to water the garden depends on recent and forecast rain, soil, and plant needs, and people either over- or under-water.  
    *home gardeners* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: stick a finger in the soil · source: knowledge
1785. **Water bill tiers and sewer charges** — Water bills combine tiered usage, sewer, stormwater, and fixed charges so people cannot tell what lowering consumption would actually save.  
    *metered water customers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pay it without understanding · source: knowledge
1786. **Watering restrictions unclear** — During droughts, watering days and permitted hours change by area and are announced on local news or council pages that nobody reads.  
    *gardeners and homeowners with lawns* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: copy what neighbours do and risk a fine · source: knowledge

<a id="immigrants-expats-language"></a>

## Immigrants, expats & language barriers

*visas, paperwork abroad, translation, banking abroad, remittances, integration*


### Banking abroad

1787. **Cannot open a bank account without an address** — Banks require proof of address, landlords require a bank account, and employers require a bank account for salary, creating a loop new arrivals cannot break.  
    *new arrivals in their first weeks* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: neobanks, friends' addresses, hostel letters · source: knowledge
1788. **Filing taxes in two countries** — Immigrants must know whether they owe tax at home, abroad or both, what treaties apply and which foreign accounts to declare, with heavy penalties for mistakes.  
    *expats, especially US citizens abroad* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: expensive specialist accountants · source: knowledge
1789. **No credit history in the new country** — Years of good credit at home count for nothing, so immigrants are denied phone contracts, credit cards and mortgages or charged high deposits.  
    *skilled immigrants and expats* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: secured cards, paying deposits, waiting years · source: knowledge
1790. **Home bank blocks logins from abroad** — The home-country bank app requires a local SIM for one-time codes, flags foreign logins as fraud, or closes accounts of non-residents.  
    *immigrants keeping home accounts* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: keeping an old SIM alive, relatives with access · source: knowledge
1791. **Managing accounts in two or more currencies** — Salary, rent, savings and family support sit in different currencies and banks, and no single view shows total net worth or monthly spend in one currency.  
    *expats with home-country obligations* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheets with manual exchange rates · source: knowledge
1792. **Pension and social contributions lost across borders** — Years of contributions to different national pension systems are hard to track, combine or claim, and people do not know what they will get.  
    *long-term migrant workers* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1793. **Proving income and finances across countries** — Landlords, lenders and visa officers want income proof in local formats, but the applicant's payslips, tax returns and statements are foreign and unrecognized.  
    *recently relocated professionals* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: translations plus cover letters · source: knowledge
1794. **Cost of living surprises in a new country** — Newcomers do not know what a fair rent, utility bill, phone plan or grocery basket costs locally and overpay or get scammed.  
    *new arrivals* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking in expat groups · source: knowledge
1795. **Hidden fees on international card payments** — Foreign transaction fees, dynamic currency conversion and ATM markups are unclear at the point of payment and add up to significant monthly losses.  
    *expats paying across borders* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: carrying multiple cards, rules of thumb · source: knowledge

### Integration & social

1796. **Finding immigrant-friendly professionals** — Locating a doctor, lawyer, accountant or hairdresser who speaks your language or understands immigrant situations relies on word of mouth.  
    *immigrants of every stage* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: asking in community groups · source: knowledge
1797. **Finding local friends beyond the expat bubble** — Newcomers end up socializing only with other foreigners, and there is no low-pressure way to meet locals who are open to friendship with immigrants.  
    *expats after the first months* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: expat meetups, sports clubs · source: knowledge
1798. **Isolation of accompanying spouses** — Partners who relocated for the other's job often cannot work immediately, lack a network and the language, and struggle to find purpose and community.  
    *trailing spouses* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: expat spouse groups · source: knowledge
1799. **Verifying advice from expat groups** — Facebook and WhatsApp groups are full of confident but outdated or wrong advice about permits, taxes and rights, with no way to tell what is current.  
    *immigrants seeking information* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: cross-checking many threads · source: knowledge
1800. **Finding ingredients and products from home** — Locating familiar foods, medicines and brands, or acceptable substitutes, in a new country takes trial and error across many shops.  
    *immigrants settling in* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: community recommendations · source: knowledge
1801. **Keeping home-country ties administratively alive** — Voting from abroad, renewing a home passport at a distant consulate, and maintaining home ID cards involve deadlines and appointments easy to miss.  
    *long-term expats* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: consulate newsletters · source: knowledge
1802. **Missing unwritten cultural norms** — Tipping, punctuality, neighbour etiquette, gift customs and workplace hierarchy are learned by embarrassing mistakes rather than any reliable source.  
    *newcomers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking colleagues, observing · source: knowledge
1803. **Shipping and customs for personal belongings** — Sending or receiving personal items and gifts across borders incurs unexpected customs fees, prohibited-item rejections and lost parcels.  
    *expats and their families* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking others what got through · source: knowledge
1804. **Time-zone coordination with family at home** — Keeping in touch with family across large time differences leads to missed calls and guilt, and there is no shared view of when both sides are free.  
    *immigrants with family abroad* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mental math and fixed weekend calls · source: knowledge

### Local bureaucracy

1805. **Navigating a foreign healthcare system** — Registering with a doctor, understanding referrals, insurance coverage and how to reach emergency or out-of-hours care differs completely from home.  
    *newly arrived families* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: expat groups and trial visits · source: knowledge
1806. **Sequenced setup steps block each other** — Getting a tax ID, bank account, SIM, health insurance and lease each depends on another, and nobody explains the correct order for the specific country.  
    *arrivals in first 3 months* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: blog posts and trial and error · source: knowledge
1807. **Documents scattered across formats and countries** — Passports, apostilles, translations, certificates and permits exist as paper, scans and photos in several places, and the right version is never at hand when an office demands it.  
    *all immigrants* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: cloud folders and phone galleries · source: knowledge
1808. **Driving licence conversion rules** — Whether a home licence is valid, must be exchanged within a deadline, or requires a new test depends on country pairs and is poorly documented.  
    *immigrants who drive* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: forums and clerk visits · source: knowledge
1809. **Not knowing which office handles what** — Newcomers do not know whether a problem belongs to the municipality, the region, the tax office or a private insurer, and get bounced between counters.  
    *recent immigrants* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking in forums · source: knowledge
1810. **Registering a new address with authorities** — Many countries require registering the address with city hall within days of moving, with appointment requirements and landlord forms newcomers do not know about.  
    *immigrants moving in or between apartments* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: expat wiki pages · source: knowledge
1811. **School enrollment and communications abroad** — School systems, enrollment deadlines, grading and parent-teacher apps are unfamiliar, and school messages arrive in the local language only.  
    *immigrant parents* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: translating each message manually · source: knowledge
1812. **Understanding rental contracts abroad** — Lease clauses about deposits, notice periods, renovation obligations and utilities are unfamiliar and untranslated, and tenants sign things they do not understand.  
    *immigrant renters* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking friends to read it · source: knowledge
1813. **Deciphering foreign utility and phone bills** — Bills use local billing conventions, estimated readings, and fees with unfamiliar names, so immigrants cannot tell if they are being overcharged.  
    *immigrant households* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paying without checking · source: knowledge
1814. **Unfamiliar recycling and waste rules** — Sorting rules, collection days and fines differ by city, are posted in the local language, and newcomers get warnings from neighbours or fines.  
    *new residents* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: photographing the building notice · source: knowledge

### Remittances

1815. **Remittance transfers delayed or held** — Transfers get frozen for compliance checks with no explanation, while family at home waits for rent or medical money.  
    *remittance senders* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: calling support, sending via a second provider · source: knowledge
1816. **Comparing remittance fees and exchange rates** — Each provider quotes different fees, rates and delivery times, and the real cost is hidden in the spread, so senders cannot tell which is cheapest today.  
    *migrant workers sending money home* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: checking several apps manually · source: knowledge
1817. **Family requests for money arrive ad hoc** — Requests come by chat from various relatives at unpredictable times, making it hard to budget and creating tension over what was already sent.  
    *primary earners abroad* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1818. **Paying bills at home from abroad** — Utilities, school fees and medical bills for family at home cannot be paid directly from foreign accounts, so cash must be sent and trusted to be used correctly.  
    *migrants supporting parents* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: sending cash and asking for receipts · source: knowledge
1819. **Recipients cannot easily collect funds** — Family members in rural areas or without bank accounts must travel to pickup points, face long lines or lack ID accepted by the agent.  
    *families of migrant workers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: cash pickup, mobile-money agents · source: knowledge
1820. **Documentation of remittances for taxes or visas** — Immigration or tax authorities may ask for proof of financial support sent to family, and receipts are scattered across providers.  
    *sponsors and remittance senders* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: screenshots · source: knowledge
1821. **Tracking money sent home over time** — Senders support several relatives through multiple apps and cash and lose track of how much they have sent, to whom and for what.  
    *migrants supporting extended family* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: memory and chat messages · source: knowledge

### Translation & language

1822. **Medical appointments without interpreters** — Describing symptoms and understanding diagnoses in a second language leads to errors, and interpreters are rarely available for routine visits.  
    *immigrants and their elderly parents* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bringing a bilingual child or relative · source: knowledge
1823. **Official letters in a language you cannot read** — Mail from the tax office, city hall or insurer arrives in dense bureaucratic language, and the reader cannot tell if it is a deadline, a bill or just information.  
    *immigrants with limited local language* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: photographing it into a translation app, asking friends · source: knowledge
1824. **Certified translations are slow and expensive** — Every birth certificate, diploma or marriage record needs a sworn translation for a fee, with days of turnaround, often re-done for each new authority.  
    *immigrants filing applications* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: finding a sworn translator via word of mouth · source: knowledge
1825. **Children translating for parents** — Kids end up as the family's interpreter for landlords, schools and doctors, exposing them to adult problems and creating errors in serious matters.  
    *immigrant families* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1826. **Filling forms with untranslatable field labels** — Online and paper forms use abbreviations and local-only concepts (tax class, social security subcategories) that even translated do not explain what to enter.  
    *immigrants completing administrative forms* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: guessing, screenshots to friends, help desks · source: knowledge
1827. **Machine translation fails on bureaucratic jargon** — General translation apps produce literal nonsense for terms like specific tax forms, benefits, permit types or legal notices, and the user cannot tell what action is required.  
    *non-native speakers dealing with government* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: searching each term separately in expat forums · source: knowledge
1828. **Names mangled by forms and systems** — Names with accents, multiple surnames, or no distinct given name get rejected or garbled by forms, producing mismatched records across banks, airlines and government.  
    *immigrants with non-Latin or compound names* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: adopting an inconsistent 'system' spelling · source: knowledge
1829. **Phone calls with hold menus in another language** — Automated phone menus, customer support and hotlines in the local language are impossible to follow, and no text alternative exists.  
    *newcomers with limited fluency* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking a fluent friend to call, avoiding calls entirely · source: knowledge
1830. **Language learning does not match real needs** — Language apps teach tourist phrases while what the immigrant needs is vocabulary for rental contracts, job interviews, school meetings and doctor visits.  
    *working-age immigrants* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: generic apps plus evening classes · source: knowledge
1831. **Understanding local slang and workplace idiom** — Textbook language does not cover the dialect, slang and workplace shorthand colleagues use, so the immigrant misses jokes, instructions and social cues.  
    *immigrant employees* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking colleagues, feeling excluded · source: knowledge
1832. **Handwritten notes and signs unreadable** — Handwritten notices on doors, building announcements and shop signs are not recognized well by camera translation and are missed entirely.  
    *newly arrived immigrants* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking neighbours · source: knowledge

### Visas & permits

1833. **Assembling long immigration document checklists** — Applications need 20-40 documents in a specific order, format and age (e.g. bank statements no older than 30 days), and one missing page gets the whole file rejected months later.  
    *visa and residence permit applicants* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: printed checklists and lawyer emails · source: knowledge
1834. **Dependents' paperwork tied to sponsor** — A spouse's or child's permit depends on the main applicant's status, so one person's job change or expiry silently invalidates the whole family's documents.  
    *families on dependent visas* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: manual tracking by the sponsor · source: knowledge
1835. **Immigration appointment slots never available** — Appointment booking systems for consulates, foreigner offices or biometrics show no slots for months and new slots appear at random times and vanish in minutes.  
    *residence permit applicants and visa renewers* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: refreshing manually at odd hours, paying bots or agencies · source: knowledge
1836. **Tracking visa and permit expiry dates** — Multiple documents (visa, residence permit, passport, work authorization, driving licence) expire on different dates and missing one by a day can mean fines, loss of status or being unable to travel.  
    *expats and immigrants on temporary permits* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar reminders and a folder of photos · source: knowledge
1837. **Counting days for residency and tax rules** — Rules like the Schengen 90/180-day limit, physical-presence requirements for citizenship, or tax residency thresholds require counting days in and out of the country from years of stamps and tickets.  
    *digital nomads, green card holders, naturalization applicants* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: spreadsheets and scrolling through passport stamps · source: knowledge
1838. **Immigration rules change without notice** — Fees, quotas, required documents and eligibility rules change and the applicant only learns of it when their file is rejected or a forum post mentions it.  
    *immigrants mid-process* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: following expat Facebook groups · source: knowledge
1839. **Keeping a complete immigration history** — Later applications ask for every address, employer, trip abroad and previous visa going back 5-10 years, and nobody has kept a clean record.  
    *long-term immigrants applying for permanent residency or citizenship* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: digging through old emails and photos · source: knowledge
1840. **Opaque immigration application status** — After submitting, applicants wait months with no meaningful status beyond 'processing' and refresh the portal daily, unsure whether to book flights or sign leases.  
    *anyone with a pending application* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: checking portals and forums for processing-time anecdotes · source: knowledge
1841. **Travel restrictions while permit is pending** — People do not know whether leaving the country during a pending renewal cancels the application, and the rules differ by permit type and country.  
    *immigrants with pending renewals* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: asking forums and lawyers · source: knowledge
1842. **Understanding which visa path applies** — Government immigration sites list dozens of visa categories with overlapping criteria, and people cannot tell which one they qualify for without paying a lawyer.  
    *prospective immigrants and partners of citizens* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Reddit threads, forums, expensive consultations · source: knowledge

### Work & credentials

1843. **Foreign qualifications not recognized** — Degrees and professional licences from home require costly, slow recognition procedures, and people do not know which agency or path applies to their profession.  
    *skilled immigrants in regulated professions* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: working below qualification while waiting · source: knowledge
1844. **Job change jeopardizes visa status** — Changing employer, going freelance or being laid off triggers reporting deadlines and grace periods that vary by permit, and mistakes cost the right to stay.  
    *immigrants on employer-sponsored permits* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: lawyers and forum advice · source: knowledge
1845. **Work permit restrictions unclear to employers** — Employers do not understand what a given permit allows, so eligible candidates are rejected or asked for documents that do not exist.  
    *job-seeking immigrants* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: attaching explanations to applications · source: knowledge
1846. **Local-format CVs and application conventions** — Expected CV layout, photo, cover letter tone and reference practices differ by country, and foreign-style applications get discarded.  
    *immigrant job seekers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: career center workshops · source: knowledge

<a id="creators-sellers"></a>

## Creators & online sellers

*content creators, streamers, Etsy/eBay sellers, social media management*


### Analytics & audience

1847. **Analytics split across five dashboards** — Seeing overall reach, follower growth and engagement means logging into YouTube Studio, TikTok, Meta Business Suite, X and LinkedIn separately and adding numbers up by hand.  
    *multi-platform creators and agencies* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheet updated weekly from screenshots · source: knowledge
1848. **Building a monthly client report** — Assembling a monthly performance report per client means exporting CSVs, screenshotting charts and pasting them into a slide deck, taking hours for each account.  
    *social media managers with multiple clients* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Canva or Google Slides templates filled manually · source: knowledge
1849. **Not knowing why a post flopped** — A post that seems identical to a hit gets a tenth of the views, and native analytics give no clue whether it was the hook, the timing, the length or the topic.  
    *creators trying to grow* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Guessing, asking in creator Discords · source: knowledge
1850. **Comparing thumbnail variants before publishing** — Choosing between three thumbnail and title combinations is guesswork; getting feedback means posting in a Discord and waiting for a handful of opinions.  
    *YouTubers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask friends, post in creator communities · source: knowledge
1851. **YouTube retention drop-off points unclear** — Retention graphs show where viewers leave but not what was on screen at that moment, so creators have to scrub the video side by side to learn anything.  
    *YouTubers editing long-form videos* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Manually match timestamps to the video · source: knowledge
1852. **Best posting time keeps shifting** — Audience active hours change as followers grow across time zones, and the creator keeps posting at a time that worked six months ago.  
    *creators with international audiences* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Check platform insights occasionally · source: knowledge
1853. **Detecting follower purges and bot drops** — Follower count drops suddenly and the creator cannot tell whether it was a platform bot purge, an unfollow wave after a controversial post, or a glitch.  
    *creators watching their numbers* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Third-party unfollow trackers of dubious safety · source: knowledge

### Assets & rights

1854. **Product designs copied and undercut** — Popular handmade or print-on-demand designs are cloned by other sellers within weeks, and the original seller has no way to monitor for copies.  
    *design-driven sellers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Search own product name occasionally · source: knowledge
1855. **Videos re-uploaded by others without credit** — Creators find their videos stolen and reposted on other accounts, sometimes outperforming the original, and filing takedowns per platform is slow and repetitive.  
    *video creators* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Followers tip them off, manual DMCA forms · source: knowledge
1856. **Brand assets and passwords shared insecurely** — Social media managers receive client logos, fonts, brand colours and account logins by email and chat, and end up with outdated files and shared passwords in plain text.  
    *freelance social media managers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Drive folders and a note of passwords · source: knowledge
1857. **Finding that one B-roll clip again** — A creator remembers filming a specific shot last year but cannot find it among thousands of unnamed files, so they refilm or drop it.  
    *YouTubers and editors* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Folder naming conventions that never stick · source: knowledge
1858. **Handing over accounts when clients leave** — When a client relationship ends, untangling who owns which page, ad account and scheduler login and removing the manager's access cleanly is confusing and often forgotten.  
    *agencies and freelance managers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Checklist in a doc · source: knowledge
1859. **Raw footage eating every hard drive** — Terabytes of unedited footage and project files pile up across drives and cloud accounts, with no idea what is safe to delete or where a specific clip lives.  
    *video creators* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Buy another drive · source: knowledge

### Brand deals & collaborations

1860. **Sponsorship deliverables and deadlines slip** — Each brand deal comes with a different set of deliverables, approval rounds, usage rights and posting windows tracked only in the contract PDF, so a deadline or exclusivity clause gets missed.  
    *creators juggling several sponsors* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Calendar reminders and rereading contracts · source: knowledge
1861. **Unknown fair rate for a sponsorship** — When a brand asks for a rate, creators have no benchmark for what someone with their audience size and niche should charge and either undercharge badly or lose the deal.  
    *small and mid-size creators* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask peers, rate-card newsletters · source: knowledge
1862. **Affiliate links break or expire** — Affiliate and discount links placed in old videos and bios stop working when programs change, and the creator only finds out when a follower complains.  
    *creators relying on affiliate income* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Occasional manual click-through · source: knowledge
1863. **Disclosure rules per platform and country** — Knowing exactly how to label a paid partnership (#ad placement, paid partnership tag, AI disclosure) differs by platform and jurisdiction, and getting it wrong risks fines or demonetization.  
    *creators doing sponsored content* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Copy what bigger creators do · source: knowledge
1864. **Media kit always out of date** — Brands ask for a media kit with current stats, and the PDF from three months ago shows old follower counts and engagement rates that need to be re-collected and re-laid-out every time.  
    *creators pitching brands* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Canva template updated by hand · source: knowledge
1865. **Sorting real brand offers from scams** — Inboxes fill with fake collaboration offers, gift-for-post requests and phishing disguised as sponsorships, and the genuine offers get lost among them.  
    *creators with growing audiences* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Manually check sender domains, ignore most emails · source: knowledge

### Community & audience ownership

1866. **Giveaway winners picked by hand** — Running a giveaway means scrolling thousands of comments to verify entries followed the rules, picking a winner fairly and proving it was fair.  
    *creators and sellers running promotions* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Random comment picker sites of varying reliability · source: knowledge
1867. **Link-in-bio not tracking what converts** — A single bio link routes to many destinations but the creator cannot tell which post drove which clicks or sales, so they cannot double down on what works.  
    *creators and sellers driving traffic from social* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Linktree free tier, guesswork · source: knowledge
1868. **Newsletter and email list neglected** — Creators know they should own their audience via email, but collecting addresses and sending regular emails is another channel to feed and it gets abandoned.  
    *creators building off-platform audiences* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Occasional Substack post · source: knowledge
1869. **Patreon perks forgotten or delivered late** — Membership tiers promise early access, Discord roles, monthly Q&As or shoutouts, and tracking who is owed what each month is manual and error-prone.  
    *creators with memberships* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Spreadsheet of patrons · source: knowledge

### Content planning & publishing

1870. **Client content approvals over email chaos** — Getting a client to approve captions and visuals before posting happens over email threads and WhatsApp, with versions mixed up and approvals arriving after the post deadline.  
    *freelance social media managers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Google Sheets calendar plus screenshots sent for sign-off · source: knowledge
1871. **No idea what to post today** — Facing a blank content calendar every morning with no backlog, so posting becomes reactive and inconsistent and the algorithm punishes the gaps.  
    *creators who post daily* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Batch days, copying trending formats, skipping days · source: knowledge
1872. **Reformatting one post for every platform** — The same video or post has to be re-cropped, re-captioned and re-uploaded separately for TikTok, Reels, Shorts, X and LinkedIn, each with different aspect ratios, caption limits and hashtag norms.  
    *creators and social media managers posting to 3+ platforms* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Manually export multiple versions from CapCut/Canva and paste captions by hand · source: knowledge
1873. **Scheduler silently fails to post** — A scheduled post fails because a token expired or the platform changed its API, and the creator only notices hours later when nothing went out.  
    *social media managers using scheduling tools* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Checking each platform manually after scheduled time · source: knowledge
1874. **Content ideas lost in scattered notes** — Video and post ideas get jotted into phone notes, DMs to yourself, screenshots and voice memos, and are impossible to find again when it is time to plan the week.  
    *solo creators* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Apple Notes, Notion pages nobody maintains, saved TikToks · source: knowledge
1875. **Tracking which trends are still alive** — By the time a creator films a trending sound or format, it has already peaked; there is no easy way to see how old a trend is and whether it is rising or falling.  
    *short-form video creators* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Scrolling the For You page for an hour, trend newsletters · source: knowledge
1876. **Hashtag sets pasted from old notes** — Keeping reusable hashtag and caption template groups per niche means copying from a notes file every time, and stale or banned hashtags sneak in unnoticed.  
    *Instagram and TikTok creators* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Notes app with hashtag blocks · source: knowledge
1877. **Reposting evergreen content by hand** — Old posts that performed well could be recycled, but finding them and reposting at good intervals requires digging through the profile manually.  
    *creators with a large back catalog* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Scroll own profile and screenshot · source: knowledge

### Customer messages & reviews

1878. **Comments and DMs across six inboxes** — Replying to comments and messages requires cycling through Instagram, TikTok, YouTube, X, Discord and email, and important messages from customers or brands get buried.  
    *creators and social media managers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Check each app in turn, miss things · source: knowledge
1879. **Hate and spam comments drowning real ones** — Bot comments, scam replies and abuse flood posts as they grow, and manually deleting or hiding them takes the joy out of reading genuine feedback.  
    *creators with large audiences* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Keyword filters that catch too little or too much · source: knowledge
1880. **Impersonator accounts scamming followers** — Fake accounts copy a creator's name and photos to DM followers with giveaway scams, and finding and reporting them all is a constant manual chore.  
    *mid-size and large creators* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Followers report them, creator reports one by one · source: knowledge
1881. **Spotting a bad review before it spreads** — A one-star review or a viral negative comment sits unnoticed for hours because there is no alert across platforms, and by then it has shaped buyer perception.  
    *sellers and creators* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Refreshing the reviews page · source: knowledge
