1882. **Answering the same buyer questions repeatedly** — Questions about sizing, materials, turnaround and returns arrive dozens of times a day across marketplace messages, DMs and email, each answered by typing from scratch.  
    *sellers and creators with shops* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Saved replies in one platform only · source: knowledge
1883. **Asking for reviews at the right moment** — Happy buyers rarely leave reviews unless prompted after delivery, but sellers cannot easily tell when a package arrived or who has already been asked.  
    *Etsy and own-store sellers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Thank-you card in the package · source: knowledge

### Listings & inventory

1884. **Selling an item that already sold elsewhere** — A one-of-a-kind item sells on two marketplaces at once because inventory is not synced, forcing a cancellation that hurts seller ratings.  
    *resellers cross-listing single items* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Rush to delist manually after each sale · source: knowledge
1885. **Finding sourced inventory in the house** — Resellers with hundreds of thrifted items in bins cannot find the specific sold item quickly, delaying shipping and sometimes leading to cancellations.  
    *eBay and Poshmark resellers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Labeled bins and memory · source: knowledge
1886. **Listing the same item on multiple marketplaces** — Cross-listing a product on Etsy, eBay, Depop, Poshmark and Facebook Marketplace means retyping titles, descriptions and re-uploading photos for every site.  
    *resellers and handmade sellers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Copy-paste, cross-listing tools that cost monthly fees · source: knowledge
1887. **Writing SEO titles and tags for every listing** — Each Etsy listing needs a keyword-stuffed title, 13 tags and attributes that match how buyers search, and sellers have no feedback on whether their choices work.  
    *Etsy sellers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: eRank/Marmalead subscriptions, copying competitors · source: knowledge
1888. **Checking sold comps before buying stock** — While standing in a thrift store, deciding if an item is worth buying requires searching sold listings on several apps and mentally subtracting fees and shipping.  
    *thrift resellers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: eBay sold filter on phone, gut feeling · source: knowledge
1889. **Product photos take longer than making the product** — Getting clean, consistent, well-lit photos of small items and removing backgrounds is a tedious step that delays listing new products.  
    *small product sellers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Phone plus lightbox, background remover apps one image at a time · source: knowledge
1890. **Running out of materials mid-order** — Makers discover they are out of a specific bead, yarn colour or blank when an order comes in, because supply quantities are tracked in their head.  
    *handmade sellers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Physical inspection of bins · source: knowledge
1891. **Stale listings sitting for months** — Items that have not sold in months need relisting, price drops or better photos, but nothing surfaces which listings are dead weight.  
    *resellers with large catalogs* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Sort by date listed and eyeball · source: knowledge

### Money & taxes

1892. **Surprise self-employment tax bill** — Nothing is withheld from platform payouts, and creators and sellers reach tax time with no idea how much to set aside and no quarterly estimates paid.  
    *creators and marketplace sellers earning meaningful income* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Move 30% to savings when remembered, panic in April · source: knowledge
1893. **Chasing late brand deal invoices** — Brands pay net-60 or later and creators lose track of which invoices are outstanding, when to follow up and which agency contact to chase.  
    *creators doing sponsorships* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Email search and awkward follow-up messages · source: knowledge
1894. **Deductible expenses buried in personal purchases** — Camera gear, props, shipping supplies and software subscriptions are bought on the same card as groceries, so separating business expenses at tax time takes days.  
    *solo creators and sellers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Shoebox of receipts, scrolling card statements · source: knowledge
1895. **Income scattered across a dozen payouts** — Revenue arrives from AdSense, TikTok Creator Fund, Twitch, Patreon, brand deals, affiliate links and merch, each on different schedules, so the creator never knows what they actually earned this month.  
    *full-time creators* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Bank statements and a spreadsheet · source: knowledge
1896. **Marketplace fees hide the real profit** — After Etsy or eBay listing, transaction, payment processing, offsite ads and shipping fees, sellers cannot tell whether a sale actually made money.  
    *Etsy and eBay sellers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Fee calculator websites, rough mental math · source: knowledge
1897. **Pricing handmade items by feel** — Makers price products without accounting for material cost per unit, hours of labor, packaging and fees, and routinely discover they are working below minimum wage.  
    *handmade and print-on-demand sellers* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Copy competitor prices, guess · source: knowledge
1898. **Sales tax across states and countries** — Selling on a personal site as well as marketplaces means figuring out which sales tax, VAT or import rules apply per order, with marketplaces collecting some but not all.  
    *sellers with their own storefront* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ignore it or pay an accountant · source: knowledge

### Orders & shipping

1899. **Item not received claims with no evidence** — When a buyer opens a not-received or not-as-described case, sellers scramble to find photos of the packed item, tracking scans and message history to defend themselves.  
    *eBay and Etsy sellers* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Photograph every package and hope to find it later · source: knowledge
1900. **Custom order details lost in messages** — Personalization details (names, colours, sizes) arrive in chat messages after checkout and get lost, leading to wrong items being made and remade.  
    *custom and personalized product sellers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Screenshot the conversation, sticky notes · source: knowledge
1901. **Guessing shipping cost at listing time** — Sellers must set shipping prices before knowing box size and weight, and routinely lose money on underestimated postage or lose sales on overestimated postage.  
    *eBay and Etsy sellers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Kitchen scale and carrier calculators · source: knowledge
1902. **Packing slips and labels from several stores** — Orders from Etsy, eBay and a Shopify store each need labels printed in different dashboards, and it is easy to miss one or ship the wrong item to the wrong buyer.  
    *sellers on multiple channels* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Pirate Ship or Shippo, printed order lists · source: knowledge
1903. **Processing-time deadlines creeping up** — With dozens of open orders each with a different promised ship-by date, sellers cannot see which ones are about to be late without opening each order.  
    *made-to-order sellers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Paper list of orders on the wall · source: knowledge
1904. **Buyers asking where their package is** — A large share of daily messages are 'where is my order', requiring the seller to look up tracking manually and reply, even though the information is already available.  
    *marketplace sellers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Copy tracking number into a reply template · source: knowledge

### Streaming & live

1905. **Chat moderation during a raid** — A sudden hate raid or spam wave floods chat faster than a solo streamer can react while playing, and setting up bot rules ahead of time is confusing.  
    *small streamers without mod teams* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Followers-only mode, end the stream · source: knowledge
1906. **Clipping highlights from an eight-hour VOD** — Finding the funny or hype moments in a long stream to make Shorts and TikToks takes longer than the stream itself, so most streams are never repurposed.  
    *streamers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Ask viewers to clip, scrub through VOD · source: knowledge
1907. **Copyrighted music muting VODs** — Streams get muted or claimed because background music was in-game or on the radio, and streamers cannot tell in advance which tracks are safe.  
    *streamers and video creators* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Stream-safe playlists, muting all audio · source: knowledge
1908. **Stream going live without anyone told** — Announcing a stream on Discord, X, Instagram and TikTok every time is a manual ritual that is often forgotten, so viewers miss the start.  
    *Twitch, Kick and YouTube streamers* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Discord bots that cover one platform · source: knowledge
1909. **Stream schedule not matching reality** — Posted stream schedules go stale when life happens, and viewers in other time zones show up to an offline channel or miss unscheduled streams.  
    *part-time streamers* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Panel image edited in Canva, Discord announcements · source: knowledge
1910. **Tracking subs, bits and donations for thank-yous** — Streamers want to thank new subs, gifted subs and donors properly, but the events scroll by during the stream and there is no clean list afterwards.  
    *streamers* · daily · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Scroll the activity feed after stream · source: knowledge

### Workflow & wellbeing

1911. **Account suspended with no explanation** — A shop or channel is suspended or shadowbanned by automated systems with a vague notice, and the seller has no way to diagnose the cause or track an appeal.  
    *sellers and creators dependent on one platform* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Forum posts, repeated appeals · source: knowledge
1912. **Burnout from never-ending posting pressure** — The perceived need to post daily with no breaks leads to exhaustion, and creators have no view of how much a short break actually costs them in reach.  
    *full-time creators* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: Push through, quit for months · source: knowledge
1913. **Platform policy changes missed** — Monetization rules, dimension requirements and community guidelines change frequently, and creators only learn about them after a strike, demonetization or rejected listing.  
    *creators and marketplace sellers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Creator newsletters, forum rumors · source: knowledge
1914. **Compulsive checking of view counts** — Creators refresh analytics dozens of times a day after posting, which wrecks focus and mood without changing outcomes.  
    *creators* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: Delete the app for a day · source: knowledge
1915. **Editing backlog with no pipeline view** — Videos sit in various states (filmed, rough cut, awaiting thumbnail, scheduled) across editor, thumbnail designer and creator, and no one knows what is blocking what.  
    *creators working with editors* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: Trello or Notion boards that drift out of date · source: knowledge

<a id="seniors-aging"></a>

## Seniors & aging

*retirement, aging in place, tech help, medication, isolation, scams targeting elders*


### Aging in place & safety

1916. **Emergency info not available to responders** — When paramedics arrive, the senior's medications, allergies, conditions, and emergency contacts are not accessible in one place.  
    *seniors with complex health histories* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: 'File of Life' on the fridge, medical ID bracelets · source: knowledge
1917. **No one would notice a fall for days** — If a senior living alone fell in the bathroom, nobody would know until a missed weekly call, which is the fear that keeps both them and their children up at night.  
    *seniors living alone and their families* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: daily check-in calls, medical alert pendants they forget to wear · source: knowledge
1918. **Stove or appliance left on** — A burner or iron gets left on after a distraction and nobody is around to notice, creating a real fire risk and eroding confidence in living alone.  
    *seniors with mild memory decline* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: family removes stove knobs, automatic shutoff devices · source: knowledge
1919. **Wandering or getting lost on familiar routes** — A parent with early dementia takes a walk and does not come back on time, and the family has no way to know where they are.  
    *families of seniors with cognitive decline* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: GPS tags, restricting outings · source: knowledge
1920. **Assessing what home changes are needed** — Families do not know which grab bars, lighting, or ramp changes matter most for their parent's home until after a fall.  
    *families of seniors aging in place* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: occupational therapist visits if covered, guesswork · source: knowledge
1921. **Finding trustworthy home help** — Hiring someone to clean gutters, fix a railing, or help with bathing means vetting strangers with no easy way to check references or pricing.  
    *seniors aging in place and their families* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: word of mouth, church contacts, generic contractor sites · source: knowledge
1922. **No ride to appointments after giving up driving** — Once a senior stops driving, every doctor visit or grocery run depends on a relative's availability, and rideshare apps are confusing to book.  
    *seniors who no longer drive* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking family, expensive taxis, paratransit with long lead times · source: knowledge
1923. **Remembering to eat and drink enough** — Living alone, a senior skips meals or forgets to drink water all day, leading to dizziness and hospital visits for dehydration.  
    *seniors living alone with reduced appetite* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: family reminders, meal delivery programs · source: knowledge
1924. **Grocery delivery apps too confusing to use** — A senior who can no longer carry groceries cannot navigate the delivery app's substitutions, tips, and checkout steps and ends up ordering wrong items or nothing.  
    *seniors with mobility limits* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a relative places the order for them · source: knowledge

### Caregiver coordination

1925. **Caregiver burnout with no backup** — A spouse caring for a partner with dementia has no respite, cannot leave the house, and does not know what help exists or how to schedule it.  
    *spousal and family caregivers* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none, or adult day programs discovered by chance · source: knowledge
1926. **Coordinating who drives Mom this week** — Siblings trade texts trying to figure out who can take their mother to Tuesday's cardiology appointment, and sometimes nobody shows up.  
    *sibling groups caring for a parent* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group text threads, shared spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1927. **Paid caregivers with no shared handoff notes** — Morning and evening aides do not know what the other did, whether the senior ate, or how they slept, so problems slip between shifts.  
    *families employing home aides* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: a paper notebook on the kitchen table · source: knowledge
1928. **Siblings out of sync on parent's health updates** — One sibling attends the appointment and the others hear a garbled second-hand version days later, causing arguments and duplicated calls to the doctor.  
    *adult siblings sharing care of a parent* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: group texts, one sibling becomes the sole information hub · source: knowledge
1929. **Tracking caregiving expenses for reimbursement or taxes** — Receipts for prescriptions, supplies, and mileage pile up and cannot be sorted when it is time to split costs among siblings or claim deductions.  
    *family caregivers paying out of pocket* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: shoebox of receipts · source: knowledge

### Health & appointments

1930. **Knowing which day it is and what is scheduled** — Without work or school routines, days lose their markers and the senior shows up on the wrong day for appointments or misses them.  
    *retired seniors with mild memory decline* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: large-print wall calendar, day clocks · source: knowledge
1931. **Multiple patient portals with separate logins** — The cardiologist, primary care doctor, and hospital each use a different portal, and finding a lab result means remembering which one and which password.  
    *seniors with several providers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: calling the office instead, ignoring portals entirely · source: knowledge
1932. **Preparing questions before a short appointment** — Symptoms noticed over the past month are forgotten in the ten-minute visit, and the senior leaves without asking the things that worried them.  
    *seniors managing chronic conditions* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: notes on scrap paper, hoping to remember · source: knowledge
1933. **Tracking blood pressure and sugar readings by hand** — Daily readings are written in a notebook that is hard to summarize, so the doctor gets a pile of numbers rather than a trend.  
    *seniors monitoring blood pressure, glucose, or weight* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper logs, memory · source: knowledge

### Isolation & connection

1934. **Days pass without speaking to anyone** — A senior living alone can go three or four days without a single conversation, and there is no natural prompt for anyone to reach out.  
    *seniors living alone, widowed or far from family* · daily · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: television for company, waiting for someone to call · source: knowledge
1935. **Losing a social circle after retirement** — Work provided daily contact for forty years; after retiring the calendar is empty and there is no obvious way to meet new people with shared interests.  
    *recent retirees* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: volunteering, religious groups · source: knowledge
1936. **Daily check-in calls feel like surveillance** — Children want reassurance that mom is okay each morning, but the daily 'just checking' call feels intrusive to the parent and burdensome to the child.  
    *adult children and independent parents* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: phone calls, texts that go unanswered and cause panic · source: knowledge
1937. **Finding local activities for people their age** — Senior center schedules, library talks, and walking groups are scattered across paper flyers and church bulletins, so the senior does not know what is on this week.  
    *retirees looking for social activities* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: word of mouth, paper newsletters · source: knowledge
1938. **Grandchildren's lives only visible on platforms seniors avoid** — Family photos and updates live on Instagram and group chats the grandparent does not use, so they feel left out of everyday family life.  
    *grandparents not on social media* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: occasional printed photos, forwarded texts · source: knowledge
1939. **Making new friends after moving to a retirement community** — New residents do not know who shares their hobbies or who is open to a dinner companion, and dining alone for weeks is common.  
    *seniors newly moved to retirement or assisted living communities* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: activities director, trial and error · source: knowledge
1940. **Remembering birthdays and reaching out** — As memory slips, the senior forgets grandchildren's birthdays and loses the easy excuses to call, which deepens distance from family.  
    *seniors with large extended families* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paper calendar, being reminded by a spouse · source: knowledge

### Medication management

1941. **Adult child cannot verify remote parent's meds** — A daughter living in another state has no way to confirm her father actually took his heart medication today short of calling and nagging.  
    *long-distance adult caregivers* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: daily phone calls, hiring a home aide · source: knowledge
1942. **Forgetting whether today's pills were taken** — An hour after breakfast an older adult cannot remember if they already took the morning dose, and either skips it or doubles up.  
    *seniors on daily medications, especially living alone* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: weekly pill organizers, sticky notes, calling a family member to ask · source: knowledge
1943. **Juggling a dozen prescriptions with different schedules** — Some pills are twice a day with food, some once at bedtime, some every other day; keeping the whole schedule straight in one's head is overwhelming.  
    *seniors with multiple chronic conditions* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: handwritten chart taped to the fridge, multiple pill boxes · source: knowledge
1944. **Not knowing which pills interact** — Different specialists prescribe drugs without seeing the full list, and the patient has no easy way to check for dangerous interactions or duplicates.  
    *seniors seeing several doctors* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: bringing a bag of pill bottles to appointments, asking the pharmacist · source: knowledge
1945. **Unreadable tiny print on pill bottles** — Dosage instructions and warnings on bottle labels are too small to read, so instructions get guessed at or ignored.  
    *seniors with declining eyesight* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: magnifying glass, asking someone to read it aloud · source: knowledge

### Retirement & finances

1946. **Bills paid late after spouse who handled money dies** — When the partner who always handled finances passes away, the survivor does not know which bills are on autopay, where accounts are, or when things are due.  
    *recently widowed seniors* · monthly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: children step in, rummaging through paper files · source: knowledge
1947. **Fear of outliving retirement savings** — A retiree does not know whether their monthly withdrawals are sustainable and lives with constant anxiety about running out of money at 90.  
    *retirees living off savings* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: occasional visits to a financial advisor, rough spreadsheets · source: knowledge
1948. **Important documents scattered and unfindable** — The will, power of attorney, insurance policies, and deed are in different drawers, safe deposit boxes, and lawyers' offices, and nobody knows where.  
    *seniors and their future executors* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a binder that is never fully updated · source: knowledge
1949. **Keeping up with Medicare paperwork and deadlines** — Open enrollment windows, plan changes, and explanation-of-benefits letters arrive constantly, and it is unclear which need action and which are junk.  
    *seniors on Medicare and their helpers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: piles of mail, a knowledgeable relative or SHIP counselor · source: knowledge
1950. **Required minimum distribution deadlines missed** — Retirees forget to take required withdrawals from retirement accounts by year end and face steep penalties.  
    *retirees over 73 with retirement accounts* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: brokerage reminder letters, accountant · source: knowledge
1951. **Surprise medical bills that may already be paid** — Bills arrive from labs and hospitals months after the visit, and the senior cannot tell whether insurance covered it, whether they already paid, or whether it is a scam.  
    *seniors with frequent medical visits* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paying everything twice, or ignoring bills until collections · source: knowledge
1952. **Empty days with no structure after retiring** — Without a job the days blur together, motivation drops, and the retiree feels aimless despite wanting to stay active.  
    *recent retirees* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: part-time work, hobbies picked up haphazardly · source: knowledge

### Scams & fraud

1953. **Distinguishing real bank calls from scam calls** — A caller claims to be from the bank or Medicare and the senior has no quick way to verify whether the call is genuine before sharing information.  
    *seniors receiving unsolicited calls* · weekly · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: hanging up and calling the number on the card, or complying · source: knowledge
1954. **Family finds out about the scam too late** — Large unusual transfers, gift card purchases, or new payees go unnoticed by relatives until thousands of dollars are gone.  
    *adult children of seniors managing their own finances* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking for joint account access, periodic statement reviews · source: knowledge
1955. **Grandparent scam using a panicked family voice** — A call claiming a grandchild is in jail or hospital and needs gift cards now creates such urgency that the senior acts before checking with anyone.  
    *grandparents living alone* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: family code words, none for most · source: knowledge
1956. **Romance scam isolation goes unnoticed** — A lonely widow forms an online relationship with someone who slowly asks for money, and no one in the family sees the pattern until savings are drained.  
    *widowed or isolated seniors online* · occasional · pain 5/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
1957. **Phishing emails that mimic familiar services** — Emails styled as Amazon, Netflix, or the electric company ask to 'update payment' and the senior cannot tell them from real notices.  
    *seniors using email* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: forwarding suspicious emails to a child to check · source: knowledge
1958. **Pop-ups and fake virus alerts cause panic** — A browser pop-up saying the computer is infected looks official and the senior does not know whether to click, call the number, or unplug the machine.  
    *seniors using computers unsupervised* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: calling family, sometimes calling the scam number · source: knowledge
1959. **Robocalls flooding the landline all day** — The phone rings a dozen times a day with spam, so the senior either stops answering entirely and misses real calls or picks up every scam.  
    *seniors with landlines or basic phones* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: unplugging the phone, letting everything go to voicemail · source: knowledge
1960. **Recurring subscriptions signed up for unknowingly** — Free trials, magazine renewals, and 'protection plans' quietly charge the card monthly and the senior does not notice or cannot find how to cancel.  
    *seniors with credit or debit cards* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: a relative auditing statements once a year · source: knowledge

### Tech help & devices

1961. **Accidentally muting or switching phone settings** — A stray tap turns on Do Not Disturb, silences the ringer, or enables airplane mode, and the senior misses calls for days without knowing why.  
    *older smartphone users* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: family notices they cannot reach them and drives over · source: knowledge
1962. **Forgotten passwords locking out email and banking** — Passwords are scribbled in a notebook, half are out of date, and every login attempt ends in a reset loop that requires a code sent to a phone they cannot find.  
    *seniors managing online accounts* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paper notebooks, reusing one password, calling a child · source: knowledge
1963. **Phone updates change the interface overnight** — A software update moves buttons and changes icons, and a senior who had finally learned the phone is lost again and afraid to touch anything.  
    *older smartphone users* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: waiting for a grandchild to visit, avoiding updates · source: knowledge
1964. **Remote family cannot see the parent's screen** — Trying to talk a parent through a problem over the phone is guesswork because the helper cannot see what is on the screen and the parent cannot describe it.  
    *adult children providing remote tech support* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: long phone calls, driving over, taking photos of the screen · source: knowledge
1965. **Hearing the phone ring or the doorbell** — Hearing loss means missed calls and missed deliveries, and callers assume the senior is ignoring them or something is wrong.  
    *seniors with hearing loss* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: flashing lights, turning volume to maximum · source: knowledge
1966. **Same tech question asked every week** — Adult children get the same 'how do I send a photo' or 'where did my email go' call repeatedly because the steps are not written down anywhere the parent can find.  
    *seniors and the family members who act as tech support* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: phone calls, handwritten instruction sheets that get lost · source: knowledge
1967. **Too many apps and icons cluttering the home screen** — Pre-installed and accidentally downloaded apps crowd the phone so the three apps actually needed are hard to find.  
    *older smartphone users* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking a relative to 'clean up the phone' · source: knowledge
1968. **Two-factor codes on a device across the room** — Logging in on the computer requires a code texted to a phone that is charging in another room or has a dead battery, and the code expires before it is entered.  
    *seniors using online banking and portals* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: giving up and calling the bank · source: knowledge
1969. **Video calls that fail to connect with grandchildren** — The family sets up a video call and the senior cannot find the link, the camera is off, or the audio is muted, and the call becomes a frustrating tech session instead of a visit.  
    *seniors and families living apart* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: falling back to a voice call · source: knowledge
1970. **Websites and apps with tiny text and low contrast** — Even with reading glasses, many apps use small gray text on white, so seniors give up on tasks like paying a bill or reading a message.  
    *seniors with age-related vision loss* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: pinch-zooming, asking someone else to read it · source: knowledge

## Clothing, wardrobe & personal care

*outfits, laundry care, sizing, skincare, haircuts, booking appointments*


### Booking & appointments

1971. **Booking salons that only take phone calls** — Many barbers and nail salons only book by phone or Instagram DM, forcing calls during work hours and unanswered messages.  
    *working adults, people who dislike phone calls* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calling on lunch break or walking in · source: knowledge
1972. **Finding a barber for your hair type** — Moving to a new city and not knowing which salons can actually handle curly, Afro-textured or Asian hair, since reviews rarely say.  
    *people who relocated, people with textured hair* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking strangers with similar hair · source: knowledge
1973. **Coordinating recurring beauty appointments** — Lash fills every 3 weeks, nails every 2, brows every 4 and color every 6 all need booking on different systems, and they drift out of sync.  
    *people with multiple standing beauty services* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calendar juggling · source: knowledge
1974. **Price surprises at checkout** — A 'from $40' haircut becomes $85 after add-ons for length, texture or blow dry, discovered only at the register.  
    *salon clients, especially with long or textured hair* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking price on the phone before booking · source: knowledge
1975. **Waitlist for a cancelled slot** — A favorite stylist is fully booked, and when someone cancels there is no way to be notified and grab the opening.  
    *clients of popular stylists* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: calling repeatedly to check · source: knowledge
1976. **Walk-in wait time unknown** — Showing up at a walk-in barbershop and waiting 90 minutes because there is no way to see the queue before arriving.  
    *walk-in barbershop customers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: going at opening time · source: knowledge
1977. **Tipping etiquette uncertainty** — Not knowing whether to tip the salon owner, how much for a color service, or how to split between stylist and assistant.  
    *salon and barbershop clients* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: guessing 15-20 percent · source: knowledge
1978. **Tracking nail and lash technician preferences** — Forgetting the polish color, shape and length that looked great last time, or which technician did it, when rebooking.  
    *regular nail salon and lash clients* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: photos in camera roll · source: knowledge

### Grooming routines

1979. **Makeup shade matching online** — Foundation and concealer shades differ by brand and screen, so online orders come out too orange or too pale and cannot be returned once opened.  
    *people buying makeup online* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: shade-matching websites; buying in store · source: knowledge
1980. **Getting ready takes too long** — Morning hair, skincare and makeup routine consistently runs over and makes you late, with no sense of which step eats the time.

1981. **Running out of toiletries mid-use** — Discovering the deodorant, razor cartridges or contact solution are empty at the exact moment they are needed.  
    *anyone who buys toiletries* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: subscribe-and-save that sends too much or too little · source: knowledge
1982. **Grooming task cadence forgotten** — Beard trims, razor blade changes, toothbrush head swaps and nail care all have a cadence that nobody tracks, so they get done late or not at all.  
    *adults managing their own grooming* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: noticing when the razor pulls · source: knowledge

### Hair & haircuts

1983. **Explaining a haircut to the barber** — Walking out with the wrong haircut because 'a bit shorter on the sides' means something different to every barber and there is no shared reference.  
    *anyone who gets haircuts* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: showing a random photo from Instagram · source: knowledge
1984. **Hair color formula lost** — Switching stylists means the exact color formula and toner from previous appointments are gone, leading to mismatched results.  
    *people who color their hair* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking the old salon for their notes · source: knowledge
1985. **Haircut timing drifts too long** — Hair gets noticeably overgrown before it occurs to anyone to book, and then the good barber has no slots for two weeks.  
    *men with short hair, kids* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: noticing in the mirror · source: knowledge
1986. **Knowing hair type and matching products** — Curly, coily and fine hair all need different products and routines, and the labels do not say which hair type a product actually suits.  
    *people with textured or curly hair* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: curly-hair forums and trial and error · source: knowledge
1987. **Remembering last haircut details** — Not recalling which guard number, length or style was used last time, so a good cut can not be repeated at a new barber.  
    *people who move or switch barbers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: asking the old barber; guessing · source: knowledge
1988. **Wash-day scheduling for textured hair** — Wash day takes hours and protective styles have a lifespan, but planning them around events and work weeks is done in the head and often goes wrong.

### Laundry & garment care

1989. **Shared laundry room machine availability** — Hauling a basket downstairs to find every machine taken, or someone's finished load sitting in the dryer with no way to contact them.  
    *apartment renters and dorm residents with shared laundry* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: going at odd hours; leaving notes · source: knowledge
1990. **Shrunk or ruined by wrong wash** — A wool sweater goes in a hot wash by mistake and comes out child-sized; the care instructions were on a label that got cut off.  
    *people who own delicate garments* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: washing everything cold and hoping · source: knowledge
1991. **Dry cleaning pickup forgotten** — A suit or dress sits at the dry cleaner for weeks; the ticket is lost and it is unclear which cleaner even has it.  
    *professionals who dry clean regularly* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: paper ticket in wallet · source: knowledge
1992. **Forgetting wet laundry in the machine** — Clothes sit in the washer overnight and come out smelling musty, forcing a rewash and wasting water and time.  
    *busy households, anyone multitasking* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: setting a phone timer, if remembered · source: knowledge
1993. **Reading laundry care symbols** — Care labels use cryptic triangles, circles and dots and nobody remembers which means 'no bleach' or 'tumble dry low', so items get ruined.  
    *anyone doing their own laundry* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: googling the symbol or ignoring it · source: knowledge
1994. **Running out of clean basics** — Realising on a busy morning that there are no clean socks, underwear or work shirts because laundry was not planned around actual usage.  
    *single working adults, students* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: emergency wash or buying more underwear · source: knowledge
1995. **Sorting laundry loads correctly** — Not knowing whether the new red shirt will bleed, or whether the towels should go with the sheets, leading to pink whites and grey towels.  
    *students, new independent adults* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking parents or trial and error · source: knowledge
1996. **Stain treatment by type** — Red wine, grease, deodorant marks and grass each need a different treatment and the wrong one sets the stain permanently.  
    *parents, anyone who eats* · weekly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: frantic googling with the stain still wet · source: knowledge
1997. **Delicates handwash pile-up** — Bras, silk blouses and wool that must be handwashed accumulate in a pile for weeks because handwashing is never scheduled.  
    *owners of delicate garments* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 3/5 · workaround: mesh bag on gentle cycle and hoping · source: knowledge
1998. **Lost single socks** — A drawer full of unmatched socks with no way to know which partner is missing or which are worth keeping.  
    *families, anyone with a laundry pile* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 2/5 · workaround: buying identical socks in bulk · source: knowledge
1999. **Sneakers and shoes needing care** — Leather shoes crack and white sneakers yellow because there is no reminder to condition, polish or clean them before damage sets in.  
    *sneaker collectors, people who wear leather shoes* · monthly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: cleaning only when visibly dirty · source: knowledge

### Outfit planning

2000. **Decoding event dress codes** — Invitations say 'smart casual', 'cocktail' or 'festive attire' and it is unclear what that actually means or what you own that qualifies.  
    *wedding and party guests, new employees* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: googling and texting friends for advice · source: knowledge
2001. **Dressing for unpredictable weather** — Leaving the house in a sweater on a morning that turns hot by noon, or getting caught without a layer when it cools off, because the outfit was chosen without checking the hourly forecast.  
    *commuters in variable climates* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: checking weather app separately and guessing · source: knowledge
2002. **Learning to shop for a new job dress code** — Starting a corporate or client-facing job and not knowing what a minimal work wardrobe needs, buying either too much or the wrong things.  
    *new graduates, career changers* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: copying colleagues · source: knowledge
2003. **Morning outfit decision paralysis** — Standing in front of a full closet every morning and still feeling like there is nothing to wear, wasting 10-20 minutes and starting the day stressed.  
    *office workers and students who dress daily* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: wearing the same 5 safe outfits on rotation · source: knowledge
2004. **Packing outfits for trips** — Overpacking clothes that never get worn while forgetting one key piece, because there is no plan for which items combine into which day's outfits.  
    *frequent travelers, business travelers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: generic packing checklists; laying everything on the bed · source: knowledge
2005. **Color-matching outfits under bad light** — Picking clothes in a dim bedroom and only realising outside that the navy and black do not match or the shades clash.  
    *people who dress before dawn, color-blind people* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking a partner or using phone flashlight · source: knowledge
2006. **Recreating outfits seen on others** — Seeing an outfit on the street or in a show and having no way to find similar affordable pieces or check whether the closet already has equivalents.  
    *fashion-interested shoppers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: screenshots that are never revisited · source: knowledge
2007. **Repeating outfits in front of same people** — Not remembering what you wore to last week's meeting or the last two dates, and worrying you are visibly re-wearing the same look.  
    *professionals, people dating, teachers* · weekly · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mental notes or scrolling through photos · source: knowledge

### Sizing & fit

2008. **Bra size confusion** — Most people are wearing the wrong bra size and sizing conventions differ between US, UK and EU brands, making online purchase a gamble.  
    *women shopping for bras* · occasional · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: in-store fitting or calculator sites · source: knowledge
2009. **Inconsistent sizing across brands** — A medium in one brand is a large in another and a small in a third, so every new brand means guessing and returns.  
    *online clothing shoppers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: ordering two sizes and returning one · source: knowledge
2010. **Return-window deadlines missed** — Clothes that did not fit sit in the bag past the 30-day return window, turning a free return into a sunk cost.  
    *online shoppers* · monthly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: leaving the package by the door as a reminder · source: knowledge
2011. **Clothes that no longer fit after weight change** — After weight loss, gain or pregnancy, half the closet fits and half does not, with no easy way to tag what is wearable now versus later.  
    *people changing weight, postpartum parents* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: separate boxes labeled by size · source: knowledge
2012. **Kids' clothes outgrown unnoticed** — Discovering a child's pants are too short or shoes too tight on the morning of school, with no record of when sizes were last checked.  
    *parents of young children* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: buying a size up and hoping · source: knowledge
2013. **Measuring yourself accurately at home** — Size charts ask for chest, hip and inseam measurements but taking them alone with a tape is awkward and error-prone.  
    *online shoppers, people ordering custom or formal wear* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: asking a family member to help · source: knowledge
2014. **Remembering own size per brand** — Forgetting that you are a 32 waist in one brand's jeans but a 31 in another, and repeating the wrong order months later.  
    *repeat online shoppers* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: notes app or checking old orders · source: knowledge
2015. **Shoe size conversion and width** — EU, UK and US shoe sizes convert inconsistently and width or brand-specific fit (runs small, runs narrow) is rarely captured.  
    *online shoe buyers, runners* · occasional · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: reading reviews for 'runs small' · source: knowledge
2016. **Tailoring and alteration tracking** — Pants need hemming and a jacket needs taking in, but which tailor has what, what was asked for, and when it is ready gets lost.  
    *people who buy suits or alter clothes* · occasional · pain 2/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: paper receipts · source: knowledge

### Skincare

2017. **Conflicting skincare ingredients** — Using retinol and vitamin C or benzoyl peroxide together causes irritation because product labels do not warn which actives should not be layered.  
    *people using active skincare ingredients* · daily · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: searching ingredient lists on blogs · source: knowledge
2018. **Identifying what caused a breakout** — Skin flares up and there is no way to tell whether it was the new moisturizer, stress, diet, period or a pillowcase, because nothing was logged.  
    *people with acne or sensitive skin* · weekly · pain 4/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: mental guessing; dropping all new products · source: knowledge
2019. **Building a multi-step skincare routine** — Not knowing the correct order to apply cleanser, toner, serum, retinol, moisturizer and sunscreen, and whether to use them morning, evening or both.  
    *skincare beginners* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: watching YouTube and TikTok tutorials · source: knowledge
2020. **Deciphering ingredient lists** — Ingredient lists are long and unreadable, making it hard to spot fragrance, alcohol or a known allergen before buying.  
    *people with allergies, sensitive or eczema-prone skin* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: ingredient-checker websites · source: knowledge
2021. **Forgetting to reapply sunscreen** — Sunscreen is applied in the morning and never again, though it needs reapplying every two hours outdoors.  
    *outdoor workers, beachgoers, people with sun-sensitive skin* · daily · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 4/5 · workaround: none · source: knowledge
2022. **Judging if a product is working** — After 8 weeks on a new acne or anti-aging product it is impossible to tell whether skin improved because there are no comparable before photos.

2023. **Product expiry after opening** — Sunscreen, mascara and serums expire months after opening but the open date is never written down, so expired products get used.  
    *anyone with a bathroom shelf of products* · monthly · pain 3/5 · app-solvable 5/5 · workaround: sharpie on the bottle · source: knowledge
2024. **Too many half-used products** — Drawers full of half-empty bottles bought on hype, with duplicates of the same category and no clear idea what to use up first.
